# Engine Health: Watchdog, Xrun Counter, Clock and State Surfaces

## Motivation

An entire engine-health layer exists in the tree with zero production wiring — dropouts, stalls, and a dead audio engine are all invisible to the user:

- **No watchdog exists anywhere.** A grep for "watchdog" across all main source returns zero hits; the only heartbeat in the codebase guards the project lock file (`ProjectLockManager`), not the engine. Nothing detects "the callback has not run for N ms while the stream is nominally open" — which is exactly the observable signature of a dead PortAudio upcall, a silently-dead JavaSound loop, a stalled ASIO driver, and a physically unplugged interface alike.
- **Xruns are never detected, counted, or reported.** `XrunDetector` is constructed and rebuilt on every reconfigure, but there are zero production callers of `beginTick`/`recordTick`/`reportDropped`/`reportGraphOverload` — the render loop never reports timing into it. Its event stream is exposed (`DefaultAudioEngineController.java:466-467`) with no subscriber, and the story-123 `XrunCounterLabel`/`XrunLogDialog` exist only as a javadoc mention (`XrunDetector.java:105-111`). Worse, the detector's own publish path is not RT-safe: `recordTick` calls `SubmissionPublisher.offer(...)` inline on the caller thread (`XrunDetector.java:203-208`), whose `offer` acquires a `ReentrantLock` — the javadoc's claim that the audio thread "never blocks" (`:33-36`) is false, so it cannot simply be fed from the callback as it stands.
- **Engine state has no UI.** `setEngineState` offers every transition — RUNNING, RECONFIGURING, DEVICE_LOST, STOPPED — to a publisher (`DefaultAudioEngineController.java:661-672`, accessor `engineStateEvents()` at `:481-483`); grep finds no production subscriber. Transport silence never has a visible cause.
- **The clock-lock surface is finished and dead.** `ClockStatusIndicator` — a complete control with a notification hook and a recording-pause callback (`ClockStatusIndicator.java:105`) — is never instantiated in main source. The only `ClockLockEvent` publisher anywhere is the mock backend's test fixture (`MockAudioBackend.java:451`); the `AudioBackend` default is an empty publisher (`AudioBackend.java:568`) that no real backend overrides.

The studio-engineer consequence: a mid-take dropout leaves no evidence, a dead engine looks identical to a project that renders silence, and an external-clock rig losing lock records garbage with no warning — on the primary Windows + ASIO + multi-channel USB target where clocking and USB stalls are routine facts of life.

## Goals

- **Callback-heartbeat watchdog.** The story-337 render guards already bump a heartbeat timestamp in the RT health record (one wait-free atomic store). A low-priority daemon monitor — owned by the engine controller, rebuilt on reconfigure — checks the gap while a stream is nominally open; a gap exceeding a few buffer periods (scaled from the active format, with a floor for scheduling jitter) is a stall verdict that drives the **existing** reaction machinery: engine state → DEVICE_LOST, the controller's notification (visible via story 339's injected sink), and a restart offer. The watchdog *observes* the callback; it never runs on it. It is deliberately backend-agnostic — it detects PortAudio upcall death, JavaSound loop death, ASIO driver stalls, and device disappearance identically, including on backends offering no error callback at all.
- **XrunDetector publish side made RT-pure first.** Tick recording keeps only atomics on the caller thread; event *publication* moves off the hot path (drained by the same monitor/pulse machinery, or offered only from non-RT callers). The `:33-36` javadoc claim becomes true before anything feeds it.
- **Then feed it:** elapsed-time ticks recorded around the render body, dropped buffers reported from the story-337 guards, graph overloads from the worker pool's new failure counters.
- **Xrun counter surfaced:** the story-123 counter lands as a cell in the story-295 session status strip, with click-through to the xrun log dialog.
- **Engine-state cell:** a status-strip cell subscribes to `engineStateEvents()` via the dispatcher — RUNNING quiet, RECONFIGURING informational, DEVICE_LOST in the warning-accent token treatment (tokens, never raw hex). Transport silence permanently gains a visible cause.
- **Clock-lock surfaces live:** mount `ClockStatusIndicator` in the transport/status area bound to the active backend's `clockLockEvents()`, and implement the ASIO-side `ClockLockEvent` publisher in the shim (resync-request / sample-position-not-advancing detection) — so external-clock studios get the lost-lock warning and recording auto-pause the control was built for.

## Goals — Tests

- **Watchdog stall test:** killing the mock/JavaSound callback mid-stream flips the engine-state cell to DEVICE_LOST and raises the (now-visible) device-lost notification within the watchdog window, with a restart offer.
- **Story 327 hardware proof (manual, primary platform):** with the watchdog live, yank the ASIO-capable multi-channel USB interface mid-take on Windows — within the watchdog window the take is sealed under the project (`sealed-by: device-loss`), the rescued take is registered, a WARNING names the device with a Review action, the status reads "Device lost", REC is off; replugging offers resume (record state IDLE, armed set kept, transport at the take anchor). Deferred here from story 327 by user decision (2026-10-10) because no shipped backend publishes `DeviceRemoved`/`DeviceArrived`.
- **Watchdog no-false-positive test:** healthy streaming at the largest supported buffer size never trips the verdict (threshold scales with format, jitter floor respected), and a nominally *closed* stream is never watched — stopping the transport raises nothing.
- **Xrun publish RT-purity test:** extend the bytecode-sentinel idiom — the tick-recording path references no `SubmissionPublisher`, `ReentrantLock`, or logging on the caller thread; publication happens only off-RT.
- **Xrun feed test:** forced-late blocks increment the status-strip counter and populate the xrun log dialog; a dropped-buffer report from a story-337 guard and a graph-overload report from the worker counters each count too.
- **Engine-state cell test:** the cell tracks RUNNING → RECONFIGURING → RUNNING through a settings apply, and shows the warning-token treatment on DEVICE_LOST.
- **Clock-lock test:** a mock clock-unlock event pauses an active recording and shows the indicator's warning state; relock clears it and surfaces the recovery per the indicator's existing contract.
- **Injected-sink test:** the watchdog's DEVICE_LOST notification is asserted through a real injected `NotificationManager` — never `noop()` — proving the story-339 dependency direction end-to-end.

## Non-Goals

- The RT health record, heartbeat store, callback guards, and worker-pool counters — **story 337** (prerequisite; this story reads that substrate).
- Production notification injection — **story 339** (prerequisite; Book 4's stage order runs 339 *before* this story because the watchdog drives controller notify() calls that are discarded by `noop()` until 339 lands).
- OS/backend device-change detection (`DeviceArrived`/`DeviceRemoved` producers) — **story 327** (`RECORDING_RELIABILITY_DESIGN_BOOK.md`), per the story-214 split; this watchdog is deliberately the detection-independent backstop, not a hot-plug watcher.
- Honest PLAYING/RECORDING state when the stream fails to *open* — **story 317** (`AUDIO_ENGINE_WIRING_DESIGN_BOOK.md`).
- Meter/analyzer feeds — **stories 318/319**; the health drain and the metering tap bus are siblings on the same pulse, never one mechanism (book §7).
- Clock-source *selection* UI — existing story **216** (this story supplies the lock-status surfaces it needs); driver-initiated reset notices — existing story **218** (landed; its notices fold into this one health picture rather than growing a parallel surface).

## Technical Notes

- Implements **Stage 5 — Engine Health: Watchdog, Xrun Counter, Clock and State Surfaces** of `docs/design/FAILURE_SURFACING_DESIGN_BOOK.md` (§4.4 architecture, §5.3 health-fact table, §6.2 escalation). **Stage order deviation (deliberate):** Book 4's stages run 313, 336, 337, 339, **338**, 340 — this story lands *after* 339 because its watchdog notifications are noop until the sink is injected, and *after* 337 because its heartbeat, drop reports, and overload counts are story-337 substrate.
- The engine-state accessor is **`engineStateEvents()`** (`DefaultAudioEngineController.java:481-483`) — not `engineStates()`.
- Files: `daw-core/.../core/audio/performance/XrunDetector.java` (`:203-208` publish path made RT-pure; `:33-36` javadoc), `daw-app/.../ui/DefaultAudioEngineController.java` (watchdog ownership + rebuild-on-reconfigure; streams at `:466-467`, `:481-483`; state transitions `:661-672`), `daw-app/.../ui/ClockStatusIndicator.java` (`:105` — mount it), `daw-sdk/.../sdk/audio/AudioBackend.java` (`:568` default publisher), `daw-sdk/.../sdk/audio/AsioBufferSwitchShim.java` (ASIO `ClockLockEvent` publisher, off the RT drain thread), the story-295 status strip (xrun + engine-state cells), `daw-app/.../ui/marshal/FxDispatcher.java` (drains; counters are continuous channels, transitions are events per the `CONTROL_SYNCHRONIZATION_DESIGN_BOOK.md` signal taxonomy).
- The `ProjectLockManager` heartbeat is the in-tree precedent for the watchdog shape (`PROJECT_MANAGER_DESIGN_BOOK.md`); one heartbeat substrate serves every backend — per-backend bespoke stall detectors are book §9.14 rejects.
- Completes existing story **123 — Buffer-Underrun Detection and Reporting** (detector landed; feeding and the counter UI were the missing half). Serves existing story **216 — Hardware Clock Source Selection**; folds existing story **218 — Driver-Initiated Reset Request Handling** (landed) into the same health picture. Cross-references: **337** (substrate), **339** (sink), **327**/**214** (detection half), **317** (open-failure truth), **314** (a silent Play now points at engine state instead of guesswork).
- Research backing: `research-daw` §3 (real-time engine monitoring and engine/UI separation); SKILL `dawg-native-libs` (ASIO shim drain-thread discipline for the clock publisher).

## Note from Story 336

- **The render pump's death is logged but never shown.** `EngineStreamPump` sets its own uncaught-exception handler on the `engine-render-pump` thread (`daw-core/.../audio/EngineStreamPump.java:209-213`). A per-thread handler pre-empts the process-wide default handler that story 336 installs. So when a throwable the render loop does not survive ends the pump (the loop survives `RuntimeException`s only), the only trace is that handler's SEVERE record, "engine-render-pump thread died; audio output is dead until the stream is stopped and reopened". Story 336's log sink now writes it to the log file, but it never reaches a toast or a status cell. For a live session it is the most important failure there is.
- **This story owns surfacing it.** Either the pump's death becomes an engine-health state transition that this story's surfaces show, or that handler delegates to `Thread.getDefaultUncaughtExceptionHandler()` after its log line, so story 336's funnel shows it. daw-core cannot reference daw-app, so the handler must not name the funnel. Test: an `Error` thrown on the render pump reaches a visible surface once, and the log file keeps the SEVERE record.

## Note from Story 337

- **The heartbeat is two counters, not one timestamp.** Story 337 built the heartbeat as two monotonic period counters (its ruling E5), and the watchdog samples them with its own monotonic clock. This story's Goal text that "the story-337 render guards already bump a heartbeat timestamp" is superseded by them.
  - **The render drive.** `EngineStreamPump.renderLoop` beats its lane once per block, after `backend.sink` returns normally, so a `sink` that throws does not beat. Read `AudioEngine.audioHealth().renderDriveBeats()`, monotonic across streams.
  - **The device callback.** PortAudio's `CallbackInvoker.invoke` and ASIO's `AsioBufferSwitchShim.bufferSwitchUpcall` count each period at entry, before their guard. Read `audioHealth().deviceCallbackPeriods()`, an `OptionalLong`: PortAudio and ASIO always report one, and it is empty for a backend with no device thread of its own (Java Sound, WASAPI, CoreAudio, JACK, and the Mock backend unless a test sets its counters), where the watchdog uses the render drive's beats instead.
  - **Why two.** A heartbeat that only the render drive stores keeps advancing through a PortAudio upcall death or an ASIO driver stall: `CallbackBackendAdapter.awaitSinkCapacity` returns at its timeout whether or not the device drained the ring, so the drive keeps cycling. Only the device-callback count stops.
  - `audioHealth().renderDriveEpoch()` counts the render drives claimed, one per render drive started (every stream open and every resume of a paused stream; story 337, ruling E59), and `AudioEngine.isRenderDriveAlive()` is true only while the stream is `RUNNING` and its pump runs. `AudioEngine.isRenderDriveHalted()` tells a halted drive from a paused or closed one: it is true only while the stream is `RUNNING` and its current pump has halted itself (story 337, ruling E69).
  - **An input device opened beside the stream has no heartbeat of the stream's.** Its counters count as input failures, and `deviceCallbackPeriods()` never includes it (story 337, ruling E86). A backend whose own counter report fails reads `deviceCallbackPeriods()` empty from then until it is closed (ruling E78), so the watchdog falls back to the render drive's beats there too.
  - **Java Sound needs a capture heartbeat.** A Java Sound capture line that stalls ends nothing and counts nothing: its read blocks with no native error, and the backend reports no device heartbeat. Detecting that stall needs a capture heartbeat this story's watchdog can read (story 337's Known limits). Until then a stalled line leaves unflagged silence in a take with no notice, which story 337's ruling E84 forbids ("never unflagged silence in a take"). So when this story's capture heartbeat declares a stall during a take, each armed track of that input is flagged in the take's `take.manifest` from the stall on and warned once, as for an input that stops, while the take keeps rolling and the other inputs' tracks keep recording. Verify it with a test whose capture read blocks mid-take without returning: once the stall is declared, that input's tracks are flagged in `take.manifest`, no other input's track is, and the take is still recording.
- **Treat a halted drive as handled, not as DEVICE_LOST.** When `processBlock` faults for one second of blocks in a row (`EngineStreamPump.haltThreshold`, at least 8), a `VirtualMachineError` reaches the render loop, or any other throwable escapes the loop's own catches (story 337, ruling E9), the drive stops and then writes one terminal `audio.render.halted` record, so a reader that sees the record already reads the drive dead (story 337, ruling E60). `isRenderDriveAlive()` then reads `false` while `isStreamOpen()` still reads `true`, and the window's `AudioHealthDrain` stops the transport, closes the stream once the take allows it, posts the halt notice with Restart Audio Engine (for a `VirtualMachineError`, the FATAL copy with Open Log Folder; story 337, ruling E13) and latches the engine-fault fact — the Stop and the close only while that stream's drive is still the halted one (`isRenderDriveHalted()`), the close through `AudioEngine.closeHaltedStream()`, which never waits for the lifecycle lock. A halt whose stream was already reaped, replaced, paused or closed stops and closes nothing and shows the halt notice without a context sentence (story 337, rulings E69 and E70). The render-drive heartbeat stops on purpose there, so the watchdog's stall verdict must first check `audioHealth().total(AudioFailureCategory.RENDER_HALTED)` or the latched fact.
- **The engine-fault fact is this story's cell to render.** `AudioHealthDrain.engineFaultProperty()` is a `ReadOnlyObjectProperty<Optional<EngineFault>>` read on the FX thread; `EngineFault` carries the category, the count, the first and last times and the action. The drain sets it at a sustained failure (three consecutive one-second buckets), a render-drive halt or an input halt, and clears it on a successful restart (story 337, ruling E62) or after one fault-free second of render-drive beats on a render drive started after the latch — a reopened or resumed stream (ruling E59) — with no delta in a restart-eligible category, `audio.render` or any latched category (ruling E63). A halt's fact clears on any render drive started after the halted one (ruling E71); an input halt's only on a drive on a stream opened after it, or a successful restart, never on a resume (ruling E87). The window hands its drain out through the package-private `MainController.audioHealthDrain()`. In story 337 the fact shows only as one ERROR toast and its history entry, with the action (ruling E41); book §3.4 rules out a new `EngineState` constant for it.
- **Restart Audio Engine is one method.** `public Thread MainController.restartAudioEngine()` applies the current settings through `audioEngineController.applyConfiguration(...)` on a virtual thread named `daw-audio-engine-restart`, then, when it succeeded — it returned normally and, if a stream was open when it began, one is open again — clears the fact on the FX thread (ruling E62); a declined record guard keeps the take and restarts nothing (ruling E12). The watchdog's restart offer can reuse it.
- **What the xrun feed can read.** The guards count failures, not dropped buffers: `audioHealth().total(...)` for `CALLBACK`, `SINK`, `PACING`, `INPUT` and `WORKER`, and `audioHealth().backendCounters()` for a backend's own counts. `audio.worker` counts worker tasks that threw, not graph overloads. The ring-overflow drop counters (`CallbackBackendAdapter.droppedOutputBlocks()` and `droppedInputBlocks()`, `EngineStreamPump.droppedInputBlocks()`) are unchanged, package-private and not in the record. `AudioHealthRecord.read`, the drain's one reading per frame, has exactly one consumer, the window's drain, so the watchdog and the xrun feed read totals and heartbeats only. Each single-fact reader (`total`, `backendCounters()`, `renderDriveBeats()`, `deviceCallbackPeriods()`) is one consistent cut of its own; a reader that combines several facts reads one cut of each, not one cut of all (story 337, rulings E79 and E82). Do not copy `XrunEventRingBuffer.poll`: it advances `head` before its caller reads the slot (`head.lazySet(h + 1); return slot;`), so the producer can reuse a slot still being read; the lanes copy a slot, null its references, then advance.
- **Take dropout counts stay this story's.** Story 337 did not put the capture ring's overflow and truncation counts on its channel (ruling E47): `CaptureRing`'s producer-only counters are read by the flush thread, which reports through `RecordingPipeline`'s warning sink. The Recording Reliability book gives dropout counters during a take to this story.
- **What is left of the Note from Story 336.** The pump now catches `Throwable` throughout (ruling E9): a render fault is counted, a sustained one or a `VirtualMachineError` is the halt above, and an outer catch-all turns anything else into the same halt, so the thread no longer dies by unwinding. Its per-thread uncaught-exception handler stays as a last-resort backstop, for a throwable the catch-all itself cannot handle, and that handler's SEVERE record ("engine-render-pump thread died; …") still reaches only the log file. Surfacing that backstop is still this story's.

## Resolution

A dead or stalled audio device is no longer silent. A device that stops calling back and a render drive that stops beating are declared lost on every backend, an unplugged interface among them when its driver stops calling back, within max(2 s, 8 buffer periods) of the last period (5 s while a stream is still starting), by a watchdog that only reads story 337's two heartbeat counters: the stream is closed through the same steps as a host-reported removal, a take in flight is sealed and rescued, the transport stops where the audio stopped, the input meters fall to the floor, the session status strip's new Engine cell reads "Device lost: <name>" in the warning accent, and the notice offers Reconnect, which reopens only the lost device. An input that stops delivering raises story 337's input-halt fault, and during a take its tracks are marked in `take.manifest` at the frame where it stopped and warned once while the take keeps recording. The strip's new Xruns cell counts observed gaps only and opens an xrun log that also lists late blocks, tracks over their CPU budget and stalls; a take's dropouts show beside the count and stay after the take until Clear. The ASIO clock source shows beside the transport status, its lock measured from the driver's sample position and resync messages, so it can warn only of a lost clock that those reports show (ruling E147, a Known limit); an unlock warns and marks the take and never stops it. The render pump's last-resort death is a halt with one notice. Every Goal and every Goals — Tests bullet is answered below, by a named test or by the ruling that changed it ("**Goals and Goals — Tests, one by one.**"); the story 327 hardware proof is the owner's, written out under Verification.

**The ASIO driver messages, and a live defect fixed on the way.** `AsioFormatChangeShim`'s selector constants did not match the ASIO SDK's enum: at HEAD the resync request was 4, supports-time-info 5 and buffer-size change 7. A driver's supports-time-info message, which a driver may send while its buffers are created, therefore ran the buffer-size-change teardown, latched a driver reset and quiesced the stream at its open. The constants are now `kAsioBufferSizeChange = 4`, `kAsioResyncRequest = 5`, `kAsioLatenciesChanged = 6`, `kAsioSupportsTimeInfo = 7` and the new `kAsioOverload = 15` (1, 2 and 3 were right), pinned by `AsioFormatChangeShimTest.selectorConstantsMatchTheAsioSdkEnum`. `dispatch` answers an overload message with ASE_OK and counts it on the backend (`AsioBackend.noteDriverOverload`), and answers a resync request "supported" and counts it (`noteDriverResync`: the arrival time, then the count), never stopping or reopening the stream and never publishing a format-change request (rulings E37 and E38); a reset request and a real buffer-size change keep story 218's reopen. Tests: `AsioFormatChangeShimTest.resyncRequestIsCountedOncePerMessageAndPublishesNothing` and `overloadIsAnsweredCountedOnTheBackendAcrossShimsAndPublishesNothing`; `resyncRequestLeavesTheStreamingBridgeRunning` now asserts that nothing is announced; `resyncRequestSelectorEmitsClockSourceChange`, which pinned the overruled behaviour, is removed.

**What a backend counts.** daw-sdk's new `record BackendDropoutCounters(outputStarvedPeriods, outputDiscardedBlocks, driverOverloads, driverResyncs, inputBlocksDropped, reportsOutputStarvation)` (`NONE`, `plus`, negative counts refused) is returned by the new default `AudioBackend.dropoutCounters()`; its counts are monotonic for the backend object's life, a backend that rebuilds its bridge per open folding the old bridge's counts at close. On ASIO, `AsioBufferSwitchShim` keeps single-writer `volatile long` counters on the threads that already run there: a period that found the output ring empty, counted only once the bridge has played a rendered block; an input block the full capture ring refused; an output block the full output ring refused, once primed; and the older queued blocks a callback skipped to play the newest (`AudioBlockRing.drainLatestCountingInto`). On PortAudio, `CallbackBackendAdapter` counts starved periods in `deviceCallback` once primed and its rings' overflows. A block an input publisher drops for a subscriber is counted by no backend: it is told to the subscriber it was dropped for (`InputBlockLossListener`, ruling E208, under PR #994's second review below, which replaced amendment A3's count in `AudioBackendSupport` and the adapter's count of its drain loop's drops), so `JavaxSoundBackend` and `MockAudioBackend` report `NONE`; and a backend's own device callback reports the captured blocks a failed period lost through the new default `AudioBackend.failedCallbackInputBlocks()` (ruling E211). `AudioEngine.dropoutCounters()` reads the open stream's backend lock-free and answers `NONE` with no stream or when the backend's report throws. `AudioBackend.takeDriverResyncArrivals()` (`DriverResyncArrivals.NONE` by default; `AsioBackend` overrides it from its `ResyncArrivalRing`) hands the xrun feed the arrival of each resync request no earlier call took up (rulings E270 and E279). Tests: the ten new tests of `AsioBufferSwitchShimTest`, among them `anEmptyRingAfterTheFirstPlayedBlockCountsOneStarvedPeriod`, `emptyCallbacksBeforeTheFirstRenderedBlockAreStartUpNotStarvation`, `aFullCaptureRingCountsEachBlockItLoses` and `olderBlocksACallbackSkipsToPlayTheNewestAreCounted`; `AsioBackendDropoutCountersTest` (6, through the backend's own open and close); `AudioBlockRingTest.theCountingDrainReportsEverySlotItConsumedAndPlaysTheNewest`; `CallbackBackendAdapterTest.emptyPeriodsCountAsStarvedOnlyAfterTheFirstPlayedBlock`, `aReopenStartsUnprimedAndACallbackWithoutARingCountsNothing`, `ringOverflowsReachTheDiscardedAndDroppedInputCounts` and `aBlockDroppedAtPublishIsToldToTheSubscriberThatDroppedItAndNotCounted`; `AudioBackendSupportTest.aDroppedBlockIsToldOnlyToTheSubscriberThatDroppedIt` and `aListenerThatThrowsLeavesTheOtherDroppingSubscribersTold`; `BackendDropoutCountersTest` (4); `ClockSourceTest.aBlockTheMockDropsIsToldToTheSubscriberThatDroppedItAndNotCounted`; `JavaxSoundBackendTest.aBlockThePublisherDropsIsToldToTheSubscriberThatDroppedItAndNotCounted`; and `AudioEngineBackendCountersTest.dropoutCountersAreTheOpenStreamsBackendsAndNoneWithoutAStream`.

**The xrun detector, real-time pure and fed.** `XrunDetector` is rewritten. `recordTick(long)` is its one real-time method, `@RealTimeSafe`: plain stores into single-writer `volatile long` fields, the overshoot before its count, with no allocation, logging, publisher, lambda or atomic read-modify-write, and it cannot throw. `reset()`, `beginTick()`, `Tick` and the injected clock are gone. `publishPending()` is `synchronized`, runs off the real-time thread and offers at most `MAX_EVENTS_PER_PUBLISH` (16) events per call, the counts staying exact; `reportDropped(count)` makes one offer per call; a closed publisher never makes a method throw; the borrowing constructors leave the publisher open at `close()`. The class Javadoc's claim that the render thread never blocks is now true. `EngineStreamPump.renderOneBlock` reads `System.nanoTime()` around `engine.processBlock(...)` and ticks only when it returned `true`; the pump takes the detector once at construction from `AudioEngine.setXrunDetector`. The controller owns one `SubmissionPublisher<XrunEvent>` that every detector borrows, so `AudioEngineController.xrunEvents()` survives every rebuild; both rebuild sites publish what is pending, swap the detector and hand the new one to the engine before the reopen. `RealTimeSafeContractTest.theRenderDriveLoopIsGuardClean` follows the drive into `XrunDetector` and must reach `recordTick`; its allow-list is still two entries and the class then documented fourteen invariants (invariant 10's wording, in both copies of the `dawg-annotations-reflection` skill too); it documents fifteen since the last change set of PR #994's second review added ruling E272's. Tests: `XrunDetectorTest` (10, rewritten in place: `onTimeBufferEmitsNoEvent`, `lateBufferEmitsBufferLateEvent`, `veryLateBufferEmitsBufferDroppedEvent`, `recordTickAssignsIncrementingFrameIndices` and `resetClearsAllCountersAndFrameIndex` are gone with the API they pinned), among them `ticksAreClassifiedAndCountedWithoutTouchingThePublisher` and `publishPendingOffersAtMostTheCapAndKeepsTheCountsExact`; `EngineStreamPumpTest.aRenderedBlockSlowerThanTwoPeriodsTicksTheEnginesDetectorAsDropped` (the real wiring) and `aFaultedBlockIsARenderFaultAndNeverATick`; `EngineReconfiguringStateTest.theXrunPublisherOutlivesEachDetectorRebuildAndTheEngineTicksTheNewDetector`.

**The pump's last-resort death.** `EngineStreamPump`'s per-thread uncaught-exception handler writes its SEVERE record ("engine-render-pump thread died; …") first, then calls the pump's own `halt(failure)` inside a catch-all, so the death is recorded as the drive's halt and story 337's drain gives it the halt treatment once: the transport stopped, the stream closed once the take allows it, the fact latched, the ERROR with Restart Audio Engine or, for a `VirtualMachineError`, the FATAL copy with Open Log Folder (ruling E33). Exactly one method is named `halt`, and main code never calls `getUncaughtExceptionHandler()`. Test: `EngineStreamPumpTest.aLastResortThreadDeathIsLoggedThenRecordedAsTheDrivesHalt` (one SEVERE record carrying the error, written before the `RENDER_HALTED` count moves; then the count 1, the error as last failure, the pump halted).

**The heartbeat watchdog and the engine health monitor.** `EngineHeartbeatWatchdog` (daw-app `ui`) is pure decision logic over a small probe and an injected clock; it owns no thread. Each tick samples epoch, alive, counters and inputs, alive, epoch, and a sample counts only when both alive reads are true and the epoch did not move. It watches the device-period count where the backend reports one and the render drive's beats otherwise (the Note from Story 337). The window is `windowFor` = max(2 s, 8 buffer periods) (`MIN_WINDOW_NANOS`, `WINDOW_PERIODS`), re-read from the engine's format at each re-baseline, and until the first advance in a render-drive epoch it is `startupAllowanceFor` = max(5 s, the window) (`MIN_STARTUP_ALLOWANCE_NANOS`; rulings E1 and E2); `allowForAClockSourceSwitch()` grants the same allowance after a clock-source switch the person sent (ruling E107), and after a session of the driver's control panel ends (ruling E228, under PR #994's second review below). It re-baselines when the epoch changes, when the drive is not alive, when the watched kind changes, when a tick arrives later than twice its interval and when the controller refuses a verdict; one verdict per epoch is latched, and only when the controller accepts it. A paused or closed stream, a halted drive and a stream being closed are never judged (ruling E9). Inputs are judged per capture input (`CaptureInputSnapshot`): quiet time accrues only over ticks in which the render beats advanced, and the device periods too where the backend reports them, so a dead device is the stream's verdict, not an input stall; an input that delivered and then stayed quiet for the window is declared `STOPPED`, and one that delivered nothing within the start-up allowance while a recording capture is active is declared `NEVER_DELIVERED`, each handle once. `DefaultAudioEngineController` runs it on one single-thread daemon scheduler, "daw-engine-health-monitor", at normal priority and a 100 ms fixed delay (`ENGINE_HEALTH_TICK_MILLIS`), created only by `startEngineHealthMonitor()` (lock-free, idempotent, a no-op after a stop or a shutdown) and stopped by a flag and `shutdown()`, never `shutdownNow()`. One monitor serves the controller's life; a reconfigure does not rebuild it, the watchdog re-baselining on the new epoch instead. Each tick runs the watchdog, then the xrun feed, then `publishPending()`, inside a catch of `RuntimeException` and `VirtualMachineError` that logs and abandons that tick only. `MainController` starts the monitor right after the engine-state bridge subscribes and stops it in window disposal before the bridge is cancelled. Tests: `EngineHeartbeatWatchdogTest` (18, a scripted probe and a fake clock), among them `healthyStreamingAt2048And4096FramesAt44kNeverTripsOverTenSimulatedSeconds`, `aTwentyMillisecondSchedulingGapAt32FramesAt192kIsNoVerdict`, `aStillnessShorterThanEightPeriodsOfALargeBufferIsNoVerdict`, `aDriveThatIsNotAliveIsNeverJudgedHoweverLongItsCountersFreeze`, `oneLateTickReBaselinesAndNeverFires`, `aStreamStillStartingGetsTheStartUpAllowance`, `theDevicePeriodsAreWatchedWhereTheBackendReportsThemWhileTheRenderDriveKeepsCycling`, `anInputIsNeverDeclaredWhileTheDevicePeriodsFroze` and `anInputThatNeverDeliveredIsDeclaredOnlyWhileATakeCapturesAndOnlyAfterTheStartUpAllowance`; `EngineHealthMonitorTest` (13, a real engine and controller), among them `healthyStreamingAt2048And4096FramesOnRealThreadsNeverTripsAcrossStopPauseResumeAndApply`, `aStreamClosedMoreSlowlyThanTheWindowIsNeverDeclaredLost`, `theStartedMonitorDeclaresAStallOnItsOwnThread`, `theMonitorStartsOnlyWhenAskedOnceAndNeverAfterItWasStopped` and `aTickThatThrowsIsLoggedAndTheScheduleSurvivesIt`; `FailureSpineWiringSourceTest.theWindowStartsTheEngineHealthMonitorAfterTheBridgeAndStopsItBeforeTheBridgeIsCancelled`.

**The stall verdict and the loss it declares.** `onHeartbeatStalled` is `private synchronized` and re-checks the verdict under the controller's monitor: the controller not closed, the monitor not stopped, no configuration change in progress, no driver control panel open and no start-up allowance asked for that the watchdog has not taken up (`EngineHeartbeatWatchdog.isAllowanceRequestPending()`, ruling E228), no driver format change pending or scheduled and not yet done, the drive alive, the same epoch, and the same heartbeat still reading the stalled count; a refusal makes the watchdog re-baseline. On acceptance it logs a WARNING under the slug `audio.watchdog`, then runs `declareDeviceLost(STALLED, notice)`, the one method the host's removal (`onDeviceRemoved`) also uses: it reads the render-drive epoch and whether a recording capture was active before anything closes, then halts recording capture, stops the output, stops the engine and publishes DEVICE_LOST with its cause, and only then sends the notice; while a loss is being declared `isDeviceLost()` already answers `true`. The stall's notice is ruling E4's, with Reconnect, and the xrun log gets one Stall entry (ruling E19). The removal notice now reads "Audio device disconnected — playback stopped" and the arrival texts "playback stays stopped" (ruling E67). `EngineStateChange` gains `LossCause {NONE, REMOVED, STALLED, REOPEN_AFTER_RESET_FAILED}`, NONE exactly when the state is not DEVICE_LOST; its two-argument constructor keeps every earlier call site (REMOVED for a loss), and the cause travels through `EngineStateBridge`, `TransportController.onEngineState` and `RecordCoordinator.onDeviceLost` overloads that keep the old signatures. A driver-requested reopen (`FormatChangeRequested`, any reason) that fails over a stream that was open ends as a loss with the cause REOPEN_AFTER_RESET_FAILED and ruling E113's notice with Reconnect, after "Reconfiguring audio engine…" (ruling E134); with no stream open a request reopens nothing and ends "Audio engine reconfigured". A driver request while the device is lost is dropped at its arrival and again under the monitor, and one that concerns a stream Reconnect has just reopened is held and handled once the loss has ended. Tests: `EngineHealthMonitorTest.aRenderDriveWhoseBeatsStopIsDeclaredLostThroughTheRemovalsStepsWithTheStallsNotice` (the order of the steps pinned on the acting thread), `frozenDevicePeriodsAreAStallWhileTheRenderDriveKeepsCycling`, `theHostsRemovalSaysPlaybackStoppedAndCarriesTheRemovedCause`, `anOpenDriverPanelRefusesTheVerdictAndAStallThatOutlastsItIsDeclaredAfterwards`, `aConfigurationChangeInProgressRefusesTheVerdictUntilItEnds` and `aDriverFormatChangeThatWasReopenedNoLongerHoldsBackAVerdict`; `HeartbeatStallDuringATakeTest.aRecordingDeviceThatStopsCallingBackSealsRegistersAndReportsTheTake` (through the production bridge: `sealed-by=device-loss`, the take registered, the Review notice led by the stall, the engine notice with Reconnect); `DriverRequestWhileTheDeviceIsGoneTest` (10), among them `aResetWhoseReopenFailsIsADeviceLossWithItsOwnNoticeAndReconnect`, `aRequestThatArrivesWhileTheDeviceIsLostIsNotRunAfterTheDeviceReturns` and `aReopenTakenOffTheQueueButNotDoneRefusesTheStallVerdict`; `StalledDeviceLossCopyTest` (5, one per report site); `EngineStateChangeTest` (3); `EngineStateBridgeTest.theFirstListenerReceivesTheWholeChangeBeforeTheTransportInTheSameTurn`; `ClockBadgeWhileALossIsDeclaredTest.theBadgeShowsNothingFromTheStartOfALossDeclarationNeverAClosedStream`.

**Reconnect.** `DefaultAudioEngineController.reconnectLostDevice(boolean takeRescued)` runs on a virtual thread named "daw-audio-device-reconnect", one at a time (`reconnectInFlight`), and does nothing unless the state is DEVICE_LOST. It opens only the lost device, through `AudioEngine.reopenAndPark(DeviceId)`: the open, the device check and the park or the close run in one section of the engine's lifecycle lock, and the announcements after the unlock; the answer is `PARKED`, `CLOSED_ON_ANOTHER_DEVICE` or `OPENED_BY_ANOTHER`, so a stream another caller opened (a Play that won the lock) is never parked or closed, and that caller's own announcement ends the loss. The lost device's own return (`onDeviceArrived`) shares the method. With a take rescued at the loss the record coordinator speaks ("<name> reconnected. Tracks are still armed; press Record to continue."); with none the controller says "<name> is responding again. Press Play to continue."; a failure says "<name> could not be reopened: <reason>. Check that it is connected and powered, then choose Reconnect again, or choose another device in Audio Settings." with Reconnect, and the state stays DEVICE_LOST (rulings E6, E7 and E57). `<reason>` is one of ruling E112's four, read from daw-core's new `StreamOpenRefusedException` and its `Reason` (`PREVIOUS_STREAM_NOT_RELEASED`, `UNUSABLE_FORMAT`, `NOT_AVAILABLE`), thrown at nine engine sites; a landing on another device reads "it is not available", anything untyped reads "the audio driver refused to open it", and the engine's own message goes to the log only. A Reconnect whose thread cannot be made or started raises `RECONNECT_NOT_STARTED_MESSAGE` once, at the warning level, with Reconnect (ruling E139). Whether a take was rescued is read on the FX thread when the person clicks, from the record coordinator's state (`DeviceLossReactions.takeRescued`), and the Engine cell's click goes through `MainController.reconnectTheLostDevice`. A Reconnect does not reset the xrun count (ruling E71). Tests: `ReconnectLostDeviceTest` (12), among them `theDeviceBackWithARescuedTakeReopensParksAndLeavesTheWordsToTheRecordCoordinator`, `aLadderThatLandsOnAnotherDeviceIsClosedAgainAndTheLostDeviceIsStillWatched` and `aFailureTheEngineDoesNotNameIsTheDriversRefusalAndItsMessageNeverReachesTheNotice`; `ReconnectAgainstOtherOpensTest` (20), among them `aPlayThatWaitedBehindReconnectsOpenFindsTheStreamParkedAndPlaysIt`, `aPlayWhoseOpenWasInFlightKeepsItsRunningStreamAndItsOwnAnnouncementEndsTheLoss`, `requestsWhileOneReconnectIsUnderWayAreDroppedAndALaterOneRuns`, `theReasonANoticeGivesIsAlwaysOneOfTheFourAndNeverTheEnginesWords` and the six tests of a Reconnect whose thread cannot be made or started; `TheReconnectQueryReadsTheRecordStateTest` (2); `StreamOpenRefusalReasonTest` (4).

**After a loss: the transport, the playhead, the meters and the leads.** `DeviceLossReactions`, the engine-state bridge's first listener, reacts to DEVICE_LOST of every cause in the FX turn of its delivery, before the transport: the health drain's `streamLost(lostDriveEpoch)` with the epoch the controller read as it declared the loss (before it closed the stream, for a removal or a stall; after the reset's own close, for a reset whose reopen failed), then `InputLevelMonitorRegistry.silenceAll(lostDrive)` with the same epoch (each input meter whose state belongs to that drive or an older one to the floor, its clip latch kept, and every meter muted through that drive until the engine builds a newer one, rulings E175, E185 and E202), each reaction caught and logged on its own; the window's status model is fed next (`andThen`). A playback-only loss stops the transport in place (`TransportController.stopInPlaceAfterTheDeviceLoss`: state Stopped, the playhead where the audio stopped, one Stopped event, never `stopTransport`), keeping the message of a take start the loss aborted (ruling E65). After a take's rescue the playhead returns to its position at the loss, as the rescue's last step; rescued clips are placed from `TrackCapture.firstWrittenBeat` through `laneStartBeat`, never from the playhead (ruling E66). Output meters needed no change (ruling E64). The rescued take's lead takes the loss cause (`RecordCoordinator.deviceLossLead`): "<name> stopped responding during recording", "<name> could not be reopened after a driver reset during recording" or "<name> was disconnected during recording", each followed by the existing tails (rulings E5, E113 and E125); the refusal of Record or Play while the device is lost (`unavailableWhileDeviceLostMessage`) and the arrival of another device follow the cause too (ruling E126), and a loss that ends on another device says which device is which (`lossEndedOnAnotherDeviceMessage`, ruling E130). Tests: `DeviceLossLeavesTheTransportAndMetersTruthfulTest` (4: `aPlaybackOnlyLossStopsTheTransportWhereTheAudioStoppedWithOneStoppedAnnounced`, `aRescuedTakeKeepsItsOwnStopAndThePlayheadReturnsToWhereTheLossStoppedIt`, `aMidiCountInOverAPausedTransportEndsStoppedAndKeepsTheAbortsMessage`, `theInputMetersFallToTheFloorAtTheLossAndKeepTheirClipLatch`); `DeviceLossReactionsTest` (7); `DeviceLossLeadsDuringATakeTest` (2); `PlayWhileTheDeviceIsLostIsRefusedNamingTheDeviceTest.aRefusalAfterAStallSaysTheDeviceStoppedRespondingAndHasNotBeenReopened`; `InputLevelMonitorTest.silenceDropsTheLevelToTheFloorAndKeepsTheClipLatch` and `InputLevelMonitorRegistryTest.silenceAllDropsEveryMonitorToTheFloor`; `TransportControllerFxThreadBytecodeSentinelTest.theDeviceLossTurnTouchesNoStorageAndNeverWaits`; `FailureSpineWiringSourceTest.theWindowHandsTheBridgeItsDeviceLossReactionsAndTheReconnectQueryBeforeItSubscribes`.

**Device names.** daw-app's new `DeviceNames` holds one rule (ruling E111): a device has no name to show when its name is blank, when it is the default placeholder (`DeviceId.isDefault()`) or when it is `TransportController.UNKNOWN_DEVICE`; a sentence then says "The audio device" at its start and "the audio device" inside. `DeviceNames.audioNoticeDevice` gives story 337's device-fault notice the backend's name for the placeholder (`MainController.audioDeviceDisplayName` delegates to it), and that notice reads "1 error" for one and "from the audio device" with no name at all (ruling E128; `failure.audio.fault.device` and its new `.unnamed` form). On the automatic device the loss surfaces use the unnamed form, while the clock notices name the driver the backend resolved (ruling E131, a Known limit). Tests: `DeviceNamesInTheSentencesThePersonReadsTest` (7 methods, 10 tests with the parameterised ones) and `FailureCopyTest.theDeviceFaultCountsOneErrorAndSaysTheAudioDeviceWithNoNameAtAll`.

**The drain at a loss.** `AudioHealthDrain` holds a device-side run (`audio.callback`, `audio.sink`, `audio.pacing`, `audio.input`, `audio.device`) back from its sustained escalation while the heartbeat has not advanced since the run began (`FailureTrack.Heartbeat.advancedSince`: the render beats advanced, or the device periods advanced where both readings have them), and lets it proceed as before once it advances (ruling E68). `streamLost(lostDriveEpoch)` restarts the device-side tracks, which drops the held run, gates device-side escalation and input-halt escalation for readings of that epoch or older (an input halt that such a reading carries is counted and logged with the input's other failures, with no toast of its own and no latch: its own record says it is part of the device loss, or, while the input's 30-second window is open or its fault is latched, the input's next record accounts for it, or a later summary line whose entry repeats the input's last notice, the one shown or the record of an earlier fold, rulings E70, E155 and E156; one of the lost stream that the drain first reads on a newer drive escalates, rulings E151 and E159, a Known limit), and clears a fact latched on a drive at or before that epoch when every category latched since the fact last cleared is device-side, so never a halt's latch, never one latched together with a processing fault, and never a newer drive's (rulings E69 and E161). The drain hands that halt to the funnel's `foldIntoDeviceLoss` with the input's own lost-block warning as its sentence: the fold's own record says "part of the device loss; no toast of its own (story 338, ruling E70)", while story 337's two folds keep their detail (ruling E156), and a history-only summary entry whose notice is the fold's own record repeats that warning, never the audio-device-fault sentence (ruling E155). A device-side count read on the lost drive or an older one after the loss reached the drain, that halt included, still goes into its track's total, and the drain then restarts the track's run, so the count begins or joins no sustained run (ruling E157); a count of the lost stream that the drain first reads on a newer drive is that drive's and can begin its run, and any device-side count other than an input halt and the lost blocks of its frame, read on the lost drive or an older one after the loss reached the drain, still goes through its kind's warning path (rulings E159 and E160, Known limits); a category that is not device-side keeps its run across the loss, as before, unless the loss clears a device-side fact (ruling E69), which restarts every track's run, as every clear has since story 337 (ruling E158). The drain's nested `Track` and `OneShot` moved to the package-private `FailureTrack` and `OneShotSubject`, so `AudioHealthDrain.java` is 899 lines. Tests: the eight new `AudioHealthDrainTest` tests (`aDeviceSideFaultOnEveryBlockWithAStandingHeartbeatNeverEscalatesNorLatchesBeforeTheLoss`, `aHeldRunEscalatesAsBeforeOnceTheHeartbeatAdvancesAgain`, `theLossDropsTheHeldRunSoALaterDriveCountsAfresh`, `theLossClearsADeviceSideFactLatchedOnTheLostDrive`, `theLossClearsNeitherAHaltNorANewerDrivesFact`, `readingsOfTheLostDriveNeverEscalateAndANewerDriveEscalatesAgain`, `anInputHaltOfTheLostStreamRaisesNothingAndOneOfALaterStreamDoes` and `theDeviceLossReactionsHandTheDrainTheEpochTheControllerReadBeforeTheClose`); the 40 existing ones stay, `oneFrameReadsTheBackendOnce` included, and two of them, `anInputHaltEscalatesAtOnceWithTheDeviceFaultCopyAndRestart` and `anInputHaltThatLandsMidFrameIsCountedOnce`, now expect "1 error" for one (ruling E128). For rulings E155 to E157, eight more `AudioHealthDrainTest` tests: `anInputHaltFoldedAtTheLossWritesARecordThatSaysItIsPartOfTheDeviceLoss` (E156); `aFoldedHaltAndALostBlockOfTheLostDriveAreSummedUpAsLostInput`, `aFoldedHaltAndALostBlockOfALaterStreamAreSummedUpAsLostInput` and the control `aHaltFoldedInsideTheWindowOfAShownWarningIsSummedUpInThatWarningsWords` (E155); `anInputHaltFoldedAtTheLossStartsNoRunThatALaterStreamCompletes`, `aLostBlockOfTheLostDriveStartsNoRunThatALaterStreamCompletes` and `aCallbackFaultOfTheLostDriveStartsNoRunThatALaterStreamCompletes`, each with the later stream's third second escalating as its negative control, and the control `aProcessingFaultKeepsItsRunAcrossTheLoss` (E157). For ruling E158, `aLossThatClearsALatchedDeviceFaultRestartsAProcessingRun`: with a callback fault latched on the drive, a worker that failed in the two seconds before the loss and fails again in the next second gets no ERROR and the cell no fact, and it escalates at its third consecutive second read after the loss, its count and "since" time including the failures from before the loss. For rulings E159 to E161, three more `AudioHealthDrainTest` tests, each pinning a Known limit as built: `aLostBlockOfTheLostStreamFirstReadOnANewerDriveJoinsThatDrivesRun` (E159 (c)): a lost input block of the lost stream that the drain first reads after the loss reached it and after the stream was reopened on a new render drive shows the input's warning and no ERROR, and with the new drive beating and one more lost block in each of the next two seconds, the second of them raises one ERROR with Restart Audio Engine and latches the input fault, one second sooner than the new stream's own three (`aLostBlockOfTheLostDriveStartsNoRunThatALaterStreamCompletes` stays its other half, with the drain's frame before the reopen); `aDeviceSideCountReadOnTheLostDriveAfterTheLossStillShowsItsWarning` (E160): a callback fault of a kind never shown before, read on the lost drive after the loss reached the drain, gives one toast and one history entry at the warning level with Restart Audio Engine in the callback's first-occurrence words, and no "Audio engine fault" entry, no engine fault and no escalation; and `theLossClearsNoFactWhileAProcessingFaultIsLatched` (E161): after a worker's ERROR and then a callback fault's ERROR on the drive, the loss clears nothing, the fact staying with the category CALLBACK and the callback fingerprint escalated, and one fault-free second on a newer render drive then clears it, while in its second run, `aProcessingFaultThatKeepsFailingKeepsTheFactTheLossDidNotClear`, a worker failure in each frame of that second keeps the fact. The funnel's part is pinned by `FailureFunnelRealTimeReportTest.aDeviceLossFoldsRecordSaysItIsPartOfTheLossAndItsSentenceIsTheOneATailRepeats`; story 337's fold tests (`aRenderFailureInTheFrameOfAHaltIsFoldedIntoTheHaltNoticeWithARecordOfItsOwn`, `aFirstWorkerFailureCoincidingWithAFirstRenderFailureIsOneNotice` and `FailureFunnelRealTimeReportTest.aFoldedSurfaceWritesOneRecordCarryingItsWeightAndNoToast`) still read "folded into the notice of the same frame; no toast of its own", and `anInputHaltOfTheLostStreamRaisesNothingAndOneOfALaterStreamDoes` still has a later stream's halt escalate.

**The capture heartbeat and a stalled input.** `EngineStreamPump.InputSubscriber.onNext` and `AudioEngine.AdditionalInputSubscriber.onNext` count each delivered block first. `AudioEngine.captureInputs()` returns an immutable snapshot of the open stream's inputs (`CaptureInputSnapshot`: an opaque handle, the source name, the delivered count, whether it is the stream's own input), built lock-free. `declareInputStalled(handle, InputStallKind)` is lock-free and idempotent and acts on the handle's own object, so a reopen between the decision and the call cannot end the new stream's input: the stream's own input sets its terminated flag, an input opened beside the stream goes through `terminateInputSource`, and story 326's path then flags and warns. `STOPPED` records the stall in the engine's `InputStallLedger`, attached once to the health record as an input counter source, which stores the fault (`input from “<name>” stopped delivering audio`, at most 120 characters) before its count, so story 337's drain raises the input-halt ERROR with Restart Audio Engine and latches the fact (ruling E11); `NEVER_DELIVERED` does not (ruling E52). `InputStallLedger`'s writer joined `RealTimeSafeContractTest.BACKEND_COUNTER_WRITERS`. `isRecordingCaptureActive()` answers whether a take's capture is installed. The starved-block stamp (amendment A6): the pump sets `AudioEngine.setCapturedInputStarved(true)` before `processBlock` when it polled no captured block while the stream's input is live, and clears it after on every path; the stream's own capture callback copies it into the ring slot, and siblings never read it; `setCapturedInputStarved` and `isCapturedInputStarved` are `@RealTimeSafe` and walked by the render-drive and capture sentinels. Blocks already queued when the stream's input is declared ended are rendered at full width before the zero-width view takes over (ruling E47), and since PR #994's second review a block polled after the first zero-width block is discarded unrendered (ruling E276). On the flush thread an input that stops writes `input-stopped` with the routing flag (ruling E13), and the seal writes it for an input's silence that did not reach the take after the last block kept from its ring (rulings E277, E282 and E303), as does the block with which an input delivers again after its dropped silence in the same take (ruling E302; PR #994's second review below), a closed starved run of the stream's own input is one counted `input-silence` line per track of that input (rulings E34 and E43), the trailing run at a seal is written and counted except at a device-loss seal (rulings E48, E79 and E90), and a run that ends in a declaration is that declaration (ruling E49). The warnings are ruling E14's, with ruling E127's unnamed forms (`CaptureFlushService.flagUnavailable`), and in a take recorded with loop recording the stop reads "… at m:ss.s into lap N of the take; …" (ruling E172); an opened width too narrow for the route keeps the routing-mismatch sentence (ruling E54, `flagNarrowRoute`). Tests: `InputStallCaptureTest` (26, eleven of them the capture-loss tests of PR #994's second review below), among them `theStreamsInputDeclaredStalledMidTakeFlagsOnlyItsTrackAndTheTakeKeepsRecording`, `anInputOpenedBesideTheStreamDeclaredStalledRecordsItsStopAndWhereClockedSilenceResumed`, `anInputThatNeverDeliversDuringATakeIsFlaggedFromTheTakesStartWithoutAnInputHalt`, `aStallRescuedSealMarksTheTrailingSilenceOnTheStreamInputsTracksOnlyAndCountsNone` and `aHealthyTakeStoppedByThePersonWritesNoSilenceAndCountsNoStarvedBlock`; `AudioEngineInputStallTest` (4); `MultichannelInputCaptureStory326Test.terminatedPrimaryInputKeepsClockedSilenceUnavailableAndFlagged`, changed for ruling E47; and the production wiring, `InputStallThroughTheMonitorTest` (3: `theMonitorDeclaresAStreamInputThatStoppedDeliveringThroughTheProductionWiring`, `aTakeWhoseInputStopsKeepsRecordingAndItsTrackIsFlaggedAndTheWarningReachesThePerson`, `anInputThatNeverDeliveredIsDeclaredDuringATakeAndNotOutsideOne`).

**The take manifest.** `TakeManifest` gains three keys, with no new field on `gap=` or `routing-unavailable=` and `MANIFEST_VERSION` still 1: `input-stopped=<trackId>|<stopFrame>|<resumeFrame>` (`InputStopped`), `input-silence=<trackId>|<startFrame>|<frames>` (`InputSilence`) and `clock=<unlocked|locked|resync>|<frame>` (`ClockMark`, `ClockEvent`). Frames are transport frames at the take's rate, like `gap=` (ruling E50); for an input opened beside the stream the stop frame is the take's start plus the frames it delivered and the resume frame is where clocked silence resumed (ruling E55). In a loop take `input-stopped` is never split, its frames normalised into the loop range, a resume frame smaller than the stop frame meaning the wrap lies between them, and a frame may lie in lap 1's pre-roll from the take's start (rulings E89 and E115); a delivered end exactly on a loop boundary is the loop end (ruling E176), and a resume frame larger than the stop frame does not rule out a wrap (rulings E186 and E191); `input-silence` lines are split at the wrap (ruling E75). The grammar Javadoc gives every key with its unit, `routing-unavailable` included; the parser is strict for the new keys and an older manifest reads them as empty. Tests: `TakeManifestTest.theInputStoppedInputSilenceAndClockKeysRoundTrip`, `eachNewKeyIsPartOfTheManifestsIdentity`, `aManifestWithoutTheNewKeysReadsThemAsEmptyAndAsBefore`, `theNewKeysLeaveTheGapsAndRoutingFlagsAnOlderReaderSees` and `aMalformedNewKeyFailsTheManifestNamingTheLine`.

**Punch and loop takes, and what the file holds.** Three package-private classes do the flush thread's frame arithmetic: `FrameSpans`, `StarvedRuns` and `FileLossCounts`. `CaptureCallback.fillBlock` stamps each slot with the loop range when a loop is on (`setLoopStartFrame`, `setLoopEndFrame`), and with the transport's lap (`setTransportLap`, PR #994's review below). A starved block adds only its frames inside the punch region and counts as one block when that part is not empty; a run wholly outside the region writes and counts nothing; a tail at the seal follows the same rule and is clipped to the frames the file received before a disk-exhaustion or write-failure seal (rulings E76, E87 and E90). A ring-overflow gap is counted whole when its extent (to the next block received from that ring, or its own blocks when none follows, normalised into the loop range) overlaps the punch region, and since PR #994's second review below its `gap=` line stands at the end of the block the ring delivered before the run, and a run whose next block starts at or before that line with no loop wrap between them is judged by its blocks' own extent forward from the line or back from that block's start (rulings E86a, E104, E273 and E285); a gap a lap long or longer counts when any frame the transport passed, followed across every wrap, lies in the region (rulings E180 and E182); truncated frames count only inside the region (ruling E86c); losses before the rings stay counted over the whole span (ruling E86b, a Known limit). An input that ends before punch-in is flagged from punch-in with "delivered no audio", one that ends after punch-out gets nothing, and at a loop re-entry the sentence depends on whether the take ever delivered audio to the file (rulings E77, E88 and E118, and the manager's reading under the rulings); when the input's own frames are in a later lap, the flag's stop is the later of the punch-in and the loop start (rulings E179 and E186). The take's figures read the file's counts (`RecordingPipeline.getFileOverflowBlocks`, `getStarvedInputBlocks`, `getFileTruncatedFrames`, `getFileTruncationEpisodes`). A healthy take stopped by the person writes no `input-silence` line (ruling E79). Tests: `TakeMarksInPunchAndLoopTakesTest` (44), among them `aStarvedRunAcrossTheLoopWrapIsTwoLinesThatEndAtTheLoopEndInALoopRecordTake`, `aStarvedRunAcrossPunchInIsMarkedFromPunchInAndItsFirstBlockCountsOnce`, `aDiskExhaustionSealCountsItsTailUpToTheLastFrameWritten`, `overflowGapsCountOnlyWhenTheirExtentReachesIntoThePunchRegion` and `anInputThatEndsBeforePunchInAndIsDeclaredAfterPunchOutIsFlaggedFromPunchIn`; `PunchTakeLossesAgainstTheFileTest` (4); `FrameSpansTest` (7), `StarvedRunsTest` (4) and `FileLossCountsTest` (19).

**The xrun feed and the xrun journal.** `XrunFeed` (daw-app `ui`), ticked by the monitor, takes one sample per tick in the watchdog's order, with one `totals()` read of the health record and one `dropoutCounters()` read of the engine. The number counts the growth of callback, render and sink faults and of the backend's starved periods, never-played blocks, driver overloads and driver resyncs (rulings E17 and E38; amendment A15), only while both samples saw the drive alive in one epoch, after the start-up allowance has ended at the first advance of the watched heartbeat, and in an interval in which that heartbeat advanced. What grows in an interval without an advance is carried and counted at the next advancing sample of the same stream, and dropped on a stall, a close, a pause or a reopen, a carried resync's take mark with it (rulings E73 and E231). Each tick also takes up the arrivals of the resync requests the backend counted (`AudioEngine.takeDriverResyncArrivals()`), whether or not it writes, carries them with the growth and forgets them with it, and tells the journal the dates it holds (rulings E279 and E291). Late blocks (the detector's late and dropped ticks) become Late entries and never raise the number (ruling E17). Every gap is also reported to the detector, which keeps the SDK stream fed. A new detector after a settings change, Restart Audio Engine or a driver's reset request restarts the count and keeps the entries (rulings E22 and E148). Log lines carry the slug `audio.xrun`. `XrunJournal` keeps the exact counts since the last reset, the newest 100 entries (`MAX_ENTRIES`), the gap arrivals of the last 30 s (`RECENT_WINDOW_SECONDS`), whether the driver reports output underruns and, apart from the log, the resync marks owed to the take (`ResyncMark`, one per arrival of the requests a resync entry stands for (`appendResyncs`), at most 600, `RESYNC_MARKS_KEPT`, the two oldest merged past that with their counts summed and the earlier arrival kept), which neither the 100-entry limit nor Clear nor a restarted count removes (rulings E225 and E226); its kinds are `GAP`, `DISCARDED`, `OVERLOAD` and `RESYNC`, which raise the number, `LATE`, counted apart, and `SHED` and `STALL`, which raise nothing; entries carry their detail's typed arguments and their arrival time, a Resync entry's the arrival of its first request, or, when the feed holds no arrival for it, the feed's read before the one that counted it (rulings E279 and E292); `BLOCK_NEVER_PLAYED` is ruling E72's one detail for both causes; every instance method is `synchronized` and calls nothing out; the static `keepMerging` works only on its caller's marks. `ShedLogSubscriber` writes `SHED` entries, shown as "Budget", for the `TrackDegraded` events whose policy is not `DoNothing`, one per run of a track's markings (PR #994's review below), saying only that the track went over the CPU budget, nothing for `TrackRestored`, and one "not logged" entry for the markings its full buffer lost (rulings E42, E174, E181, E184, E199 and E200; amendment A5). Tests: `XrunFeedTest` (29), among them `eachObservedGapRaisesTheCountByExactlyItsOwnCountAndReachesTheSdkStream`, `aLateBlockIsALateEntryAndNeverRaisesTheCount`, `whatAccruesWhileTheDevicePeriodsStandStillIsTheStallsAndNotCounted`, `aHiccupWhileTheDevicePeriodsStandStillIsCountedWithItsFullCountWhenTheyAdvanceAgain`, `blocksDiscardedBeforeTheDevicesFirstPeriodAreNeverCountedAndOneAfterItIs`, `aResyncIsLoggedAtTheTimeTheDriversMessageArrived`, `aStallVerdictWritesOneStallEntryAndRaisesNothing` and `aSettingsChangeRestartsTheCountAndKeepsTheEntries`; `XrunJournalTest` (16); `ShedLogSubscriberTest.eachDegradationThatNamesAPolicyIsOneEntrySayingOnlyThatTheTrackWentOverItsBudget`.

**A take's dropouts and its clock marks.** `TakeDropouts(blocksLost, truncationEpisodes, framesTruncated, recording)` gives `dropouts()` = blocks lost plus truncation episodes. `TakeDropoutTally` counts the blocks lost as the file's overflow blocks, plus the starved blocks, plus the engine's `capturedInputBlocksLost()` since its read made as the take started recording (`capturedInputBlocksLostAsATakeStarts()`, rulings E261 and E262), the losses before the rings at every site the code can see, except those future story 481 owns (rulings E45, E206, E208 and E211); what the take lost before the rings never falls while it records and is latched when it stops, also when that count cannot be read then (rulings E214 and E245), while the take's own file counts are read live, so its seal can still raise them after its Stop; Clear forgets only a finished take, a MIDI-only take clears the figures, and another project's transport shows none of them (rulings E24 and E109). `TakeFrameHistory` keeps four seconds (`SPAN_NANOS`) of `(System.nanoTime(), transport frame)` samples taken on the FX pulse while an audio take records, and the newest sample at or before the span's edge; and each sample it gives up that is the newest at or before a date a resync mark may still be owed at (the arrivals the xrun feed holds and the marks the journal has not handed over, `XrunJournal.resyncDatesOwed()`, read at each sample through `RecordCoordinator.setResyncDatesOwed`; ruling E291); an unlock and a resync use the newest sample at or before the event's evidence time, a relock the oldest sample at or after it, and evidence from before the take's reach sample, or after the reach when the newest sample at or before it is no longer kept, is dated at the take's start-frame (amendment A11; rulings E62, E177, E183 and E221), through `RecordCoordinator.noteClockEvent`, which marks only while the take records with a pipeline whose capture has not been sealed (ruling E280; PR #994's second review below), and `RecordingPipeline.transportFrameOf(double)`, which converts with the take's own tempo and rate. A relock with no sample at or after its evidence yet is held, written at the next sample at or after its evidence or at the Stop, and dropped at a device loss, an early seal or a retire; an unlock while the take is marked unlocked writes nothing, and so does a relock while it is marked locked; an unlock, or a start mark, that finds a relock held cancels it: neither the clock=locked line nor a clock=unlocked line at the start-frame is written, and the take stays marked unlocked from the clock=unlocked line it already holds (rulings E221, E258). Each resync the Xruns count counts is marked once, at the frame sampled at or before its own arrival, whatever the log's limit or its Clear dropped (rulings E40, E225, E226, E270 and E279). An unlock that is on show at the badge's first measuring look of the take, or replayed there with its evidence before the reach, is marked clock=unlocked at the start-frame, once (rulings E173 and E222), and so is an unlock that is the first decision of a measurement begun before the take's reach, such as that of the stream Record reopens (ruling E269). In take.manifest the clock=unlocked and clock=locked lines are written in the order of their evidence: read in line order they alternate, starting with clock=unlocked, each clock=locked line ends the region the clock=unlocked line before it opened, and a clock=unlocked line with no clock=locked after it marks the take to its end. Their frames need not rise from line to line, because a loop take wraps and each mark is dated to bracket its evidence. A clock=resync line marks a point and is not ordered against them in either direction: it can follow a clock=unlocked or clock=locked line whose evidence is later than the resync's arrival, and precede one whose evidence is earlier. A reader pairs clock=unlocked and clock=locked lines in line order, reads each clock=resync line at its own frame, and never sorts the lines by frame (ruling E253, as consults 47 and 51 corrected it). Tests: `TakeDropoutTallyTest` (16), `TakeFrameHistoryTest` (20), `TakeDropoutsAndClockMarksTest` (25, among them `aClockEventIsDatedAtTheFrameSampledWhenItsEvidenceWasSeenAndOnlyWhileTheTakeRecords`, `theTakesFiguresFollowItFromItsStartToAfterItsStopUntilTheNextTakeOrAClear`, `aClockEventDuringTheCountInMarksNothingAndTheTakeStartsAfterIt` and `anUnlockWhoseFirstBadAnswerPrecedesTheTakeIsMarkedAtTheTakesStartFrame`), `TakeClockMarksOfTheTransportTest` (5) and `EngineHealthFeedTest.aResyncIsMarkedAtTheFrameOfItsArrivalNotOfThePulseThatHandsItOver`, `eachResyncOfAnEntryIsMarkedAtTheFrameSampledAtOrBeforeItsOwnArrival`, `resyncsCarriedThroughAStandstillOfFourPointSixSecondsAreEachMarkedAtTheirOwnFrame` and `resyncsCarriedThroughAStandstillOfTwentySecondsAreEachMarkedAtTheirOwnFrame`.

**The Engine cell, the Xruns cell and the xrun log.** `EngineHealthStatus` (daw-app `ui/status`) is the window's FX model: one `ReadOnlyObjectProperty<EngineStateChange>`, so a reader never sees a new state with an old device or cause; the latched fault, bound to `AudioHealthDrain.engineFaultProperty()` in `attachAudioHealthDrain` and unbound in window disposal; the xrun figures; the take's dropouts; the newest 100 log lines; and `UnderrunReporting {NOT_ASKED, REPORTED, NOT_REPORTED}` (ruling E116). It is fed by the bridge's first listener, by that binding and by `EngineHealthFeed`, one pulse participant that drains the journal into worded lines, stamps "near m:ss.s" only while the transport rolls, hands every resync mark the journal keeps for the take to the take's clock marks every pulse (rulings E225 and E226) and refreshes the figures. `SessionStatusStrip` takes it through a third constructor; the two existing constructors delegate with a model that is never fed. `EngineHealthCells` builds the two cells. The Engine cell is hidden while there is nothing to say (RUNNING, STOPPED), informational for RECONFIGURING ("Engine: reconfiguring…"), in the warning accent for DEVICE_LOST ("Device lost: <name>", "Device lost: audio device"), and critical for a latched fault ("Engine fault: <words>"), with ruling E27's tooltips and spoken texts as amended by rulings E93, E95, E96 and E133; a click, Enter, Space or the accessible fire action runs Reconnect while the device is lost and otherwise the fault's own action; it never goes into the overflow (ruling E98). RECONFIGURING is published inside `applyConfigurationWithPermit` after the permit and before the stream stops, so a declined record guard publishes nothing (rulings E26 and E63). The Xruns cell always shows "Xruns N", quiet at zero and in the warning accent while the count grew in the last 30 seconds or while a take's or the last take's dropout figure above zero is on show ("Xruns N · Take dropouts K", "Xruns N · Last take dropouts K"; rulings E22, E24 and E92), with ruling E23's tooltip and spoken text as amended by rulings E94, E102 and E116 and ruling E18's underrun sentence only for a driver that answered that it reports none. `StatusStripCell` gains an action (`onActionProperty`, `fire()`, primary click, Enter, Space, the accessible fire action; the BUTTON role and focus while it has one) and a focus ring through the forwarded `-ssc-focus` token. `XrunLogDialog extends DawgDialog<Void>`: non-modal, one per window, titled "Xrun Log", with the sections "Take" and "Log", the take line ("This take: …", "Last take: …" or "No take to report."), the live list ("Nothing logged." when empty), Clear in the body (enabled when the list is empty) and Close (ruling E97). The copy is in `Messages.properties`, read through `EngineHealthText`. Tests: `EngineHealthCellsTest` (15), `EngineStateCellTest` (3: `theCellTracksRunningReconfiguringRunningThroughASettingsApply`, `aRemovedDeviceShowsTheWarningTreatment`, `aStalledDeviceShowsTheWarningTreatmentItsNoticeOffersReconnectAndTheCellReconnects`), `EngineReconfiguringStateTest` (5), `XrunCountAndLogSurfacesTest.aLateBlockIsLoggedButNotCountedAGapIsBothAndClearEmptiesBoth`, `EngineHealthFeedTest` (17), `EngineFaultReachesTheEngineCellTest.theWindowsEngineHealthModelMirrorsTheDrainsFaultUntilTheWindowIsDisposed` and `FailureSpineWiringSourceTest.theWindowWiresItsEngineHealthModelToTheBridgeTheDrainTheStripTheFeedAndTheShedLog`.

**The status strip's collapse and its keyboard path.** The collapse thresholds, in 100 % pixels, are Workspace 1200, Xruns 1170, Schema 1140, Lock 1080, Disk 1020 and Journal 960 (`SessionStatusStripSkin`'s `T_*` constants and `SessionStatusStrip.TWO_ROW_MIN_WIDTH`), so the order is Workspace, Xruns, Schema, Lock, Disk, Journal, and the Engine cell never collapses. While the Xruns cell carries its accent it stays on the strip, each later cell goes into the overflow one band earlier, and squeezed further its text is ellipsized with its tooltip and spoken text whole (ruling E103). The "⋯" overflow button is focus-traversable, with the tooltip "More status" and the spoken text "More status. Press to show the status cells that do not fit." (ruling E132); the keyboard opens it, the focus moves into the popover onto a cell that can fire, never stays on a hidden cell, and the popover closes itself when its content changes. Tests: `StatusStripKeyboardAndXrunsAccentTest` (8), among them `anAccentedXrunsCellStaysOnTheStripAtEveryWidthAndOtherCellsGoFirst`, `whileTheAccentKeepsTheXrunsCellEachLaterCellCollapsesOneBandEarlier`, `theOverflowOpensFromTheKeyboardItsCellFiresOnEnterAndEscapeClosesIt` and `theOverflowButtonSaysWhatItDoesAndEnterOpensIt`; `StatusStripOverflowPriorityTest.betweenXrunsAndWorkspaceOnlyWorkspaceCollapses` and `betweenSchemaAndXrunsWorkspaceAndXrunsCollapse`, which replace `betweenSchemaAndWorkspaceOnlyWorkspaceCollapses`; `SessionStatusStripUiScaleTest`, `StatusStripNoLabelSetTextTest` and `UiScaleLayoutTest`, updated for the sixth collapsible cell.

**The ASIO clock-lock monitor.** daw-sdk's new package-private `AsioClockLockMonitor`, owned by `AsioBackend`, runs one platform daemon thread, "asio-clock-monitor", per open, polling every second (`POLL_INTERVAL`). It starts at the end of a successful open unless a driver reset is latched, is stopped by a flag, a generation bump and an unpark as the first statement of `close()`'s locked body, never by an interrupt (an interrupted caller of `AsioControlThread.call` would poison every later ASIO call), pauses for a driver reset and never takes the driver lifecycle lock. The stream that answers a driver's reset request starts with its first two polls as switch intervals (ruling E271). Each stream starts fresh (ruling E82): the source is read on the first tick, and a device with a single source or a source classified internal is never polled (ruling E60). A bad answer is "no clock" (-995), "not advancing" (-996) or an OK answer with an unchanged sample position, each counted only if the device periods advanced since the previous poll (rulings E29 and E59). The poll interval is the unit (ruling E106): an interval is bad if it holds a bad answer or a resync; an interval whose periods stood still, or whose poll only set a new position baseline with no resync, is not judged and breaks both runs (rulings E120, E121 and E140); two consecutive bad intervals unlock (`BAD_ANSWERS_TO_UNLOCK`); the unlock is dated at its first bad answer, or no later than the measurement's start when no good answer of that measurement came before it (ruling E269); a bad answer that resync requests make is dated at the arrival of its interval's first request, or at the interval's start when the backend no longer keeps that arrival (ruling E279), and three consecutive good ones relock an unlock of this measurement (`GOOD_ANSWERS_TO_RELOCK`). `AsioBackend.selectClockSource` calls `switchSent()` before its driver call and `switchAnswered(accepted)` after it on every exit; while a switch is pending every interval is a switch interval, and the window lasts two polls (`SWITCH_INTERVALS`) counted from the driver's answer, accepted or refused, during which resyncs are counted, logged and marked but judge nothing; only an accepted switch makes the source be read again (rulings E122, E137 and E138). A source that cannot be read keeps the lock state, judges nothing and is read again every ten intervals (`SOURCE_RETRY_INTERVALS`, ruling E80). While the driver's control panel is open nothing is judged: a poll that reads it open sends the driver nothing, and a poll judges nothing when it reads the panel open before it sends anything or after its answer and evidence, or when a session of the panel closes between its two reads of the closed-session count. After a close, the source is read again by the first poll that sends anything once a poll has found the close counted at its first read of the count, and resyncs belong to the person's change from the end of the last poll interval judged before the panel opened to the end of the second poll that reads the panel closed, finds the close counted at its first read of the count and is not stopped by its second reads (rulings E248, E249 and E260). `AsioControlThread` counts every session that ends, however short, also when the panel's call throws, and raises the count before the panel reads closed; in a close's two switch intervals resyncs judge nothing while a bad poll answer is still judged (rulings E227, E248, E249 and E260; consult 49's words; PR #994's second review below). The monitor publishes a level, `ClockLockStatus(device, sourceId, serial, lock, evidenceNanos, transitions)` with `Lock {NOT_MEASURABLE, CHECKING, LOCKED, UNLOCKED}`, whose transitions are the measurement's newest unlocks and relocks (`ClockLockStatus.Transition(sequence, locked, evidenceNanos)`, an odd sequence an unlock, at most `TRANSITIONS_KEPT`, 32; the five-argument constructor is kept), each appended in the same write as its change and so before its event is offered, a start, and a change of the source or of whether it can be measured, emptying them, read lock-free and without a driver call through `AudioBackend.clockLockStatus()`, `AudioEngine.openStreamClockLockStatus()` and `AudioEngineController.clockLockStatus()`. `ClockLockEvent` (now with `evidenceNanos` and a compatibility constructor; amendment A4) is still published on `AsioBackend.clockLockEvents()` and re-published by the controller's own publisher, but the badge does not subscribe. The native shim gains `asioshim_getSamplePosition` (`asioshim.h`, `asioshim.cpp`, the README's export table), bound as an optional symbol by `AsioCapabilityShim` with `int64_t` outputs read as `JAVA_LONG`; no agent has compiled it, and without it every external source reads `NOT_MEASURABLE` (ruling E100). Tests: `AsioClockLockMonitorTest` (88 runs, with one test's six parameterised rows), among them `aSwitchAnsweredWhileAPollRunsExemptsTheResyncsOfThatPollAndTheNextOnly`, `resyncsJustBeforeAndRightAfterASourceSwitchAreNotABadAnswerForTheNewSource` and the panel, transition and reset-window tests of PR #994's second review below; `AsioBackendClockLockTest` (18, through the backend with stub driver, streaming and capability shims), among them `closeNeverInterruptsAPollInsideTheControlThread`, `aDriverResetPausesThePoll` and the two reset-window tests of PR #994's second review below; `ClockLockStatusTest` (9); `AsioControlThreadTest.eachModalCallCountsOneClosedSessionWhenItReturnsOrThrows`; `AsioBackendResyncArrivalTest` (1); `ResyncArrivalRingTest` (6); `AudioEngineClockLockStatusTest` (2); `ClockSourceTest.mockClockLockPublisherOutlivesCloseAndStampsTheEvidenceTime` and `aClockLockEventBuiltWithoutEvidenceReadsUnknown`; `ClockSourceSelectionTest` (5); `ControllerClockLockEventsTest` (1); `NativeLibraryDetectorTest.asioshimExportsRequiredSymbolsOnWindows`, whose export list gains the symbol.

**The clock badge.** `ClockStatusIndicator` (821 lines; its take-mark seam is the package-private `TakeClockMarks`) is mounted once, immediately before the transport status label (`MainController.mountClockStatusIndicator`, ruling E32), and installed by `installClockStatusIndicator` as a pulse participant whose stream-opened callback only asks for an early look; window disposal undoes both. It is a model of facts, events that only change the model, and one pure decision: `decide(Facts)`, whose one call site is `showTheDecision`; the state on show is never an input. The facts are whether the device is lost, whether the stream is open, the label (`ClockLabel`: device, source, rate), what this stream's own reads answered (`OwnRead`: `UNANSWERED`, `NO_SOURCES`, `LABELLED`) and the lock status of this stream. The decision: lost, or no label, is `HIDDEN`; a closed stream is `CLOSED`, the dim label with the closed texts; an open stream whose own read has not answered is `STARTING`, the dim label with ruling E119's texts; one whose device answered that it has no clock source is `HIDDEN`; a labelled open stream is `CHECKING` while its status names another source, `QUIET` for an internal source, `NOT_MEASURABLE` with no status or a `NOT_MEASURABLE` one (ruling E100's dim view), `QUIET` when locked, `UNLOCKED` when unlocked and `CHECKING` otherwise. The badge notices a new stream only by the serial of daw-core's new `OpenedStream(serial, device, sampleRate)`, which `AudioEngine` publishes once per open (`lastOpenedStream()`) under the lifecycle lock, after the open and before the stream reads as open, and replaces only by a later open's, so the serial never decreases, also when a start fails.
