A label is carried into a new stream only on the same device and rate, compared exactly by backend and name (rulings E142 and E143), and a label that this stream's own status had already replaced is not a closed view (ruling E144, built as "hidden"). The stream's own read asks about the device the stream opened (`StreamClock.read(DeviceId)`), also after the stream closed, which is what keeps another device's answer out of the closed view (ruling E136). The source list is read off the FX thread, asked again no sooner than a second after a failed read and never with two reads in flight (ruling E141). Notices and take marks are the package-private `ClockLockAnnouncer`'s, from the transitions the status carries (PR #994's second review below): a measuring look replays the transitions past the last one the badge applied for that serial, a serial's first measuring look replays them from its start unless a loss or a close consumed that serial, a first kept transition more than one past what was applied is a gap, and a status that carries no transitions is judged by its level alone (rulings E217 to E220); one look gives at most one notice, an unlock at the warning level, a relock at the information level (ruling E110), or, when unlocks came and went between two looks, one warning that says how many (ruling E216), the take's sentence chosen by `TakeRecording {NONE, AUDIO, WITHOUT_AUDIO}` and, for the combined warning during an audio take, by whether the take is marked unlocked after the look (rulings E108 and E223); an unlock of the same serial on show when the badge first sees an audio take recording is marked once at the take's start-frame, before the changes that look hands over (rulings E173 and E222). Each loss and each close consumes, at its first look, the serial the badge last applied, whatever status that look read, and a look reads the lock status before the loss (rulings E252, E259 and E268). The texts are rulings E83, E100, E101 and E119; the unlocked flash is a pseudo-class on style tokens, steady under Reduce Motion: with motion allowed it steps every 0.5 s, with no opacity, effect or transition, between the rest look and a two-tone ring over the badge's outer 2 px (`-text-hi` in the outer 1 px, `-text-on-accent` in the next), with the `-danger` fill and the words inside it unchanged (rulings E232 and E233). Every state carries a 1 px border, transparent except in the four views where the lock is not checked (closed, starting, checking, not measurable), which are an outlined chip with no fill, `-text` words in a `-text-mute` outline (ruling E234), so the badge is 2 px wider and taller than before in every state and a change of state between views that show the same words never resizes it. The old pause handler and the "recording quality compromised" notice are gone (rulings E30 and E31). Tests: `ClockStatusIndicatorTest` (129 runs), among them the table test `eachRowOfTheDecisionTableShowsItsState`, `theDecisionKeepsItsInvariantsOverEveryCombinationOfTheFacts`, `theStateOnShowIsDecidedInOnePlaceAndNeverRead`, `aQuickPlayThenStopBeforeTheLabelReadLandsNeverHidesTheBadge`, `aNewStreamWhoseDeviceAnswersNoClockSourcesHidesTheCarriedLabelForGood`, `aStreamOnAnotherDeviceCarriesNoLabelAndShowsItsOwnAnswer`, `aStopWhileTheStatusNamesAnotherSourceHidesTheLabelWhateverTheReReadAnswersInEitherOrder`, `aReopenOnAnotherDeviceWhoseStartFailsDuringPlaybackNeverBringsBackTheEarlierLabel` and `underReduceMotionTheUnlockedTreatmentIsSteady` (six tests that pinned the old badge are gone with it); the real wiring, `ClockBadgeDuringATakeTest` (23), among them `anUnlockDuringATakeIsShownNotifiedAndMarkedTheTakeKeepsRecordingAndARelockClearsIt`, `aDeviceLossHidesTheBadgeWithNoRelockNoticeOrMark`, `aPlayWhoseStartFailsAfterItsOpenIsReadOnceAndKeepsTheClosedView`, `anApplyWhoseRestartFailsAfterItsOpenShowsTheStartingViewThenTheClosedOne` and `theControllersApplyOfAnUnchangedRequestKeepsTheDimView`; `AudioEngineOpenedStreamTest` (10), among them `theSerialNeverDecreasesOverAnOpenAStopAFailedStartAndAnOpen`; `ClockBadgeWhileALossIsDeclaredTest.theBadgesReadsOfTheControllerAreNotSynchronized`; `FailureSpineWiringSourceTest.theWindowBuildsWiresAndMountsItsClockBadgeOnceAndUndoesItInItsDisposal`; `UiScaleLayoutTest`, which mounts the badge through the same static method.

**What the reviews found and what each round changed.** The story was built in ten slices (S1, its fix pass, S2a, S2b, S3a, S3b, S4a, S4b-1, S5 and S4b-2), then reviewed and reworked:
- **The independent review** had five lenses, each leaving the tree as it found it (A: an adversarial mutant and probe run; B1 and B2: the closed set; C: the native side and the driver contract; D: what the person at the desk sees and hears; E: the audio path and the take on disk). It found, by category: production wiring no test ran (the capture-input stall reached the engine through `WatchdogReactions.inputStalled`, whose emptied body survived the whole daw-app module; the scheduled half of the stall refusal; the stall lead at the MIDI-stop-failure site; the device-loss FX turn outside the FX-thread sentinels; `setCapturedInputStarved` outside the render walk); a clock badge that followed one-shot events and so could stay on "Checking lock." for a whole stream, never saw a clock source chosen in Audio Settings and dropped events it was not looking for; a driver reset during a take that never reached DEVICE_LOST when its reopen failed; a Reconnect that could park or close a stream another caller had opened; engine prose and "<default>" in front of the person; and a start-up gate of the xrun feed that no test pinned.
- **Rework R1** redesigned how the badge learns the lock: it reads the driver side's lock level (`ClockLockStatus`) on every FX pulse instead of following events, because a level read on every pulse cannot be missed, replayed late or held by a refresh in flight. **Rework R2** built the rest: ruling E113's cause, the drops of a driver request during a loss, `startAudioOutputOwned` for Reconnect and the device's return, one Reconnect at a time, `StreamOpenRefusedException` and E112's reasons, `DeviceNames` (E111), the feed's start gate on the watched heartbeat, the resync's arrival time and `UnderrunReporting` (E116); it adopted reviewer A's probe as `InputStallThroughTheMonitorTest`.
- **The confirming review** answered must-fix with five defects: a Play that waited behind Reconnect's open was then parked by it; "<default>" on the input-stall ERROR; the unnamed removal lead; "is still disconnected" after a stall or a failed reset reopen; and a badge that hid at every open and vanished at a quick Stop. Its shoulds included a flaky new test, the "already open" path of Reconnect, the unpinned resync mark at the arrival time, ruling E106's "consecutive" not held, a reset request dropped at Reconnect's open, the overflow's focus landing on a cell that cannot fire and the "⋯" button without accessible text. Consult 8 ruled E119 to E134 on them.
- **The fix round** (F1 and F2) built the badge's starting state (E119), the loss read as a level only, the lock monitor's one interval judge with E120's gate on good answers too and E122's exemption, `AudioEngine.reopenAndPark` (the open, the device check and the park or the close in one section of the lifecycle lock), the Reconnect that finds a stream already open, the reopen-under-way flag for driver requests, the answer generation of the underrun question, the sentences of E125 to E132, and the strip's bands and focus rules. Its confirmation found every item confirmed but one: the badge kept an older stream's label in the starting view on a device that answered "no clock sources" (ruled by E135 to E142).
- **The redesign R3.** Three reviews in a row had each found a missing row in the badge's state table, each row made by the fix before it. The cause was the badge's inner design: four places chose a state, and the state on show was an input of the next decision. So the badge was not patched again: it was redone as a model of facts, events that only change the model, and one pure decision with a table test over every row. The same round built E137 and E138 (the switch window starts at the send, holds while the driver has not answered and lasts two polls after its answer, also for a refused switch) and E139's notice, and replaced the code comments that cited the manager's section numbers ("decision 4.8"); the amendment numbers the comments still cite resolve under Decision records. Its confirmation judged the redesign right and found two defects in what fed the decision: one stream open counted as two new streams, and "same device" decided by name across backends.
- **F3** made the engine publish one fact per stream open (`OpenedStream`) and the badge notice a new stream only by its serial, compared devices exactly, and covered a Reconnect thread factory that fails or makes nothing. Its confirmation (a first run was cut off by an interrupted session before it reported; a second reviewer run finished it from the saved probes, mutants and logs) found one root defect: F3 put the previous stream's record back when a start failed after its open, so the serial went backwards and, after a Settings change of device whose new stream failed to start, the old device's dim label could come back.
- **F4** removed the put-back: the engine's record of the last opened stream is replaced only by a later open's, its serial never decreases, the badge has no rule of its own for a failed start, and ruling E144 is one remembered status and one check where a label leaves a stream. Its confirmation passed: the published serial cannot go back or repeat (one write site, under the lifecycle lock, after the open and before the stream reads as open), and one open cannot be raised as new twice. It traced every path that starts a stream and named two that start one off the FX thread without the engine controller's monitor (an instrument put on a channel while the transport is stopped, and the start of a recording), which consult 12 ruled a recorded limit (E146).
- **T1**, tests only, took the confirmation's three shoulds: `ClockBadgeDuringATakeTest.anApplyWhoseRestartFailsAfterItsOpenShowsTheStartingViewThenTheClosedOne` (a failed restart through the controller's real `applyConfiguration` off the FX thread: the starting view, then the closed one, one read, no notice, no mark; E146's condition), `theControllersApplyOfAnUnchangedRequestKeepsTheDimView` (E81 through the real apply), and `TakeClockMarksOfTheTransportTest.aTakeBeingPreparedRecordsNothingAndAClockChangeThenMarksNothing` (E146's first two Nevers), plus one Javadoc sentence on `ClockStatusIndicator`'s remembered status. No production behaviour changed.
- **The acceptance read and the closing round.** The studio engineer's acceptance read (consult 13) answered NOT ACCEPTED. It found that the build keeps what was ruled, apart from two rulings it accepted as built and amended (E22's Never by E148, E9's timing by E150), and asked for changes to words only: one tooltip (E101's quiet tooltip promised a warning the lock measurement cannot always give), four limits the person will meet that were not written down, the owner's checks (one of them expected a result the build cannot give, by design) and one acceptance case of future story 472; it ruled E147 to E150. The manager took them in one closing round: the code part changed one message key (`clockBadge.tooltip`, now E147's sentence), its pin in `ClockStatusIndicatorTest` and three Javadoc passages (`EngineState.RECONFIGURING`, `EngineState.DEVICE_LOST` and the class Javadoc of `EngineFault`), and no code path; the gate then ran again; then these documents were corrected; then a second fact-check confirmed each required item by quoting it. That fact-check also found the `EngineFault` class Javadoc inexact (its new sentences read as holding an input halt back until the heartbeat advanced, which the code does only for a sustained device-side run, and as never clearing an input halt's fact at a loss, which the code does when ruling E69's conditions hold; its older sentence left out story 337's ruling E87, that an input halt's fact clears "never on a resume of the paused stream", which ruling E154 reads for story 338's cell as a resume of a stream opened before the halt was latched) and four older Javadoc passages stale (`XrunJournal`'s class Javadoc and `restartCount`, which named only a settings change and not the driver's reset request of ruling E148; `DefaultAudioEngineController.declareDeviceLost`, which left out ruling E113's caller; and `RecordCoordinator.onDeviceLost`'s `@param cause`, which left out REOPEN_AFTER_RESET_FAILED). The five passages were corrected in a comments-only step, whose sentences the manager wrote from the code and checked afterwards, and the gate ran a third time after it. The engineer had written that it accepts once the items marked REQUIRED land as written. The owner's process does not take a conditional for an acceptance, so the manager asked for a second acceptance read.
- **After the closing round.** Three checks then ran side by side, and none changed the repository. Consult 14, the engineer's second acceptance read, answered ACCEPTED, with no conditions and no new ruling. It went through the 27 items of consult 13 one by one and found nothing missing. It wrote that its verdict covers the story as it then stood, and that a later correction from the two agents then running, the third fact-check and the second Linux run, that changes no sentence about what the person at the desk hears or sees, or what they get on disk, does not reopen it. The third fact-check answered NOT CONFIRMED. It found four of the comments-only step's five passages true in every clause. In the remaining one, the `EngineFault` class Javadoc, it found two inexact clauses that the step had kept from story 337's text. It found four more passages in the same files that contradicted the corrected ones, and five sentences of the last document touch false or overtaken. Every number of the third gate that it recounted was true. The second Linux run, on the third gate's sources, was green (Verification). Two corrections one passage at a time had left stale comments, so the second comments-only step swept by fact instead: four facts (who declares a loss and when capture halts, when the Xruns count restarts, when the engine fault is set and cleared, and the render-drive epoch read at the loss), in twelve files, comment lines only. The fourth fact-check read it before any gate, five times, each read by an agent that wrote none of it, with a correction pass after each of the first four. The first read found one false clause the step wrote, which repeated a gap in the manager's own brief. The second found one more clause the step wrote. The third found the step's own text true in every clause, and three false statements about the engine fault in comments the step had left. The manager had set a limit of three reads, and the third reached it with those three open. The manager then ordered a correction pass by enumeration, every sentence about the fault checked against every site that sets or clears it, and two more reads. The fourth read found three more false statements, one of them written by that pass. The fifth answered CONFIRMED. The fact-check's observations that were left as they stood were true sentences that could say more, words outside the four facts, history labels, test sources and three questions about the code. The fourth gate ran on those sources: a first start on 2026-11-18 was cut off, a second start on 2026-11-21 failed on three daw-core tests outside the story, and a third start passed (Verification). While it ran, the manager put the three questions to the engineer. Reading the code, the fifth read had listed three narrow cases near a device loss in which the Engine cell's fault does not do what a ruling said; no test and no hardware had found them. They are questions of what the person at the desk sees, so the manager did not decide them. Consult 15 accepted two cases as built and sent one, story 337's behaviour, already shipped, to a later story: rulings E151 to E154, four Known limits and future story 473. The third comments-only step then corrected the comments in `AudioHealthDrain` that the amended E70 overtook, comment lines only, and a document touch wrote consult 15 into the story. A fact-check agent read each, the step before the next gate. The fifth fact-check found one false clause in the step ("where it is only logged") and confirmed the step after a correction. The sixth found one defect, in future story 473's test list, and confirmed the touch after a correction. The fifth gate ran on those sources and passed. The fifth fact-check's first read had also read the code behind the amended E70's words "only a log record". It found that an input halt folded at a loss is also counted, is not always recorded at once, and is recorded in story 337's words for another kind of fold. Its second read added that such a count can start the three-second run that a new stream then completes. The manager put these to the engineer too. Consult 16 ruled that story 338 must change in three points (E155, E156 and E157), after consult 14's acceptance. It rewrote E70 whole, changed one Known limit and added one. The Linux run planned after the fifth gate was stopped before it began, because the code was about to change.
- **Fix round L.** Round L built E155 to E157 in `AudioHealthDrain` and `FailureFunnel`, with the engineer's acceptance cases as tests, written first and run red (Verification). Its review, by an agent that wrote none of it, passed at its first read, with no must-fix and four "should" items: two comment sentences that were true in their place but loose read alone; round L's reading of E157 as covering the device-side categories only; and four test classes missing from round L's targeted run, which the reviewer ran green. Beside the review, a document touch wrote consult 16 into the story, and the manager asked the engineer whether round L's reading of E157 was the ruling. Consult 17 answered yes, as built, with no new ruling, and gave E157 and E68 words that say so; another document touch wrote them. A fourth comments-only step then corrected the two loose sentences, in `AudioHealthDrain` and `FailureFunnel`, and added the new entry point to one list in `RealTimeReport`'s Javadoc: three files, comment lines only. The seventh fact-check read that step and the two touches once the code was final and before the sixth gate. Its first read confirmed the comments step and every quoted sentence. It found one case that the code decides otherwise than two new sentences said. A loss that clears a latched device-side fault restarts every run, a processing failure's included, as every clear has since story 337. So "keeps its run across the loss" was false there, in consult 17's own sentence in E157 and in a clause of the Resolution. A correction pass rewrote the Resolution's clause and left the engineer's sentence for the engineer. Consult 18 accepted the case as built, with no production change: one new ruling, E158, which amends E157; no Known limit; new words for E157's sentence and for the control test's Javadoc; and one test owed. Test round M added that test to `AudioHealthDrainTest` and the control's new Javadoc, and changed no production file. A document touch wrote consult 18 into the story. The second read checked the test as a reviewer that wrote none of it, and the touch, and confirmed both. It found one more window in which the engineer's own sentences were false: the lost stream's last device-side counts, first read by the drain on a newer drive, begin that drive's run. The fifth fact-check, round L's reading of E157 and the seventh fact-check's first two reads had each found one more window of the same kind. So the manager had the whole category swept, read-only, before going back to the engineer: every ordering of events at a loss, with what the person sees and the ruling or Known limit that covers it, and every sentence that speaks of a loss, true or false in each ordering. The sweep found 27 orderings, 4 of them covered by no ruling or Known limit, and 39 sentences false in some ordering. Consult 19 ruled on the whole sweep, with no production change: three new rulings, E159 to E161; every uncovered ordering accepted as built, and ruled or recorded as a Known limit; a replacement for every sentence the sweep found false; future story 473 grown; three tests owed; and a closing statement that no ordering at a loss remains unruled. Test round N added those tests to `AudioHealthDrainTest` and gave three comments the engineer's words, comment lines only in production. A document touch wrote consult 19 into story 338, future stories 473 and 468 and story 337's Note. The third read checked the tests as a reviewer that wrote none of them, and the comments and the touches against the engineer's words and the code. It confirmed the code, the comments, the tests of rounds M and N and the copying. It found 17 sentences false in some ordering or as written: 16 of them consult 19's own words, and one an agent's. No Java source changed after that read. The sixth gate, the build of record, ran on those sources, and the third Linux run on the same sources, beside the wording work that followed (Verification).
- **The wording, verified before it was written.** Four reads in a row had found the engineer's sentences false after they were in the documents. So from consult 20 on, the engineer answered in replacement blocks (the place, the old text, the new text). A verifier that reads the code checked every new sentence, and the engineer revised until the verifier found the answer clean. Only then did a document touch apply it as written, and the seventh fact-check read it. Consult 20 corrected the words of consult 19 and made one ruling, E162: how cases that meet and losses that follow one another are judged. It changed no code and owed no test. Its verifier read it four times, with 7, 6, 2 and 0 findings. The second pass found, and the engineer agreed, that the documents treated a Stop and Play as a resume of the paused stream. The fourth read confirmed the applied blocks byte for byte. It found nine sentences false only in orderings with more than one loss, or in a name. Beside it, a second read-only sweep checked every sentence that says or assumes what Stop, Pause, Play and Record do to the stream, against the code with and without an instrument insert in the mixer: of 160 sentences and passages, 43 were not true in both, and 5 of those were true in neither. Consult 21 gave the per-case sentences one scope sentence: each describes one loss, and where losses follow one another, E162 decides. It ruled E163 to E165, each right as built: the clock badge and the watchdog follow the stream, not the transport; when the clock source is read again; and a fault on a stream that an instrument insert keeps running. It reworded sentences and owner's checks, with no code, no test and no future story. Its verifier read it five times, with 4, 2, 2, 2 and 0 findings. The fifth read confirmed the blocks, the losses under the scope sentence and every transport sentence but four. Two are steps of the owner's checks that cannot be done as written: clock check 7's Apply that changes nothing, which the Settings dialog does not offer, and clock check 6's change of backend, which applies only at a relaunch. The other two, ruling E145 and its Accepted-as-built bullet, rested on the first. A third read-only sweep then checked every step of the owner's hardware checks: whether it can be done as written, and whether its expected result is what the code does. Of 86 steps, 9 could not be done as written and 10 only under a condition; of 106 expected results, 9 were not what the code does and 3 were one of several. Consult 22 reworded the checks step by step, restated ruling E145, ruled E166 to E169 and gave future stories 474 and 475, with no code and no test. Its verifier read it four times, with 7, 3, 1 and 0 findings. The sixth read confirmed every block and every reworded check, and found two things: future story 475 not yet created, and one sentence of E166. Consult 23 corrected E166, ruled E170 and E171, and gave three more future stories, 476 to 478, for what the reading of the owner's checks had found in the program outside story 338; for one more such finding it judged that no future story is owed. Its verifier read it three times, with 3, 1 and 0 findings. A document touch created 475 to 478 and applied the blocks. The seventh read answered CONFIRMED, with no defect. That closed the seventh fact-check after seven reads. None of consults 15 to 23 gave a verdict on the story. Consult 24 read the story and the build as they stood and answered NOT ACCEPTED, with one required change to clock check 10's words (the check changes the interface's rate and did not set it back before the later clock checks, which name 48 kHz) and no new ruling. A verifier read that sentence against the code before it was written and found one gap, a go-on test that read only the interface, and consult 25 gave its final words, with no verdict and no ruling. They were applied in a document touch, which a fact-check agent that wrote none of it read and confirmed. The studio engineer then read that change, its fact-check and the hashes that show nothing else changed since consult 24 (which had read the whole story and the build, with the rulings of consults 15 to 23, the Known limits, the future stories, the owner's checks and the code and tests of fix round L and test rounds M and N), as consult 26, the fourth acceptance read, and answered ACCEPTED, with no condition and no new ruling; this record of its answer was added afterwards and read by a fact-check agent that wrote none of it. After consult 26's ACCEPTED, two further reads of the last stretch were made: the Java agent's found the code and tests in order and one false clause in the "Skills used" list, corrected here. The studio engineer's, consult 27, found that the story's words would mislead on two things (what a take recorded at a rate mismatch sounds like; the Xruns check's set-back), required three replacement blocks and said that consult 26's ACCEPTED did not stand as given. A verifier read the blocks against the code four times before they were written, consult 28 gave their final words, taking word for word the one change of words that the fourth read gave for R1a, and they were applied in a document touch, which the eleventh fact-check, by an agent that wrote none of it, read and confirmed. Consult 29, the acceptance read after the skill audits, answered NOT ACCEPTED: reading the owner's checks in order, it found two more places where a check leaves behind state that a later check depends on (UI Scale after the status strip check; the interface's own rate before the clock checks), and required two replacement blocks, with no new ruling; they were applied in a document touch, which a fact-check agent that wrote none of it read and confirmed. The studio engineer then read that touch, its fact-check and the hashes that show nothing else changed, and the two blocks whole with their neighbours in the owner's checks, as consult 30, and answered ACCEPTED, with no condition and no new ruling; this record of its answer, which changes no other sentence and does not reopen that verdict, was added afterwards and read by a fact-check agent that wrote none of it.

**PR #994's review.** Copilot's review 6015185361 of PR #994 left six comments, and CI run 45083279538 failed on `InsertParameterRealtimeTest`. The studio engineer ruled E172 to E192 in consults 31 to 34, E193 to E200 in consult 36, E201 to E204 in consult 38 and E205 in consult 39. The six comments:
- **Sustained faults on a negative clock** (`FailureTrack`). `lastBucket` started at zero, so with `System.nanoTime()` negative, failures in buckets −4, −3 and −2 never escalated. A run now starts from its own bucket. Tests: `FailureTrackTest` (6) and `AudioHealthDrainTest.aNegativeMonotonicClockEscalatesAtItsThirdConsecutiveSecond`.
- **CPU budget entries the log's listener drops** (`ShedLogSubscriber`). The enforcer offers each event and never waits, so a degradation the listener's full buffer could not hold left no entry. The enforcer now tells a `PerformanceEventLossListener` of each event dropped for it, with how many events it offered before (`eventDropped(event, offeredBefore)`); its publications go through `TrackCpuBudgetEnforcer.publish`, which offers nothing to a closed publisher. The listener counts the dropped markings, not entries, because the audio thread cannot tell whether a dropped marking would have started a run; a marking the listener received and did not log is not in the count (ruling E199). The count is handed over once the listener has delivered every event queued for it ahead of the latest counted drop: at that delivery, at the window's next read of the log when that drop was counted only after it, or at the completion, whichever comes first (ruling E197). The journal writes one entry for it at the window's next read, dated then; the read hands a due count over first (`EngineHealthFeed.pulse` calls `ShedLogSubscriber.handOverAtTheRead` before `XrunJournal.drain`), so that count is in the same read's entry (rulings E184 and E197). The entry reads "N CPU budget markings never reached the log: it fell behind", and "1 CPU budget marking never reached the log: it fell behind" for one (ruling E199). A track gets a Budget entry at its first marking, at its first marking after any 30-second stretch with no CPU budget event for it, and at its first marking after each Clear of the log; while its events keep coming less than 30 seconds apart, it gets no further entry (rulings E199 and E200). The listener keeps a track only while that track's last event is less than 30 seconds old. At a replacement the old enforcer is detached from the engine and closed once the block in flight has ended, or at once when no stream is running, and its listener stays subscribed until that close, so every marking the old enforcer makes, its last block's included, gets a Budget entry under that listener's runs or is counted in its "never reached the log" entry; the new enforcer's listener starts with no runs, so a track marked in the old enforcer's last block and again by the new one gets an entry from each (ruling E203). `CpuBudgetEnforcerRetirer` (daw-app `ui`, FX thread) does it: `retire` detaches the engine's enforcer, then reads `AudioEngine.blocksBegun()` and then the metering block index (`MeteringTapBus.blockIndex()`). A block counts itself begun after the running check and before it reads the enforcer, and ended after its last use of it, so equal counts close the old enforcer at once; otherwise the first window pulse that finds either count changed closes it. A close first asks the listener to hand over a count that is due, and while enforcers wait, the window's read of the log asks their listeners too (`EngineHealthFeed.pulse` runs `CpuBudgetEnforcerRetirer.handOverAtTheRead` before it asks the current listener). The window's disposal closes every waiting enforcer (`closeAll`). Every marking is measured against the block time of the stream that rendered the block: after a sample-rate or buffer-size change, no marking of the old enforcer is made from a block of the new format (ruling E204). The engine keeps it in three places: a render drive built for a format of another block time detaches the installed enforcer by compare-and-set before its first block (`AudioEngine.newRenderDrive`); each block uses the enforcer only when the enforcer's block time (`TrackCpuBudgetEnforcer.getBlockBudgetNanos()`) is its format's (`AudioEngine.processBlock`); and `AudioEngine.setFormat` throws `IllegalStateException` while a render pump whose exit a stop could not confirm is still alive after a retried join, so no render thread is alive across a format change. Tests: `ShedLogSubscriberTest` (26); `EngineHealthFeedTest.theReadHandsTheCurrentAndTheRetiringListenersDueCountsOverAndWritesThemInThatReadsEntry`; `TrackCpuBudgetEnforcerTest` (20); `XrunJournalTest` (8); `FailureSpineWiringSourceTest.theWindowWiresItsEngineHealthModelToTheBridgeTheDrainTheStripTheFeedAndTheShedLog`; `CpuBudgetEnforcerRetirerTest` (10), among them `aMarkingInTheBlockInFlightAtTheReplacementIsLoggedByTheOldListenerAndTheNewEnforcersMarkingAgain`, `aMarkingInTheBlockInFlightThatTheOldListenersFullBufferCannotHoldIsCounted` and `theWindowsDisposalClosesEveryWaitingEnforcer`; `AudioEngineBlocksBegunTest` (2); `AudioEngineCpuBudgetEnforcerFormatTest` (3), among them `anEnforcerOfAnotherBlockTimeMeasuresNoBlockOfTheNewFormatAndTheNewFormatsEnforcerMeasuresAgainstItsBlockTime` and `anEnforcerOfAnotherBlockTimeInstalledOnARunningStreamMeasuresNoBlock`; and `AudioEngineOutputTest.aFormatChangeIsRefusedWhileAStopCouldNotConfirmThePumpExited`.
- **Clock marks with evidence from before the take** (`TakeFrameHistory`). Evidence older than the take's first sample was dated at the current frame. Evidence before the take's reach sample is now dated at the take's start-frame; the reach is the first sample at or past the start-frame for a take anchored at a punch-in (`RecordingPipeline.isRecordingStartAPunchIn()`), and the take's first sample otherwise (rulings E173, E177 and E183). Tests: `TakeFrameHistoryTest` (13) and `TakeDropoutsAndClockMarksTest` (7).
- **A late block of the lost stream on the input meters** (`InputLevelMonitor`). A block the lost stream rendered after the DEVICE_LOST delivery overwrote the floor. `InputLevelMonitorRegistry.silenceAll(lostDrive)` now mutes every monitor through the lost render drive and floors each monitor whose state belongs to that drive or an older one, and each snapshot reads the floor with the live clip latch until the engine builds a newer drive; a stream a Reconnect opens on another device and closes at once mutes the meters again (`AudioEngine.muteInputMetersThrough`) (rulings E175 and E185). A monitor's level record, clip latch, routing flag, physical-input flag, frame count and true-peak detector belong to the stream of one render drive: at every drive build (`AudioEngine.newRenderDrive`) `liftAll(newDrive)` gives each monitor whose state belongs to an older drive a new state for that drive, at the floor, with a true-peak detector of its own and the clip latch, routing and physical-input flags and frame count the old state holds, then lifts the mute when the drive is newer than the lost one. Every meter write of the tap (`AudioEngine.tapInputSource`: level, clip, routing flag and physical-input flag) from an input device beside the stream carries the render drive built for that device's stream (`AdditionalInputSubscriber.drive`), and a monitor refuses it when its state belongs to a newer drive; the stream's own input passes `Long.MAX_VALUE` and is never refused. No block of a closed stream shows on the input meters once a newer render drive was built, however late it arrives (ruling E201): a block an input device beside the closed stream delivers after that build is refused, and one being measured at the build completes into the closed stream's level and true-peak history, which nothing shows, so neither shows a level, lights the clip LED or leaves samples in the newer stream's true-peak history. From the moment the close of the input devices beside a stream stops waiting for a block one of them is still delivering, nothing more of that delivery shows on the input meters, whether or not a newer render drive is built after that moment (ruling E205): the meters take nothing more from the input devices beside that stream, so what the delivery still writes is refused, or completes into state nothing shows when it was being measured at that moment, and it shows no level, lights no clip LED and sets or clears no routing flag, on the stopped stream's meters or, through the clip latch and routing flag a newer stream takes over at its build, on the newer stream's. A loss delivered after a newer render drive was built changes nothing on the meters: no floor and no mute, so the newer stream's levels and clip latches stay as they are (ruling E202). Closing the input devices beside the stream waits, 1 s in all (`AudioEngine.INPUT_DELIVERY_WAIT_NANOS`), for the block each device is delivering. When the wait passes with a block still being delivered, `AudioEngine.closeAdditionalInputs` decides under the graph lock whether that delivery is left behind; a delivery that ends while the close waits for that lock is measured as the closed stream's last block, except what it writes after the same close left an earlier device's delivery behind. At the first delivery left behind, in the same hold of the lock, `InputLevelMonitorRegistry.refuseTheDevicesBesideTheStream()` gives every monitor a state of that stream's drive that keeps what it showed and refuses those devices' writes, and a monitor created later in that drive refuses them too, until the next build; the log then gets, for each device left behind, "Input device '<name>' was still delivering a block when its close stopped waiting for it; the input meters show nothing more from it". A clear of the clip LED (`InputLevelMonitor.reset`), a loss's floor (`silenceThrough`) and the install of a state exclude each other under one lock per monitor, which no measuring method, routing write or snapshot takes. A monitor is created under the registry's lock (`getOrCreate`), which a build's and a refusal's installs hold too, with the state the registry requires: the newest built drive's, refusing when that drive's devices are refused. Tests: `InputLevelMonitorTest` (37), among them `aBlockOfAnOlderDriveThanTheMetersWritesNothing`, `aBlockMeasuredAcrossTheBuildOfANewerDriveCompletesIntoTheClosedStream`, `aClearMadeWhileABuildHoldsTheLatchItCopiedWaitsForTheInstallAndClearsTheInstalledState`, `aFloorMadeWhileARefusalHoldsTheLevelItCopiedWaitsForTheInstallAndFloorsTheInstalledState` and `onlyAClearOfTheLatchALossFloorAndTheInstallOfAStateTakeALockAndAllTakeTheMonitorsOwn`; `InputLevelMonitorRegistryTest` (25), among them `aLossDeliveredAfterANewerDriveWasBuiltChangesNothingOnTheMeters`, `aMonitorCreatedBeforeOrAfterABuildCarriesThatBuild`, `aMonitorCreatedBeforeOrAfterTheCloseStoppedWaitingRefusesTheStreamsWrites`, `aCreationABuildAndTheRefusalTakeTheRegistrysLockAndNothingElseTakesALock`, `aPhysicalInputWriteCheckedBeforeANewerBuildLeavesALaterEditToNoInputStanding` and `aPhysicalInputWriteCheckedBeforeTheCloseStoppedWaitingLeavesALaterEditToNoInputStanding`; `InputMonitorPreparationTest` (4); `DeviceLossReactionsTest` (7), among them `aLossDeliveredAfterANewerDriveWasBuiltKeepsTheLevel`; and `AudioEngineInputDeviceCloseTest` (12), among them `aBlockOfTheClosedDeviceThatOutlastedTheWaitShowsNothingOnTheNextStreamsMeter`, `aDeliveryTheCloseStoppedWaitingForShowsNothingMoreOnTheStoppedStreamsMetersOrTheNextStreams`, `aDeliveryThatEndsWhileTheCloseWaitsForTheGraphLockIsMeasuredAndLeavesNothingBehind`, `aDeliveryStillInsideItsClaimWhenTheCloseTakesTheGraphLockIsLeftBehindAndShowsNothing` and `everyMeterWriteOfTheTapPassesTheBlocksDrive`.
- **A delivered end on a later loop boundary** (`FrameSpans.intoLoop`). `intoLoop(2048, 0, 1024)` gave the loop start. A point exactly on a loop boundary is now the loop end, however many laps it passed (ruling E176). Tests: `FrameSpansTest` (6) and `TakeMarksInPunchAndLoopTakesTest.aLastDeliveredBlockTwoLapsLongThatEndsOnTheWrapStopsTheInputAtTheLoopEnd`.
- **The stop warning in a loop-record take** (`TrackCapture`, `CaptureFlushService.flagUnavailable`). After a wrap, "m:ss.s into the take" gave a position in one lap's file without naming the lap. In a take recorded with loop recording the warning now reads "… stopped delivering audio at m:ss.s into lap N of the take; the track records silence from there.", N being the lane of the input's last delivered block plus one (`TrackCapture.laneAtLastDelivery()`) (ruling E172). Other takes keep E14's sentence. Test: `TakeMarksInPunchAndLoopTakesTest.aStopTwoSecondsIntoLapThreeIsWrittenAsThatLapsPosition`.

Found on the way and fixed, in the same categories:
- **A loop-plus-punch re-entry and its resume** (`CaptureFlushService`). The re-entry flag carried the punch-in in every lap, and the resume of an input beside the stream was always raised to its stop. The stop is now the first frame of the punch region in the lap of the input's own frames: the punch-in in lap 1, the later of the punch-in and the loop start in a later lap. The resume is raised to the stop only when the stop's lap is the transport's lap at the flagging block, and otherwise to that lap's region start (rulings E179 and E186). A delivered block that runs past the loop end puts its end in a later lap (`FrameSpans.wrapsBeforeTheEnd`).
- **The lap carried with the block on the capture path** (`CaptureCallback`, `CaptureRing`). The lap judgment above first read a flush-thread count of ring 0, so the result depended on the order in which the flush thread applied the rings. The render thread now stamps the transport's lap on every block it runs (`CaptureRing.Slot#setTransportLap`; the count is `CaptureCallback.TransportLaps`, shared through the start gate), and a block an input beside the stream delivers on its own thread carries `CaptureRing.NO_TRANSPORT_LAP`. While the start gate is still the take's origin, the count starts from the gate's beat whichever callback runs the take's first counted block, so a wrap between the gate's opening and that block is counted on it (ruling E198). `RealTimeSafeContractTest.CAPTURE_PATH_REQUIRED_REACH` lists `setTransportLap`. Tests: `CaptureCallbackTransportLapTest` (10 methods, 11 runs) and `TakeMarksInPunchAndLoopTakesTest` (44).
- **An overflow gap a lap long** (`FileLossCounts`). Such a gap was judged by its end points. It is now judged by the frames the transport passed, followed across every wrap, and its blocks count in the take's dropouts when any of those frames lies in the punch region (rulings E180 and E182). Tests: `FileLossCountsTest` (13).
- **A take that starts on an unlocked clock** (`ClockStatusIndicator`, `TakeClockMarks.takeStartsUnlocked`, `RecordCoordinator.noteTakeStartsUnlocked`). An unlock declared during the count-in wrote no mark. An unlock on show when an audio take starts recording is now marked `clock=unlocked` once at the take's start-frame (ruling E173). The badge's take-mark seam moved to the package-private `TakeClockMarks` (73 lines then, 83 since the second review below), and `ClockStatusIndicator` was 861 lines (821 since the second review). Tests: `ClockBadgeDuringATakeTest` (15), `ClockStatusIndicatorTest` (98) and `TakeClockMarksOfTheTransportTest` (4).
- **The Budget log's words.** No production code applies a degradation policy (ruling E181). The xrun log's kind word is "Budget", and for every policy the entry reads "Track 'T': over the CPU budget (averaging NN % of the block time); its processing was not reduced". The toast reads "Track 'T' went over the CPU budget; its processing was not reduced" (`TrackBudgetUiBinding`) and the ⚠ tooltip "Track went over the CPU budget; its processing was not reduced" (`MixerView`). Tests: `EngineHealthFeedTest` (11), among them `noXrunLogDetailClaimsAReductionTheEngineDoesNotMake`, and `TrackBudgetUiBindingTest.shouldFlagDegradedTrackAndEmitSingleNotification`.
- **Three CPU budget texts made true** (rulings E193 to E195). The master CPU budget's description in Settings (`audio.masterCpuBudgetFraction.description`) reads "Master CPU budget per audio block; while the tracks together average more than this fraction of the block time, tracks are marked over budget, highest CPU first, as each track's CPU Budget dialog describes. At 1.0 they are marked only when together they average longer than the block time." (ruling E193). The CPU Budget dialog (`ChannelCpuBudgetDialog`) shows one note under the policy choice, set from its value when the dialog opens and again on every change (ruling E194). For `BypassExpensive`, `ReduceOversampling` and `SubstituteSimpleKernel` it reads "This policy is not applied: when the track goes over this budget, or the master CPU budget in Audio Settings marks it, its processing is not reduced; it gets a ⚠ on its mixer strip, a notification and a Budget entry in the Xrun Log." For `DoNothing` it reads "With DoNothing, going over budget changes and shows nothing: the track's processing is not reduced, and there is no ⚠ on its mixer strip, no notification and no Xrun Log entry." The insert's context-menu item in `InsertEffectRack` reads "Expensive (no CPU budget policy bypasses it)" (ruling E195). Tests: `SettingsTruthVerbatimTextsTest` (4), among them `noSettingDescriptionSaysDegradedCeilingOrCap`, `MasterCpuPolicyRemovedWiringTest.theCatalogueAndTheSettingsDialogHaveNoCascadePolicyRow`, `ChannelCpuBudgetDialogTest` (3), `OfflineInsertSurfacesTest` and `InsertRackEditsAreRefusedWhileProjectActionsAreNotAllowedTest`.
- **The enforcer's offer after its close** (`TrackCpuBudgetEnforcer`). A degradation or restore evaluated after `close()` was offered to the closed publisher, which threw `IllegalStateException` on the render thread. `publish` now offers nothing to a closed publisher, and an offer refused by a publisher closed in between is neither counted nor thrown. Test: `TrackCpuBudgetEnforcerTest.whatIsEvaluatedAfterTheCloseIsNeitherOfferedNorThrown`.

The CI failure and three scheduling-dependent tests, each fixed in the test only:
- **`InsertParameterRealtimeTest`** (CI run 45083279538). A C2 compile request made on the measuring thread loaded `PluginParameterStore$UiWriteGate` and interned string constants there, which the test counted as allocation. The measurement now runs in a child JVM under `-Xint`, with the VM's log on standard error (`InsertParameterAllocationMain`), and `theMeasurementReportsASetterThatAllocates` shows that it reports an allocating setter (`AllocatingParameterProcessor`).
- **`AudioHealthRecordTest.aWriterRacingTheReaderNeverLeavesACountWithoutItsSample`** (main's run 43874880919). A reading drains a ring until it finds it empty, so against a writer that never paused, 1,000 readings in one second depended on how often the OS interrupted the writer. The writer now records a burst of four rings' worth and starts the next only once a reading has ended, a latch waits for the 1,000 readings, and a last reading must count at least two bursts.
- **`AudioHealthLanePublicationOrderTest.aDrainRacingTheWriterNeverSeesAHalfWrittenSlot`.** Its premise of 10,000 drained samples in one second failed on one CPU. The writer yields after a lost record, the drain after an empty poll, and a latch waits for the premise.
- **`RestoredArchiveAsksAgainTest.aDoorAskedWhileTheArchiveIsExtractedIsRefusedWithOneInfo`** (a Linux run of CI's command on 4 CPUs). It never held the extraction, so the extraction's outcome could reach the FX thread before it read the notification list, which then held the restore's SUCCESS after the one INFO. It now holds the extraction's rewrite of `project.daw` while it makes the three clicks and asserts the one INFO.

Accepted limits of this round:
- A block whose end the engine never counts keeps a replaced CPU budget enforcer open, detached and marking nothing, while the window's reads of the log still ask its listener to hand over a count that is due: one that never ends keeps it open until the window's disposal; one that throws after it counted itself begun and before the `try` whose `finally` counts it ended leaves the engine's two block counts one apart, so a later replacement is closed only once a block begins or ends after it, and with no stream running it is not closed at once but stays open until a stream runs again or the window's disposal (`AudioEngine.blocksBegun()`; `CpuBudgetEnforcerRetirer`'s class javadoc; the engineer's notes on ruling E203).
- With Record pressed while the transport rolls, evidence from before the take's first sample is dated at a start-frame that can be up to the preparation time before the take's first recorded frame; clock marks are written only to `take.manifest` (ruling E183).
- A clip in a block rendered by a stream that a Reconnect closes at once still lights the clip LED (ruling E185 (c)).
- A resume frame larger than the stop frame does not rule out a wrap between them: an input beside the stream first clocked in a later lap, at a frame later in the loop than its stop, and an input whose stop lies in lap 1's pre-roll and whose resume lies in a later lap (rulings E186 (2) and E191).
- A seek backwards while the loop is enabled counts as a wrap for the lap stamp, as it does for the lanes (`CaptureCallback`'s class javadoc).
- When a seek is accepted between two callbacks of the take's first render period, a wrap between the start gate's opening and that period is in every lap stamp of the take but not in that input's own laps; it matters only when Record is pressed while a loop rolls and the transport wraps before that period. Nothing in the application shows it; in the `take.manifest` of a loop-plus-punch take, an input beside the stream can have its `input-stopped` resume frame raised to its stop across a wrap, or left below its stop within one lap (ruling E198).
- An overflow of the stream's own ring a lap long or longer can hide a wrap from the lanes and the per-source count; the overflow's gap line, its warning and the take's dropout count report it (ruling E190).
- `TrackBudgetUiBinding`, the mixer badge's listener, subscribes with a bounded buffer of its own and can still lose an event: a lost restore leaves a stale ⚠, a lost degradation a degraded track without one. No ruling covers it; Verification lists it for the owner.
- As intended (ruling E188): for a last delivered block that ran across the loop end, the warning names the lap whose file holds the block, while the manifest's stop frame lies where the next lap's file begins.

Declined: a hand-off that retains every event for the Budget log, Copilot's first option. Ruling E174 chose the count: a hand-off that never drops is unbounded or blocking, and a bounded one still needs the count. Moving the enforcer's publication off the render thread is future story 448's.

**PR #994's second review.** Copilot's review 6059273652 of PR #994 left four comments. The studio engineer ruled E206 to E237 in consult 41, E238 to E247 in consult 42, E248 to E257 in consult 43, E258 to E260 in consult 44, E261 to E266 in consult 45, E268 to E270 in consult 47, E271 and E272 in consult 50, E273 to E280 in consult 51, E281 to E292 in consult 52, E293 to E299 in consult 53 and E300 to E304 in consult 54; consults 42, 44, 46, 47, 48, 49, 50, 51, 52, 53 and 54 corrected earlier rulings in place, as each ruling's entry under Rulings says, and E267 was never used. Consults 45, 47 and 49 each had a fact check of their premises against the code, which wins over the consult on facts about the code. The counts and line numbers in the account of the first review above are that round's; this round's are here and under Verification. The four comments:
- **Blocks an input thread could not build or publish** (`JavaxSoundBackend`). Java Sound's capture thread counts a block it cannot build or publish in `threadCounters().inputBlocksLost()`, which the take's figure did not read, so a take could lose audio while "Take dropouts" read 0 beside the notice "If you are recording, the take has a gap there." `AudioEngine.capturedInputBlocksLost()` now adds the input blocks the backends' own threads lost, as the health record counts them (`AudioHealthRecord.backendCounters().inputBlocksLost()`), for an input opened beside the stream its failed device callbacks too (story 337, ruling E86), never an input halt (rulings E206, E207 and E211 (i)). A failed device callback of the stream's own device counts too: the new default `AudioBackend.failedCallbackInputBlocks()` (0) counts each period whose callback failed before that period's captured input reached the backend's input ring, none for an output-only stream and none for a failure after the input reached the ring or was counted as a ring overflow; `AsioBufferSwitchShim` and PortAudio's `CallbackInvoker` set a period's pending block at the callback's entry and clear it once the period's input reached the ring (on PortAudio, once the callback returned), `AsioBackend` and `PortAudioBackend` (through its `InvokerInputLoss` record) keep the counts of closed streams, `CallbackBackendAdapter` reports its wrapped backend's, and the engine reads it for the stream's own backend only (ruling E211 (ii), built in this round by ruling E254). On PortAudio the callback adapter contains a failure of its wake of the input ring's drain thread, which only an error of the Java runtime itself, such as a stack overflow, can cause: the period's output plays as rendered and the callback has not failed, so the failure counts nothing in the take's dropouts, the callback faults or the Xruns number and gives no notice and no xrun-log entry, and the drain publishes the period's block from the ring at its next wake, the next period's wake or its own 20 ms backstop, whichever comes first; if that later delivery leaves the render drive without the block when it renders, that block counts as the starved block it is, as any late input does (ruling E272). As built, `CallbackBackendAdapter.deviceCallback` wraps its `LockSupport.unpark` of the drain alone in a catch-all whose handler is empty, after the output is staged, the input is interleaved and written to the ring and a full ring's overflow is counted, so a block the full ring refused was counted before the wake and the failure counts and delivers nothing more. The ASIO bridge, for which consult 50's note beside the ruling judged the same outcome right, contains its wake the same way: `AsioBufferSwitchShim.bufferSwitch` wraps its wake of the drain alone in a catch-all whose handler is empty, after the period's input is written to the ring (or counted as the ring's overflow) and its pending block cleared, and before the output half is written, so a failed wake neither silences that half nor reaches the upcall's guard, which would count a callback fault. A block Java Sound's capture thread could not build or publish after its stream closed counts nowhere, and one that fails in the instant before the close counts only when the thread still finds its stream live after the failure (ruling E246; Known limits). The ledger behind the figure is `AudioEngine.CapturedInputLoss`: each attached backend's counts are one immutable value (the baseline, the highest good read, whether the next good read counts its gap, and whether a fold sealed it) replaced by compare-and-set; the first good read to land is the baseline and the highest good read only rises; a failed, `null` or negative report changes nothing; a `VirtualMachineError` freezes the source and propagates to the one read that met it; and a backend attached again while still attached is folded first, so the figure never falls and never brings in an earlier stream's losses (ruling E214). A fold seals the source's counts before it takes them: no read moves sealed counts, and a read already in flight when a backend is detached never reads that backend's report again (`AudioEngine.CapturedInputLoss`, its `Counts` and `UNREAD` are package-private for that test). No new words, no take.manifest line or key (ruling E210). Tests: `InputStallCaptureTest.theBlocksTheStreamsInputThreadLostCountFromItsOpenAndItsHaltsAndCallbackFaultsDoNot`, `theBlocksAnInputBesideTheStreamLostCountWithItsFailedCallbacksAndItsHaltsDoNot`, `aStreamReportThatThrowsKeepsTheCountItHadRead`, `aVirtualMachineErrorFromTheStreamsReportReachesOnlyTheReadThatMetIt`, `aFailedCallbackThatLostItsInputWhileTheQueueHeldABacklogCountsOneDropout`, `aFailedCallbackThatLostItsInputWithAnEmptyQueueCountsOneDropout` and `aFailedCallbackOnAnInputBesideTheStreamCountsOnce`; `AsioBufferSwitchShimTest.aFaultWhileCopyingThePeriodsInputCountsItsBlockLostAndOneCallbackFault`, `aFaultInTheOutputHalfCountsNoBlockLost` and `anOutputOnlyStreamsFaultCountsNoBlockLost`; `CallbackInvokerTest.aCallbackThatFailsCountsItsPeriodsBlockLost`, `aFaultAfterTheCallbackTookThePeriodsInputCountsNoBlockLost`, `anOutputOnlyStreamsFailedCallbackCountsNoBlockLost` and `aFaultBeforeTheCallbackCountsOneBlockLost`; `AsioBackendDropoutCountersTest.blocksFailedCallbacksLostAreFoldedInAtCloseAndKeepGrowingAfterAReopen`; `PortAudioBackendTest.aRetiredInvokersLostInputBlocksAreKept`; `CallbackBackendAdapterTest.theFailedCallbackCountIsTheWrappedBackends`; `JavaxSoundBackendTest.aBlockThatCannotBeBuiltAfterItsStreamClosedCountsNothing`; `AudioEngineBackendCountersTest.aDropoutReportThatFailsMidStreamKeepsTheCountItHadRead`, `aDropoutReportThatFailsAtTheCloseFoldsTheCountItHadRead`, `aDropoutReportThatFailsAtTheOpenCountsFromItsFirstGoodRead`, `aLateEarlierReadAfterAFailedAttachNeverRaisesTheFigure`, `aVirtualMachineErrorFromTheDropoutReportAtTheCloseCostsThatStopAloneAndTheReopenCountsAgain` and `aBackendReopenedWhileStillAttachedNeverCountsTheSameBlocksTwice`; from the round's last change set, `CapturedInputLossTest.aReadInFlightWhenAnotherSourceIsDetachedNeverReadsThatSourcesReportAgain` and `noReadMovesTheCountsAFoldSealed`, `RealTimeSafeContractTest.theNativeCallbackBridgeContainsItsDrainWakeAndNothingElse` and `theAsioBridgeContainsItsDrainWakeAndNothingElse` (each bridge's one handler catches `Throwable` around the wake alone, the ring write comes before it, the handler stores no field, can throw nothing and does not return, and on ASIO the output half comes after it), and `CallbackBackendAdapterTest.aDrainNoWakeReachesStillPublishesEveryBlockInOrder` (with no wake of the device callback reaching it, the drain's backstop publishes every block in order and nothing is counted); and, after the confirming review of the round's last change set, `AsioBufferSwitchShimTest.aBlockThatReachesTheCaptureRingWithNoWakeIsStillPublished` (a block written into the ASIO bridge's capture ring while its drain is parked on the empty ring, with no `bufferSwitch` and so no wake, is published by the drain's own timed park).
- **Another subscriber's drops counted as the take's** (`AudioBackendSupport`). `SubmissionPublisher.offer` answers a negative number when any subscriber drops the block, so a slow second subscriber could make a lossless take report lost blocks. daw-sdk's new `InputBlockLossListener` is a subscriber that is told of each captured block its publisher dropped for it: every input publisher offers with the one handler `InputBlockLossListener.reportDrop` (`AudioBackendSupport.DROP_INPUT_BLOCK`, `CallbackBackendAdapter.DROP_INPUT_BLOCK`), which tells the subscriber the block was dropped for when it is a listener, never asks for a retry, keeps a listener's throw to that listener's notice and lets a `VirtualMachineError` through. The engine's two capture subscribers are listeners and count a block dropped for them, the render pump's while its pump runs and its input has not ended, one beside the stream while it is live; `BackendDropoutCounters.inputBlocksDropped()` counts only losses before publication (the full input rings of ASIO and of the PortAudio adapter), and Java Sound and Mock report `NONE` (ruling E208). A drop only another subscriber suffered reaches no surface the person reads. Tests: `InputBlockLossListenerTest` (4); `AudioBackendSupportTest.aDroppedBlockIsToldOnlyToTheSubscriberThatDroppedIt` and `aListenerThatThrowsLeavesTheOtherDroppingSubscribersTold`; `JavaxSoundBackendTest.aBlockThePublisherDropsIsToldToTheSubscriberThatDroppedItAndNotCounted`; `ClockSourceTest.aBlockTheMockDropsIsToldToTheSubscriberThatDroppedItAndNotCounted`; `AsioBackendDropoutCountersTest.theInputPublishersDropsAreToldToTheSubscriberAndOnlyRingOverflowsAreCounted`; `CallbackBackendAdapterTest.aBlockDroppedAtPublishIsToldToTheSubscriberThatDroppedItAndNotCounted`; `InputStallCaptureTest.aBlockDroppedForTheStreamsSubscriberCountsOnlyWhileItsPumpRunsAndItsInputLives`, `aBlockDroppedForTheSubscriberBesideTheStreamCountsOnlyWhileItIsLive` and `aBlockOnlyAnotherSubscriberDroppedNeverCountsAndOneTheStreamsSubscriberDroppedCountsOnce`; `RealTimeSafeContractTest.theInputBlockDropHandlerAndItsListenersTakeNoLockAllocateNothingAndLogNothing`; and `InputBlockPublishersShareTheDropHandlerScanTest.everyInputBlockPublisherOffersWithTheSharedDropHandler`, which finds the files that hold a publisher of captured input blocks, build a publisher beside them, or, since the last touch round, call the SDK support's `currentInputPublisher()` (six) and checks the handler of every offer in them.
- **A lock change between two looks** (`ClockLockStatus`). The badge read the lock as a level at each look, so an unlock and its relock that both fell between two looks, while the FX thread was busy, left no notice and no mark: R1's reason above, that a level read on every pulse cannot be missed, did not hold for a change and its reversal. `ClockLockStatus` now carries its measurement's newest unlocks and relocks (`transitions`, at most 32 kept by `AsioClockLockMonitor`, with `lastSequence()` counting every change, so a consumer can tell that some were dropped), and the badge's notices and take marks moved to the new package-private `ClockLockAnnouncer`. A measuring look replays every transition of its serial it has not applied, in order; a serial's first measuring look replays from its start unless a loss or a close consumed it; more changes than the status keeps is a gap, marked unlocked from where the first missed change can have begun (rulings E217 to E220). One look gives at most one notice: the unlock and relock notices as before when the level changed, and otherwise, when unlocks came and went or a gap was found, the new warning "External clock on <name> (<source>) was lost and has locked again. Check the clock cable and the clock master." ("was lost <n> times", "was lost <n> or more times" for a gap), ending " Recording continues; the take is marked where it was lost." while an audio take records and is marked unlocked after the look, " Recording continues." otherwise during a take (rulings E216 and E223; six new `clockBadge.notice.lostAndRelocked*` keys). The take's marks follow ruling E221 (an unlock at the newest frame sampled at or before its evidence, a relock at the oldest at or after it, held until such a sample or the Stop) and E258, the take's start mark E222, and every resync the Xruns count counts is marked once whatever the xrun log kept or cleared (rulings E225 and E226), as "A take's dropouts and its clock marks" above says. The driver's control panel is the person's switch: every session of it that ends, however short, is counted, and the first poll that sends anything once a poll has found the close counted at its first read of the count reads the source again (rulings E227 and E249); the monitor judges nothing while it is open, and its close's resyncs judge nothing (rulings E248, E249 and E260), so the badge keeps what it last measured through the session (rulings E250 and E251, a Known limit below); and from a session's end the stall verdict waits for the start-up allowance (ruling E228: `releaseOpenControlPanel` asks `allowForAClockSourceSwitch()`, and the verdict is refused while `isAllowanceRequestPending()`). - **The unlocked flash's contrast** (`styles.css`). The flash dimmed the whole badge to 0.6 opacity, its words included, below the 4.5:1 that 10 px text needs. The flash is now a hard step every 0.5 s, the house cadence, between the rest look and a two-tone ring over the badge's outer 2 px (`-text-hi` in the outer 1 px, `-text-on-accent` in the next), with the `-danger` fill and the words inside it unchanged, no opacity, no effect and no transition; under Reduce Motion the rest look stays, steady (rulings E232 and E233). The four views where the lock is not checked are an outlined chip with no fill, `-text` words in a `-text-mute` outline, and every state carries the same 1 px border, transparent outside those views (ruling E234); the padding stays 2 8 2 8, so the ring stays inside border plus padding at every UI scale, and the badge is 2 px wider and taller in every state. The comment that called the 0.5 s cadence an interface's own clock-lock LED's is gone (consult 41's premise correction). The CPU Budget dialog's policy note already rendered in the dialog pane's label style (`.dialog-pane .label`, `-text-hi` at 12 px), so ruling E236 held; the dead `dialog-hint` class and its orphaned rule are removed. The warning amber of the Engine and Xruns cells in Atelier, and the older dims and flashes, are future story 483 (rulings E235 and E237, a Known limit below). Built in the round from the engineer's rulings on what the traces of the four comments found:
- **Blocks behind the Stop's final sweep** (rulings E213, E240, E242 and E256). At the person's Stop the seal's look gives each ring's blocks left behind one gap= line from the end of the last block applied from that ring, and the drops no applied block follows, whichever look found them (ruling E275, the closing change sets below), a second line right after them, with the existing ring-overflow warning and a place in overflow-blocks; each line counts in the take's dropouts by its own extent (`FileLossCounts.reachesTheRegionByItsOwnExtent`, ruling E104), in a punch take only when it reaches into the punch region. What a callback drops after the seal last read a ring's overflows is a Known limit (ruling E255). Tests: `FileLossCountsTest.blocksBehindTheFinalSweepAreJudgedByTheirOwnExtent` (named `aGapAfterTheFinalSweepIsJudgedByItsOwnExtent` until the closing change sets rewrote the class); `StopFenceTailIntegrityContractTest.aRingThatOverflowsBehindTheFinalSweepRecordsTheBlocksLeftBehindAndTheDropsOnceEach`, with `aBlockPublishedBehindTheFinalSweepIsRecordedAsAGapWithAWarning` now also asserting the count; `CaptureFlushServiceTest.aDropBehindTheFinalSweepWithNoBlockLeftBehindIsOneGapAtTheEndOfTheLastAppliedBlock`, `inAPunchTakeEachLineBehindTheFinalSweepCountsOnlyWhenItReachesIntoTheRegion`, `eachRingsLateDropsAreCountedAgainstWhatItsOwnSweepCounted`, and from the fix round `aStopsSealRecordsAndCountsABlockBehindItsFinalSweep`, `aLateOverflowWhollyInThePreRollOfAPunchTakeCountsNothing` and `aLateOverflowReachingIntoThePunchRegionCountsItsBlocks`.
- **A closed stream's block never reaches a later take** (ruling E215). The PortAudio adapter's drain pins its ring, scratch, publisher and block shape (`DrainedStream`) and stops on its flag, on an interrupt from `stopDrainThread` or when its pinned publisher closes, and a block it could not publish once its stream ended counts nothing, and one that fails in the instant before that end counts only when the drain still finds its stream live after the failure (ruling E246; Known limits); the ASIO shim pins its publisher at construction; a failed ASIO open that marked the support completes that publisher (`support.close()`), and the next open hands out a fresh one. Tests: `CallbackBackendAdapterTest.aDrainThatOutlivesCloseCannotPublishIntoTheNextStreamOrCountItsTeardown`, `theDrainPublishesEveryBlockIntoThePublisherItStartedWith`, `aDrainLeftBehindByAFailedOpenStopsAfterTheBlockItWasPublishingWhenTheAdapterReopens` and `aBlockADrainLeftBehindByAFailedOpenCouldNotPublishCountsNothing`; `AsioBufferSwitchShimTest.aDrainThatOutlivesItsCloseCannotPublishIntoTheNextStream`; `AsioBackendDropoutCountersTest.aDrainThatOutlivesAFailedOpenCannotPublishIntoTheNextStream`; `AsioBackendStreamingTest.aFailedOpenCompletesItsInputPublisherAndTheNextOpenHandsOutAFreshOne`.
- **The figure at a take's start and Stop** (ruling E245). `TakeDropoutTally.takeStarted` reads the count before it changes anything, so a read that throws leaves the last take's figures; `takeStopped` marks the take stopped and latches what it lost before the rings at least at what the cell showed before it reads, so a read that throws leaves that figure; the take's own file counts stay live, and its seal can still raise them after the Stop. Tests: `TakeDropoutTallyTest.aCountThatCannotBeReadWhenATakeStartsLeavesTheFiguresAsTheyWere`, `aCountThatCannotBeReadWhenATakeStopsKeepsTheFigureTheCellShowedAndStopsCounting` and `aTakeNeverShowsLessThanItShowedBefore`.

Found by the round's independent review and fixed in the round:
- **The take-start read of the loss ledger** (review A-4, must-fix; rulings E261 and E262). "A backend's dropout report that fails never brings blocks lost before the take, or by an earlier stream, into the take's dropouts, apart from the race in the Known limits: when it fails at the read made as the take starts recording, that backend counts from its first good read in the take (ruling E261)." "When a backend's dropout report fails later in the take, its next good read counts every block that backend lost before the rings since the take's last good read (ruling E262)." As built: `AudioEngine.capturedInputBlocksLostAsATakeStarts()` is the same total, at which a failed report makes that backend's next good read count nothing for its gap; `TakeDropoutTally.takeStarted` reads it once, every later figure and the Stop's latch read `capturedInputBlocksLost()`, and `RecordCoordinator` hands the tally both. It covers a take on a stream already running, an instrument-only take's. Tests: `AudioEngineBackendCountersTest.aReportThatFailsAtTheTakesStartReadImportsNoEarlierLoss`, `aNullReportAtTheTakesStartReadImportsNoEarlierLoss`, `aReportThatFailsMidTakeCountsEveryBlockLostSinceTheTakesLastGoodRead`, `aReportThatFailsAsATakeStartsOnARunningStreamImportsNothingSinceTheLastTake`, `aTakeStartReadThatFailsAfterAFailedOpenCountsFromTheFirstGoodRead` and `aStaleLowerReadAfterAFailedTakeStartReadNeverRaisesTheFigure`; `TakeDropoutTallyTest.aTakeBaselinesTheReadMadeAsItStartsAndCountsOnFromTheOrdinaryRead`, `aReportThatFailsAsTheTakeStartsImportsNothingLostBeforeIt` and `theRecordCoordinatorGivesTheTallyTheEnginesReadMadeAsATakeStarts` (a source pin of the coordinator's wiring); `InputStallCaptureTest.aReportBesideTheStreamThatFailsAsTheTakeStartsImportsNothingLostBeforeIt`. The race in the Known limits is a take's start inside a reopen of a backend whose count is still attached; it needs no failed report (consult 49 and its fact check).
- **Nothing that can throw inside the ledger's fold window** (review A-4, should-fix). A `VirtualMachineError` inside a fold's odd version window would have left the version odd, so every later `capturedInputBlocksLost()` spun on the FX thread. A fold now reads the source, then seals its counts, so no read moves them again, and computes and allocates everything before the window; inside it, it only publishes the new total and sources. Test: `RealTimeSafeContractTest.theCapturedInputLossFoldWindowRunsNothingThatCanThrow`.
- **A take sealed early, and a device-loss seal, record no ring loss behind them** (review A-1, should-fix; rulings E263, E264, E274, E275 and E286). "After a take is sealed early (the disk headroom floor, a write failure, or a failure of the capture thread's own loop), its dropout figures and take.manifest describe the take up to the early seal. A run of blocks a capture ring dropped keeps its gap= line, its place in overflow-blocks, its warning and its count in the take's dropouts, as any ring overflow does, exactly when a block the take kept from that ring follows it: a block the capture thread applied in full before it decided the early seal, written to the file or passed over outside the punch region; the block whose write failed and the block the headroom floor discarded are not kept, so a run before either lies past the file's end. Every other drop, whenever the capture thread first saw it, and every block a ring discards or is left holding until the take stops, gets no gap= line, no place in overflow-blocks, no warning and no count: it lies past the last audio the take keeps from that ring, where the early seal's notice and the manifest's sealed-by line already say the take ends (rulings E263 and E274)." (Consult 51's sentence, which replaces consult 48's.) "At a device-loss seal, a block a capture ring receives or drops after the take's final sweep — the block a callback stalled across the loss was delivering — is part of the device loss: it gets no gap= line, no count in overflow-blocks and none in the take's dropouts, as the loss's own silent tail is not counted (rulings E48, E49, E90 and E264)." take.manifest's overflow-blocks holds exactly the dropped blocks that have a gap= line (rulings E263 and E273). As built: a run is lined only once a block the take keeps from its ring follows it (`CaptureFlushService.lineTheRunBefore`, after the block's write), so a run before the block whose write failed or the block the headroom floor discarded gets nothing; every early-seal path first gives the pass's ring-overflow warning (`lineThePassBeforeTheEarlySeal`), so the record coordinator shows it while the take still records; at the seal the drops no applied block follows are lined only when the take was not sealed early, at a device-loss seal from the counts the final sweep's look read (ruling E286); the counts `awaitFlushed` waits for are noted at every look. Tests: `CaptureFlushServiceTest.aDropWhileAnEarlySealIsSealingIsNoLossOfTheTake`, `aDropWhileAnEarlySealIsSealingIsNoLossOfAPunchTake`, `aDropTheDrainHadNotNoticedWhenTheHeadroomFloorSealsTheTakeGetsNothing`, `aDropTheDrainHadNotNoticedWhenTheHeadroomFloorSealsAPunchTakeGetsNothing`, `aDropWhileTheWriteThatFailsIsHeldIsNoLossOfTheTake`, `aDropTheDrainHadNotNoticedWhenAWriteFailureSealsTheTakeGetsNothing`, `aDropTheDrainHadNotNoticedWhenItsLoopFailsGetsNothing`, `aDropOnAnotherRingSinceItsLastLookIsNoLossOfATakeSealedEarly`, `aDeviceLossSealRecordsAndCountsNothingTheRingGetsBehindItsFinalSweep`, `aDropTheDrainSawPastTheBlockTheHeadroomFloorDiscardsGetsNothing` and `aDropTheDrainSawPastTheBlockTheHeadroomFloorDiscardsInAPunchTakeGetsNothing` (until the closing change sets below, `aDropNoticedBeforeTheHeadroomFloorSealsTheTakeKeepsItsLineAtTheFilesEnd` and `aDropNoticedBeforeAnEarlySealOfAPunchTakeIsJudgedByItsOwnExtent`, re-expected to no line) and `overflowBlocksOfATakeSealedEarlyHoldsOnlyTheDropsThatHaveALine`, with `afterAnEarlySealTheSealsLookCountsNothingMore` re-expected to no line; from the closing change sets, `aRunsWarningComesBeforeTheEarlySealWhenALaterBlockOfItsPassFailsItsWrite`, `aRunsWarningComesBeforeTheEarlySealWhenTheHeadroomFloorDiscardsALaterBlockOfItsPass` and `anEarlySealInADeviceLossSealsFinalSweepLinesNoRingsTrailingDrops`; and, from the last touch round, `aDropBehindTheFinalSweepOfADeviceLossSealIsStillAccountedForAwaitFlushed` (a device-loss seal's late drop gets no line and no count, and `awaitFlushed` still finds it noted).
- **The callback adapter's failed open** (review A-3, note; ruling E215). A failed open on the callback adapter completes and replaces its input publisher once it has stopped its drain, as a close does, so a drain the failed open left behind can never deliver into a stream later opened on the same adapter; a failure to close the publisher rides on the open's failure as a suppressed exception. Test: `CallbackBackendAdapterTest.aBlockADrainLeftBehindByAFailedOpenNeverReachesTheReopenedStream`, with the two tests of the drain left behind re-expected.
- **A panel session inside one tick** (review BC-1, must-fix; rulings E248 and E260, "in every interleaving"). A session that opened after a tick's first reads, while its poll waited, could have its resyncs judged by that tick: a false unlock, or after a switch made in the panel a false relock of the old source. After its poll and evidence reads every tick now reads the panel's level again, then the closed-session count; when the panel reads open or the count moved, it judges nothing, keeps no position baseline, stores the periods and resyncs seen, writes no status and leaves the count it saw as it was, so the first tick that sends anything once a tick has found the close counted at its first read of the count reads the source again, and the first two ticks that read the panel closed, find the close counted at their first read of the count and are not stopped by their second reads are the close's two switch intervals (ruling E249 in consult 49's words). Tests: `AsioClockLockMonitorTest.aPanelSessionThatOpensAndClosesWhileATicksPollWaitsIsNotJudged`, `aPanelSessionThatSwitchesTheSourceWhileATicksPollWaitsRelocksNothingForTheOldSource` and `aPanelThatOpensWhileATicksPollWaitsAndIsStillOpenWhenItJudgesIsNotJudged`; from the last touch round, `aPanelThatClosesBetweenTheReadsAfterATicksPollLeavesThatTickUnjudged`, which pins the order of the second reads, and `aPollThatReadsThePanelClosedAlsoSeesItsSession`, changed to pin the order of a tick's first reads.
- **Which serial a loss consumes** (review BC-5's unchecked case; ruling E268). Consult 49's sentence, which replaces consult 47's after consult 47's fact check found that the declaration does not always close the lost stream, and which consult 49's own fact check found true, with its sentence on the stopped stream as the engineer's acceptance read of this round corrected it, having found it false in one interleaving: "Each loss and each close consumes, at its first look, the serial the badge last applied, whatever status that look read, with no notice and no mark. A look reads the stream's lock status before it reads the loss, so the serial last applied is always that of a stream open before the loss or the close began, while a status read at that first look can belong to a stream opened since, for example by a Play, an instrument, a record check or the lost device's return. Once the declaration has stopped the lost stream its status is never applied again: a stopped stream, closed or pending its release, never reads as open, a look applies a status only when its first read found a stream open, and the engine names a new stream's backend before that stream reads as open, so a look that reads the lost stream's status once more, while a stream opens on another backend between its reads, found no stream open and applies nothing; a reopened stream draws a new serial. Later looks of the same loss or close consume nothing, so a stream opened during a loss, or just after a close, has its own transitions replayed when the badge first measures it (rulings E219, E220, E252, E259 and E268). A look that reads no status applies nothing." The fact check did not check the four openers its example names. Tests: `ClockStatusIndicatorTest.aLossWhoseFirstLookReadsANewStreamConsumesOnlyTheSerialLastApplied` and `aStreamOpenedAsALossBeginsInsideALookHasItsChangesReplayedOnceTheLossEnds`, and `aSerialFirstReadAtALossIsReplayedOnceTheBadgeMeasuresIt`, renamed and re-expected.
- **An unlock that is its measurement's first decision** (review BC-5's unchecked case; ruling E269).
