"A clock unlock that is the first decision of its measurement, with no good poll answer of that measurement before its unlocking run, is dated no later than that measurement's start (the stream's start, or the first source read the monitor applies that names a newly chosen source or finds a change of whether the source can be measured), because nothing showed the clock good since; so a take whose stream Record reopens while the external clock is unlocked is marked clock=unlocked at its start-frame, with or without a count-in, while the badge shows the new stream's checking view for its first polls and then gives the unlock notice again (ruling E269)." "A change of whether the current clock source can be measured, on the same source, also starts a new measurement, at the first applied read that finds it: a source that has just become measurable has had nothing show its clock good, so an unlock that is that measurement's first decision is dated no later than that read (ruling E269)." (The parenthesis and the second sentence are consult 49's; "the first applied read" is its fact check's, since a read set aside starts nothing.) As built: the measurement's start is the monitor's clock at `start()` and, when a known source is replaced or its pollable flag flips, just before that tick's source read; the transition, the status and the event carry the same date. Before the checking view the badge shows the new stream's starting view, and a look during the reopen's open the closed view (the fact check of consult 47); the case with a count-in is read in the code only. Tests: `AsioClockLockMonitorTest.anUnlockThatDecidesANewlyChosenSourceIsDatedAtThePollThatTookItUp`, ten tests re-expected, and, from the last touch round, `anUnlockThatDecidesAnExternalSourceChosenAfterTheInternalClockIsDatedAtThePollThatTookItUp` (a switch from source 0, an internal clock); `ClockBadgeDuringATakeTest.anUnlockDatedAtTheStartOfTheStreamRecordReopensMarksTheTakeAtItsStartFrame` (Record while playing, no count-in).
- **The Stop's three steps** (review BC-4, should-fix). When a take leaves RECORDING, the coordinator writes a relock still held at the transport's frame now, then latches the take's dropout figures, then stops sampling its frames; each step runs whatever the one before it threw, and when two throw the later one propagates. Tests: `TakeDropoutsAndClockMarksTest.aStopWhoseDropoutCountCannotBeReadStillStopsSamplingTheTake` and, from the round's last change set, `aStopWhoseHeldRelockCannotBeWrittenStillLatchesTheTakesFiguresAndStopsSamplingIt`, which makes the held relock's write throw through the coordinator's package-private test seam for that write (`RecordCoordinator.setHeldRelockWriteForTest`): nothing that write reads can be made to throw without one.
- **Tests and texts the reviews showed missing, weak or untrue.** A held relock written by the sampler marks the take locked again, so a later unlock is marked (`TakeDropoutsAndClockMarksTest.anUnlockAfterAHeldRelockTheSamplerWroteIsMarkedAgain`); a device loss and a retire drop a held relock (`aHeldRelockIsDroppedAtADeviceLoss`, `aHeldRelockIsDroppedWhenTheCoordinatorIsRetired`), and the Javadoc says an early seal drops it too; the running flash leaves the badge exactly as a hand toggle of its pseudo-class does, its padding and border compared too since the round's last change set (`ClockStatusIndicatorTest.eachStepOfTheRunningFlashIsTheLookOfItsPhaseToggledByHand`); two resync marks merged out of arrival order keep the earlier arrival (`XrunJournalTest.aMergeOfTwoMarksWrittenOutOfArrivalOrderKeepsTheEarlierArrival`); a ring overflow followed by a fault in the same period's output half counts once (`AsioBufferSwitchShimTest.aBlockTheFullRingRefusedIsNotCountedAgainWhenItsOutputHalfFaults`); the per-period reset is pinned on both backends (`AsioBufferSwitchShimTest.aFaultInTheInputHalfAfterAHealthyPeriodCountsThatPeriodsBlock`, `CallbackInvokerTest.aCallbackThatFailsAfterAHealthyPeriodCountsThatPeriodsBlockLost`); PortAudio's callback stub attaches its invoker to the failed-period record (`PortAudioBackendTest.theCallbackStubAttachesItsInvokerToTheFailedPeriodLedger`, a class-file pin); the publisher scan finds a holder by its construction as well as its declared type (and, since the last touch round, by a call of the support's `currentInputPublisher()`), decides whether a file names `AudioBlock` before it collapses whitespace, and checks every offer in a holder; `TakeDropoutTallyTest.aLossBeforeTheRingsJoinsTheTakesFileCountsNotItsRingsRawCount` lost the stub nothing read and was renamed for what it pins; the empty-queue failed-callback test is named for, and asserts, exactly one dropout; `ClockStatusIndicatorTest.aRelockAndAnUnlockWhileUnlockedIsShownAreMarkedWithNoNotice` is renamed for its assertion; the seal's force hold and two block-observer holds of `CaptureFlushServiceTest` wait with no timeout of their own, and every caller releases them in a `finally`; and the Javadoc of `InputBlockLossListener`, `CallbackBackendAdapter.DROP_INPUT_BLOCK`, `AudioEngine.capturedInputBlocksLost()` and `CapturedInputLoss`, `CaptureFlushService`, `RecordingPipeline.requestStop`, `TakeDropouts`, `TakeManifest.overflowBlocks()` and `ClockEvent.UNLOCKED`, `ClockLockEvent`, `AudioBackend.clockLockStatus()`, `MockAudioBackend.setClockLockStatus`, `ClockLockAnnouncer`, `XrunJournal.ResyncMark`, `EngineHealthFeed` and the badge's two comments in `styles.css` says what the code does.

Built after the engineer's acceptance read of the round, in the round's last change set, from consult 50 and the second probe run (Verification):
- **A panel change that the driver answers with a reset request** (the engineer's acceptance read's Known limit; ruling E271). A driver's reset request is the person's switch for the stream that answers it: the stream the engine reopens for the driver's request to be reset or to change its buffer size, or, when no stream was open, the engine made no such reopen, that reopen failed before the backend's own open succeeded, or another open of the same ASIO backend came before it, the next stream the same ASIO backend opens, starts with its first two polls as switch intervals (`SWITCH_INTERVALS`), so resync requests from that stream's start to the end of its second poll, a little more than 1 s after the start (longer when the monitor's driver calls are slow), are counted in the Xruns number, logged and, during a take, marked clock=resync, but never declare the clock lost, while a bad poll answer in an interval that holds no resync request is still judged. This holds whatever caused the request, a change made in the driver's control panel or the driver itself, whatever the order of the panel's close, the request and the closed stream's last poll, and also for a panel whose call returns at once; no other stream gets such a window, a plain reopen included. With a healthy clock the badge shows the reopened stream's starting and checking views and then the clock bright, with no clock-loss notice and no clock=unlocked line; resync requests past about 2 s after the reopened stream's start are judged as on any stream. A clock lost across the change is declared on the reopened stream from its third poll at the earliest, about 2 s after its start, when its polls answer badly, or from its fourth, about 3 s after it, when only resync requests show it, and, as the first decision of its measurement, it is dated at the reopened stream's start (ruling E269): a take recording across the reopen (future story 464) is marked clock=unlocked from about the reopen, or gets no new line when it is already marked unlocked, and the unlock notice comes again (rulings E248, E249, E260, E269 and E271). (Consult 50; the clauses on which stream gets the window as the engineer's acceptance read of the round's last change set corrected them against the code: a reset request that comes while a stream is still being opened stops that stream before it judges anything, and an open that fails starts no clock monitor, so the request's window passes to the reopen the engine makes for it, or to the next stream the backend opens. A further check against the code then gave "when no stream was open, the engine made no such reopen, that reopen failed before the backend's own open succeeded, or another open of the same ASIO backend came before it" for that read's "when no stream was open or that reopen failed": the window goes to the first stream that backend opens and starts after the request, so a stream opened on that backend before the engine's reopen runs, for instance by a Stop that closes the stream and a Play, takes the window and the engine's reopen gets none; a stream closed before that reopen runs leaves it no stream to reopen, and a device loss still in force when that reopen would run drops the request; and a reopen that fails only after the backend's own open succeeded, when the engine's render drive cannot start for instance, has taken the window, so the stream after it gets none. The engineer's ruling on this further correction accepted it: every close of an ASIO backend releases the driver (`ASIOExit`) and every open initialises it again (`ASIOInit`), so the first stream that backend opens and starts after the request is the one in which the driver comes up on what it asked to be reset for, whichever open that is, and a later reopen initialises again a driver whose request was already answered, as a plain reopen does.) As built, `AsioClockLockMonitor.pause()`, which only the backend's reset handling calls, is the latch: `stop()` leaves it, and the next `start` reads and clears it and starts its stream with `SWITCH_INTERVALS` switch polls, which count down as a closed panel session's do (ruling E260). A session of the driver's control panel taken up, or a switch answered, inside that window sets the count back to two, so neither window shortens the other. Tests: `AsioClockLockMonitorTest.aStreamThatAnswersADriverResetJudgesNoResyncInItsFirstTwoPolls`, `aBadPollAnswerInTheFirstPollsOfAStreamThatAnswersAResetIsStillJudged`, `aStreamWithNoResetSinceItsBackendsLastOpenIsJudgedFromItsSecondPoll`, `aStreamThatAnswersAResetAfterAPanelChangeGetsItsWindowWhateverTheOrder` (six orders of the panel's close, the request and the closed stream's last poll), `aStreamThatAnswersAResetGetsItsWindowAfterAPanelWhoseCallReturnedAtOnce`, `aPanelSessionClosedInsideTheResetWindowGetsItsOwnTwoIntervals`, `aSwitchAnsweredInsideTheResetWindowGetsItsOwnTwoIntervals` and `aResetRequestedWhileNoStreamRunsGivesTheNextStreamTheWindow`; `AsioBackendClockLockTest.aStreamReopenedForADriverResetJudgesNoResyncInItsFirstTwoPollsAndAPlainReopenDoes` and `aStreamReopenedForADriverResetGetsItsWindowAfterTheStreamBeforeItTookThePanelSessionUp`, through the backend's own reset handling, close and open.
- **PortAudio's wake of its drain thread** (the engineer's acceptance read's Known limit; ruling E272). The ruling's sentence, what is built on both bridges and its tests are in the bullet on blocks an input thread could not build or publish above.
- **The drain's interrupt clause** (the second probe run's surviving break). With `!Thread.currentThread().isInterrupted()` removed from `CallbackBackendAdapter.drains`, `CallbackBackendAdapterTest` stayed green: since the callback adapter's failed open came to complete and replace its publisher (above), a drain left behind is stopped by its cleared flag and then by its closed publisher on every path but one. The clause was kept for that one: when completing the publisher fails before the old publisher is closed, which for the adapter's own `SubmissionPublisher` only an error of the JVM itself can cause, the drain's publisher is still open at the next open, which pins it for the next stream too, and only the interrupt stops the drain left behind. The Javadoc of `drains` and of the drain loop now says which clause stops a drain on each path. Test: `CallbackBackendAdapterTest.aDrainLeftBehindWhosePublisherStayedOpenStopsOnItsInterruptWhenTheAdapterReopens`, in which a publisher that refuses its close stands in for that error.
- The same change set pinned the outer step of the Stop's three and the `sealed` component of the ledger's per-source value, both declined before (the bullets on the Stop's three steps and on blocks an input thread could not build or publish above), and added the badge's padding and border to the running flash's comparison (the bullet on tests and texts above).

Built after the round's acceptance, in four change sets, because the owner required the round's Known limits closed by mechanism wherever a bounded change can close them, from consults 51, 52 and 53 (each change set from a specification, a critic and an addendum; Verification), and, after the four confirming reviews of those change sets, from consult 54 (rulings E300 to E304), which the fix round after it and the last fix pass built with every finding of those reviews and of the review of that fix round (Verification):
- **The early seal's window** (review BC-4, finding 4; ruling E280). "From the moment the capture thread seals a take early until the record coordinator stops the take for it, the coordinator, which reads whether the take's capture has been sealed, treats the take as no longer recording for clock marks and clock notices: an unlock the badge hands over then is not marked, and its notice ends with no take sentence ("External clock lost on <name> (<source>). Check the clock cable and the clock master."), as does E216's notice for unlocks that came and went; a relock is neither written nor held; a resync is counted in the Xruns number and logged but not marked. The coordinator reads that before it hands the take a mark, and again after it hands an unlock or a start mark over, and the notice's ending is read after that, so a notice never says the take is marked when take.manifest does not hold the line; in the instant the seal lands, a line can reach the take without its notice saying so. A relock held when the seal comes is dropped, and a clock event the take receives only after its early seal is not marked, as after a Stop (ruling E229; Known limits)." (Consult 51's words, with the sentence on the order of the reads as consult 52 replaced it: the change set's critic found consult 51's "The coordinator hands a mark over before it reads that" false as built, since the coordinator also reads the flag before any hand-over and hands nothing over when it already reads sealed.) It closes the Not-built item on the early seal's window and the declined test of an early seal dropping a held relock; the relock dropped in that window stays a Known limit, in consult 51's words, and so do the clock events seen only after an early seal (ruling E229; future story 482). As built: `RecordingPipeline.isCaptureSealed()` reads `CaptureFlushService.ignoresClockEvents()`, the flush service's volatile `sealed` and `terminated`, the one predicate its `noteClockEvent` drops a mark on; it allocates nothing and takes no lock, so the badge's take marks read it on every display frame (`TakeClockMarks.takeRecording()` through `RecordCoordinator.isTakeCaptureSealed()`, read before the audio check, so a take recording MIDI and audio never reads as recording without audio; pinned from the bytecode by `TakeRecordingReadBytecodeSentinelTest`). The coordinator reads it before it hands the take an unlock, a relock, a resync or a start mark, and again after the hand-over for an unlock's or a start mark's answer; the sampler and the Stop write a held relock only while it reads unsealed. The app's tests set the read through the package-private seam `RecordCoordinator.readTheCaptureAsSealedForTest`, since no app fixture can seal a take early. Tests: `TakeDropoutsAndClockMarksTest.anUnlockOrAStartMarkAfterTheEarlySealMarksNothingAndTheTakeReadsAsNotRecording`, `aRelockAfterTheEarlySealIsNeitherWrittenNorHeld` and `aRelockHeldWhenTheEarlySealComesIsDropped`; `ClockBadgeDuringATakeTest.anUnlockHandedOverAfterTheEarlySealIsNotMarkedAndItsNoticeHasNoTakeSentence`, `aLostAndRelockedLookAfterTheEarlySealHasNoEnding` and `aSealLandingBetweenTheHandOverAndTheNoticeNeverClaimsTheMark`; `EngineHealthFeedTest.aResyncAfterTheEarlySealIsLoggedAndCountedButNotMarked`; `TakeClockMarksOfTheTransportTest.aMidiAndAudioTakeAfterTheEarlySealGetsNoRecordingContinues`; `CaptureFlushServiceTest.aClockEventIsIgnoredFromTheMomentAnEarlySealBeginsAndTheFlagSaysSo`; `DiskExhaustionSealsCleanlyContractTest.exhaustionSealsTheTakeWithEverythingCapturedSoFarAndKeepsDraining` and `aWriteFailureMidAppendSealsWithWriteFailureAndTheSameGuarantees`, which now read the pipeline's flag before and after the seal; and the new `TakeRecordingReadBytecodeSentinelTest.theBadgesReadOfTheTakeAllocatesNothingAndReadsOnlyVolatileFlagsInTheCore` (the read, walked with the Class-File API from the `takeRecording()` that `TakeClockMarks.of` builds, allocates nothing but its FX-thread check's exception, reads only volatile flags in the core, and `noteClockEvent` drops a mark on the same predicate) and `theWalkReportsEveryForbiddenKindOnAFixture`.
- **A reset request while the driver's control panel stays open** (ruling E278; future story 489, closed). "When a driver asks to be reset, or announces a new buffer size, and the engine's reopen for that request, 250 ms after its last request, finds the driver's control panel that Audio Settings opened still open, the reopen waits until the panel has closed and then reopens the stream, instead of taking the refusal for a reopen that failed. While it waits the person sees "Reconfiguring audio engine…", the Engine cell's "Engine: reconfiguring…", the clock badge's closed view and, during playback or a take, a transport that stands still; no stall verdict, no device loss and no Reconnect come, the window stays usable, and a Stop works as any Stop does. Requests that arrive during the wait join the one reopen, which uses the latest of them, a reset request with no format of its own reopening at the format the engine runs (ruling E290). Once the panel has closed and the engine has released the stopped stream's handle, the stream reopens as it would with no panel open, open or paused as the request found it, with no transport started, or, when a Play opened the stream after the close and before the reopen, as that Play left it, the transport it started rolling on after a short stand-still (ruling E289), and "Audio engine reconfigured" follows. The first stream the backend opens after the request answers it, normally the reopened one, so its first two polls are switch intervals (ruling E271) and a clock lost across the change is dated at its start (ruling E269). An Apply of Audio Settings or a Restart Audio Engine that reaches the engine before that reopen has run ends the wait: the person's settings stand and nothing reopens for the request; while the panel is still open its open is refused, as any open is then, and the engine comes to rest stopped (ruling E288). A reopen that still fails once the panel has closed declares the device lost with ruling E113's notice; a device loss declared during the wait stands with its own cause and notice, and nothing reopens; a panel that never closes keeps the engine reconfiguring, with no loss, until the person closes it, and closing the application ends the wait without a reopen (rulings E113, E271, E278 and E288 to E290)." (Consult 51's words as consult 52 replaced them.) Consult 54 then extended the Apply's clause to a request no reopen waits for yet, from the window-reset review's note 2 (an Apply or a Restart while a driver's request was still in its 250 ms window, before any reopen waited, left that request to reopen at its own format over the person's settings once the panel had closed, or, with no panel open, to stop and reopen the stream a second time after the Apply's own reopen): "An Apply of Audio Settings or a Restart Audio Engine also ends a driver's format-change request still in its 250 ms window, whether or not a reopen waits for a driver's control panel and whether or not a panel is open: the person's settings stand and nothing reopens for the request. With a panel open the Apply's open is refused as in E288, the engine rests stopped at the person's format, and the panel's close brings no reopen and no "Audio engine reconfigured"; with none, the Apply's own reopen re-initialises the driver. A driver's rate change so ended is not announced. A request that arrives after the Apply has taken up the requests still in their window is answered as before; an Apply the record guard declined ends nothing." (Ruling E304, built by the last fix pass; its "has taken up the requests still in their window" the engineer's acceptance read's, for its "has begun", since the Apply ends each request still in its window when it takes them up under the controller's monitor, not when it begins.) The manager's additions, true as built and not read by the engineer: an unplug during the wait is declared as the removal it is, with "Audio device disconnected — playback stopped", because the stream the reset stopped still holds the device (ruling E278); with no stream open at the request, the reopen likewise waits for the panel before it asks the driver anything, and once the panel has closed it sets the request's format and opens nothing, as it does with no panel open, and an unplug of that idle device meanwhile is no loss, as at any time no stream is open (rulings E278 and E290). It closes the Known limit on a driver that applies a change while its control panel stays open, and future story 489, which this round built; the Known limit on a driver reset during a take stays, in consult 51's words (Known limits, ruling E41). As built, `DefaultAudioEngineController` keeps the waiting reopen in a field (`reopenAwaitingPanel`), never in a waiting thread: the reopen stops the stream (step 1) and then, while a driver's control panel is registered, stores itself instead of asking the driver anything, so no capability query, format change or open meets the panel on the driver's control thread under the controller's monitor; a request that reaches the worker meanwhile joins it and replaces its request whole (ruling E290); the panel's close hands it back to the format-change worker after the engine's drain, which reopens unless a panel is registered again or a request is still in its 250 ms window; a Settings apply or a Restart ends it and restores the stream itself, and a declared loss and the controller's close clear it. Every `applyConfiguration` call that `beginConfigurationChange()` lets through, past its refusal on the FX thread and the record guard, runs `endTheWaitForTheSettings` under the controller's monitor, which takes up the request still in its window (`latestFormatChange.getAndSet(null)`) whether or not a reopen waits, and, when none waits, logs "Audio settings applied while a format change <device> asked for was in its window; the settings stand" (ruling E304); the request's task takes the request up under the same monitor and does nothing when it finds none, so whichever of the two takes the monitor first decides. Besides the Settings dialog's Apply and Restart Audio Engine, `applyConfiguration` is also the start-up apply of the saved settings (`MainController.applyStartupAudioSettings`) and the Settings dialog's rollback after a failed apply (`SettingsDialog.restoreAudioRuntime`), so each of them ends such a request too; the rollback, a second `applyConfiguration`, also ends a request that arrived during the failed apply, after that apply's take-up, and had not yet been taken up by its own reopen. Restart Audio Engine counts such a stream as open when it judges whether it succeeded (`isAStreamAwaitingItsReopen()`, story 337, ruling E62). Tests: the new `ResetRequestWhileTheDriverControlPanelIsOpenTest` (19: `aResetRequestWhileThePanelIsOpenReopensOnceItCloses`, `aResetRequestWithNoPanelOpenReopensAsBefore`, `aReopenThatFailsAfterThePanelClosesIsALoss`, `requestsDuringTheWaitJoinOneReopenAtTheLatestFormat`, `aRequestStillInItsWindowWhenThePanelClosesJoinsTheOneReopen`, `theWaitHoldsNoMonitor`, `aDeviceLossDuringTheWaitStandsAndNothingReopens`, `closingTheControllerEndsTheWaitWithoutAReopen`, `aPausedStreamWaitsAndComesBackPaused`, `aStopDuringTheWaitIsAnOrdinaryStopAndTheReopenStillFollows`, `aRequestTakenUpAfterTheControllerClosedNeitherReopensNorWaits`, `aRequestWithNoStreamOpenWaitsForThePanelBeforeAnyDriverCall`, `aRestartDuringTheWaitEndsItAndItsRefusedOpenIsNoSuccess`, `aSettingsApplyAtThePanelsCloseEndsTheWaitBeforeTheReopenRuns`, `aReopenThatRunsBeforeASettingsApplyIsFollowedByIt`, `aPlayAfterTheCloseAndBeforeTheReopenIsRestoredAsThePlayLeftIt` and `aResetRequestWithNoFormatThatJoinsTheWaitReopensAtTheEnginesFormat`, and, from the last fix pass, `aSettingsApplyWhileARequestIsInItsWindowAndThePanelIsOpenEndsItAndTheCloseReopensNothing` (the format stays the Apply's 1024 frames, the engine rests stopped with no stream open and one notice, "Audio output could not be started: …Retry once the panel closes", the panel's close brings no second notice and no capability query, and the next Play opens at 1024) and `aSettingsApplyWhileARequestIsInItsWindowWithNoPanelEndsIt` (the Apply's reopen is the only one, at 1024, the stream running and no notice), ruling E304), with `SpyStreamingBackend` now counting the capability queries made while its panel runs; `DeviceLossLeadsDuringATakeTest.aTakeRecordingAtTheRequestGoesOnAcrossTheWait` (no rescue; the Stop seals the take `sealed-by=stop`); `AsioBackendClockLockTest.aResetWhileThePanelIsOpenIsAnsweredByTheStreamOpenedAfterTheClose`; and `FailureSpineWiringSourceTest.theWindowRegistersTheAudioHealthDrainRemovesItOnDisposalAndRestartsThroughTheController`, which now pins that the window's Restart counts a stream awaiting its reopen as open.
- **Each resync dated at its own arrival** (ruling E270, built by E279; future story 485, closed). "The ASIO backend keeps the arrival of each resync request, the clock reading its driver's message call takes, on the driver's message path with no allocation, lock or blocking, and the xrun feed takes the arrivals up at each engine-health tick, whether or not the device callbacks advanced (ruling E73): each clock=resync line in take.manifest is dated at the newest frame sampled at or before the arrival of the request it stands for, however many requests one tick, one stretch carried while the device callbacks stood still or one poll interval holds, and however long that stretch, since the take's frame history keeps the newest sample at or before the oldest arrival the feed still holds or the journal still owes the take (ruling E291); an xrun-log Resync entry that stands for several requests shows the arrival of the first of them. The clock-lock monitor dates a bad poll interval that holds resync requests at the arrival of its first request, or, when the backend no longer keeps that arrival, at the interval's start, so a clock=unlocked line declared from resync requests lies at or before the first request of its first bad interval, or at the measurement's start under ruling E269. Between two of the feed's reads the backend keeps the arrivals of the first 256 requests, more than one tick holds at one request per buffer period down to 32-frame buffers at 48 kHz, but for one whose message call is under way as the feed reads after a stretch of more than 256; a request not kept is dated at the last arrival kept before it, at most the rest of that tick early, and an entry whose arrivals the feed does not hold, as when a read of the backend fails or the stream closes between the feed's reads, at the feed's read before the one that counted it. No date is later than the driver's message call that brought the request: one can fall inside that call, after its clock reading, when the driver sends from two threads at once or the call straddles a read, by the few instructions of the call unless the operating system holds the driver's thread there (ruling E292). The Xruns count, how intervals are judged, one mark per resync and what Clear removes do not change (rulings E40, E106, E120 to E122, E225, E226, E248, E249, E260, E279, E291 and E292)." (Consult 52's words, which replace consult 51's.) It closes the Not-built item on several resyncs of one interval dated at the newest arrival, the Known limit of ruling E270 that replaced E123's, and future story 485, which this round built. As built: `AsioBackend.noteDriverResync` writes the new daw-sdk `ResyncArrivalRing` (`KEPT`, 256): a clock read, an atomic claim, the arrival stored and the count last; `take()` hands the requests since the last take over as runs (`DriverResyncArrivals`, new), and `arrivalOf(request)` is the monitor's lock-free read. `AudioBackend.takeDriverResyncArrivals()` (default `DriverResyncArrivals.NONE`) and `AudioEngine.takeDriverResyncArrivals()`, which answers `NONE` with no open stream or when the backend's answer is `null` or throws, replace `newestDriverResyncNanos()`, and the monitor probe's `driverResyncArrivalNanos(request)` replaces `lastDriverResyncNanos()`; `AsioClockLockMonitor` dates a resync-bad interval at its first request's arrival, else at the interval's start (`seenResyncsNanos`). `XrunFeed` takes the arrivals at every tick, before its gates, carries them with its growth, forgets them at every tick that leaves no resync growth carried, forgets every arrival it holds at a sample that is not valid, as when the stream has closed (the resync review's second should-fix finding), and, at a new render-drive epoch or a change of the watched heartbeat's kind, every arrival kept from earlier ticks, since growth is then measured from that sample's count, which holds each of their requests or is another backend's (the review of the fix round after consult 54: `keepArrivals` returns the number of the first request the tick took up, or, when it took none, the number after the last arrival kept, and `startAfresh` forgets every arrival numbered below it), dates an entry it cannot date at its read before the one that counted it (each sample's `readNanos`), and tells the journal the dates it holds (`XrunJournal.noteResyncDatesHeld`). `XrunJournal.appendResyncs` dates the entry at the first arrival and keeps one `ResyncMark` per arrival, and `resyncDatesOwed()` answers the undrained marks and the feed's dates. `TakeFrameHistory` keeps each sample it gives up, at the span or at its capacity, that is the newest at or before such a date, with the time of the sample after it, drops at each sample every kept sample whose interval holds no owed date, not only the oldest, so a kept sample behind one still owed goes too (the resync review's first should-fix finding; the prune is skipped when none is kept), and reads the dates at each sample through `RecordCoordinator.setResyncDatesOwed`, which `MainController` wires to the window's journal; with no dates owed it behaves as before. `RealTimeSafeContractTest.theDriversResyncPathTakesNoLockAllocatesNothingAndLogsNothing` walks the message path. The frame history does not keep samples for the clock-lock monitor's evidence of a decision not yet made (consult 52 left that to the manager, who did not build it; Known limits). Tests: the new `ResyncArrivalRingTest` (6: `eachRequestTakenUpIsDatedAtItsOwnArrivalInRequestOrder`, `beyondTheFirst256SinceTheLastTakeARequestIsDatedAtThe256th`, `aTakeFreesTheBoundForTheRequestsAfterIt`, `aRequestsArrivalIsReadUntilItsSlotIsReused`, `aRequestBeyondTheBoundIsNotKept` and `driverThreadsSendingAtOnceLoseNoRequestAndEachIsTakenUpOnce`); `AsioBackendResyncArrivalTest.eachRequestsArrivalIsCountedAndTakenUpOnceInRequestOrder`, which replaces `theNewestResyncsArrivalIsKnownOnceOneArrived`; `AsioClockLockMonitorTest.anUnlockFromResyncsIsDatedAtTheFirstRequestOfItsFirstBadInterval`, which replaces `anUnlockWhoseFirstIntervalHeldTwoResyncsIsDatedAtTheNewest`, and `aBadIntervalWhoseFirstArrivalIsNoLongerKeptIsDatedAtItsStart`; `AsioBackendClockLockTest.anUnlockFromResyncsIsDatedAtTheFirstRequestOfItsFirstBadInterval`; `AudioEngineBackendCountersTest.resyncArrivalsAreTakenFromTheOpenStreamsBackendAndNoneWithoutAStream`; `RealTimeSafeContractTest.theDriversResyncPathTakesNoLockAllocatesNothingAndLogsNothing`; `XrunFeedTest.twoResyncsInOneTickAreOneEntryAtTheFirstArrivalAndAMarkAtEachArrival`, `threeHundredResyncsInOneTickAreOneEntryAtTheFirstArrivalWithEachDateTheBackendGave`, `theFeedTakesTheResyncArrivalsUpAtEveryTickWhetherOrNotItWrites`, `anArrivalTakenUpBeforeItsRequestIsCountedWaitsForTheTickThatCountsIt`, `aResyncCarriedIntoAPauseIsDiscardedWithItsArrival`, `aResyncCountedInTheStartUpAllowanceLeavesTheNextOneDatedAtItsOwnArrival`, `aResyncWithNoArrivalIsDatedAtTheReadBeforeTheOneThatCountedIt`, `resyncsCarriedWithNoArrivalAreDatedAtTheReadBeforeTheFirstIntervalThatCountedThem` and `aResyncCarriedThroughAStandstillAndOneAfterItAreEachMarkedAtTheirOwnArrival`, which replaces `aResyncCarriedThroughAStandstillIsLoggedAtItsArrival`; `XrunJournalTest.aResyncEntryOfSeveralRequestsIsDatedAtTheFirstAndKeepsEachArrivalForTheTake`, `aResyncEntryOfNoRequestIsRefused` and `theDatesOwedToTheTakeAreItsUndrainedMarksAndWhatTheFeedHolds`; `EngineHealthFeedTest.eachResyncOfAnEntryIsMarkedAtTheFrameSampledAtOrBeforeItsOwnArrival`, `resyncsCarriedThroughAStandstillOfFourPointSixSecondsAreEachMarkedAtTheirOwnFrame` and `resyncsCarriedThroughAStandstillOfTwentySecondsAreEachMarkedAtTheirOwnFrame`; `TakeFrameHistoryTest.aSampleGivenUpIsKeptWhileItIsTheNewestAtOrBeforeAnOwedDate`, `pastItsCapacityTheHistoryStillKeepsTheSampleAtOrBeforeAnOwedDate` and `aKeptSampleGoesOnceNoDateItIsTheNewestBeforeIsOwed`; and `FailureSpineWiringSourceTest.theWindowWiresItsEngineHealthModelToTheBridgeTheDrainTheStripTheFeedAndTheShedLog`, which now pins the frame history's wiring to the journal; from the fix round after consult 54, `TakeFrameHistoryTest.aKeptSampleBehindOneStillOwedGoesOnceNoDateItIsTheNewestBeforeIsOwed` and `XrunFeedTest.aResyncCarriedUntilTheStreamClosesTakesItsArrivalWithIt`; and, from the last fix pass, `XrunFeedTest.aResyncCarriedAcrossAReopenNoTickSawTakesItsArrivalWithIt` (the stream stopped and started between two ticks, a new epoch with the drive alive: nothing is owed afterwards, and a resync the reopened backend counts later with no arrival is dated at the read before the one that counted it, ruling E292). Future story 485's acceptance is met piecewise, by the backend's ring, the engine's delegation, the feed's marks and the take's lines, and no single test drives a stub ASIO driver through to take.manifest.
- **Exact ring-loss accounting** (ruling E273; consult 49's Known limit on a gap= line placed at the end of a pass, closed). "The audio callback, the only writer of a capture ring, stamps each block it publishes with the ring's count of dropped blocks at that moment, with how many of those blocks carried audio a track of the take records (a block with a channel of input, or a recorded instrument's audio), and with how many were input-starved blocks, the render's silence for an input still live, so the capture thread knows, for every block it takes up, exactly how many blocks the ring dropped immediately before it and what they carried. Each run of dropped blocks gets one gap= line, at the end of the block the ring delivered before the run, counting the run's blocks that carried audio: drops are never merged across a delivered block and never split, and the ring-overflow warning names the line's own frame. The line is written once the capture thread has applied the block after the run, normally in the pass after the one that first saw the drop. In a punch take a run counts in the take's dropouts, all its lined blocks, exactly when the frames it would have held, from its line to the start of the next block the ring delivered, reach into the punch region, and by its own extent when no delivered block follows it (rulings E86, E104, E180 and E182): a drop inside the region always counts, and one outside it never does. overflow-blocks holds exactly the dropped blocks that have a gap= line. A pass over a ring rewrites take.manifest for the lines it added at most once, as before, and gives at most one ring-overflow warning, for the first run it lined, whose "dropped in total" is the take's overflow-blocks so far; the other runs of that pass are in that total and in take.manifest (ruling E273)." (Consult 51's words, its first sentence as consult 52 replaced it.) "**A dropped input-starved block** (ruling E281). A block a full capture ring drops that the render passed with no captured block while its input was live counts as the starved block it would have been: with its gap= line, in overflow-blocks and in the take's dropouts when its run of starved blocks ends with the input delivering again, or at the person's Stop or an early seal with no declared stop of that input after it; and with no line, no overflow-blocks, no warning and no count when that run continues into the input's declared stop, whose input-stopped line and warning report the stretch (rulings E49 and E277), or at a device-loss seal, whose loss reports it (rulings E48, E90 and E264). Its run's one line waits until that starved run has ended." (Consult 52; its run that "continues into the input's declared stop" gets nothing only when that stop flags every physical track of the input, as ruling E301 next bounds it.) Consult 54 bounded the declared stop's part, on the second must-fix finding of the confirming review of this change set's capture-thread half, which found a regression of this round against the commit under review: at `d49ea241` the capture thread lined and counted every block a ring dropped, whatever it carried, while this round's code gave an input-starved block dropped after the input's last delivery to the declared stop even when that stop wrote no line for the input's tracks, so nothing reported it: "An input-starved block the ring dropped after the input's last delivery is the declared stop's only when that stop, by its own line or an earlier flag, flags every physical track of the input (E49). Applied, such a run waits from the stop's block until a later block of the input flags them all (the stop's, no count), the input delivers again, or the take is sealed. At the person's Stop's seal and at an early seal a run no stop covers is lined where its run begins and counted by its own extent, as a starved run with no declared stop after it (E281, E295), once for the input whichever of its tracks are flagged. At a device-loss seal it stays the loss's (E281, E286; Known limit). Restores, for that case, the line and count HEAD gave every drop." (Ruling E301; the reviewer's own fix, "when a track of the input is flagged", was not built, since the ruling asks every physical track.) On that review's sixth note consult 54 added to E281, with no change of code: "a delivery the stop fence cut whole, claimed and never published, ends the starved run as a published or dropped one does: the input delivered it (consult 54)". "**Drops no delivered block follows** (ruling E275). A run of dropped blocks that no block the take applied follows has no next block to place it: at the person's Stop, its blocks that carried audio join the late drops' gap= line the seal writes at the end of the last block the take applied from that ring plus the blocks left behind on it (ruling E242), whether the final sweep's look or the seal's own look found them, and count in the take's dropouts by that line's own extent (rulings E104, E213, E240 and E256); at a device-loss seal not sealed early in its final sweep (ruling E286), those the final sweep's look finds were delivered before the dropout and get their line at the end of the last block the take applied and their count by its own extent, while every block a ring receives or drops after that look is part of the loss (ruling E264); after an early seal they get nothing (ruling E274). A block a callback drops after the seal's last look at the person's Stop is still not seen (ruling E255; Known limits)." (Consult 51, with consult 52's clause on an early seal in the final sweep.) Three further rulings settle the edges: a run whose next delivered block starts at or before its line with no loop wrap between them, as when the transport was moved back or stood still, counts when its blocks' own extent forward from its line or back from that block's start reaches into the punch region (ruling E285); an early seal in a device-loss seal's final sweep, on any ring, makes ruling E274 govern every ring, whichever ring the sweep took first (ruling E286); and a run on the stream's own ring in which a block that carried only silence came before one that carried a recorded instrument's audio, which an instrument insert removed and added again while the ring was full can make, is judged over the whole run's frames (ruling E287). The one place where a starved stretch the take received and one the ring dropped are judged differently is a Known limit (ruling E294). It closes the three Known limits of the round on ring drops: drops lined in a take sealed early that lie past the file's end, the one dropped block a race with the capture thread's last release could hide, and a gap= line that need not lie where its blocks were dropped, with its punch-take consequence (rulings E86 and E263; consult 49). As built: `CaptureRing.claim(int)` takes `DELIVERED`, `STARVED` and `INSTRUMENT` (neither of the first two for the silence after a declared stop) in place of the no-argument `claim()`; a drop counts the total and, by kind, the drops that carried audio, the input-starved ones and those with no input, with the input-starved drops since a block with delivered input was claimed (`starvedSinceDelivery`, reset by every `DELIVERED` claim, kept or dropped) and the drops of audio that came after a dropped block of silence in the same run (`audioAfterSilence`); every count is stored before the total, each slot is stamped with them at its claim, and `CaptureRing.stampOf` and `dropCounts` read them total first, orders `RealTimeSafeContractTest.theRingsDropCountsAreStoredAndReadInTheOrderTheirReadersRestOn` pins from the bytecode; the ring's `droppedAfterSequence` and the flush service's gap anchor are gone, and `CaptureRing` is 899 lines. `CaptureCallback` claims with the block's kind. `CaptureFlushService.lineTheRunBefore` lines a run once the block after it is kept, after that block's write; a run whose starved drops lead into a kept starved block, or into a kept zero-width block after which a physical track of the input is still unflagged (`stopCovers` false, ruling E301), waits whole (`WaitingRun`) until its starved run ends (`resolveWaitingRuns`): on the next delivery, kept or dropped, on a kept block with channels of the input after drops that held its silence (ruling E302), at the Stop with no declared stop after it that leaves every physical track of the input flagged, or at an early seal (`lineThePassBeforeTheEarlySeal`, in `failAndSeal`, the headroom branch and the run loop's catch), with its starved blocks, and on a kept zero-width block after which every physical track of the input is flagged, or at a device-loss seal with its starved run still open, without them. `hasAnUncoveredTrack` asks the tracks `endStarvedRun` gives an entry, so the kept and the dropped part of one starved stretch are judged alike; at the person's Stop the seal now marks the dropped silence before it decides (`lineWhatTheSweepLeft`'s `stopAfter`), and the device-loss seal is unchanged (ruling E301). `lookAtTheRing`, the look that ends each pass, writes the pass's lines once, gives its one warning and notes the ring's drops for `awaitFlushed`, whose fence ends at the look (`sourceLookedBlocks`) and reads itself once more when it finds the thread stopped. At the seal, `noteBlocksLeftBehind` calls `lineWhatTheSweepLeft` at the person's Stop and, at a device-loss seal not sealed early, `lineTheDropsBeforeTheDeviceLoss` with the counts `lookAtTheRing` kept in the final sweep; neither runs after an early seal. `FileLossCounts`, rewritten, judges a run by its next block (`runReachesTheRegion`) or by its own extent (`reachesTheRegionByItsOwnExtent`), and keeps each source's punch region and loop window (`windowOf`); `FrameSpans.wrapsBeforeTheRegion` is new. Tests: `CaptureRingTest.eachBlockCarriesTheDropCountsOfItsClaim` and `peekAtReadsAPublishedBlockInPlaceAndNothingOutsideTheQueue`, with `aDropMarksTheLastBlockPublishedBeforeIt` removed and every other claim in the ring's tests made with its kind; the new `CaptureCallbackDropCountsTest` (5: `aDeliveredBlockTheFullRingDropsCarriedAudio`, `aZeroWidthBlockWithNoRecordedInstrumentThatTheFullRingDropsCarriedOnlySilence`, `aStarvedBlockTheFullRingDropsIsCountedAsStarved`, `theBlockAfterTheDropsCarriesTheirCounts` and, from the fix round after consult 54, `aZeroWidthBlockWithARecordedInstrumentsAudioThatTheFullRingDropsCarriedAudio`, which pins the instrument kind at the callback); `FileLossCountsTest`, rewritten, among them `aRunReachesTheRegionWhenItsFramesToTheNextBlockDo`, `blocksBehindTheFinalSweepAreJudgedByTheirOwnExtent`, `aRunWhoseNextBlockStartsAtItsLineReachesTheRegionByEitherOwnExtent` and `aBackwardStepWithNoLoopIsJudgedByTheRunsOwnExtentsOnly` (ruling E285), and, from the fix round after consult 54, `aRunWhoseNextBlockStartsAtItsLineReachesTheRegionByItsExtentForwardFromTheLine` and `aBackwardStepWithNoWrapInALoopTakeIsJudgedByTheRunsOwnExtentsOnly`, since the two before them were met by the backward extent alone and a looped take stepped back with no wrap had no test (the confirming review's first should-fix finding and fifth note); `FrameSpansTest.wrapsBeforeTheRegionCountsTheWrapsBeforeTheSpansFirstFrameInside`; `CaptureFlushServiceTest.aRunIsLinedAtTheEndOfTheBlockBeforeIt`, `twoRunsInOnePassGetTwoLines`, `aDropInsideThePunchRegionAlwaysCounts`, `aDropOutsideItNeverCounts`, `aRunNoBlockFollowsYetIsLinedWhenTheNextBlockIsApplied` and `oneManifestRewritePerPassThatLinesRuns` (ruling E273); `theDropsBeforeTheBlockTheHeadroomFloorDiscardsCountNothing`, `aRunBeforeABlockKeptBeforeAnObserverFailureCounts`, `aRunBeforeTheBlockWhoseWriteFailedCountsNothing`, `aRunsWarningComesBeforeTheEarlySealWhenALaterBlockOfItsPassFailsItsWrite` and `aRunsWarningComesBeforeTheEarlySealWhenTheHeadroomFloorDiscardsALaterBlockOfItsPass` (ruling E274), with the two renamed tests of the bullet on a take sealed early above; `atTheStopTheDropsTheFinalSweepsLookFoundJoinTheLateDropsLine` and `atADeviceLossTheDropsTheFinalSweepFindsAreLinedAtTheLastBlock` (ruling E275); `anEarlySealInADeviceLossSealsFinalSweepLinesNoRingsTrailingDrops`, with the trailing drops on either ring (ruling E286); `aStarvedDropWhoseRunEndsWithADeliveryCountsWithTheDeliveryAndNotBefore`, `aStarvedDropWhoseRunContinuesIntoTheDeclaredStopGetsNothing`, `atTheStopAStarvedDropWithItsStarvedRunOpenCounts`, `aRunOfADeliveredThenAStarvedDropBeforeAKeptStarvedBlockAndAStopCountsItsDeliveredBlock`, `aRunOfADeliveredThenAStarvedDropBeforeAKeptStarvedBlockAndAStopIsJudgedByItsOwnExtent`, `aDeliveredBlockTheRingDropsEndsTheStarvedRunBeforeIt`, `atADeviceLossAStarvedDropWithItsStarvedRunOpenGetsNothing`, `atADeviceLossAWaitingRunWhoseStarvedRunADroppedDeliveryEndedCounts` and `atADeviceLossAStarvedDropADroppedDeliveryEndedIsLinedWithIt` (ruling E281; the last two from the second critic of the change set); `aRunWhoseSilenceCameBeforeAnInstrumentsAudioIsJudgedOverTheWholeRun`, in both orders (ruling E287), and, from the fix round after consult 54, `dropsNoKeptBlockFollowsWhoseSilenceCameBeforeAnInstrumentsAudioAreJudgedOverTheWholeRun`, in both orders at the person's Stop and at a device-loss seal (ruling E287 at the seal); from the fix round after consult 54, `atTheStopStarvedDropsBeforeADeclaredStopThatFlagsNoTrackAreLinedAndCounted` (the seal's half: one `gap=` line at 72 for three blocks, three dropouts and the ring-overflow warning, with no stop line for a file that holds no frame, which also pins E296's "a track whose file holds no frame gets no line"), `aStarvedDropBeforeAKeptDeclaredStopThatFlagsNoTrackCountsAtTheStop` (the applied half: no line during the take, then the same line and count at the Stop), `aStarvedDropBeforeADeclaredStopIsTheStopsOnceALaterBlockOfItsSilenceFlagsTheTrack` (the stop's once a later block of its silence flags the track: no line, no count) and `inALoopTakeAStarvedDropBeforeADeclaredStopThatFlagsNoTrackCountsAsAnAppliedOneWould` (the reviewer's case, set one lap later to fill the ring: the dropped block's line at the end of the block before it, two `input-silence` lines for the kept starved blocks and four dropouts, as if every starved block in the region had been applied) (ruling E301); and, from the fix pass after consult 53, `receivedStarvedBlocksBeforeADroppedDeliveryAndTheDeclaredStopAreTheStopsAndOnlyTheDropsCount`, which pins both halves of ruling E294's Known limit; `eachRingsLateDropsAreCountedAgainstWhatItsOwnSweepCounted` (its setup), `overflowBlocksOfATakeSealedEarlyHoldsOnlyTheDropsThatHaveALine`, `RecordingPipelineFlushServiceTest.ringOverflowIsCountedAndRecordedAsAGapLine`, `MultichannelInputCaptureStory326Test.onlyTheOverflowingInputDeviceReceivesGapEntriesAndAllCountersAgree` and the fixture of `StopFenceTailIntegrityContractTest` changed for the exact lines; and `RealTimeSafeContractTest.theRingsDropCountsAreStoredAndReadInTheOrderTheirReadersRestOn`, with the capture walk's reach and producer-side lists and its class Javadoc's capture-path item changed.
- **Silence after an input's declared stop, and what the take's seal says after the Stop** (rulings E276, E277, E282 to E284 and E293 to E299). "**Silence after an input's declared stop** (rulings E265 and E266 as amended by E276 and E277; future story 484, closed). Once the render has passed the first zero-width block of the stream's own input after the engine declared that input stopped, it renders that input at zero width to the end of the stream: a block the input's queue still yields, the one a delivery already past the subscriber's gate can add as the declaration lands, is discarded unrendered, so no track holds audio of that input after its input-stopped line, and the discarded block is neither heard nor metered, gets no gap= line and counts nowhere; blocks queued before the declaration are still rendered first (ruling E47). With the drop count of ruling E273, each block a capture ring publishes carries how many of the blocks dropped just before it carried only the silence the output clock delivers after an input's declared stop, that is, no channel of input and no recorded instrument's audio. Such a block, dropped by a full ring during the take, or left behind or dropped at the Stop, gets no gap= line, no place in overflow-blocks, no warning and no count in the take's dropouts, whether or not the take already holds that input's input-stopped line, since that line and its warning report the stop (rulings E49 and E207); a run of drops in which the input stopped is lined and counted for its blocks that carried audio only. When none of an input's silence reaches the take before a seal by the person's Stop or by a device loss not sealed early in its final sweep (ruling E286), because a full ring dropped it before the final sweep's look or, at the Stop, the ring was left holding or dropped it, the seal writes, for each track that records that input, the input-stopped line the take would have written had all of that silence reached it, with its warning in the words of ruling E284: the line of the first of those blocks that would have flagged the track, judged with its own header when the ring still holds it and otherwise with the punch region, loop window and legacy range of the last block the take received from that input over the frames the dropped ones would have held; its stop at the end of the last block the take received from that input with no region, and by rulings E77, E88, E176, E179 and E186 in a punch take, with no line when the input delivered to punch-out or the take ended before that silence reached the region; its resume read from that block's header when the ring holds it and at the stop otherwise (rulings E13, E14, E277, E282 and E283)." (Consult 51's words, their clause on a device loss and their tail as consult 52 replaced them; their silence dropped by a full ring gets nothing only when no kept block with channels of that input follows it, and their "when none of an input's silence reaches the take" reads "for the input's silence that did not reach the take after the last block kept from its ring", as rulings E302 and E303 below amend them.) Consult 53 then ruled the cases a critic and the build found, each in its own words: "In a punch take whose input ended at or before punch-in, silence of that input none of which reached the take, whose first block starts at or after punch-out with no loop end before it, flags each of the input's tracks whose file holds frames at the region's first frame, as an applied block there does (E77, E88), in E284's words; the starved run before it is the stop's (E49)" (ruling E296); "In a loop take, silence none of which reached the take, of an input that ended at or before punch-in, whose first block the transport played at or after punch-out, flags, before the re-entry rule is considered, each of the input's tracks whose file in that block's lap holds frames, at the region's first frame in that lap, the resume equal (E283: the first silence block that would have flagged the track); only the other tracks are judged by the re-entry rule (E88, E179). Where the block was played and which file is asked are E300's." (ruling E297, the one change consult 53 owed, in the words consult 54 amended it to); a seal-written stop's warning in a loop-record take "names the lap holding the last delivered frame the take received, at that frame's position in that lap's own file (E172)", a held block's lap moving only the stop's frame (ruling E293, which withdraws consult 52's "the warning names lap 2"); "A block left behind at the person's Stop that the render passed input-starved counts with its gap= line (E265)" (ruling E295); and "An input none of whose blocks reached the take holds no frame on any track; a stop the seal writes for it stands at the take's start frame with E284's "delivered no audio; the track holds no audio"" (ruling E298). Consult 54 then ruled on the case the fix pass after consult 53 left open and on the confirming review's findings, each in its own words: "E297's test is made on the start where the transport played the silence's first block, after the wraps before it (E283), and asks for frames of the file that block's lap writes. With no loop recording that is the take's one file: silence first played at or after punch-out in a later lap flags each of the input's tracks whose file holds frames at the region's first frame in that lap, the resume equal, the starved run before it the stop's (E49). With loop recording a lap after the last block of that input the take received has no file, so that silence gives no stop and the kept starved run is written and counted as input-silence. Blocks of that lap the ring dropped change neither: they wrote no frame. Replaces E297's "before the loop end"." (ruling E300: built to E297's "before the loop end", such silence gave no stop in either configuration, and built before that, by its played start alone, a stop in both, which under loop recording asked lap 1's file; E301 then lines and counts that lap's dropped starved blocks, which no stop covers, as the bullet above says); "An input that delivers again after its declared stop in the same take, which only a reopened stream gives (E276 holds within one pump), may have had that stop's silence dropped by a full ring: the block with which it delivers again (a kept block with channels after drops holding its silence) first gives the input's tracks the input-stopped line the seal gives such silence (E282), judged by the state of the block before it, warned as "… stopped delivering audio at m:ss.s into the take." or "… delivered no audio before it stopped.", and then lines every block the ring dropped since the last kept block, the silence among them, at the end of that block, counted as any run a kept block follows (E273, E285), the input's waiting runs ending counted (E281): the audio after them sits early in the file by that many blocks. Silence no kept block of that input follows stays E277's; the seal places a dropped silence before the audio its stamp shows after it. Amends E277's premise that every delivered block of an input precedes its silence." (ruling E302, on the first must-fix finding of the confirming review of the change set's capture-thread half: such silence got no input-stopped line and no `gap=` line, so the audio after it sat early in the file with nothing to say so, and the fix pass after consult 53 had pinned that outcome; a Known limit below); and "Silence of an input the take kept that flagged none of its tracks does not stop the seal marking that input's later silence the ring dropped or left behind: the seal judges the silence dropped since the last kept block, and the blocks left behind, as E282 and E283 do whether or not silence was kept; a track already flagged gets nothing again. Amends E277's "when none of an input's silence reached the take" to "for the input's silence that did not reach the take after the last block kept from its ring"." (ruling E303, on that review's second note: an input that delivered to punch-out, whose silence the take kept after punch-out flagged nothing, got no line for the next lap's silence the ring dropped inside the region). "**What the take's seal says after the Stop** (ruling E284). Every warning the capture thread gives about a take after the person's Stop, from its final sweep and its seal (blocks left behind, a ring overflow, an input's stop the seal writes, a callback that did not leave the ring, a segment that could not be sealed), reaches the person once: it is kept in the Notifications history and shown on the notification bar after the take's "Recording stopped" report, or when it comes for a take that publishes no clip, unless that report is an error, which stays on the bar. After a device loss that ended the take (a rescue, or a loss during the count-in) these warnings are kept in the Notifications history only, so the loss notice and its Reconnect stay on the bar; a take of a project that was replaced shows none. The warning for an input's stop that the seal writes says what the file holds: "Track 'T': input from 'D' stopped delivering audio at m:ss.s into the take; the track holds no audio from there." ("into lap N of the take" in a loop-record take), or "Track 'T': input from 'D' delivered no audio; the track holds no audio." (rulings E14, E172, E277 and E284)." (Consult 52's words, "After a device loss that ended the take (a rescue, or a loss during the count-in)" consult 53's for its "After a device loss", ruling E299, its ending as consult 54 corrected it against the code: "a take the person had stopped, still being written when a device loss comes, gives its report and then its seal's warnings on the bar as any stopped take, the loss notice kept in the Notifications history with its Reconnect action where the loss gives one (a stall, or a reset whose reopen failed; a removal's notice has none)".) It closes the Not-built item on the silence after an input's declared stop, and future story 484, which this round built. As built: `EngineStreamPump.inputEnded`, set on the pump thread once the render passed the ended input's first zero-width block, makes the render discard a block it polls after that, unrendered and counted nowhere (`EngineStreamPump` is 899 lines). The seal's stop comes from `CaptureFlushService.markTheDroppedSilence`, with `FileLossCounts.windowOf`, `FrameSpans.wrapsBeforeTheRegion` and the legacy range by block beat, and, for an input that stopped at or before punch-in whose dropped silence's first block the transport played at or after punch-out, the laps it lies past the loop end counted (`lapsAhead`), first flags the tracks whose file holds frames at the region's first frame in that lap (`flagTheInputsTracks`), which under loop recording only the input's current lap can do (`theLapsFileIsCurrent`), then judges the other tracks by the re-entry rule (rulings E296, E297 and E300); `markTheFirstHeldSilence` and `markTheInputStoppedAt` count the held blocks' wraps into the lap; the silence counted at the seal is the drops with no input since the last kept block, not the ring's count of them since the take began (the change set's second critic's defect 6), whether or not the take kept silence of that input before them, a track already flagged getting nothing again (ruling E303: the `silenceKept` gate is gone from both seals and from `markTheFirstHeldSilence`, and the field with it); the stop of an input none of whose blocks reached the take is the take's start frame (ruling E298). `markTheSilenceBeforeTheInputDeliversAgain`, the first statement of `applyBlock`'s `try`, before the wrap, the headroom check and `blockReceived`, so it judges by the window, lap, lane and delivered end of the block before, gives the stop for a kept block with channels of the input after drops that held its silence, the silence placed before the audio the stamp shows came after it, and `lineTheRunBefore` then lines and counts the whole run and ends the input's waiting runs counted (`deliversAfterItsSilence`); at both seals a dropped silence is placed before the audio the stamp shows after it (ruling E302). A successful reopen for a driver's reset request does not halt the take's capture (`haltRecordingCapture()` is called only where a device loss is declared), so the take records on across it (`DeviceLossLeadsDuringATakeTest.aTakeRecordingAtTheRequestGoesOnAcrossTheWait`), which is how an input can deliver again in the same take. The words are `recordInputStopped`'s while `sealWritesTheStops`, and E302's while `stopBeforeADelivery`: "Track 'T': input from 'D' stopped delivering audio at m:ss.s into the take." ("into lap N of the take." in a loop-record take) or "Track 'T': input from 'D' delivered no audio before it stopped.", with "the audio input " for "input from 'D' " when the device has no name to show, as in the other input-stopped warnings. `RecordCoordinator.onCaptureWarning` holds a warning that comes once the take has stopped recording or counting in and while it is being written, and `giveTheWarningsAfterTheStop` gives the held warnings at the take's publication: after the report on the bar, or to the history alone after an error report, a rescue or a count-in loss (`takeStoppedByALoss`); a retired controller gives none; a device loss while a stopped take is being written finds the take no longer RECORDING and changes nothing (ruling E299). Audio that could not be read back is an error report too, whose ERROR stays on the bar; a take whose completion failed keeps its ERROR on the bar and gives its held warnings to the history once (`endWrittenTake`); a warning of a start that failed is held for no take, since its take is not the one being written, so no later take's report is followed by it; and a warning while the take of a project that was replaced still records shows nothing, since the retired coordinator leaves that take recording. Tests: `EngineStreamPumpTest.aBlockTheStalledInputDeliversAfterItsFirstZeroWidthBlockIsDiscardedUnrendered`, which places the late block where a delivery past the gate leaves it, after the render's first zero-width block, and reads the outcome only after a render that polled it (consult 52); `CaptureFlushServiceTest.atTheStopASilentBlockLeftBehindAfterTheInputStoppedLineCountsNothing`, `atTheStopTheInputsFirstSilenceLeftBehindGivesItsInputStoppedLineAtTheSeal`, `atTheStopABlockLeftBehindWithARecordedInstrumentsAudioCountsWithItsLine`, `duringTheTakeSilenceARingDropsAfterTheInputStoppedLineCountsNothing`, `aDeliveredBlockDroppedBeforeTheFirstSilenceIsLinedAtTheBlockBeforeIt`, `theInputsFirstSilenceDroppedGivesOneStopLineAndNoGap`, `aRunInWhichTheInputStoppedIsLinedForItsDeliveredBlock`, `aRunInWhichTheInputStoppedIsJudgedByItsOwnExtent`, `atADeviceLossSilenceDroppedBeforeTheFinalSweepsLookGivesTheStopLineAtTheSeal`, `atTheStopSilenceDroppedAfterTheLastBlockGivesTheStopLineAndCoversTheStarvedRun` and `atTheStopTheInputsSilenceDroppedWithARecordedInstrumentsAudioIsLinedAndGivesTheStopLine` (ruling E277); `inAPunchTakeTheSealsStopForDroppedSilenceIsTheDeliveredEndInsideTheRegion`, `inAPunchTakeTheSealsStopForDroppedSilenceADeliveredEndPastPunchOutGetsNoLine`, `inAPunchTakeTheSealsStopForDroppedSilenceAnEndBeforePunchInIsMarkedAtTheRegionsFirstFrame`, `inAPunchTakeTheSealsStopForDroppedSilenceThatEndsBeforeTheRegionGetsNoLine`, `inALoopAndPunchTakeTheStopIsTheRegionsFirstFrameInTheLapTheSilenceReachesItIn` and `withTheLegacyRangeTheStopIsTheDeliveredEndOnlyWhenTheSilenceStartsInsideIt`, in two cases (ruling E282); `aLaterSilenceBlockLeftBehindAfterDroppedSilenceGivesTheDroppedSilencesStop`, `aHeldSilenceBlockAtTheRegionAfterDroppedSilenceBeforeItIsReadByItsHeader` and `aHeldSilenceBlocksLapCountsTheWrapsAmongTheBlocksBeforeItTheTakeDidNotReceive`, which since the fix pass after consult 53 also asserts the warning "delivered no audio; the track holds no audio." (rulings E283 and E293); and, from that fix pass, `inALoopTakeSilenceFromPunchOutBeforeTheLoopEndFlagsTheTrackAtThisLapsRegionStart` (ruling E297), `inALoopTakeSilenceStartingAfterAWrapBeforeTheRegionIsJudgedByTheReentryRule`, `silenceDroppedBeforeTheInputDeliveredAgainIsNotTheSilenceTheSealCounts` (re-expected by the fix round after consult 54 to ruling E302: the stop at the delivered end, 72, with "… stopped delivering audio at 0:00.0 into the take.", and `gap=` lines at 72 for the dropped silence and at 160, four dropouts) and `anInputNoneOfWhoseBlocksReachedTheTakeGetsTheSealsStopAtTheTakesStartFrame` (ruling E298); from the fix round after consult 54, `inALoopTakeSilenceFirstPlayedFromPunchOutInTheNextLapFlagsTheTrackAtThatLapsRegionStart` (no loop recording: the stop at lap 2's region start, no `input-silence`, no starved block counted, no `gap=` line, and "delivered no audio; the track holds no audio.") and `underLoopRecordingSilenceFirstPlayedInALapNoBlockOfWhichReachedTheTakeGivesNoStop` (loop recording: no stop, lap 1's starved run written and counted as `input-silence`, and, by ruling E301, the dropped lap-2 starved block lined at 96 and counted) (ruling E300); `aStarvedBlockAfterDroppedStarvedAndSilenceBlocksLinesTheWholeRunAndMarksTheStop` (a kept starved block after the drops ends the run: one line for the three dropped blocks, the stop, and no `input-silence` for the flagged track) and `theSealPlacesDroppedSilenceBeforeTheDeliveredBlockDroppedAfterIt`, at the Stop and at a device-loss seal (ruling E302); `atTheStopSilenceDroppedInTheNextLapAfterKeptSilenceThatFlaggedNothingGetsTheReentryStop`, `aHeldSilenceBlockAfterKeptSilenceThatFlaggedNothingIsReadByItsHeader` and `theSealMarksDroppedSilenceAfterKeptSilenceThatFlaggedNoTrack`, at the Stop and at a device-loss seal (ruling E303); `aStarvedDropBeforeTheInputsFirstSilenceLeftBehindAtTheStopIsTheStops` (a silence block left behind makes the starved drop before it the stop's, rulings E49 and E281) and `withTheLegacyRangeTheSilencesBlockStartsAreFollowedAcrossTheLoopWrap` (ruling E282); `PunchTakeLossesAgainstTheFileTest.anInputEndedBeforePunchInWhoseEndTheRingDroppedAfterPunchOutIsFlaggedFromPunchInAgainstTheFile` (ruling E296), which asserts that the file holds punch-in to punch-out and, since the fix pass, no starved frames and no overflow blocks; the new `CaptureWarningsAfterTheStopTest` (10: `aWarningAfterTheStopIsShownAfterTheTakesReportAndKeptInTheHistoryOnce`, `afterADeviceLossTheWarningIsKeptInTheHistoryOnlyAndTheLossNoticeStaysOnTheBar`, `aTakeOfAProjectThatWasReplacedShowsNone`, `anErrorReportStaysOnTheBarAndTheWarningIsKeptInTheHistoryOnly`, from the fix pass, `aDeviceLossWhileAStoppedTakeIsWrittenLeavesTheWarningOnTheBarAfterTheReport`, and, from the fix round after consult 54, for guards no test had pinned, `anUnreadableTakesErrorStaysOnTheBarAndTheWarningIsKeptInTheHistoryOnly`, `aFailedCompletionsErrorStaysOnTheBarAndTheWarningIsKeptInTheHistoryOnce`, `aCountInTheDeviceLossCutShortKeepsItsErrorOnTheBarAndTheWarningInTheHistoryOnly` ("Recording aborted — no take was started: … was lost during the count-in" stays on the bar and the seal's warning is kept in the history only, with no action; since the last fix pass the test hands the warning to the take's sink before the loss in the loss's FX turn, so its turn always runs after the loss and before the take's publication, and no longer waits inside an FX turn), `aWarningWhileTheTakeOfAReplacedProjectStillRecordsShowsNothing` and `aWarningOfAFailedStartIsNotGivenAfterTheNextTakesReport`), with a getter of its bar added to `SteppedTakeFixture` and, since the fix round, the controller built before each test and the take recorded by each; and, re-expected because E284 now keeps the seal's warnings in the history after an error report, `DeviceLossRescueStepIndependenceTest.aRescueWhoseManifestCannotBeWrittenIsReportedAsAnErrorAndNeverRegistered` and, from the fix pass, `aRescueWhoseSealFailsIsReportedAsAnErrorAndNeverAsSaved` (the ERROR, then the seal's WARNING "Could not seal a segment of track Guitar; the streaming file is left under" the track's directory, no SUCCESS, the ERROR left on the bar), `TransportControllerTest.aStopWhoseManifestCannotBeWrittenOnDiskIsReportedAsAnErrorThroughTheProductionWiring`, and `TakeFinalizationFailureReportTest.aStopWhoseSealFailsOnDiskIsReportedOnceAsAnErrorAndNeverAsSuccess` and `aFailedSealIsShownAfterTheMidiNotesOfTheSameTake`, each with the seal's WARNING after its entries, the bar's level and message unchanged, and the class Javadoc rewritten.

Known limits of this round, each in the words of the newest consult that gives one:
- On PortAudio, input the host discarded or zero-filled before the callback, input frames cut from an oversized host period, and output gaps the host inserted are in neither the take's figures nor the Xruns number; future story 481. (Ruling E212.)
- On the stream's own device, a failed device callback that lost its period's input during a take raises Take dropouts by one, but its notice speaks only of output played as silence and does not say the take has a gap; the xrun log's Take line and Gap entry show both (rulings E211 (ii) and E239).
- A block that Java Sound's capture thread or the PortAudio adapter's drain cannot build or publish in the instant before a close of its stream during a take counts in the take's dropouts only when that thread's check after the failure still finds its stream live, its count then written before the close's wait for the thread ends; when the close reaches the thread between the failure and that check, or the thread is held up past the close's wait before it counts, the block counts nothing, as a block the close itself makes fail does, so the take's dropouts can be one block short for that input at that close, about 5.3 ms at a 256-frame buffer and 48 kHz; read in the code only (ruling E246). (The engineer's confirmation of the write-up of this round, with "before the close's wait for the thread ends" for its "while the close waits for the thread" as a check against the code corrected it: the check can find the stream live before the close begins that wait, for example while the PortAudio adapter's close is still stopping and closing the wrapped stream, which it does before it stops the drain and waits for it, and the engine folds the backend's counts only once the close has returned.)
- A block an audio callback drops into a full capture ring after the take's seal last read that ring's overflows (a callback stalled across the Stop past the stop's wait) is missing from the end of the take with no gap= line, no count in the manifest's overflow-blocks and none in the take's dropouts; it is at most the one block that callback was writing, about 5.3 ms at a 256-frame buffer and 48 kHz (ruling E213).
- When a full capture ring drops the block with which an input resumed delivering after input-starved blocks, and the input's declared stop follows with no delivered block of that input reaching the take in between, the take's input-stopped line stands at the end of the last delivered block it received, and that starved stretch counts in the take's dropouts only for its blocks the ring dropped, with their gap= line; its blocks the take received are reported under the stop. Every frame of the stretch is reported, by a gap= line or by the stop, and the dropped delivered block counts as any ring overflow (rulings E49, E281 and E294). (Consult 53. Closing it is feature-sized: the producer's stamp cannot tell a dropped delivered block from a dropped input-starved block that carried a recorded instrument's audio on the stream's own ring, both of which count as audio, nor show a delivery after a received starved block when no starved drop lies between them, so it needs a new producer count in the capture ring, now 899 lines, and a stop frame placed by a dropped block's own extent. Ending a received starved stretch at a dropped delivery without moving the stop would report that stretch twice, which ruling E49 forbids, and ending a dropped stretch only at a received delivery would undo ruling E281 and move the figure further from the truth.) The stop's part here is that of a declared stop that flags every physical track of the input: under ruling E301 one that leaves a track unflagged covers none of the starved blocks the ring dropped after the last delivery, which are then lined and counted as a starved run with no declared stop after it, and the received ones get their `input-silence` entries on the tracks it left unflagged, so every frame of the stretch is still reported (the fix round after consult 54).
- At a device-loss seal, input-starved blocks the ring dropped after the input's last delivery are part of the loss and get no gap= line, whether or not the input declared its stop before the loss; the kept blocks of that stretch are written as input-silence and not counted (rulings E281, E286 and E301). (Consult 54, which kept it as a Known limit: it predates this round, E301 does not reopen it, and lining the dropped part at a device-loss seal would count frames the loss reports.)
- When an input delivers again after its declared stop in the same take (a reopened stream), input-starved blocks the ring dropped before that stop which the take had already given to the stop (a kept silence block that flagged every track came first) get no gap= line, though the audio after the stop sits early in the file by them too; the warning of a stop a kept silence block gave still says the track records silence from there; and a track is flagged once per take, so a second declared stop of that input gives no second input-stopped line (rulings E49, E281, E301 and E302). (Consult 54. Not built: as the engineer said, its first part needs retroactive lines for runs already resolved.)
- An out-of-memory error or a stack overflow met while an audio take starts recording, which story 337 lets through (story 337, ruling E95), shows the 'Serious internal error' or 'ran out of memory' notice and leaves the start unfinished: the take records, but the status line never says "Recording —" and no "Recording started" notice is shown; the take gets no dropout figures, so the Xruns cell and the xrun log's Take line go on reporting the take before it as the last take, also after this one stops, until the next take starts or the log is cleared; and its clock marks are written where the transport was when each reached the take, so an unlock can be marked up to about 2 s after the clock was lost and a relock only at the Stop. As the notice says, save a copy and restart, and listen through that take rather than trust its figures and marks. Read in the code only. (Ruling E257.)
- When a backend's dropout report fails at the read made as a take starts recording, the blocks that backend loses before the capture rings from then until its report next answers are not counted in the take's dropouts; when a single read fails, that is about one display frame (ruling E261).
- When a backend's own failure-counter report fails (story 337, ruling E78), the health record keeps that backend's last good totals until a close of its stream or input releases the device, so input blocks lost on that backend's own threads after the failure are not counted in the take's dropouts; a report that throws or answers nothing gets one WARNING in the log naming the backend, and one that fails with an error of the Java runtime itself, such as an out-of-memory error or a stack overflow, writes no WARNING and goes on to the read that met it (ruling E262; story 337, rulings E78 and E95). (The engineer's acceptance read of this round, in place of the two sentences recorded before, with "until a close of its stream or input releases the device" for its "until it is closed", and "an error of the Java runtime itself, such as" before its "an out-of-memory error or a stack overflow", as a check against the code corrected them: `AudioHealthRecord`'s guard keeps a failed source frozen for as long as it stays attached, and a close whose release is deferred leaves it attached; and no `VirtualMachineError` writes a WARNING, an internal error of the Java runtime included.)
- When the read made as a take starts recording reaches a backend while a reopen of the same backend is between reading its count and publishing it, and that backend's report either fails at that read or comes after the reopen has sealed the earlier counts, which happens only for a stream or input opened on a backend whose count is still attached, as after a close that did not release it, the take counts that backend from the reopen's read, so the blocks it lost between that read and the take's start join the take's dropouts. The reopen reads and publishes the count a few in-memory steps apart on its own thread, so this needs the take to start in that instant, and it adds only the blocks lost within it unless that thread is held up there (rulings E214 and E261). (Consult 49, with its first clause and "a few in-memory steps apart" as its fact check corrected them; it replaces the manager's residual recorded before, whose close cannot do this, since a close reads, seals, folds and removes the backend in one synchronized call. The fact check found no path that attaches the stream's own backend again while it is still attached, and could not decide by reading whether an input's backend can be; read in the code only.)
- When an input opened beside the stream is declared stalled while its own thread is hung inside a delivery, the take may get no input-stopped line and no per-track warning for that input, though the input's stop notice is shown, so its tracks can end early with nothing in the take that says why; read in the code only (future story 486). (The engineer's acceptance read of this round.)
