- While the audio driver's own control panel is open, the clock lock is not measured: the clock badge keeps showing what it last measured, and resync requests that arrive from up to about one poll interval before the panel opens until more than 1 s and up to about 2 s after it closes (longer when the monitor's driver calls or its thread are slow, or until the source can be read again) are counted in the Xruns number, logged and, during a take, marked clock=resync, but never declare the clock lost. A loss of the external clock in that time is shown, notified and marked in the take only from the first bad answer to a poll the monitor sends after the panel closes, at the earliest about 1 to 3 s after it closes. The take's clock=unlocked line is dated at the first bad answer of the run that declares it, not where the loss began, or, when no good answer of its measurement came before it, at the measurement's start (rulings E248 to E250, E260 and E269). (Consult 49's words.)
- An unlock that is the first decision of a measurement begun by a newly chosen clock source is dated no later than the first source read the monitor applies that names that source, which can come one poll after the first read that named it: a read is set aside when the person's switch is accepted while the poll that made it runs, and the measurement then starts at the next poll's read, about one poll interval (1 s) later, so the take's clock=unlocked line can lie up to about a second after the switch; a read set aside because a session of the driver's control panel overlapped its poll is made again by the poll that rereads the source after the panel closes, which is where E250 dates a loss in that session anyway (rulings E137, E250 and E269). (Consult 49, "accepted" its fact check's.)
- When a measurement that began during the take, at a newly chosen clock source, a change of whether the source can be measured, or the start of a stream a driver's reset request reopened under the take (future story 464), reaches an unlock as its first decision that is handed to the take more than about 4 s after the measurement began, because something kept the monitor from judging that long (the driver's control panel open, device callbacks that stall, polls the driver leaves unanswered, a source read retried), the take's frame history no longer reaches back to the measurement's start, and the take is marked clock=unlocked from its start-frame: an over-wide region that also covers the audio recorded before that measurement began (rulings E218, E221 and E269). (Consult 49; its first clause as consult 50 extended it.) The closing change sets' frame-history floor (ruling E291) keeps samples only for the resync dates the xrun feed holds and the journal owes, not for the clock-lock monitor's evidence of a decision not yet made, which consult 52 left to the manager: the manager did not widen the frame history for it, since the over-wide region is marking the engineer accepted and that widening is a separate change.
- A relock still held for a frame sample when the capture thread seals a take early is not written, as at a device loss or a retire, and one the badge hands over after that is neither written nor held: the take's last lock line (clock=unlocked or clock=locked) is then clock=unlocked, and its marked region runs to the end of the take, past the relock by at most the time from the relock's third good answer to the badge's next look and frame sample, normally within one display frame (ruling E221). (Consult 49, as consult 51 reworded it; whether that is normally within one display frame needs a run of the pulse, which no agent made.)
- After a clock unlock during a take, a change of clock source keeps the take marked unlocked until the new source's own relock, or to the end of the take when the new source never unlocks, an internal clock included. (Consult 42.)
- A resync that arrives within about the last 0.1 s of a take, or of the audio a take sealed early keeps (longer while E73 carries growth), is logged but not marked in the take. (Ruling E229; future story 482.)
- An unlock whose first bad answer is inside a take but which is declared after the Stop, or after the capture thread sealed the take early, or a clock event the window could not show before that Stop or early seal, is not marked in the take, and its notice comes without the take sentence; after an early seal such an unlock lies at the end of the take, from its date to the early seal, up to about the take's last second, or two when its first bad interval is dated at a resync, and more when it is its measurement's first decision (E269), where the early seal's 'Recording stopped' report already points. (Rulings E229 and E280; future story 482.)
- A resync inside a freeze is early by up to the freeze: a resync whose message arrives while the FX thread is busy is marked at the last frame the take sampled before it (ruling E221; E123 stands).
- Whether JavaFX keeps the display pulse running while the window is minimized is not verified; if it stops, a clock unlock and relock in that stretch are marked as one region spanning the whole stretch, and their notice comes once the window shows again. (Ruling E224.)
- Two inputs halted in one display frame give "2 errors" naming the newest input only. The log names each input, and during a take each track's warning and its input-stopped mark name its own input. (Ruling E230.)
- A Play pressed while a device loss is being declared can end the loss on screen with no stream open, so Reconnect and the device's return do nothing until the next Play or Settings change opens a stream; read in the code only (future story 487). (The engineer's acceptance read of this round.)
- In Atelier the theme's warning amber is about 3.6:1 against the status strip and 3.3:1 to 3.6:1 against the transport bar, under the 4.5:1 that normal-size text needs. It draws the status strip's Engine cell "Device lost: <name>", the Xruns cell's "Xruns N", "Xruns N · Take dropouts K" and "Xruns N · Last take dropouts K" while that cell warns, the transport's "Device lost", and every other text in that amber, the tempo readout and the strip's other warnings among them. The device's name is also in the loss notice and in the Engine cell's tooltip, and the take's dropouts are in the Take line of the Xrun Log, which a click on the Xruns cell opens. All three are above 15:1. In Onyx Refined and Studio Slate the amber texts are above 9:1 (ruling E235; future story 483).

Declined, with the reason:
- A test that pins one block per ring as the production shape at the Stop: the arithmetic tests cover the count, and the shape is the stop gate's, tested where the gate is.
- A test of the `isClosed()` clause of the adapter drain's stop check: wherever that clause stops a drain, the interrupt `stopDrainThread` sent the drain on the same path, read after it, would stop it too, so no test can observe the clause. `close()`, a failed open and the output-only fallback (`disableInputSide`) each stop the drain in `stopDrainThread`, which clears the shared flag before it interrupts the drain, and `drains` reads the flag first, so the cleared flag stops the drain until an open sets the flag again. Before any later open, `close()` and a failed open close the drain's pinned publisher, and the fallback is followed by one of the two, so from then on the closed publisher stops it. The interrupt is a second line of defence that none of those paths reaches alone. It is the one clause left on one path: when completing the publisher fails before that publisher is closed, which for the adapter's own `SubmissionPublisher` only an error of the JVM itself while building its replacement can cause, the drain's publisher is still open at the next open, which pins it for the next stream too. That path is pinned by `CallbackBackendAdapterTest.aDrainLeftBehindWhosePublisherStayedOpenStopsOnItsInterruptWhenTheAdapterReopens`, in which a publisher that refuses its close stands in for that error (the drain's interrupt clause, above).
- A test that the watchdog's allowance request and the clear of the open panel are one critical section: both methods are synchronized on the controller, the two `EngineHealthMonitorTest` cases pin their order, and observing a thread blocked on the monitor would make the test depend on scheduling.
- Replacing `ClockStatusIndicatorTest.aCloseConsumesItsSerialsTransitions`, which reopens under the same serial: it pins the consumed-serial check, and the reachable path has its own test.
- A behavioural test in place of the source-text pin of the two stores in `AsioControlThread` (`AsioBackendClockLockTest.aPanelSessionIsCountedBeforeThePanelReadsClosed`): the monitor's read order is pinned in the monitor's own tests. The confirming review then found that, once the fix round added the second reads, `AsioClockLockMonitorTest.aPollThatReadsThePanelClosedAlsoSeesItsSession` no longer pinned the order of a tick's first reads and no test pinned the order of its second reads; both breaks survived the probe run, and both should-fix findings went to the last touch round, whose tests kill both breaks (Verification).
- A test that makes either bridge's wake of its drain throw (ruling E272): on a platform thread `LockSupport.unpark` fails only on an error of the JVM itself; a seam that let a test make the PortAudio adapter's wake throw would take the wake out of `RealTimeSafeContractTest.everyRtCallbackBridgeIsGuardClean`'s walk, and the ASIO bridge's drain thread is a final field, which only a new seam or a reflective write to a final field could change. The containment's extent is read from each bridge's bytecode, and the PortAudio drain's backstop is tested with no wake reaching it, and, since after the confirming review of the round's last change set, the ASIO drain's too.
- The same nesting for the take's start: a `VirtualMachineError` from the take-start read still skips the later steps of `takeStartedRecording`, which ruling E257's Known limit describes.
- A test that the measurement's start is read before the source read (ruling E269): the fake clock does not move during the read.
- The held-thread waits of older tests in other classes that release themselves after a bound: only `CaptureFlushServiceTest`'s holds were changed.
- A test that tells the early seal's read after an unlock or a start mark is handed over from the guard's read before it (ruling E280): with the answer given without the read after the hand-over (`yield true` and `return true` in `RecordCoordinator`), `TakeDropoutsAndClockMarksTest`, `ClockBadgeDuringATakeTest` and `TakeClockMarksOfTheTransportTest` stay green, because no test can put a seal between the guard's read and the read after the hand-over; that a notice never claims a line take.manifest lacks rests on the ordering of the hand-over, the flush service's `sealed` store and the seal's drain of the marks handed over, read in the code only.
- A test of the closed-controller check alone in `DefaultAudioEngineController.performFormatChangeReopen` (ruling E278): `shutdown()` also clears the watched device under the same monitor, so a request taken up after the close already returns at the watched-device check, and the break that removes only the closed check stays green; the check is kept as a second guard. The window-reset review's fourth note found it unpinned, and the fix round after consult 54 argued the break equivalent from the lines: `shutdown()` and `performFormatChangeReopen` are both synchronized, `shutdown()` sets the closed flag, clears the watched device and clears the waiting reopen, so without the check a request taken up after it returns at the watched-device check and resumes nothing. Nor a break that makes a Stop end the wait (ruling E288): nothing in production carries a Stop to the controller, so no break of the controller can express it, and `ResetRequestWhileTheDriverControlPanelIsOpenTest.aStopDuringTheWaitIsAnOrdinaryStopAndTheReopenStillFollows` pins the outcome.
- A test that tells `AudioEngine.takeDriverResyncArrivals()`'s check that a stream is open from its check that a backend is named (ruling E279): a stream's close clears the engine's open backend, so the break that drops only `!isStreamOpen()` answers the same and stays green.
- A test of the branch of `CaptureFlushService.lineTheDropsBeforeTheDeviceLoss` for a ring whose drop counts the final sweep's look did not keep (`found == null`), which the confirming review of the change set's application half listed among guards no break covered: the fix round after consult 54 found it unreachable, so any break of it is equivalent. The look that ends each pass keeps every ring's counts in a device-loss seal's final sweep, the final sweep drains every ring and ends each with that look, the seal's look at the rings returns unless the final sweep is done, so a throw that skips a look skips it too, and a stop request keeps its first reason; only two threads racing their stop requests could reach it, and the recording pipeline requests the stop from the FX thread alone.
- A test of ruling E292's one request whose message call is under way as the xrun feed reads after a stretch of more than 256: no test can hold the driver's thread between its claim and its read of the bound without a seam on the path the real-time walk follows, as consult 52 said.

**Goals and Goals — Tests, one by one.**
- Goal, the callback-heartbeat watchdog: built as `EngineHeartbeatWatchdog` on the engine health monitor, with E1's window and E2's allowance; the "restart offer" is Reconnect (ruling E6), and the monitor is not rebuilt on reconfigure. Answered by `EngineHeartbeatWatchdogTest` and `EngineHealthMonitorTest`.
- Goal, the detector's publish side made real-time pure first: `XrunDetectorTest.ticksAreClassifiedAndCountedWithoutTouchingThePublisher` and `RealTimeSafeContractTest.theRenderDriveLoopIsGuardClean`.
- Goal, feed it: ticks around the render body (`EngineStreamPumpTest.aRenderedBlockSlowerThanTwoPeriodsTicksTheEnginesDetectorAsDropped`); the failures story 337's guards count, as gaps (`XrunFeedTest.eachObservedGapRaisesTheCountByExactlyItsOwnCountAndReachesTheSdkStream`); graph overloads come from the CPU budget enforcer's degradations, not from the worker failure counters, and never raise the number (rulings E21, E42 and E181; `ShedLogSubscriberTest.eachDegradationThatNamesAPolicyIsOneEntrySayingOnlyThatTheTrackWentOverItsBudget`).
- Goal, the xrun counter as a status-strip cell with click-through to the log: `EngineHealthCellsTest.theXrunsCellShowsTheCountQuietlyAtZeroAndOpensTheLog` and `XrunCountAndLogSurfacesTest.aLateBlockIsLoggedButNotCountedAGapIsBothAndClearEmptiesBoth`.
- Goal, the engine-state cell: `EngineStateCellTest` and `EngineHealthCellsTest`; RUNNING shows nothing (ruling E26).
- Goal, the clock-lock surfaces: the badge is mounted beside the transport status (`FailureSpineWiringSourceTest.theWindowBuildsWiresAndMountsItsClockBadgeOnceAndUndoesItInItsDisposal`) and reads the open stream's lock level rather than `clockLockEvents()`; the ASIO side is `AsioClockLockMonitor` in daw-sdk, not the shim (`AsioClockLockMonitorTest`, `AsioBackendClockLockTest`); recording is never paused (ruling E30).
- Goals — Tests, the watchdog stall test: `EngineHealthMonitorTest.aRenderDriveWhoseBeatsStopIsDeclaredLostThroughTheRemovalsStepsWithTheStallsNotice` and `frozenDevicePeriodsAreAStallWhileTheRenderDriveKeepsCycling`, and through the cell `EngineStateCellTest.aStalledDeviceShowsTheWarningTreatmentItsNoticeOffersReconnectAndTheCellReconnects`; the offer is Reconnect (ruling E6).
- Goals — Tests, the story 327 hardware proof: the owner's, written as steps with their expected results under Verification and run by no agent; its software half is `HeartbeatStallDuringATakeTest.aRecordingDeviceThatStopsCallingBackSealsRegistersAndReportsTheTake`.
- Goals — Tests, no false positive: `EngineHeartbeatWatchdogTest.healthyStreamingAt2048And4096FramesAt44kNeverTripsOverTenSimulatedSeconds`, `aStillnessShorterThanEightPeriodsOfALargeBufferIsNoVerdict`, `aTwentyMillisecondSchedulingGapAt32FramesAt192kIsNoVerdict`; `EngineHealthMonitorTest.healthyStreamingAt2048And4096FramesOnRealThreadsNeverTripsAcrossStopPauseResumeAndApply` and `aStreamClosedMoreSlowlyThanTheWindowIsNeverDeclaredLost`.
- Goals — Tests, the RT-purity sentinel: `RealTimeSafeContractTest.theRenderDriveLoopIsGuardClean`, which must reach `XrunDetector#recordTick`.
- Goals — Tests, the xrun feed: a late block is logged and not counted (ruling E17: `XrunFeedTest.aLateBlockIsALateEntryAndNeverRaisesTheCount`, `XrunCountAndLogSurfacesTest.aLateBlockIsLoggedButNotCountedAGapIsBothAndClearEmptiesBoth`); a guard's failure counts (`XrunFeedTest.eachObservedGapRaisesTheCountByExactlyItsOwnCountAndReachesTheSdkStream`); an overload is a Budget entry (`ShedLogSubscriberTest.eachDegradationThatNamesAPolicyIsOneEntrySayingOnlyThatTheTrackWentOverItsBudget`).
- Goals — Tests, the engine-state cell: `EngineStateCellTest.theCellTracksRunningReconfiguringRunningThroughASettingsApply`, `aRemovedDeviceShowsTheWarningTreatment` and `aStalledDeviceShowsTheWarningTreatmentItsNoticeOffersReconnectAndTheCellReconnects`.
- Goals — Tests, the clock-lock test: an unlock during a take is shown, notified and marked, the take keeps recording, and a relock clears it (ruling E30: `ClockBadgeDuringATakeTest.anUnlockDuringATakeIsShownNotifiedAndMarkedTheTakeKeepsRecordingAndARelockClearsIt`); a `ClockLockEvent` alone changes nothing on the badge (`aClockLockEventAloneChangesNothingOnTheBadge`).
- Goals — Tests, the injected sink: `EngineHealthMonitorTest` and `EngineStateCellTest` assert the stall notice and its Reconnect action through `RecordingNotifications`, a real `NotificationManager` and never `noop()`; MainController's own sink shows a notice with its action on the notification bar at the warning level, pinned by `FailureSpineWiringSourceTest.theWindowStartsTheEngineHealthMonitorAfterTheBridgeAndStopsItBeforeTheBridgeIsCancelled`. Story 339 had not landed (the stage order changed).

**Corrections to the story text.** The Motivation records what the code did when the story was written, and the Goals, Goals — Tests, Non-Goals, Technical Notes and the two Notes record what it set out to do; they are kept as written. Where their wording is now false against the code, each line below gives what is true, by symbol rather than by line number:

1. Motivation, "No watchdog exists anywhere": `EngineHeartbeatWatchdog` and the engine health monitor watch every open stream.
2. Motivation, the detector "constructed and rebuilt on every reconfigure" with no production caller of `beginTick`/`recordTick`/`reportDropped`/`reportGraphOverload`: `beginTick` is gone; the pump calls `recordTick`, the xrun feed calls `reportDropped`; `reportGraphOverload` still has no production caller, because graph overloads are Budget entries (rulings E21, E42 and E181).
3. Motivation, "the story-123 `XrunCounterLabel`/`XrunLogDialog` exist only as a javadoc mention": there is no `XrunCounterLabel`; the count is the strip's Xruns cell, and `XrunLogDialog` exists.
4. Motivation, `recordTick` calling `SubmissionPublisher.offer` inline and the false "never blocks": `recordTick` stores plain fields only, `publishPending()` offers off the real-time thread, and the Javadoc's claim is now true.
5. Motivation, "Engine state has no UI" and no subscriber of `engineStateEvents()`: `EngineStateBridge` is the one subscriber, and its first listener feeds the Engine cell's model.
6. Motivation, `ClockStatusIndicator` "with a notification hook and a recording-pause callback", never instantiated: the class is redone, has no pause callback, and the window mounts one; no real backend overrode `clockLockEvents()` — `AsioBackend` now does, and the badge reads `clockLockStatus()` instead.
7. Goals, "the story-337 render guards already bump a heartbeat timestamp": the heartbeat is two monotonic counters (the Note from Story 337; story 337, ruling E5).
8. Goals, the monitor "rebuilt on reconfigure", "a few buffer periods … with a floor for scheduling jitter": one monitor for the controller's life; the window is max(2 s, 8 buffer periods), and max(5 s, the window) until a new drive's first advance (rulings E1 and E2).
9. Goals and Goals — Tests, "a restart offer": the offer is Reconnect, which reopens the lost device; Restart Audio Engine is never offered for a loss (ruling E6).
10. Goals, "visible via story 339's injected sink": story 338 landed before 339; the window's own notification sink shows the controller's notices with their action.
11. Goals, "graph overloads from the worker pool's new failure counters": worker tasks that threw are not xruns; overloads are the CPU budget enforcer's degradations, logged as Budget entries that never raise the number (rulings E21, E42 and E181).
12. Goals, the engine-state cell "subscribes to `engineStateEvents()` via the dispatcher" with "RUNNING quiet": it renders `EngineHealthStatus`, fed by the bridge's one subscription, and is hidden while the engine runs or is stopped (ruling E26).
13. Goals, the indicator "bound to the active backend's `clockLockEvents()`", "the ASIO-side `ClockLockEvent` publisher in the shim" and "recording auto-pause": the badge reads the open stream's `ClockLockStatus` on every pulse; the ASIO measurement is `AsioClockLockMonitor` in daw-sdk, the shim only counts and answers messages; an unlock marks the take and never pauses or stops it (rulings E29 and E30).
14. Goals — Tests, "forced-late blocks increment the status-strip counter": late blocks are listed in the log and the tooltip and never raise the number (ruling E17).
15. Goals — Tests, "a mock clock-unlock event pauses an active recording": an unlock during a take keeps it recording and writes `clock=unlocked` and `clock=locked` marks (ruling E30); a clock event alone changes nothing on the badge.
16. Goals — Tests, the 327 proof's "replugging offers resume": the return is the person's Reconnect (rulings E6 and E7); no shipped backend publishes `DeviceArrived` (Known limits).
17. Goals — Tests and Non-Goals, the story-339 dependency direction and "Book 4's stage order runs 339 *before* this story": the order became 313, 336, 337, 338, 339, 340 by the manager's decision; the injected-sink test uses a real `NotificationManager` in the test and MainController's sink in production.
18. Non-Goals, OS/backend device-change detection as story 327's: no story built it; it is future story 461.
19. Technical Notes, "Stage order deviation (deliberate): … this story lands *after* 339": it landed before 339, as item 17 says.
20. Technical Notes, `AsioBufferSwitchShim.java` as the "ASIO `ClockLockEvent` publisher, off the RT drain thread": the publisher is `AsioClockLockMonitor`'s, on its own platform daemon thread.
21. Technical Notes, `FxDispatcher` "drains; counters are continuous channels": the xrun figures, the log and the take's dropouts reach the model through one pulse participant (`EngineHealthFeed`), and engine-state changes through the bridge's FX turn.
22. Technical Notes, "Completes existing story 123": the detector is fed and surfaced; story 123's overload notice with its opt-in bypass and its 60-second test are future story 462, and its rolling 30-second count and its reset at transport start and stop are not built (the count resets only on Clear, a settings change, Restart Audio Engine or a driver's reset request, rulings E22 and E148).
23. Technical Notes, the line references (`DefaultAudioEngineController.java:466-467`, `:481-483`, `:661-672`, `XrunDetector.java:203-208`, `:33-36`, `ClockStatusIndicator.java:105`, `AudioBackend.java:568`): they no longer point at those symbols; the symbols are named above.
24. Note from Story 336, the two options for surfacing the pump's death: the handler records the death as the drive's halt (ruling E33), so story 337's drain shows it; it does not delegate to the default handler.
25. Note from Story 337, "The watchdog's restart offer can reuse it": a loss offers Reconnect (ruling E6); Restart Audio Engine stays the fault's action.
26. Note from Story 337, a stalled Java Sound line "flagged … from the stall on": each armed track of that input gets an `input-stopped` line at the frame after the last block it delivered (ruling E13), and the input's window is judged over the engine's period (Known limits).

**Rulings.** The studio engineer's rulings in fifty-four consults: 1 (E1–E36), 2 (E37–E71), 3 (E72–E80), 4 (E81–E85), 5 (E86–E91), 6 (E92–E99), 7 (E100–E118), 8 (E119–E134), 9 (E135–E142), 11 (E143–E145), 12 (E146), 13, the acceptance read (E147–E150), 15 (E151–E154), 16 (E155–E157), 18 (E158), 19 (E159–E161), 20 (E162), 21 (E163–E165), 22 (E166–E169), 23 (E170–E171), and, in PR #994's review, 31 (E172–E176), 32 (E177–E181), 33 (E182–E187), 34 (E188–E192), 36 (E193–E200), 38 (E201–E204) and 39 (E205), and, in PR #994's second review, 41 (E206–E237), 42 (E238–E247), 43 (E248–E257), 44 (E258–E260), 45 (E261–E266), 47 (E268–E270), 50 (E271–E272), 51 (E273–E280), 52 (E281–E292), 53 (E293–E299) and 54 (E300–E304); consults 10, 14, 17, 24, 25, 26, 27, 28, 29, 30, 35, 37, 40, 46, 48 and 49 made no new ruling (consult 10 accepted R3's seven readings, below; consult 14, the second acceptance read, answered ACCEPTED; consult 17 confirmed that E157 covers the device-side categories only and gave words for E157 and E68; consult 24, the third acceptance read, answered NOT ACCEPTED with one change to the words of clock check 10; consult 25 gave the final words of consult 24's change to clock check 10, answering its verifier's one finding (a go-on test that read only the interface), with no verdict and no ruling; consult 26, the fourth acceptance read, read the change to clock check 10, its fact-check and the hashes and answered ACCEPTED, with no new ruling; consult 27, a skill audit of the last stretch's rulings, Known limits, owner's checks and future stories, not an acceptance read, required two changes to words, in three replacement blocks, and said that consult 26's ACCEPTED did not stand as given, with no new ruling; consult 28 gave the final words of those three blocks, taking word for word the one change of words that their verifier's fourth read gave for R1a, with no verdict and no ruling; consult 29, the acceptance read after the skill audits, answered NOT ACCEPTED and required two additions to the owner's checks (the status strip check sets UI Scale back; the interface's rate is confirmed before the clock checks), with no new ruling; consult 30, the acceptance read after consult 29's two additions went in, answered ACCEPTED, with no condition, no required block and no new ruling; consult 35, the acceptance read of PR #994's review round as it stood before consult 36, answered ACCEPTED, with no condition and no new ruling; consult 37, the acceptance read of the passes made after consult 35, answered ACCEPTED WITH CONDITIONS, with no new ruling and one condition, on the wording of E198's accepted limit, which was applied; consult 40, the acceptance read of E201 to E205 as built, answered ACCEPTED WITH CONDITIONS, with no new ruling and one condition, on the wording of E203's accepted limit, which was applied; consult 46 corrected E263, E265 and E266 in place after consult 45's fact check; consult 48 gave E263's final words and the requirement and Known limit of E265 and E266, which consult 51 then replaced (rulings E274, E276 and E277) and the round built; consult 49, with its fact check, corrected E214, E249, E250, E252, E260, E268 and E269 in place and gave the Known limits of the early seal's window, of a gap= line that falls back in a punch take, and of E214's race with a reopen). A later ruling wins where it amends an earlier one; the amendments are named on each bullet, and each bullet of PR #994's second review names the consult that gave it and any that corrected it. E13, E15, E30, E37 to E266 and E268 to E304 deviate from the story text and the book, and are recorded here as rulings; E267 was never used. After consult 49, the engineer's acceptance read of PR #994's second review round made no new ruling and answered "NOT ACCEPTED, but only for wording. No code change is owed.", and asked for sentences in the round's documents; they were landed, with clauses that a check against the code corrected, as each place says, and one Javadoc sentence of `ClockLockAnnouncer.consumeAtALoss` changed with the corrected sentence on the stopped stream (E268). The engineer's confirmation of the write-up then made no new ruling: "Every sentence from my acceptance read stands where it was landed" and "The three corrections the landing agent made against the code all stand"; E246 stands as built and no fix is owed, its race at a close does not over-count, and what it can do is under-count, so it gets a Known limit; and its verdict, in the engineer's words: "NOT ACCEPTED, but only for wording. No code change is owed." What it said is still owed: "E246 needs a Known limit and two qualifying clauses in story 338", with "Once those land as given below, the round is accepted from the desk, and they need no further acceptance read." The Known limit and the two clauses were landed afterwards (Known limits; the Resolution's bullets on the blocks an input thread could not build or publish and on a closed stream's block), together with the confirmation's should-fix sentences in story 337 and the Recording Reliability book, but not exactly as given: one clause of the Known limit was corrected as a check against the code required (its note there says how). The engineer then read the landed E246 sentences, with no new ruling and no condition: the corrected clause stands, "It is true of the code and says what I meant", the engineer's own "while the close waits for the thread" having been "too narrow", since "Inside the race itself the count can land before that wait begins"; the note after the Known limit "is also true of the code"; the two Resolution clauses and the sentences in story 337 and the Recording Reliability book stand as the engineer gave them; and its verdict, in the engineer's words: "ACCEPTED" and "The round stands as the documents now state it. No code change is owed and nothing more has to be written." Consult 50 came after that acceptance: it answered two questions the manager then put to it, on two Known limits the engineer's acceptance read of the round had recorded, and made two rulings that owe code, E271 (the stream that answers a driver's reset request) and E272 (a failure of the PortAudio adapter's wake of its drain thread), which close those two Known limits and change the words of E254 and of the Known limit on a first decision handed to the take late. The round's last change set built both (Resolution; Verification). The engineer's acceptance read of E271 and E272 as built then read, with no new ruling, the clock monitor's and the ASIO backend's code for E271 in every order consult 50 named and in three it did not (a reset request sent from inside the reopen's own creation of the driver's buffers, a reopen that fails, and a request that lands after the open has checked for one and before the monitor's start reads it), the two bridges' contained wakes of their drain threads and their tests for E272, the facts future story 489, since built in this round (ruling E278), rested on, and the round's sentences on both rulings; it confirmed that "E272's outcome stands on ASIO as built", and its verdict, in the engineer's words: "NOT ACCEPTED. The code does what E271 and E272 rule and owes no change in behaviour. Six pieces of text still have to change". Five of the six were landed: the E271 item of the Resolution, the parenthesis of the E271 entry, the confirming review's clause on which streams get the window, E272's reference to E254, and a Known limit on a driver that applies a change while its control panel stays open (future story 489, since built in this round); in the first two a further check against the code corrected one clause, as each place says. The sixth, new words for the Javadoc of `AudioBackend.clockLockEvents()` that name every interval the person's own change accounts for, was then made in the engineer's words, and the confirming review's clause that reports it took the engineer's words. The engineer then ruled on the further correction of the first two and accepted it, and found the Javadoc as it had given it and true of the code. Its verdict, in the engineer's words: "NOT ACCEPTED: the code owes no change in behaviour, and the corrected clause stands as landed." What it asked for was that the sentences of this record that said its ruling or the Javadoc was pending be replaced by its own words, and they were, word for word (in the E271 item of the Resolution, in the parenthesis of the E271 entry, in the confirming review's clause on the Javadoc, and in the account of the sixth piece of text above); it added: "Once these four land word for word, I owe no further read of this round." The engineer then read the four sentences where they stand, found each word for word as given, and gave its verdict on the round as it then stood: "ACCEPTED". After that verdict, the owner required the round's remaining Known limits closed by mechanism wherever a bounded change can close them, and three consults ruled for that. Consult 51 ruled E273 to E280 on five questions the manager put to it: exact ring-loss accounting (E273 to E275), the silence after an input's declared stop (E276 and E277, which replace both exceptions of E265 and E266), a reset request while the driver's control panel stays open (E278), resync dating (E279, which builds E270) and the early seal's window (E280), each with its Resolution sentence, its ruling entry and the Known limits it closes or rewords. Consult 52, after the four specifications and their critics, ruled E281 to E292 on what they had left open or stated other than as ruled: a dropped input-starved block (E281), the stop line the seal writes in a punch, loop or legacy-range take and a later silence block left behind (E282 and E283), the capture thread's warnings after the Stop and their words (E284), a run whose next block starts at or before its line (E285), an early seal in a device-loss seal's final sweep (E286), an instrument insert removed and added while a ring was full (E287), an Apply, a Restart, a Play and a later request during or after the panel wait (E288 to E290), a resync carried through a standstill of any length (E291) and the true words of resync dating (E292); it replaced consult 51's Resolution sentences on the reset wait and on resync dating, the first sentence of its sentence on ring-loss accounting and its sentence on the order of the early seal's reads. Consult 53, after the ring change set's build and that change set's second critic, ruled E293 to E299: E293 withdraws consult 52's "the warning names lap 2", E294 amends E281 for one case and gives its Known limit, E295, E298 and E299 confirm what was built, E296 and E297 add to E282 and E283 the silence that starts at or after punch-out, and E297 owed the one change of code, which the fix pass after it made; it also changed the words of E284's Resolution item (E299). Consults 51 to 53 were not acceptance reads. Consult 54 came after the four confirming reviews of the closing change sets, and was not an acceptance read either. It was first asked about the case the fix pass after consult 53 had left open: as built to E297's "before the loop end", silence first played at or after punch-out in a later lap of a loop take got no stop, where an applied block of it would flag the track in the take's one file. It ruled E300 (the file that block's lap writes decides: the take's one file with no loop recording, flagged; a lap with no file under loop recording, no stop), amended E297's words to match, and checked consult 53's premises against the code: E295 confirmed, with sharper words; E298 confirmed in substance, its second sentence corrected; E299 confirmed, its ending corrected, since a removal's notice carries no Reconnect. Its three follow-ups ruled on what the independent reviewers had found: E301 on the second must-fix finding of the review of the ring change set's capture-thread half, a regression of this round against the commit under review (starved drops after the input's last delivery before a declared stop that flags no track of the input were reported nowhere), with a Known limit at a device-loss seal; E302 on that review's first must-fix finding (silence a full ring dropped before the input delivered again in the same take, which a reopened stream gives, left the audio after it early in the file with no line), with a Known limit, E303 on its second note (silence the take kept that flagged no track switched off the seal's mark for the input's later dropped silence) and, on its sixth note, a sentence added to E281 with no change of code; and E304 on the second note of the review of the reset wait (an Apply or a Restart inside a driver request's 250 ms window, before any reopen waited, left the request to reopen at its own format over the person's settings), which extends E288. The fix round after consult 54 built E300 to E303 and the last fix pass built E304 (Resolution; Verification). Each was reviewed, and the final gate, the round's build of record, ran after them (Verification). The engineer's acceptance read of the round as the closing change sets left it then made no new ruling, found the code as ruled, and asked for sentences of this record to be changed, which were changed word for word; in the engineer's words: "Rulings E273 to E304 are built as recorded: the take's gap= and input-stopped lines, its dropout figures and the warnings the person reads say what the take's files hold in each case the round ruled, the reset wait and an Apply in a driver request's window leave the person's settings standing with one stream at a time, and what the code does not yet do is stated as a Known limit, a Not-verified item or an owner's check."; last verdict on the round: "ACCEPTED".

- **E1** — The verdict comes when the watched heartbeat has not advanced for max(2 seconds, 8 buffer periods of the active format); the watched heartbeat is the device-period count where the backend reports one, and the render drive's beats otherwise. A tick later than twice its own interval re-baselines and decides nothing. Never a verdict from a window shorter than 2 seconds, or from a sample taken across a process pause.
- **E2** — Until the first advance on a newly started render drive, the allowance is max(5 seconds, the E1 window). Never DEVICE_LOST on a stream that is still starting. Amended by E107 (a clock-source switch gets the same allowance).
- **E3** — The rescued take keeps the zero-filled silence of the window, marked in `take.manifest`; never silently rewrite sealed audio, never unmarked silence at the end of a rescued take. Amended by E46 (a new key, not `gap=`) and E48 (only the stream's own input's tracks, from the first block of the trailing starved run).
- **E4** — A stall verdict gets its own wording: "<name> stopped responding — audio stopped. Check that it is connected and powered, then choose Reconnect." (unnamed: "The audio device stopped responding — audio stopped. …"), action Reconnect. Never "disconnected" for a watchdog verdict. Amended by E67 (the removal notice's wording).
- **E5** — During a take the coordinator's lead is "<name> stopped responding during recording" with every existing tail unchanged, action Review; both notices are shown. Never the "disconnected during recording" lead for a stall. Amended by E111 (the unnamed form).
- **E6** — Reconnect (mechanism B) only: the action of the E4 notice and the Engine cell's click while it shows the loss. Never offer Restart Audio Engine as the answer to a device loss.
- **E7** — A Reconnect during a take keeps "<name> reconnected. Tracks are still armed; press Record to continue." (record state IDLE, armed set kept, transport at the take anchor); with no take "<name> is responding again. Press Play to continue."; a failure "<name> could not be reopened: <reason>. Check that it is connected and powered, then choose Reconnect again, or choose another device in Audio Settings.", the state staying "Device lost". Never "reconnected" for a reopen the person asked for that failed. Amended by E111 (unnamed forms) and E129 (the arrival-path sentence).
- **E8** — Two notices for a sink that throws are acceptable, the warning usually first; device-side counts from the stream the verdict closed that the drain reads on the lost drive or an older one after the verdict reached it never escalate or latch, and what it reads before that, or on a newer drive, it judges as any count of the drive it reads them on, a Known limit (E159). Amended by E68 (the mechanism), E159 and E162 (these words; it said "Two notices for a sink that throws are acceptable; counts from the stream the verdict closed never escalate or latch afterwards.").
- **E9** — A paused stream is not watched: an unplug while the stream is paused, or parked by a Reconnect, is noticed about 5 s after a later resume of that stream, the start-up allowance of a new render drive. A Play can resume it, and so can a Record that records no audio input (a MIDI-only take, for instance), and until the notice the transport then rolls over silence; a Record with audio inputs does not resume it: past its route checks it closes the paused stream and then tries to open a new one, as any open does (rulings E2, E9 and E150). A Pause can pause the stream, but not while an instrument insert in the mixer keeps it running, and a stream whose render drive runs is watched whatever the transport shows (ruling E163). Amended by E150 (its 5 s; it was "one window after the next Play or Record") and E163 (these words; they said "an unplug during a pause, or while a Reconnect has the stream parked, is noticed about 5 s after the next Play or Record resumes it" and "until then the transport reads Playing over silence").
- **E10** — The stream closes at the verdict and recovery comes only from Reconnect; a stall shorter than the window raises no verdict. Never a DEVICE_LOST that keeps the stream open.
- **E11** — A stalled input gets story 337's input-halt treatment: the ERROR `failure.audio.fault.device` with the reason `input from “<name>” stopped delivering audio`, Restart Audio Engine and the fact latched until a stream opened after it (E87). Never a dead input shown more mildly than a halted one. Amended by E52 (only an input that delivered and then stopped), E127 (the reason with no name) and E128 (the count and the name in story 337's key).
- **E12** — The input window is the E1 formula over that input's own block period, with the same late-tick re-baseline. Never a shorter window for inputs than for the output. Built over the engine's period (Known limits).
- **E13** — The stall's start frame must be on disk: the take frame after the last block the input delivered, per affected track, in a key older readers ignore; for every input that ends mid-take. Never a mid-take input end recorded without its frame. Amended by E77 and E88 (punch and loop takes).
- **E14** — The per-track warning: "Track 'T': input from 'D' stopped delivering audio at m:ss.s into the take; the track records silence from there.", or for an input that never delivered "Track 'T': input from 'D' delivered no audio; the track records silence." The routing-mismatch sentence stays only for a real routing mismatch. Amended by E77, E127 and E172 (a loop take). A stop the seal writes for the input's silence that did not reach the take after the last block kept from its ring has ruling E284's words (E303), and a stop marked by the block with which the input delivers again after its dropped silence has ruling E302's words.
- **E15** — During a take an input that delivered nothing within the E2 allowance is declared like a stall, flagged from the take's first frame and warned once; outside a take it is not declared. Never unflagged silence in a take. Amended by E52.
- **E16** — Detection runs whenever the input is open and the render drive beats, take or not; outside a take it raises only the E11 notice and writes nothing to disk.
- **E17** — The one number counts observed gaps only: silenced callback periods and render blocks and sink refusals, an output ring full with a rendered block discarded, an output ring empty in the device callback, and the ASIO driver's overload message. Late renders appear in the log and the tooltip only. Never a count that includes something that was not a gap. Amended by E38 (a resync is counted).
- **E18** — Device starvation is counted on PortAudio and ASIO; on a backend with no starvation signal the tooltip adds "This audio driver does not report underruns; late blocks are listed in the log." Never a zero the driver could not have raised. See E116 for when the sentence shows.
- **E19** — Discards and empty periods that accrue while the device period count is not advancing belong to the stall; a verdict adds one entry, "Device stopped responding: <name>". Never a stall reported as hundreds of xruns. Amended by E73 (the mechanism).
- **E20** — Each log entry reads "<hh:mm:ss.mmm>  <kind>  <detail>[ ×n]  [near m:ss.s]", the position only while the transport rolls, with the kinds Gap, Discarded, Overload, Late, Shed and Stall and their details; counts of one frame are one entry with ×n. Never an exception class name in an entry. Amended by E38 (Resync), E42 (the Shed details), E72 (the Discarded detail) and E181 (the Shed kind word is now Budget).
- **E21** — Graph overload is fed only from the budget enforcer's sheds, read off the audio thread, as a Shed entry that does not raise the number; worker tasks that threw are not xruns. Amended by E42.
- **E22** — The cell always shows the count since the last reset: "Xruns 0" quiet at zero, the warning accent while the count grew in the last 30 seconds; it resets at the engine-health monitor's next tick after the engine controller rebuilds its xrun detector, as it does for a settings change, Restart Audio Engine or a driver's reset request, whether or not a stream reopens, and on Clear, never on transport start or stop; the dialog keeps the last 100 entries. Never a reset caused by neither the person (Clear, a settings change, Restart) nor the driver's own reset request. Amended by E148 (a driver's reset request restarts the count, also when its reopen fails; this Never in its words). Amended in consult 21 (its reset clause's words; it said "it resets when a render drive starts after a reconfigure or restart").
- **E23** — The tooltip "N dropouts since hh:mm:ss (M in the last 30 seconds); L late blocks. Click for the xrun log." and the spoken "Xruns: N since hh:mm, M in the last 30 seconds. Press to open the xrun log."; the cell opens on click, Enter or Space. Never a mouse-only cell. Amended by E102 (the spoken take figure) and E116.
- **E24** — While a take with dropouts records, the cell reads "Xruns N · Take dropouts K" in the warning accent and the dialog "This take: K dropouts (B blocks lost, F frames truncated)"; after the take both stay as "Last take: …" until the next take or Clear. Settled by E92 and E94.
- **E25** — Input blocks lost before the capture ring count into the take's dropouts and are marked in `take.manifest` at their take frame. Amended by E44 (the marks are deferred) and E46 (their vocabulary).
- **E26** — One Engine cell, fed from the engine state and the latched fault, hidden while there is nothing to say; RECONFIGURING for a Settings apply, a restart and a driver reset; the record state's "Device lost" stays on the transport label. Never RUNNING or STOPPED printed as health.
- **E27** — The Engine cell's texts: "Engine: reconfiguring…" (informational, "Applying audio settings; audio resumes when done.", "Audio engine reconfiguring."); "Device lost: <name>" (warning accent, "<name> stopped responding or was disconnected. Audio is stopped. Click to reconnect, or choose another device in Audio Settings.", "Audio device <name> lost. Press to reconnect."); "Engine fault: <words>" (critical, the words by category, "<Words> failed N times between hh:mm:ss and hh:mm:ss. Click to <action in lower case>.", "Audio engine fault: <words>. Press to <action>."). The loss wins over a fault; a click, Enter or Space reconnects while lost, otherwise runs the fault's action. Never a raw colour value. Amended by E93, E95, E96 and E133.
- **E28** — While the cell shows a loss or a fault it never collapses into the overflow; RECONFIGURING may. Settled by E98.
- **E29** — On ASIO, unlock is two consecutive bad answers of the one-second sample-position poll, or one resync request, while the source is external; relock is three consecutive good answers; an internal source reads locked; the selector constants are corrected first. Never an unlock from a message that is not a resync request. Amended by E39, E59, E82, E106, E120 and E121.
- **E30** — An unlock during a take does not stop the take: it keeps rolling, the unlock and relock frames are written to `take.manifest`, playback keeps running, and a device loss wins over an unlock that arrives with it. Never a clock event that ends a take or changes a device-loss seal.
- **E31** — Unlock notice "External clock lost on <name> (<source>). Check the clock cable and the clock master.", during a take with " Recording continues; the take is marked from this point."; relock "External clock locked again on <name> (<source>)." Never "recording quality compromised". Amended by E108, E110 and E111; extended by E216 (one combined warning for unlocks that came and went between two looks; these texts stay).
- **E32** — The indicator is hidden when the open device reports no clock sources, shows the source quietly ("INT 48k") while internal and the unlocked treatment while external and unlocked, immediately beside the transport status label, never in an overflow. Amended by E74, E81 and E119; confirmed over a carried label by E135.
- **E33** — The pump's death is a render-drive halt with story 337's halt treatment once; the log keeps the SEVERE record. Never a dead render thread only the log knows about, never two notices for one death.
- **E34** — A stall shorter than the window during a take on PortAudio or ASIO is marked as a gap with its take frame and counted into the take's dropouts. Amended by E43 (built), E46 (a new key) and E49.
- **E35** — At the verdict output and input meters fall to silence. Settled by E64.
- **E36** — After a verdict the transport does not show Playing or Recording, and the playhead is not corrected backwards; Reconnect during a take seeks to the anchor. Settled by E65 and E66.
- **E37** — A resync request (selector 5) never closes or reopens the stream; it is answered "supported" and handled in place.
- **E38** — A resync raises the number by one and adds an entry "Resync  Audio driver lost sync and resynchronized". Never more than one count per message. Amends E17 and E20.
- **E39** — One resync counts as one bad clock answer while the source is external and joins the poll's run; while internal it touches only the count and the log. Never a clock-lost notice from a single resync. Amends E29; amended by E106.
- **E40** — During a take a resync writes one manifest mark at the transport frame where it arrived (`clock=resync`), not counted into the take's dropouts. Limited by E123 (a resync whose message arrives while the FX thread is busy is marked at the last frame sampled before it); dated at each request's own arrival by E270, built by E279, E291 and E292. Its dating amended by E173. Extended by E225 and E226 (one mark per counted resync, whatever the xrun log kept or cleared).
- **E41** — A buffer-size change or a reset request during a take first stops and seals the take, then reopens, with the notice "<name> asked to be reset; recording stopped at m:ss.s. The take is saved." Deferred to future story 464 (amendment A1), except the end state E113 fixed.
- **E42** — Every `TrackDegraded` gets one Shed entry worded by the applied policy (expensive processing bypassed, oversampling reduced to n×, simpler processing substituted, each "to stay within the CPU budget (averaging NN % of the block time)"); `DoNothing` and `TrackRestored` write nothing; a removed track reads "Track (removed)". Amends E20 and E21. Amended by E174 (lost entries are counted), E181 (the words) and E199 (one entry per run, not one for each delivered degradation).
- **E43** — Every closed starved run of the stream's own input is written as a new-key line with its start frame and length and counted into the take's dropouts. Amends E34; amended by E75, E76 and E79.
- **E44** — The take-frame marks for losses before the capture ring may go to a future story with E25, E45 and E46; never close it with counter deltas at the block where they were noticed. Amended by E86 (b); filed as future story 463.
- **E45** — Story 338 still counts every such loss into the take's dropouts: the backend input rings, the render pump's input queue, the blocks an input thread could not build or publish, the blocks a publisher dropped for one of the engine's capture subscribers, and the device callbacks that lost a period's input (E211); never a block dropped only for another subscriber (E208). Amended by E76, and by E206 (these words, with E244's "build or publish", as E254 left them; it said "the ASIO input-ring write and both publishers' drop handlers included"); E86 (b) records its punch-take limit.
- **E46** — Losses before the ring use `gap=` when their marks are built; E3, E13, E30, E34 and E40 use new keys. Never zero-filled frames as `gap=`, never a field added to `gap=` or `routing-unavailable=`, never a `manifest-version` bump. Amends E3, E25 and E34.
- **E47** — Input blocks already queued when the stream's own input is declared ended are rendered into the take; E13's frame is the end of the last of them.
- **E48** — The tail mark covers only the tracks fed by the stream's own input, from the first block of the trailing starved run reached by the final sweep to the seal, and is not counted into the take's dropouts. Amends E3; amended by E76, E79 and E90.
- **E49** — One event gets one report: a starved run that ends in, or continues into, a declaration is that declaration. A dropped input-starved block of such a run is that declaration's too (E281) when the declaration flags every physical track of the input (E301).
- **E50** — Frames in the new keys are transport frames at the take's rate; for an input beside the stream the stop frame is the take's start plus the frames it delivered; the grammar Javadoc states every key's unit. Amended by E75.
- **E51** — "m:ss.s into the take" is the track's recorded length at the stop frame over the take's rate. Amended by E77 and E172 (per lap in a loop take).
- **E52** — During a take an input that delivered no block within the allowance gets the flag from the take's first frame and E14's "delivered no audio" warning once, with no ERROR and no latch; the ERROR and latch are for an input that delivered and then stopped. Amends E11 and E15.
- **E53** — "Delivered" means blocks arrived, whatever their content; digital silence is never a dead input.
- **E54** — An opened width below the route's need, found at open, keeps the routing-mismatch sentence; E14's sentences are only for blocks that stopped or never came.
- **E55** — No padding for an input beside the stream: its captured frames stay at their timeline positions, and a second frame on its mark records where clocked silence resumed.
- **E56** — A reopen that fails because the previous stream has not let go reads "the previous audio stream has not shut down yet"; a reason with no message reads "the audio driver refused to open it". Amended by E112.
- **E57** — Reconnect opens only the lost device; a landing on another device is a failure, "it is not available", the state staying Device lost. Play after a loss keeps today's behaviour.
- **E58** — A hang inside audio processing reads as a stall on Java Sound and Mock and as climbing xruns on PortAudio and ASIO: a recorded limit, with future story 465's requirement.
- **E59** — A bad answer is "no clock", "not advancing" or an unchanged position between two answered polls, each only if device periods advanced since the previous poll; a poll the driver did not answer resets both runs; polls run only while the stream runs. Amended by E106; extended by E120 and E121.
- **E60** — Poll only a source not classified internal on a device with more than one source; an unknown name counts as external.
- **E61** — A clock event while the indicator shows no source is dropped with no notice and no mark, unless the badge later measures the same measurement with no loss or close between; it is then replayed there. A resync still counts, logs and marks. Amended by E219 (the replay).
- **E62** — The unlock mark is at the transport frame sampled at the first bad answer of the unlocking run, or, when no good answer of its measurement came before that run, no later than the measurement's start (amended by E269), the relock at the oldest frame sampled at or after the third good answer (amended by E221). Limited by E123. Its dating amended by E173 and E177.
- **E63** — The start-up apply's RECONFIGURING may show for that apply and no longer.
- **E64** — Output meters need no change; input meters drop to the floor at the DEVICE_LOST delivery, keeping each clip latch, on both paths. Settles E35. Settled for a late block by E175.
- **E65** — A playback-only loss stops the transport in place: Stopped, the playhead where the audio stopped, one Stopped event, the status and buttons stopped. Settles E36.
- **E66** — After a take's rescue the playhead is restored to its position at the loss; placement was confirmed to read the record anchor, not the playhead. Settles E36.
- **E67** — "Audio device disconnected — playback paused" becomes "… — playback stopped", and "playback stays paused" becomes "playback stays stopped". Amends E4.
- **E68** — Device-side sustained escalation is held while the watched heartbeat has not advanced since those counts began, proceeds if it advances, and the closed stream's held counts are discarded when the verdict reaches the drain. Once the verdict has reached the drain, no device-side count read on the lost render drive or an older one counts toward a sustained run, so no such count begins or completes a run of a stream opened after the loss (E157); a count of the lost stream that the drain first reads on a newer drive is that drive's, a Known limit (E159). Amends E8; E114 records its limit. Amended by E157 and E159 (these words; they said "discarded at the verdict" and "After the verdict, no device-side count read on the lost render drive or an older one counts toward a sustained run, so a device-side fault of a stream opened after the loss escalates only on that stream's own three consecutive seconds of failures (E157).").
- **E69** — When a device loss reaches the drain, the drain clears the latched engine-fault fact if three conditions hold: a fact is latched; the escalation that set the latch (the latest one, or the latest render-drive halt while one is latched, story 337's E41) was read on the lost drive or an older one, the lost drive being the latest loss's when more than one loss comes before a delivery (E162); and every category latched since the fact last cleared is device-side (E154). Otherwise the loss clears nothing: not a render-drive halt's latch, not a fact latched since the last clear together with a processing fault (E161 records what the cell then shows), and not a fact whose latch an escalation read on a newer drive set, whatever was latched before it (E162). The clear releases every escalation latched since the fact last cleared and restarts every sustained run (E158); the notices already shown stay. Amended by E161 and E162 (these words; it said "A device-side fault latched for the lost drive is cleared by the loss; a halt latch and another drive's fault are not.").
- **E70** — An input halt of the lost device's own stream is part of the loss as far as the drain can tell: the drain judges a halt by the render drive newest when it first reads it, not by the stream that produced it (E151, E159). If the drain first reads it after the loss reached the drain, while the lost render drive or an older one is the newest drive, it gets no toast of its own, no ERROR and no latch; it is counted and logged with the input's other failures. Counted: it is in the running total that a later input notice and the Engine cell's tooltip show. Logged: when the input has had no notice in the 30 seconds before and no input fault is latched, by its own record, which says it is part of the device loss (E156); otherwise by the input's next record, or by a summary line about 30 seconds after the input's last failure, whose history-only entry repeats the input's last notice: the one the person was shown, or, when an earlier fold's own record opened the window, that record's lost-input sentence (E155). No toast and no history entry shows it as an audio device fault the person was not shown (E155). If the drain read it on an earlier frame, before the loss reached the drain, on the lost drive or an older one, it escalated as any input halt does, and the loss clears its latch when ruling E69's conditions hold; its notice stays. An input opened beside the stream for a take is closed with the stream by the loss's own close, or by a later retry of that close when it was deferred because the render drive did not stop in time (wedged in a backend's write or hung in audio processing, for instance), and is treated the same way: its halt first read at or after the loss's delivery, on the lost drive or an older one, is counted and logged with the input's other failures, with no toast of its own and no latch. Its fault latched on the lost drive is cleared at the loss when ruling E69's conditions hold, and its notice stays. Never a second fault for one loss from a device-side count read on the lost drive after the loss reached the drain: no ERROR, no latch, and no sustained-fault sentence ("Audio device fault — …", "Audio engine fault — …") the person was not shown. Amended by E151, E153, E155, E156, E159 and E162 (these words; it was "An input halt from the lost device's own stream, at or after the loss, is part of the loss: it gets no ERROR and no latch. A sibling input on a device that is still present is unaffected. Never two faults for one loss.").
- **E71** — A Reconnect does not reset the xrun count.
- **E72** — One Discarded detail for a refused block and a skipped older block: "One rendered block was skipped and never played", ×n per block. Amends E20.
- **E73** — Growth in an interval without a heartbeat advance is carried and counted at the next advancing sample of the same stream, discarded, and a carried resync's take mark with it, only at a stall, a close, a pause or a reopen. Amends E19's mechanism. Its words amended by E231.
- **E74** — The badge is hidden while no stream is open; an unlock showing at the loss is dropped with no relock notice; it comes back with the next stream. Amends E32; amended by E81 and E84.
- **E75** — In a loop take a starved run or tail across the wrap is split there; which lap a mark belongs to is a recorded limit, with a future story. Amends E43 and E50; amended by E89; filed as future story 466.
- **E76** — In a punch take only the part of a starved run inside the region is written and counted; the tail follows the same rule. Amends E43, E45 and E48; amended by E86 and E87.
- **E77** — An input that ends before punch-in is flagged from punch-in with "delivered no audio"; one that ends after punch-out gets no flag and no tail; one that ends inside the region is E13. Amends E13, E14 and E51; amended by E88.
- **E78** — With no device name the Stall detail is "Device stopped responding", with no colon.
- **E79** — The uncounted tail applies only to a device-loss seal; at an ordinary seal a trailing starved run is a counted `input-silence` line, and a healthy take stopped by the person writes none. Amends E48; amended by E90.
- **E80** — While the source list cannot be read the badge keeps its last source and lock state, judges nothing, and reads again every 10 poll intervals; never read since the open, it stays hidden. Amended by E119 and E141.
- **E81** — While the stream is closed on purpose the badge stays visible in the dim not-measured treatment with the last source and rate; an unlock showing at the close drops into it with no relock notice or mark; a change of device, source or rate in Settings while closed hides it. Amends E74 and E32; extended by E142 and E144; confirmed for a fallback by E145; amended by E169 for the ASIO interface.
- **E82** — Every stream that opens starts with fresh counters: an external source shows not-measured until the poll decides; an internal one shows quiet. Amends E29 and E74.
- **E83** — The spoken texts "Clock: <name>, <rate> kilohertz." with "Lock not checked while the audio stream is closed.", "Checking lock." or "Not locked.", and the closed and checking tooltips. Amended by E119.
- **E84** — On DEVICE_LOST the badge is hidden, an unlock showing is dropped with no relock notice or mark, and it returns with the next stream. Confirms E74.
- **E85** — A deferred input-stream stop leaves the stream open and the poll running, so the badge keeps measuring.
- **E86** — (a) A ring-overflow gap's extent runs to the next block the take received from that ring and is counted whole when it overlaps the punch region; (b) losses before the rings stay counted over the whole span, a recorded limit; (c) truncated frames count only inside the region. Amends E76 and E44; (a) amended by E104. A ring-overflow gap is now judged from its exact line (E273). Consult 46's and consult 49's notes on "one line per look" no longer apply.
- **E87** — A starved block partly inside the region counts as one block, its line carrying only the frames inside.
- **E88** — At a loop-plus-punch re-entry the track is flagged, its sentence depending on whether the take delivered audio to the file in an earlier lap. Amends E77.
- **E89** — `input-stopped` is never split; its frames are normalised into the loop range, a stop equal to the loop end is valid, and resume smaller than stop means the wrap lies between them. Amends E75; amended by E115, E176 (an end on a loop boundary), E186 and E191 (a larger resume does not rule out a wrap).
- **E90** — The tail stays uncounted only at a device-loss seal; at every other seal it is counted and clipped to the frames the file holds. Amends E79.
- **E91** — Whether the poll runs while paused decides between E81 and E85; a resume of the same stream keeps its lock counters.
- **E92** — After the take the cell reads "Xruns N · Last take dropouts K" in the warning accent until the next take or Clear; E22's 30-second rule does not apply to it. Settles E24.
- **E93** — While the device is lost the tooltip gives the loss's and then only the fault's "failed" sentence; never two "Click to …" sentences. Amends E27.
- **E94** — Singular and plural forms: "failed 1 time", "1 dropout", "1 late block", "1 block lost", "1 frame truncated".
- **E95** — The spoken action is lower case: "Press to restart audio engine." Amends E27.
- **E96** — With no name: "The audio device stopped responding or was disconnected. …" and "Audio device lost. Press to reconnect." Confirms E27.
- **E97** — The dialog's title "Xrun Log", the sections "Take" and "Log", Clear's spoken text, Clear enabled on an empty list, "Nothing logged." and "No take to report.", and a finished take with no loss still shows its line.
- **E98** — The Engine cell never goes into the overflow, in any state. Settles E28.
- **E99** — A Shed figure above 100 % is written as measured.
- **E100** — An external source whose lock cannot be measured is dim, with "Clock source. Lock cannot be checked on this device, so the badge will not warn if the external clock is lost." and "Clock: <name>, <rate> kilohertz. Lock not checked on this device."; a resync there is never a bad answer.
- **E101** — One tooltip for both motion settings, never a motion word: "Hardware clock source. Shows ⚠ UNLOCKED in red when the audio driver reports that the external clock is not locked." and "Hardware clock source. The external clock is not locked. Check the clock cable and the clock master." Amended by E147 (the first tooltip's words; it said "if the external clock is not locked").
- **E102** — The spoken Xruns text carries "This take: K dropouts." or "Last take: K dropouts." before the action. Amends E23.
- **E103** — A warning-accented Xruns cell stays on the strip like the Engine cell; other cells go into the overflow first, then its text is ellipsized. Extends E98's rule to an accented Xruns cell.
- **E104** — A gap's extent is its line's start frame plus its blocks times the block length when no block follows; with no punch region it is always counted. Amends E86 (a).
- **E105** — One block lost before the rings on the stream's own input, of any kind the take's dropouts count (such as an input-ring overflow, a block its input thread could not build or publish, a block a publisher dropped for the engine, or a device callback that lost its period's input), can count twice, as that loss and as the starved render block that filled its place: a recorded limit with a requirement for future story 463. Amended by E209 (these words, as E254 gave them; it said "One input stall can count its blocks twice (input-ring overflow and starved render)").
- **E106** — The poll interval is the unit: one bad answer if it holds a bad poll answer or a resync; two consecutive bad intervals unlock. Never an unlock from messages inside one interval. Amends E39 and E59; amended by E120, E121 and E122.
- **E107** — From the moment a switch the person made is sent, the verdict is withheld until max(5 s, the E1 window) with no advance; no notice and no mark of the switch itself. Amends E2; amended by E122; E124 keeps it for a refused switch; extended by E228 (the end of a driver control-panel session gets the same allowance).
- **E108** — During a MIDI-only take the unlock notice ends "Recording continues." with no claim of a mark. Amends E31. Extended by E216 and E223 (the combined warning's endings).
- **E109** — Opening another project clears the take figures from the strip and the dialog.
- **E110** — The relock notice is at the information level. Amends E31.
- **E111** — A device name is missing when blank or the default placeholder; then "The audio device" or "the audio device", in every surface. Amended by E125, E130 and E131.
- **E112** — A closed list of four reasons ("the previous audio stream has not shut down yet", "it opened with an audio format that cannot be used", "it is not available", "the audio driver refused to open it"); the engine's message goes to the log only. Amends E56.
- **E113** — A driver-requested reopen that fails over an open stream has its own notice, "<name> asked to be reset and could not be reopened — audio stopped. Check that it is connected and powered, then choose Reconnect.", and lead "<name> could not be reopened after a driver reset during recording"; no Stall entry. Never "disconnected", "stopped responding" or "Audio engine reconfigured" for it.
- **E114** — The fault hold does not hold through a callback-backend stall whose fault run began before the periods froze: a recorded limit, with future story 468's requirement.
- **E115** — A loop take's stop or resume frame may lie in lap 1's pre-roll, at or after the take's start. Amends E89.
- **E116** — The tooltip says nothing about underrun reporting until the first stream on the selected driver has answered, keeps that answer through a closed stream, and forgets it on a change of driver or device.
- **E117** — At most one block of unmarked silence at the end of a take a write failure sealed: a recorded limit, with future story 469's requirement.
- **E118** — Confirmed as built: an input whose delivered end is at or before punch-in, declared after punch-out with the file holding frames, is flagged from punch-in with "delivered no audio" and no separate line.
- **E119** — From the open until the first source read answers, the badge keeps the label it showed while closed, dim, with the starting texts "Clock source when the audio stream last ran. Lock is not checked yet on this stream." and "Clock: <name>, <rate> kilohertz. Lock not checked yet."; a failed first read keeps it; a Stop that closes the stream before the read answers returns it to the closed texts (the transport's own close at a Stop is skipped while an instrument insert is anywhere in the mixer, ruling E163). Amends E80 and E83; (a) amended by E135 and E142, (d) by E136, E143, E144 and E163 (its words; it said "a Stop before the read returns it to the closed texts").
- **E120** — An interval in which the device periods did not advance is not judged and breaks both runs; its resyncs keep their count, entry and mark. Amends E106; extends E59; amended by E140.
- **E121** — An answered interval that only set a new position baseline, with no resync, breaks both runs. Amends E106; extends E59.
- **E122** — Resyncs around a clock-source switch belong to the switch: counted, logged and marked, never a bad answer for the new source. Amends E106 and E107; amended by E137 and E138; extended to a driver control-panel session by E248 and E249, and to the stream that answers a driver's reset request by E271.
- **E123** — An unlock mark declared from resync requests is dated at or before the first request of its first bad poll interval, and each resync mark and log entry at its own arrival, never after the driver's call that brought it (E292), an entry of several at the first; a resync whose message arrives while the FX thread is busy is marked at the last frame sampled before it, early by up to the freeze: a recorded limit on E62 and E40 (amended by E270 and built by E279; it said "An unlock mark, and resync marks and log times read from the newest-arrival field, can be up to one poll interval after the first resync of the interval: a recorded limit on E62").
- **E124** — A switch the driver refuses keeps its allowance. Extended by E138.
- **E125** — The removal lead is "<name> was disconnected during recording" or "The audio device was disconnected during recording". Amends E111 and story 327's named lead.
- **E126** — The "unavailable" and "arrived, but it is not the lost device" sentences follow the cause (still disconnected; stopped responding and has not been reopened; asked to be reset and has not been reopened), named and unnamed. Amends story 327's two sentences.
- **E127** — An input with no name: "the audio input" in E14's two warnings and in E11's reason. Amends E11 and E14.
- **E128** — Story 337's device fault reads "1 error" for one, the backend's name for the placeholder, and "from the audio device" with no name at all. Amends E11.
- **E129** — The lost device's return whose reopen fails: "<name> was reconnected but could not be reopened: <reason>. Choose Reconnect, or choose another device in Audio Settings.", with Reconnect. Amends E7.
- **E130** — A loss that ends on another device names each device or says which one it is ("another audio device", "the lost device"). Amends E111.
- **E131** — On the automatic device the loss surfaces use the unnamed form; only the clock notices name the resolved driver: a recorded limit, with future story 470's requirement. Amends E111.
- **E132** — The overflow button's tooltip "More status" and spoken "More status. Press to show the status cells that do not fit."
- **E133** — The Engine cell's tooltip for E113's cause: "<name> asked to be reset and could not be reopened. Audio is stopped. Click to reconnect, or choose another device in Audio Settings." Amends E27.
- **E134** — "Reconfiguring audio engine…" before E113's notice, and a reset request with no stream open ending "Audio engine reconfigured", stay as built.
- **E135** — A stream's own read that answers "no clock sources" (the lock monitor naming no source) hides the badge at once and forgets the label; the starting view ends at the first answered read. Amends E119 (a); confirms E32 over a carried label.
- **E136** — After a Stop, an answer of no sources hides the badge; the read that settles the closed view must be about the device the stream opened. Amends E119 (d); amended by E143.
- **E137** — The switch exemption covers the poll interval of the driver's answer and the next (1 to 2 s); an unlock is never false and at worst one interval late. Amends E122. Extended to a driver control-panel session by E248, E249 and E260, and to the stream that answers a driver's reset request by E271.
- **E138** — The exemption starts when the switch is sent, covers the time until the driver answers, and holds for a refused switch, whose old source keeps its state. Amends E122; extends E124. Extended to a driver control-panel session by E248 and E249, and to the stream that answers a driver's reset request by E271.
- **E139** — A Reconnect whose thread cannot start raises exactly one warning: "Reconnect could not start. Choose Reconnect again, or choose another device in Audio Settings."
- **E140** — An interval with frozen periods is never good either; a relock needs three good intervals in which the periods advanced. Amends E120.
- **E141** — A failed first read is not E32's answer: a carried label stays starting, nothing carried stays hidden, the read is asked again once a second, never two in flight. Amends E80.
- **E142** — A Settings change of device or rate that reopens the stream carries no label into the new stream; it is hidden until the new stream's read answers. Amends E119 (a); extends E81; amended by E143.
- **E143** — A stream that opens and then fails to start is, for the badge, a stream that opened and closed at once: E119 (d) and E136 apply to its read (cases S1, S2 and S3), and the engine never puts an earlier stream's record back. Amends E119 (d), E136 and E142; its second "Never" amended by E146.
- **E144** — A label that the stream's own status had already replaced is not a closed view: hidden once the stream closes (the manager chose this of the two outcomes); while an instrument insert in the mixer keeps the stream running through a Stop, nothing closes, and the badge shows its checking view and then follows that stream's re-read. Amends E119 (d); extends E81; limits consult 11's G2 and consult 10's R5 reading. Amended by E163 (its words; it said "hidden after the Stop").
- **E145** — A stream that ran on a fallback device leaves no badge: the provision ladder's fallback rungs, PortAudio's default device where PortAudio is available and Java Sound's, are devices for which the engine controller reports no clock source, and at that stream's open the badge forgets a label read on another device (E142), so it is hidden while that stream runs and after it closes. An applied change in Audio Settings that reconfigures the engine (Worker Pool Size or the buffer size, for instance) then asks a read of the clock of the device Audio Settings names, and a read asked by an Apply while no stream is open never creates a closed view, so the badge stays hidden.
