Such an Apply hides a closed view of a device other than the one Audio Settings names, because a read of the configured device cannot prove that clock (E81); the ladder's fallback rungs leave none, and `ClockStatusIndicatorTest.aSettingsReadOfTheConfiguredDeviceAfterAStreamOnAFallbackHidesTheClosedView` drives the rule with a fallback that reports a clock. While an instrument insert in the mixer keeps the fallback stream running, such an Apply closes and reopens the stream from the ladder's first rung (the configured device's, when its backend can stream), and the badge follows the reopened stream as after any open (E119, E142 and E144). Confirms E81. Amended by E163 (its words; it said "After a stream that ran on a fallback device, an Apply") and in consult 22 (its words; it said "After a stream that ran on a fallback device has closed, an Apply that changes nothing hides the badge", an Apply the Settings dialog does not offer: Apply is enabled once a setting differs from its saved value, and an OK with nothing pending reconfigures nothing).
- **E146** — A slow failing start on the instrument-insert and record-start paths is a recorded limit (Known limits); on every path never a take mark or manifest clock event for a recording that did not begin, never a clock notice that says "Recording continues" or "the take is marked" for it, never the measured or UNLOCKED view after the failure has landed, never the clock of a device that stream did not open. Amends E143's second "Never", which still holds on Play, pre-roll, Apply, the format-change reopen and Reconnect.
- **E147** — The lock is measured from what the driver reports, its sample position and its resync messages; an interface that loses its external clock and carries on on its own clock, while its driver keeps reporting a moving position and sends at most one resync, keeps the badge quiet on the external source. So the quiet tooltip reads "Hardware clock source. Shows ⚠ UNLOCKED in red when the audio driver reports that the external clock is not locked.", the unlocked tooltip stays, and the limit is recorded (Known limits; clock check 2). Never a tooltip that promises a warning the driver's reports cannot produce. Amends E101.
- **E148** — A driver's reset request is a reconfigure under E22 and E26, so the Xruns count restarts at it, also when its reopen fails and the device is declared lost; the entries stay in the log. Accepted as built and recorded as a Known limit. E22's Never now reads: never a reset caused by neither the person (Clear, a settings change, Restart) nor the driver's own reset request. Amends E22.
- **E149** — For future story 461, which must carry it with a test before it makes the path reachable; it changes nothing in story 338's build, because no production backend publishes a device's arrival. When the lost device returns on its own and reopens, the controller's "Audio device reconnected — review recovered take and re-arm transport" contradicts the record coordinator's "Tracks are still armed" and, after a playback-only loss, names a take that does not exist. With a take rescued, the coordinator's "<name> reconnected. Tracks are still armed; press Record to continue." is the only notice; with none, "<name> reconnected. Press Play to continue." (unnamed: "The audio device reconnected. Press Play to continue."). Never "re-arm" while the armed set is kept, or "review recovered take" when no take was rescued.
- **E150** — A resume starts a new render drive, so the watchdog applies E2's start-up allowance there: an unplug while the stream is paused, or parked by Reconnect, is noticed by the start-up allowance after a later resume of that stream, about 5 s, and a Play or a Record that resumed it rolls the transport over silence until then; a Record with audio inputs does not resume it, but past its route checks closes it and then tries to open a new one, and a stream it opens gets the same allowance (E2). Accepted as built; the E9 bullet and the Known limit take its words. Amends E9. Amended by E163 (these words; they said "an unplug during a pause, or while Reconnect has the stream parked, is noticed by the start-up allowance after the next Play or Record" and "with Playing over silence until then").
- **E151** — An input halt of the lost stream that the drain first reads on a newer drive, because the lost device came back and was reopened, or an instrument insert opened a stream, before the drain's first read, escalates as story 337 shipped it, over the working new stream; the loss does not clear it, nor does a resume of the new stream; a restart, one fault-free second on the next stream opened, or a later loss that meets ruling E69's conditions does (E154, E162). Accepted as a Known limit until E152 lands, because it is story 337's behaviour in a window E70's gate does not reach, and its fix needs each input halt tied to the stream that produced it, which touches story 337's E94 and E98 to E100. Never a toast or a latch for an input halt the drain first reads on the lost drive or an older one after the loss reached it. Amends E70 (its first sentence). Amended by E159 (its Never's words, which said "the drain reads on the lost drive or an older one"; its Known limit is now the newer-drive limit of E159) and E162 (its clear's words, which said "nor does Stop and Play; a restart or one fault-free second on the next stream opened does").
- **E152** — The later story's rule, filed as future story 473 because story 337 owns the behaviour: an input halt is judged by the stream that produced it, never by the render drive current when the drain first reads it. (1) A halt produced by a stream a device loss closed, or by an input the loss closed with it, gets no toast of its own, no ERROR and no latch, however late the drain first reads it; it is counted and logged with the input's other failures as E70 rules (E155 and E156), and its count does not join a sustained run of a stream opened after the loss (E157); this also covers a stream opened or resumed between the loss reading the epoch and its close (a Record start or an instrument insert's open at that moment, for instance). (2) A halt of a stream opened after the loss escalates as story 337 ships it. (3) Once an input halt's fault has latched, a fault-free second clears it only on a stream opened after the stream that produced the halt. A stream already running when the drain read the halt counts; a resume of the producing stream never does. Its constraints (no allocation, lock or blocking call added on a real-time or backend input thread; the drain still the record's one reader; the rulings it keeps; a genuine halt of a stream opened after the loss never suppressed) and its five deterministic cases, which need no hardware, are the future story's. Landing it removes E151's Known limit and E154's second Known limit. Amended by E155 (item (1)'s words; it said "gets no toast, no ERROR and no latch, only a log record, however late the drain first reads it").
- **E153** — An input opened beside the stream for a take is closed with the stream at the loss, by the loss's own close, or by a later retry of that close when it was deferred because the render drive did not stop in time (wedged in a backend's write, as its Known limit records, or hung in audio processing, for instance). Its halt read on the lost drive after the loss reached the drain is part of the loss: counted and logged with the input's other failures, with no toast of its own and no latch; a halt read before that escalated as any halt does, and its latch on the lost drive is cleared at the loss when ruling E69's conditions hold, and its notice stays; a fault that persists shows again when the next take opens that input. Accepted as built and recorded as a Known limit, because an independent fault of that input within about one frame of the loss would be two faults coinciding by chance, and the likely real case is one event (a shared USB hub or power), where folding is right; the close itself counts no halt, since the watchdog declares a stall only on a live input and the Java Sound capture counts nothing its own teardown releases. E70's Never now reads: never a second fault for one loss from a device-side count read on the lost drive after the loss reached the drain. Amends E70 (its second sentence). Amended by E155 (its second sentence's words; it said "part of the loss, a log record only"), E159 and E162 (these words; they said "is closed with the stream at the loss, by the loss's own close", "Its halt read at or after the loss on the lost drive is part of the loss: ...; its latch on the lost drive is cleared at the loss under E69, and its notice stays; ...", "(a shared USB hub or power, or the close itself)" and "never two faults for one loss read on the lost drive").
- **E154** — Once an input halt has latched, and until the fault clears, a fault-free second clears the fault only on a stream opened after that halt was latched (story 337's E87), whatever the cell shows. Neither a drive resumed on a stream opened before that halt was latched (story 337's E59) nor a drive already running when it was latched (story 337's E71) clears it then; a drive resumed on a stream opened after it can. Restart Audio Engine (story 337's E62) clears it in every case; a device loss clears it when ruling E69's conditions hold. Accepted as built and recorded as two Known limits, because a resume does not reopen the capture line, so the halted input is still dead and clearing the fault would make the cell go quiet over it; only the cell's label is untidy when a later fault replaced the input halt's. Never the Engine cell going quiet while an input it reported is still dead, but for one ordering out of reach on hardware: a halt produced by a stream opened after the loss's close, in the moment between its backend's attach and its render drive's claim, that the drain reads on the lost drive before the loss reaches it raises its ERROR and latches as E159 (a) says, and the loss then clears that fault when ruling E69's conditions hold, so the cell can go quiet over that stream's dead input; it needs a capture thread failing within microseconds of its open. Story 337's E59 now reads: "A resumed drive counts as ruling E41's 'stream opened after the latch', except while an input halt has latched since the fact last cleared: then only one on a stream opened after the latest such halt was latched counts." The clause of story 337's E71 now reads: "a sustained fact keeps ruling E59 as written; an input halt is ruling E87's, and while an input halt has latched since the fact last cleared, any fact clears by a fault-free second only on a stream opened after the latest such halt was latched." Amends story 337's E59, E71 and E87 for story 338's cell; story 337's text is not edited. Story 337's E87, its "never on a resume of the paused stream", is read for story 338's cell as a resume of a stream opened before the halt was latched. Amended by E162 (its second sentence, which said "Neither a resumed drive (story 337's E59) nor a drive that was already running (story 337's E71) clears it then"; its device-loss clause, which said "a device loss clears it under E69 when every latched category is device-side"; its readings of story 337's E59 and E71, which ended "except while an input halt has latched since the fact last cleared" and "and so is every fact latched while an input halt has latched since the fact last cleared"; and its reading of story 337's E87, which is new). Amended in consult 21 (its Never's exception, which is new).
- **E155** — An input halt folded at a loss (the drain first reads it on the lost render drive or an older one) is never shown to the person as an audio device fault they were not shown. It is counted and logged with the input's other failures (E70). A history-only summary entry that accounts for it, or for input failures counted after it, repeats the sentence of the input notice the person was shown, as story 337 ships it. When the fold itself is the notice that entry follows, because the input had no notice in the 30 seconds before and no input fault was latched, the entry reads as lost input, in the words of the input's own warning: "Input from “<device>” was lost for a block: <reason>. If you are recording, the take has a gap there. It happened once more after that notice, the last time at <time>." ("<n> more times" for more). It is at the warning level, with Restart Audio Engine. It never carries the audio-device-fault sentence ("Audio device fault — … Audio may stay silent or broken until you restart the audio engine."). Story 338's behaviour (the loss branch's fold and the sentence it hands the funnel; the funnel's mechanics are story 337's, unchanged), built in story 338, because as first built that entry repeated the audio-device-fault sentence: the person's first word that the loss's halt was a device fault, telling them to restart after a loss whose own notice told them to Reconnect, and a second fault for one loss in the history, which E70's Never forbids. The 30-second window that such a fold opens is accepted as built and recorded as a Known limit. Never an audio-device-fault sentence, in a toast or the notification history, for an input halt the drain first reads on the lost drive or an older one after the loss reached it, unless that sentence was shown for the input before the loss reached the drain. Amends E70 (its words) and the "only a log record" wording of E151's E70 sentence, E152 item (1) and E153. Amended by E159 (its Never's words; it said "for an input halt that is part of a loss, unless that sentence was shown for it before the loss reached the drain").
- **E156** — The log record of an input halt folded at a loss, the stream's own or an input's opened beside it, says what happened. Its header carries "part of the device loss; no toast of its own (story 338, ruling E70)". It never says "folded into the notice of the same frame", and it never cites story 337's E65. The records of story 337's folds keep "folded into the notice of the same frame; no toast of its own (story 337, ruling E65)": a first worker failure folded into the first render failure of its frame, and a render failure folded into the halt of its frame. Story 338's behaviour (its loss branch reused story 337's E65 fold, whose detail is right for story 337's folds), built in story 338, because that detail pointed a reader of the log to a notice of the same frame that does not exist, the loss's notice coming from the engine controller, and never said that the halt belonged to the device loss, the one fact that explains why there was no notice. Amends E70 (its record).
- **E157** — After a loss reaches the drain, no device-side count (one of the categories a stalled or lost device produces, E68) that the drain reads on the lost render drive or an older one counts toward a sustained run. That includes an input halt it folds and a lost input block, a callback fault or another device-side count it reports. A device-side fault of a stream opened after the loss escalates on that stream's own three consecutive seconds of failures, as story 337 ships it, unless the drain first read the lost stream's last counts on a drive newer than the lost one (E159). It is never one second sooner because a count the drain read on the lost drive or an older one began the run; a count of the lost stream that the drain first reads on a newer drive is that drive's and can begin its run, a Known limit (E159). The count its notice shows may still include those earlier counts (E70, E155). A processing failure (of the render, a worker, an effect, MIDI or a plugin) keeps its run across a loss that clears no latched fault, as story 337 ships it: it is that component failing on the lost stream's own blocks, which a stream restart does not cure, so a run that continues on the lost drive or on a stream opened after the loss is the same fault; a loss that clears a device-side fault latched for the lost drive (E69) restarts that run, as every clear has restarted every run since story 337 (E158). Story 338's behaviour (E68 and the bucket restart at the loss are story 338's, and the code did not yet meet E68's "the closed stream's held counts are discarded at the verdict" for counts read after the verdict), built in story 338, because a Reconnect within a second or two of the loss, with lost input blocks on the new stream in each of its first two seconds, gave an ERROR "Audio device fault — …" with Restart Audio Engine and "Engine fault: audio input" on the Engine cell: the lost stream's folded halt, or a count read on the lost drive, had begun the run one second earlier, a second fault for one loss. Never an ERROR for a new stream's device-side failures that a count the drain read on the lost drive or an older one helped decide. Amends E68. Confirmed by consult 17 for the device-side categories only, as built, no new ruling (its words in the first two sentences, the processing-failure sentence and the Never; it said "no count the drain reads", "a lost block or other device-side count" and "a new stream's failures", with no processing-failure sentence). Amended by E158 (the processing-failure sentence's words; consult 17's said "keeps its run across the loss" and had no clause for a loss that clears a latched fault). Amended by E159 (its third and fourth sentences and its Never; they said "... as story 337 ships it.", "It is never one second sooner because the lost stream's last counts began the run." and "Never an ERROR for a new stream's device-side failures that the lost stream's counts helped decide.").
- **E158** — A device loss that clears a device-side fault latched for the lost drive (E69) restarts every sustained run in progress, including a processing failure's (of the render, a worker, an effect, MIDI or a plugin), as every clear has since story 337 (its DEV-B2-7). Such a failure then escalates to its ERROR only on three consecutive seconds of failures that the drain reads after the loss, on the lost drive's last blocks or on a stream opened after the loss. With a stream opened within about a second of the loss and the component still failing, that is up to two seconds later than with no fault latched at the loss. A stream opened later starts a new run anyway. Until then the failure has its warning-level handling (story 337's E44), and its ERROR's count and "since" time still include its failures from before the loss. A loss that clears no latched fault restarts the device-side runs only, and a processing run continues across it (E157). Story 337's behaviour (every clear restarts every track) meeting story 338's clear at the loss (E69), accepted as built in story 338, because: every clear ends an engine-fault fact and starts every component's judgement afresh, and the fault-free second already does this to a worker's, an effect's, MIDI's or a plugin's run that is not latched; such a loss clears only when a device-side fault was latched on the dying stream, so the processing failures counted in its last seconds were counted on a stream that was itself faulting, which the drain cannot tell apart from the component's own; at the loss the cell then shows only the loss, as E69 and E114's Known limit say; nothing heard, played or written to disk differs, only when the latched ERROR comes. Never a processing run restarted by a loss that clears no latched fault; never a processing failure's earlier counts missing from the count or the "since" time of its later notice. Amends E157 (its processing-failure sentence).
- **E159** — At a device loss the audio health drain judges each count by the render drive newest in the reading that first carries it, and by whether the loss has reached it (the DEVICE_LOST delivery on the window's FX thread, which DeviceLossReactions hands it), never by the stream that produced the count. In rulings E8, E68 to E70, E151, E153 and E155 to E162, "the lost render drive" (or "the lost drive") is the drive that was newest when the engine controller read the render-drive epoch at the loss, and "after the loss" is after the loss reached the drain (E162 says when that is for more than one loss). Three cases follow, each accepted as built in story 338. (a) Read on the lost drive or an older one before the loss reaches the drain, even after the stream's close, a count is judged as any count: an input halt raises its ERROR "Audio device fault — …" with Restart Audio Engine and latches, a device-side run whose heartbeat advanced escalates at its third second (E114), and a first occurrence shows its warning (E160); at the delivery the loss restarts the device-side runs and clears the latched fact when ruling E69's conditions hold (E161, E162), and the notices stay. (b) Read on the lost drive or an older one after the loss, a device-side count is part of the loss: no ERROR, no latch and no sustained run (E68, E70, E155 to E157); an input halt, with the input's lost blocks read in its frame, has no toast of its own (E155, E156); any other device-side count goes through its kind's warning path (E160). (c) Read on a drive newer than the lost one, a count is that drive's, whichever stream produced it: this happens when a stream is opened before the drain first reads the last counts of the lost stream or of an input closed with it, or when the stream the loss closes was itself opened or resumed between the epoch read and the close (by a Record start or an instrument insert's open at that moment, for instance); an input halt among them escalates (E151), and any other device-side count among them, read after the loss, begins or joins a sustained run of its kind, so two more seconds in a row of failures of that kind, of the stream running then, can raise its ERROR with Restart Audio Engine one second sooner than that stream's own three, and "Engine fault: <words>" once the loss has ended, until a successful Restart Audio Engine, one fault-free second on the next stream opened or on a drive resumed after that ERROR, or a later loss that meets ruling E69's conditions, unless a later escalation replaces that fault first; while an input halt has latched since the fault last cleared, that second counts only on a stream opened after that halt was latched (E154); a Stop and Play can open the next stream and a Pause and Play can resume the stream, but neither starts a drive while an instrument insert in the mixer keeps the stream running through them; and while a render-drive halt latched before that ERROR holds the fact, the cell keeps the halt's fault instead, until it goes as E162 says, which can be at one fault-free second on the stream running then. Whose: (c) is story 337's behaviour (at HEAD every count joined its run and every halt escalated) in a window story 338's gate does not reach, as E151's is; (a) is story 338's gate beginning at the delivery, with story 337's escalation. Accepted because: on hardware every stream opened after a loss comes from an action, the display frame that first shows the loss notice has already read what the lost stream recorded, and only the mock backend announces a device's return, so (c) needs a stream opened after the loss's close and before the drain's next frame (an instrument insert's open can be that quick), an open or a resume between the epoch read and the close, or a close deferred because the render drive did not stop in time (wedged in a backend's write or hung in audio processing, for instance) whose retry is followed by an open before the drain's next frame (the next open retries the close first: Reconnect's or an instrument insert's, for instance); its fix needs every count tied to the stream that produced it, which touches story 337's E94 and E98 to E100 and which future story 473 now carries; a drain-only stand-in that took the first reading of a newer drive as the lost drive's would hold back the first frame of every later stream's own counts and still leave the halt of the same window; and (a)'s notices were true when the drain read them (E8), the loss then clearing their latch. Three more orderings are ruled here, with no Known limit of their own, because on hardware each either costs at most about a second before a correct ERROR or is out of reach: a new stream's backend counts that the drain reads in the moment between that backend's attach and its render drive's claim, the first time after a loss, are taken as the lost drive's (that stream's run begins on its next reading; a halt in that moment would be folded with no ERROR, which needs a capture thread failing within microseconds of its open, and E155's Known limit records it); the loss's delivery restarts a newer stream's device-side run that began before the delivery (it begins again on that stream's next failure, losing nothing inside the same second); and a DEVICE_LOST that is never delivered, which needs the engine-state publisher's buffer full, as the bridge's unbounded request and non-blocking hand-off rule out, is no window of the drain. Future story 473 now also requires that every device-side count of a stream a device loss closed begins and joins no sustained run of a stream opened after the loss, however late the drain first reads it, and that a device-side count of a stream opened after the loss begins or joins that stream's own run whenever the drain reads it. Recorded as two Known limits: the newer-drive limit, which replaces E151's, and the before-delivery limit, with E160's warning. Story 338's test `aLostBlockOfTheLostStreamFirstReadOnANewerDriveJoinsThatDrivesRun` pins (c) as built. Never an ERROR, a latch or a sustained run from a device-side count the drain reads on the lost drive or an older one after the loss. Amends E8, E68, E70, E151 (its Never and its Known limit), E153, E155 (its Never) and E157 (their words, as consult 19 gives them). Amended by E162 (the words of its definitions, of (a)'s clear, of (b)'s input halt, of (c)'s routes, run and clear, of the routes in its reasons, of the three orderings ruled with no Known limit of their own and of its test sentence).
- **E160** — After a device loss has reached the drain, a device-side count other than an input halt that the drain reads on the lost render drive or an older one (a lost input block, or a callback, sink, pacing or device failure) still goes through its kind's warning path, as story 337 ships it, except a lost input block read in the frame of an input halt, which that halt's report counts (E70): no ERROR, no latch and no sustained run (E68, E157); when its kind has no latched fault and had no notice in the 30 seconds before, and, for a lost input block, no record of an input halt folded at a loss in those 30 seconds, a warning toast with Restart Audio Engine beside the loss notice, its history entry and its WARNING record, in the kind's ordinary words, which can say "Audio continues."; otherwise it is counted, and accounted for by its kind's next record or, about 30 seconds after the kind's last failure, by a history-only entry that repeats the last notice kept for its kind, at that notice's level and with its action. That notice is the last one shown for the kind, so the entry is at the error level and repeats the ERROR when the last notice shown was an ERROR before the loss reached the drain (E114), which is honest accounting of a fault the person was shown; or it is the record of an input halt folded at a loss, when that fold wrote its own record after the last notice shown, and the entry then repeats that record's lost-input sentence at the warning level (E155's Known limit). Story 337's behaviour (the warning path), which story 338's gate leaves in place, accepted as built and recorded as a Known limit, because the warning is a true account of a failure of the lost stream's last blocks and carries no ERROR, latch or run (E8's two notices, here in the other order); because the render pump and the Java Sound capture count nothing their own teardown releases, so it needs a device-side failure that the lost stream counts in its last moments before its close, of a kind with no notice in the 30 seconds before, which a stall that froze the device for the stall window makes unlikely; and because folding it into the loss as E155 folds the halt would open its kind's 30-second window as a notice would and hold back the reconnected stream's first warning of that kind. A notice about a count of the lost stream carries its kind's action and is not the answer to the loss (E6). Story 338's test `aDeviceSideCountReadOnTheLostDriveAfterTheLossStillShowsItsWarning` pins it as built. Never an ERROR, a latch or a sustained run from such a count (E159). Amends E8 (its "the WARNING and then the stall" reading, in Accepted as built) and E114's Known limit (its window sentence). Amended by E162 (the words of its first clause's exception, of its toast and summary clauses and of its test sentence).
- **E161** — A device loss clears the latched engine-fault fact only when every category latched since the fact last cleared is device-side (E69, E154); when a processing fault (of the render, a worker, an effect, MIDI or a plugin) has escalated since then, the loss clears nothing, and a device-side fault latched on the lost drive stays with it. The person sees "Device lost: <name>" with the latched fact's sentence in its tooltip, and once the loss has ended "Engine fault: <words>" in the critical treatment with that fact's action: the latest escalation's, so the lost device's when its fault escalated after the processing one, or the latest render-drive halt's while one is latched (story 337's E41); it stays until a successful Restart Audio Engine, or one fault-free second on a drive started after the latch, or after the halted one while a render-drive halt is latched (story 337's E71), with no failure of the render, of the device side or of any latched kind (after an input halt, on a stream opened after it, E154), so a processing fault that keeps failing on the reopened stream keeps it on the cell as long. The device-side fingerprint stays escalated, so its later failures are counted without a record of their own until the fact clears (story 337's E44), except an input halt read on a drive newer than the lost one, which escalates as E159 (c) says. Story 338's behaviour (its clear's condition, accepted with E154 and E158), accepted as built and recorded in E114's Known limit, because it needs two independent sustained faults, a processing one and a device-side one, latched before a loss in one run of the engine; because clearing only the device-side part needs one latch per fingerprint, where story 337's E41 keeps one fact; and because the cell never goes quiet over a fault that is still failing (E154's Never). Story 338's tests `theLossClearsNoFactWhileAProcessingFaultIsLatched` and `aProcessingFaultThatKeepsFailingKeepsTheFactTheLossDidNotClear` pin it as built. Never the loss clearing a processing fault's latch. Amends E69 (its words) and E114's Known limit (its clear sentence). Amended by E162 (the words of the fact's action and of the drive its fault-free second needs, of the escalated fingerprint's later failures and of its test sentence).
- **E162** — What the sentences of rulings E8, E68 to E70, E151, E153 and E155 to E161, of the Resolution's paragraph "The drain at a loss." and of the Known limits that cite those rulings say about how the drain judges a count at a device loss, and about what that loss then clears, describes one loss: it holds for a count that the drain reads while no other loss whose lost drive is the render drive newest in that reading or a newer one has reached the drain, as this ruling says when a loss has. Where losses follow one another, or two or more of the cases of E159, E160 and E161 meet at one loss, the outcome follows from the rules of those cases, applied as follows, also where one of those sentences, read alone, would say otherwise. A device-side count is judged by the case its own reading falls in: the render drive newest in that reading, and whether the loss had reached the drain when it was read (E159); a processing count and a render-drive halt are judged as any count, whichever drive reads them (E69, E157, E158). Each DEVICE_LOST delivery hands the drain the render-drive epoch the engine controller read at its latest loss, and the drain keeps the newest epoch it was given, so a loss has reached the drain from the first delivery that carries its lost drive or a newer one, and from then on a device-side count read on that drive or an older one is case (b), whichever stream produced it. The engine-fault fact is one latch, as story 337's E41 keeps it: a render-drive halt replaces any fact, and any other escalation, an input halt's included, any fact but a render-drive halt's, so the fact is the latest escalation's, or the latest render-drive halt's while one is latched. At each delivery the drain restarts the device-side runs, and it clears the fact when ruling E69's conditions hold for the epoch that delivery carries; otherwise it clears nothing, and the fact, which later escalations replace as above, and every escalation latched since the fact last cleared stay until the fact clears: at a successful Restart Audio Engine, at a fault-free second (story 337's E41, E59 and E71, as E154 amends them), or at a later loss that meets ruling E69's conditions. So when, with no render-drive halt latched, a device-side fault latched on the lost drive before the loss reached the drain (E159 (a)) is followed, still before that, by a device-side escalation read on a drive newer than the lost one (E159 (c)), for example the lost stream's input halt first read on a stream that an instrument insert opened right after the close, the fact is the newer drive's and the loss clears nothing: both notices stay in the notification history; the Engine cell shows "Device lost: <name>" with the newer fault's sentence in its tooltip and, once the loss has ended, "Engine fault: <words>" of the newer fault, until the fact clears or a later escalation replaces it, as the newer-drive Known limit says; and the lost drive's fault stays latched with it, its fingerprint escalated, its later failures handled as E161 says. And when a render-drive halt latched before a device-side escalation read on a drive newer than the lost one still holds the fact, that escalation leaves the halt's latch in place: the Engine cell keeps the halt's fault until a later render-drive halt replaces it, a successful Restart Audio Engine, or one fault-free second on any drive started after the halted one, which can be the drive running at that escalation, though while an input halt has latched since the fact last cleared, that second counts only on a stream opened after the latest such halt was latched (E154; story 337's E71). Story 338's clear at the loss (E69) meeting story 337's single latch (E41) in the window of E151 and E159 (c), accepted as built and recorded in the newer-drive Known limit, because it needs a fault latched before an escalation read on a newer drive, a device-side one on the lost drive with that escalation still before the loss reaches the drain, or a render-drive halt, and that escalation on hardware needs one of that limit's routes; because the cell then shows what that limit already records, as it would without the earlier device-side fault, or the earlier halt's fault as story 337 ships it, and each earlier notice was true when it was shown (E8); and because the earlier fault's fingerprint, which stays escalated until the fact clears, has its later failures handled as E161 accepts for a loss that clears nothing, while clearing the lost drive's part alone would need one latch per fingerprint, where story 337's E41 keeps one fact. No test of its own is owed: it composes story 337's single latch (E41), which story 338 does not change, with behaviours that the tests pin: the clear's epoch condition (`theLossClearsNeitherAHaltNorANewerDrivesFact`), its category condition and the latest escalation's latch (`theLossClearsNoFactWhileAProcessingFaultIsLatched`), an escalation read on a newer drive (`aLostBlockOfTheLostStreamFirstReadOnANewerDriveJoinsThatDrivesRun` for the lost stream's counts, `anInputHaltOfTheLostStreamRaisesNothingAndOneOfALaterStreamDoes` for an input halt), and a replaced render-drive halt's fact cleared by the drive already running (story 337's `aReplacedHaltsFactClearsAfterOneFaultFreeSecondOfTheDriveAlreadyRunning`). Never the loss clearing a fact whose latch an escalation read on a newer drive set. Amends E69 (its words) and E159 (when a loss reaches the drain); the words consult 20 corrects elsewhere are named on each bullet. Amended in consult 21 (its first two sentences, which were one, "Where two or more of the cases of E159, E160 and E161 meet at one loss, or losses follow one another, the outcome follows from the rules of those cases", and said nothing of how the other rulings' sentences read where losses follow one another; and its test sentence's words, which said "story 338's tests pin" and named story 337's test without its story).
- **E163** — The clock badge and the heartbeat watchdog follow the audio stream, not the transport. A Stop can close the stream, a Pause can pause it and a Play can open or resume it, but the transport's own close at a Stop and its pause at a Pause are skipped while an instrument insert is anywhere in the mixer (a Virtual Keyboard or a Signal Generator, or another plugin typed as an instrument, for instance, on an insert of any track, return bus or the master, bypassed or on a muted track too; a CLAP plugin does not count, because the host types every CLAP plugin as an effect), so such an insert keeps a running stream running through them, though a take's own cleanup can still close a take's stream, and a Play over a stream whose render drive runs opens no stream and starts no render drive. While the stream's render drive runs with the transport stopped or paused, the badge shows that stream's view as during playback, and the unlock of an external clock it measures raises the unlock notice then; the dim closed view, which shows only while no stream is open, does not show; and the watchdog watches the stream, so a device that stops calling back then is declared lost after the window as during playback (E1, E4), and the transport does not roll over silence. Right as built, because the device is still streaming then, which the insert keeps so that its instrument can be auditioned while the transport is stopped, so the stream's clock and its health matter as during playback, and the closed view's "Lock is not checked while the stream is closed." would be false of a stream whose lock is checked. No Known limit; the owner's clock check 15 shows it. Never the dim closed view while a stream is open. It corrects the words of E9, E119, E144, E145, E150, consult 12's reading, the paused-stream Known limit, Accepted as built and the owner's clock checks.
- **E164** — The clock badge reads the clock source again when something it is told of can have changed it, a stream's open, an Apply or Restart Audio Engine, or a source chosen in Audio Settings, for instance; a Play that opens no stream asks no read. So a source switched in a vendor's own application, with no reset request, stays on the badge until such a read, and while an instrument insert keeps the stream running, through any number of Plays. Right as built, because that switch tells the host nothing, and a Play from Stop reads the source only through the open it makes when no instrument insert keeps the stream running; choosing the source in Audio Settings, as the owner's clock check 3 says, has it read in both cases, and a take that records an input opens a new stream, so the source is read again at its start. Recorded in the vendor-switch Known limit, whose words it corrects.
- **E165** — While an instrument insert keeps the stream running through the transport's Stop and Pause (E163), a Stop and Play or a Pause and Play starts no render drive, so an engine fault latched on that stream, whose fault-free second needs a drive started after the latch, or a stream opened after an input halt (story 337's E41, E59 and E87, as E154 amends them), stays on the Engine cell after its failures stop, until a successful Restart Audio Engine, a fault-free second on a drive that something else starts (an applied change in Audio Settings reopens the stream, and a take that records an input opens a new one, for instance), or a later loss that meets ruling E69's conditions, unless a later escalation replaces it. A render-drive halt's own close shuts the stream with an instrument insert too, so after it the next Play can open a new stream. Right as built, because the fault belongs to a stream that is still running and that nothing has restarted, as it does in a session with no instrument insert for as long as one stream keeps playing, and a Stop and Play is not the fault's remedy in either, only a gesture that can start a newer drive. Recorded as a Known limit, because a fault whose action is Open Log Folder (a processing fault's, for instance) offers no Restart Audio Engine of its own until Restart Audio Engine is a menu command (future story 460).
- **E166** — A close of the stream soon after a clock-source switch keeps the dim closed view of the source on the badge, unless the driver's lock report at the close names another source, which hides it (E144). So the badge shows nothing when it had read the new source while the report still named the source left, the usual result, because the report trails the switch (consult 12's reading); the new source's closed view when the report had followed too; and the closed view of the source left when the close came before the badge's read of the new source answered while the report still named the source left. That last view goes at the switch's own read when the switch returns after the badge has seen the stream closed and no stream opens before that read answers, whether or not the driver took the switch, because with no stream open on the interface that read finds no clock source (E169), unless the driver is loaded all the same as the read runs and gives that read its clock sources, as during an arm's check of an input's channel count: the view stays when they name the clock on show (E81), as they can after a switch the driver refused; a read asked before the badge has seen the close of a measured stream and answered after it is void (F3's reading G2), and the view then stays until a later read of the kind E164 names, the next Play's among them, which shows it as the starting view until that read answers. Right as built, because a closed view claims only the source the driver's lock report last named for the stream that ran, with no lock checked, and the next open reads the source again; never a measured, bright or UNLOCKED view while the stream stays closed. The owner's clock check 12 shows it.
- **E167** — A clock source chosen in Audio Settings is sent by a worker of the dialog that first waits for the dialog's other audio work and for its device enumeration to give way, and the dialog's close interrupts that worker: a choice still waiting then is not sent, with no notice, and the interface keeps its source. Accepted as built and recorded as a Known limit, because what the person sees stays true: the badge keeps naming the source the driver kept, and the dialog's Clock source list is set again from the driver's list at its next open while a stream runs on the interface (with none running it lists no source, E169). The Clock source list stays unavailable from the choice until the switch has been sent and answered, so the owner's clock checks wait for it before closing the dialog. Amended by E171 (future story 477).
- **E168** — A driver that asks to be reset after its rate was changed in its own control panel gets the reopen of any reset request: the request carries no rate, so the stream is reopened at the engine's rate, and no ASIO open sets or reads the device's rate. The badge, which names the rate the engine opened the stream at, keeps its label when the stream reopens on that device: dim while the stream is closed and starting, then measured; it does not hide and does not name the rate the panel set. The badge's part is right as built, because the engine's rate is the only one the host knows, and E142 hides the label and shows the new rate once the engine opens the stream at another rate. That the engine runs on at its own rate while the device can run at the rate its panel set is not right for what the person hears and records (48 kHz audio clocked out at 44.1 kHz plays about 8 % slow and about 1.5 semitones flat, and a take captured at 44.1 kHz can be written as 48 kHz), and it is not story 338's to build: story 218 asks that a sample-rate change keep the project's rate, with a conversion at the device boundary and a notification, and the ASIO reset path never reaches that branch. Future story 474. Recorded as a Known limit; the owner's clock check 10 shows what the interface does.
- **E169** — On the ASIO interface the application loads the driver for the lifetime of a stream (story 310), and the ASIO backend answers a capability query made with no driver loaded as if the interface had no capability of its own, among them no clock source, no accepted sample rate, the default buffer sizes and no control panel, and refuses a sample-rate change. So with no stream open on that interface, a read of its clock answers no source, and an Apply in Audio Settings that reconfigures the engine (Worker Pool Size or the buffer size, for instance) hides the badge's dim closed view, whatever it changed; and a clock source cannot be chosen, and a change of rate is refused with "Sample rate … Hz was rejected by the audio driver.", so E81's change of source or rate while the stream is closed is not reached on that interface. The badge's part is right as built, because E81 keeps a label at such a read only when the read proves it names the clock on show, and with no driver loaded none can; a hidden badge claims nothing, and the next stream's read on the interface shows the clock again. `ClockBadgeDuringATakeTest.anApplyThatChangesNothingAfterAStreamOnTheConfiguredDeviceKeepsTheDimView` and `theControllersApplyOfAnUnchangedRequestKeepsTheDimView`, which keep the dim view at such an Apply, run on the mock backend, which answers a clock source with no stream open. That with no stream open Audio Settings shows none of the driver's own clock sources, buffer sizes and sample rates, keeps its control panel unavailable, replaces a saved buffer size that is not one of the defaults with 256, the size also used until one is saved, which the ASIO backend then refuses at every open on a driver that offers none of the defaults, and says that the driver rejected a rate the driver was not asked about, is not right for the person at the desk, who sets up a session with the transport stopped, and it is not story 338's to build: future story 475. Recorded as a Known limit; the owner's checks set the interface's clock source, rate and buffer size during playback, and clock check 11 shows the hide. Amends E81.
- **E170** — No ASIO open sets or reads the interface's sample rate (E168), so a stream opens with the engine at its own rate, at the start of a session the saved one (96 kHz until one is saved), on an interface that can run at another, the rate another application or the interface's own software left it at, for instance; the engine renders and writes takes at its rate while the interface clocks the audio at its own. The clock badge then names the engine's rate; its part is right as built, for the reason E168 gives. That the stream runs at the engine's rate on an interface clocked at another is not right for what the person hears and records (audio the engine renders at 96 kHz plays at half speed and an octave low on an interface that runs at 48 kHz, and a take captured at 48 kHz is written as 96 kHz), and it is not story 338's to build: future story 476. Recorded as a Known limit; the owner's checks set the interface's rate to 48 kHz during playback before they begin.
- **E171** — A clock source chosen in Audio Settings and still waiting to be sent when the dialog closes is not sent, with no notice (E167), and a switch that the driver refuses, or that fails, after the dialog has closed has its notice in that closed dialog, where nobody sees it, and in the log. That is not right for the person at the desk, who closes the dialog expecting the interface to run on the source chosen and can then record on the source left, and it is not story 338's to change (story 338 did not change the Settings dialog): future story 477. Recorded in E167's Known limit. Amends E167, whose acceptance stands for story 338.
- **E172** — In a take recorded with loop recording the stop warning names the lap that holds the input's last delivered frame, lap 1 included: "Track 'T': input from 'D' stopped delivering audio at m:ss.s into lap N of the take; the track records silence from there.", m:ss.s being that frame's position in that lap's own file. A take without loop recording, a punch take included, keeps E14's sentence, and "delivered no audio" carries no lap. Amends E14 and E51 for a loop take. A stop the seal writes for the input's silence that did not reach the take after the last block kept from its ring has ruling E284's words (E303), and a stop marked by the block with which the input delivers again after its dropped silence has ruling E302's words.
- **E173** — A clock mark is never dated before the take's start-frame nor after the frame sampled at its evidence; evidence before the take's first sampled frame is dated at the start-frame. An unlock declared before the take starts recording and still on show at the badge's first measuring look of the take is marked `clock=unlocked` at the start-frame, once (amended by E222). Amends E62 and E40's dating; E123's limit stands. Amended by E177 (its first sentence), E221 (a relock is never dated before its evidence) and E222 (its second sentence).
- **E174** — A degradation the log's listener could not receive is counted: one Shed-kind entry "N Shed entries were not logged: the log fell behind", which never raises the xrun count; a lost restore or `DoNothing` counts nothing; nothing is added on the audio thread that allocates, locks or blocks. Amends E42. Amended by E178, E181 (its words), E184 and E199 (one entry per run; the "not logged" words; "the lost entries handed to the log" becomes "the lost markings handed to the log").
- **E175** — From the DEVICE_LOST delivery each input meter reads the floor until the first block a stream opened after the loss delivers to its input; no block of the lost stream shows a level, a clip in it still latches, and nothing of it enters the new stream's first block. Settles E64 for a late block. Amended by E185.
- **E176** — The `input-stopped` mark of a delivered end exactly on a wrap is the loop end, however many laps the block spanned. Amends E89. Its last clause narrowed by E188.
- **E177** — The cut-off is when the mark's evidence was seen: evidence before the reach sample (the take's first sample at or past the start-frame), and unknown evidence declared before it, are dated at the start-frame; after the reach, any sampled frame of the loop range dates evidence, one below the start-frame included. Amends E173's first sentence and E62's dating. Amended by E183.
- **E178** — Every degradation the enforcer published while it was installed is logged or counted, the count written at the replacement; a degradation evaluated in the block in flight after the enforcer's close goes unlogged, an accepted limit. Amends E174. Amended by E184. Its accepted limit replaced by E203.
- **E179** — In a loop-plus-punch take the re-entry flag's stop is the punch-in before the first wrap and the later of the punch-in and the loop start after one; the resume is raised to the stop only when both lie in the same lap, and across a wrap stays the clock frame, raised to that lap's region start. Applies E88, E89 and E115. Amended by E186.
- **E180** — A gap's extent is every frame the transport passed from its start to the next block's start, followed across every wrap, and it counts whole when any of those frames lies in the punch region. Applies E86 (a) and E104 to a gap a lap long or longer. Amended by E182.
- **E181** — No production code applies a degradation policy, so an entry reports only that the track went over the CPU budget: the kind "Budget"; for every policy "Track 'T': over the CPU budget (averaging NN % of the block time); its processing was not reduced"; "N CPU budget entries were not logged: the log fell behind"; the toast "Track 'T' went over the CPU budget; its processing was not reduced" and the ⚠ tooltip "Track went over the CPU budget; its processing was not reduced". Applying the policy is future story 480. Amends E20's Shed kind word, E42 and E174's words. Amended by E193 (withdraws its known limit for the master CPU budget's description) and E199 (its "not logged" words).
- **E182** — A gap counts when any frame the transport actually passed lies in the punch region; it passed the whole loop range only when its frames from the loop start on number a lap, and pre-roll frames never count toward the lap. Amends E180.
- **E183** — A take anchored at a punch-in keeps E177's reach; for every other take the first sample is the reach. With Record pressed while the transport rolls, evidence from before the first sample is dated at a start-frame up to the preparation time before the take's first recorded frame, an accepted limit. Amends E177.
- **E184** — The listener hands its count over once it has delivered every entry queued when the latest counted loss happened, or at its completion; the log writes one entry at the window's next read, dated then. Amends E174 and E178. Amended by E197 (its hand-over sentence). Qualified by E192, which E197 withdraws.
- **E185** — Every stream start puts each input meter at the floor, its clip latch kept, and clears its true-peak history before the stream's first block; a stream a Reconnect closes at once does not end the loss for the meters. The one-block dip when the loss arrives after the new drive was built, the level of the last block of a closed input beside the stream shown until the new stream's first block, and a clip in a block the closed-at-once stream rendered are accepted limits. Amends E175. Amended by E196 (its (b), the last block of a closed input beside the stream). Its (a) accepted limit, the one-block dip, replaced by E202.
- **E186** — The stop's lap is the lap of the input's own cursor at the flagging block, the resume's the transport's lap there; the resume is raised to the stop only when the two are the same. A stop in lap 1's pre-roll whose resume lies in a later lap has a resume frame larger than the stop frame although the loop wrapped, an accepted limit. Amends E179 and E89. Its (2) widened by E191.
- **E187** — An unlock announced again when the lost device returns adds nothing to the rescued take, which has left RECORDING; as intended.
- **E188** — For a last delivered block that ran across the loop end, the warning names the lap whose file holds the block, while the `input-stopped` stop lies in the next lap, where that lap's file begins; both mark the same instant; as intended. Narrows E176's last clause.
- **E189** — When the take's first counted render-thread block is the clocked block of an input beside the stream that has already delivered a block, a wrap between the start gate's opening and it is not counted and every lap stamp of the take is one low; an accepted limit. Amended by E198.
- **E190** — An overflow of the stream's own ring a lap long or longer can hide a wrap from the lanes and the per-source count, and a stop flagged after it takes its region start from the lap the lanes counted; an accepted limit.
- **E191** — In a loop take a resume frame larger than the stop frame does not rule out a wrap, with or without a punch region. Widens E186 (2)'s amendment of E89.
- **E192** — If the audio thread is held between the publisher's decision to drop a degradation and the listener counting it, for as long as the listener takes to deliver its whole buffer, the count waits for the next event the enforcer offers or for the completion; an accepted limit. Qualifies E184's hand-over timing. Withdrawn by E197.
- **E193** — The master CPU budget's description is true today and stays true after story 480; it drops "ceiling" and "cap", because nothing caps the CPU: "Master CPU budget per audio block; while the tracks together average more than this fraction of the block time, tracks are marked over budget, highest CPU first, as each track's CPU Budget dialog describes. At 1.0 they are marked only when together they average longer than the block time." Withdraws E181's known limit for this text.
- **E194** — One note under the CPU Budget dialog's policy choice follows its value, set from the initial value and again on every change: for `BypassExpensive`, `ReduceOversampling` and `SubstituteSimpleKernel` "This policy is not applied: when the track goes over this budget, or the master CPU budget in Audio Settings marks it, its processing is not reduced; it gets a ⚠ on its mixer strip, a notification and a Budget entry in the Xrun Log."; for `DoNothing`, for which the enforcer publishes nothing, "With DoNothing, going over budget changes and shows nothing: the track's processing is not reduced, and there is no ⚠ on its mixer strip, no notification and no Xrun Log entry." The labels stay as they are; future story 480 makes the first note say what the policy does.
- **E195** — The insert's context-menu item "Expensive (eligible for bypass)" in `InsertEffectRack` is false, because nothing bypasses an insert for its CPU budget, and reads "Expensive (no CPU budget policy bypasses it)"; the ⚠ tooltip, the toast, the log's "Budget" kind and its over-budget detail, "CPU Budget…" and the dialog's title and header are true and unchanged.
- **E196** — The only late block left is one whose delivery outlasts the close's bounded wait, a hung input driver: an armed track's input meter can show that block's level until its next block, and the clip LED can light, which the person clears with a click; an accepted limit. E185 (a)'s one-block dip stays as its own sentence, credited to E185. Amends E185 (b). Its accepted limit replaced by E201.
- **E197** — The count is handed over once the listener has delivered every event queued for it ahead of the latest counted drop: at that delivery, at the window's next read of the log when that drop was counted only after it, or at the completion, whichever comes first. A read of the log that hands a count over writes it in that same read's entry, dated then. Amends E184's hand-over sentence; E184's other sentences and E181's words stand. Withdraws E192.
- **E198** — E189's case is counted: a wrap between the start gate's beat and the take's first counted block is stamped on that block. The case left is a seek accepted between two callbacks of the take's first render period: the later callback's ring starts at the seek's target while the count started from the start gate's beat, so a wrap between the gate's opening and that period is in every lap stamp of the take but not in that input's own laps; it matters only when Record is pressed while a loop rolls and the transport wraps before that period; nothing in the application shows it, and in the `take.manifest` of a loop-plus-punch take an input beside the stream can have its `input-stopped` resume frame raised to its stop across a wrap, or left below its stop within one lap; an accepted limit. Amends E189.
- **E199** — A marking writes a Budget entry only when its track had no CPU budget event (a marking or a restore) in the 30 seconds before it, or when the log was cleared since that track's last entry; a marking that does not writes nothing and adds no "×n". Times are when the listener receives the events. The "not logged" count still counts only the markings the publisher dropped, never one the listener suppresses, and reads "N CPU budget markings never reached the log: it fell behind" ("1 CPU budget marking never reached the log: it fell behind" for one). The listener keeps a track only while that track's last event is less than 30 seconds old; a lost restore can at worst cause one extra entry; a new enforcer starts with no runs. The Budget entry's own words, the xrun count, the toast and the enforcer are unchanged. Amends E42 and E174 (one entry per run, not one for each delivered degradation) and the "not logged" words of E174 and E181; E174's "the lost entries handed to the log" becomes "the lost markings handed to the log"; the hand-over of E184 and E197 is unchanged. Amended by E200.
- **E200** — A track gets a Budget entry at its first marking, at its first marking after any 30-second stretch with no CPU budget event for it, and at its first marking after each Clear of the log; while its events keep coming less than 30 seconds apart, it gets no further entry. A restore that finds no held run for its track makes the next marking of that track write one entry. A track that stays marked past a Clear and is never marked again gets no entry; the mark on its strip shows it. Amends E199: withdraws its line "It gets a new entry once it has gone 30 seconds with no CPU budget event, or after the person clears the log" and its bullet "A track that was stuck marked for minutes and then cycles is also suppressed".
- **E201** — A clip in a block of a closed stream does not light the clip LED, whether the meters refuse the block or it completes into the closed stream's state after a newer stream was built. E175 (2) is unchanged for blocks of the lost stream measured before a newer stream is built: they show no level, and a clip in them latches. The latch carried into a new stream is the one the meter held at that build (E185). A late clip never relights an LED the person cleared after it. Replaces E196's accepted limit; E175 (2) stands. Extended by E205 (from the moment its close stops waiting, a delivery left behind shows on no input meter, with or without a newer stream), which also amends its warning's words.
- **E202** — A loss delivered after a newer stream was built changes nothing on the meters: no floor, no dip, no mute. A loss of the newest built stream still floors and holds every meter until a newer build (E175), and so does a stream a Reconnect closes at once (E185 (c)). Replaces E185 (a)'s accepted limit.
- **E203** — Each listener applies its own enforcer's runs (E199 and E200): a marking in the old enforcer's last block is logged under the old listener's run rule, or counted if it was dropped, and the new listener starts with no runs. Two entries for one track, one from each enforcer, are acceptable, with no suppression. The old enforcer's last-block marking gets no toast and no mark on the strip, because the old UI binding is closed at once. Replaces E178's accepted limit.
- **E204** — No Budget entry, toast or mark may come from a block measured against any block time other than that of the stream that rendered it; a defect in the same seam that predates the round, fixed in it.
- **E205** — From the moment the close of the input devices beside a stream stops waiting for a block one of them is still delivering, the input meters take nothing more from those devices, before any newer stream is built and after: that delivery shows no level, lights no clip LED and sets or clears no routing flag, on the stopped stream's meters or, through the latch and flag a new stream takes over (E185), on the next stream's. The stopped stream's meters keep what they showed at that moment, as after any stop; what the delivery measured before that moment was measured with the capture plan's routing and stays, a clip in it carried into the next stream (E185). The close's warning ends "the input meters show nothing more from it". Extends E201; amends E201's warning.
- **E206** — During an audio take, the blocks an input thread could not build or publish (the Java Sound capture thread, the ASIO input drain, the PortAudio adapter's drain, on the stream's own input or on one beside it) raise the take's blocks lost and its dropouts by exactly that many, with no figure or words of their own; the Xruns count does not change. Amends E45. (Consult 41.)
- **E207** — When an input's halt count grows during a take, the take's blocks lost and dropouts rise by 0, and so does the block the failing read was reading: the halt is its own event, with its stop frame, warning and ERROR. So does a block that carried only the silence after an input's declared stop, dropped by a full ring or left behind or dropped at the Stop (rulings E265, E266 and E277), unless a kept block with channels of that input follows it, as after a reopened stream, which lines and counts it (ruling E302). (Consult 41; consult 45's addition to it fell with E265; its last sentence consult 51's, its last clause consult 54's.)
- **E208** — The take's blocks lost count each block the engine's capture subscriber for that input did not receive: a block only another subscriber dropped adds 0, one the engine's dropped adds 1, one both dropped adds 1; another subscriber's drops reach no surface the person reads. (Consult 41.)
- **E209** — A loss before the rings on the stream's own input can count twice, as that loss and as the starved block that filled its place: a recorded limit with its requirement for future story 463, never resolved by counting the loss as 0. Amends E105. (Consult 41; E105's words as E254 gave them.)
- **E210** — No text the person reads changes: the cell, the dialog's Take line, the tooltip and the spoken text stay as they are; input losses write no xrun-log entry and never raise the Xruns count; take.manifest gains no line or key and no version. (Consult 41.)
- **E211** — Failed device callbacks during a take: (i) on an input opened beside the stream each counts one block lost; (ii) on the stream's own device, while its input is open for the take, one counts one block lost when that period's captured input never reached the input ring, none when it did, one where the code cannot tell, and none for an output-only stream; the Xruns count still counts the silenced output period. (ii) built in this round (E254). (Consult 41.)
- **E212** — PortAudio's own status flags and input frames cut from an oversized host period are feature-sized: future story 481, with a Known limit now. (Consult 41.)
- **E213** — Blocks a late callback publishes after the final sweep count in the take's blocks lost exactly as a ring-overflow gap does, the "Last take" figures including them once the seal has run, and ring overflows that no block the take applied follows get their gap= line, join overflow-blocks and count; built in this round, as far as the seal's last look, at the person's Stop, for blocks that carried audio a track records (rulings E240, E242, E256, E263 to E265, E275 and E277). (Consult 41; its end as consults 43, 45, 48 and 51 left it.)
- **E214** — Requirement: while a take records, B (its blocks lost) never decreases. A backend's counter report may fail (it throws or answers nothing). That failure never erases blocks already counted and never counts, apart from the race in the Known limits, losses from before the take or from an earlier stream: when it fails at the read made as the take starts recording, that backend counts from its first good read in the take, and when it fails later in the take, the next good read counts every block lost since the take's last good read (rulings E261 and E262). A stream reopened on the same backend after a failed close counts from its own open. (Consult 41; these words consult 45's, with consult 49's "apart from the race in the Known limits"; its fact check found that the race needs no failed report.)
- **E215** — A block a closing input drain still holds after its bounded wait is discarded, never published to the next stream's subscribers. Built for the PortAudio adapter and the ASIO shim, for a failed ASIO open, and in the fix round for a failed adapter open. (Consult 41.)
- **E216** — One look gives one notice: unlocked now and locked on show, E31's unlock notice; locked now and unlocked on show, E110's relock notice at the information level; locked now and locked on show, with an unlock replayed or a gap, one warning "External clock on <name> (<source>) was lost and has locked again. Check the clock cable and the clock master." ("was lost <n> times", and "was lost <n> or more times" for a gap), with E108's endings during a take and none without one; unlocked now and unlocked on show, no notice. Extends E31 and E108. (Consult 41; confirmed by consult 42.)
- **E217** — The take gets one clock=unlocked and one clock=locked for each replayed unlock and relock, in sequence order, dated by E221. (Consult 41.)
- **E218** — More changes missed than the status keeps (a gap): the notice says "<n> or more", n the unlocks kept plus one; the take is marked unlocked from the newest frame sampled at or before the later of the evidence of the status last applied for that serial and 3 s before the look that applied it, at the start-frame when that time is before the reach or older than every kept sample, and at a serial's first measuring look, unless the take is already marked unlocked. (Consult 41; its date corrected by consult 42.)
- **E219** — At a serial's first measuring look every transition of that serial is replayed under E216 to E218 and E221, unless a loss or a close consumed it; a serial never measured stays dropped. Amends E61. (Consult 41.)
- **E220** — A loss or a close consumes, silently, every transition of that serial up to the look that next measures it, which applies the level rule only; a reopen is a new serial whose own transitions replay; a measurement that ended before the badge looked is not owed. At the first look of a loss or a close, the forgotten serial is the one last applied (rulings E259 and E268). Confirms E74, E81, E84 and E187. (Consult 41; consult 42's reading of it withdrawn by consult 47.)
- **E221** — A mark brackets its evidence: an unlock and a resync at the newest frame sampled at or before it, a relock at the oldest sampled at or after it, held for such a sample or the Stop when none exists yet (as consult 42 accepted); the newest sample at or before any pending evidence is kept while the take records, or the start-frame used; marks are ordered by evidence time, never by frame. A relock and the unlock after it with no frame sampled between their evidence times write neither mark when the unlock is handed over before the frame that would date the relock is sampled; when that frame was already sampled, the relock is written there and the unlock at the last frame before the stretch, and read in line order the take stays marked unlocked across them. Never an unlock or a resync dated after the driver's message call of the request it stands for, however long the freeze or the standstill: an unlock declared from resync requests carries the arrival of the first request of its first bad poll interval, or that interval's start, and each resync its own arrival, or, past the arrivals kept between two of the xrun feed's reads, the last arrival kept before it; the newest sample at or before any arrival the feed still holds or the journal still owes the take is kept, however long it is carried (rulings E123, E270, E279, E291 and E292). A resync inside a freeze is early by up to the freeze, a Known limit. Amends E62 and E173. (Consult 41; its cancel sentence from consult 42, its "never after its evidence" from consult 47, that sentence as consults 51 and 52 replaced it.)
- **E222** — An audio take's clock marks start from the lock at the take's reach. An unlock that is on show at the badge's first measuring look of the take, or replayed there with its evidence before the reach, is marked clock=unlocked at the start-frame, once. Its relock is dated by E221, so a relock whose evidence is before the reach is clock=locked at the start-frame too, an empty region; an unlock whose relock the badge applied before the take's first measuring look writes nothing. Amends E173's second sentence. (Consult 42, replacing consult 41's X1.)
- **E223** — During an audio take the combined warning ends " Recording continues; the take is marked where it was lost." when the take is marked unlocked after the look has handed over its unlocks, and " Recording continues." when it holds no unlock mark for them; a MIDI-only take always gets " Recording continues.", and no take no ending. (Consult 41; corrected by consult 42.)
- **E224** — If the display pulse stops while the window is minimized, the bracket rule widens a region to the whole stretch: accepted, a Known limit. (Consult 41; its Known-limit sentence accepted by consult 42.)
- **E225** — During an audio take every resync the Xruns count counts writes exactly one clock=resync, whatever the log's 100-entry limit dropped; the log still shows the newest 100. (Consult 41.)
- **E226** — The log's Clear empties the log and restarts the count, and never removes the take mark of a resync that arrived before it. (Consult 41.)
- **E227** — Every driver control-panel session that ends while the stream runs makes the monitor read the clock source again, however short the session: the first poll that sends anything once a poll has found the close counted at its first read of the count reads it (E249). (Consult 41; made precise by consults 43, 44 and 49.)
- **E228** — From the end of a driver control-panel session the stall verdict is withheld until max(5 s, the E1 window) with no advance, with no notice and no mark of the panel itself: a clock or rate change made in the panel is the person's switch. Extends E107. (Consult 41.)
- **E229** — A clock event seen only after the take stopped recording, and after an early seal (E280), is not marked in it: two Known limits, and future story 482 with the requirement "A clock unlock, relock or resync whose evidence lies inside an audio take's recording span is written to that take's manifest before it is sealed, even when declared or first seen after the take stopped recording." (Consult 41.)
- **E230** — Two inputs halted in one display frame give "2 errors" naming the newest input only: accepted as built, a Known limit. (Consult 41; its quotes from consult 42.)
- **E231** — E73 covers a carried resync's take mark: it is dropped only at a stall, a close, a pause or a reopen, and E73's words gain it. (Consult 41.)
- **E232** — The flash stays: while the badge is UNLOCKED and motion is allowed it steps every 0.5 s between its rest look and its flash look, with no fade, transition or interpolation, at opacity 1.0 in both and with the same words and `-danger` fill under every glyph; the flash look adds a band inside the badge's bounds whose outermost 1 px differs from both transport-bar stops by at least 3:1 and which holds a continuous 1 px ring at least 3:1 against `-danger`, in Onyx Refined, Studio Slate and Atelier; under Reduce Motion, the rest look, steady. The 0.5 s step is the house cadence, not an interface's. (Consult 41.)
- **E233** — In every theme, at every UI scale from 90 % to 150 % and in both phases the badge's words reach at least 4.5:1 against the fill directly under them, no glyph pixel sits on a ring colour, and the words are identical in both phases; the outline's 2 px is no "larger badge" in its sense. (Consult 41; consult 42.)
- **E234** — The four views where the lock is not checked (closed, starting, checking, not measurable) are an outlined chip with no fill, its 1 px outline at least 3:1 against both bar stops and its words at least 4.5:1; the locked view keeps its filled chip with no visible outline; a switch between a not-checked view and the locked one with the same words changes neither the badge's width nor its height. This is the "dim" of E81, E100 and E119. (Consult 41; consult 42.)
- **E235** — The Engine cell's warning text and every Xruns warning text reach at least 4.5:1 against the strip in every theme, in the single warning amber and distinguishable from the critical red: deferred, with the engineer's Known-limit sentence, to future story 483. (Consult 41; consult 42.)
- **E236** — The CPU Budget dialog's policy note reaches at least 4.5:1 against the dialog in every theme; it already did, in the dialog pane's label style. (Consult 41; its premise corrected by the specification and accepted by consult 42.)
- **E237** — The older dims and flashes go to a later story whose requirement is that every text carrying a state reaches 4.5:1 against what is directly under it, in every theme, in every phase of a flash and every dimmed state, disabled controls excepted, verified per theme on resolved colours: future story 483. (Consult 41; consult 42.)
- **E238** — The Known-limit sentence for E211 (ii) while it was unbuilt; withdrawn by E254. (Consult 42, its E216.)
- **E239** — What future story 484 owed for E211 (ii): no new words and no manifest line, the whole recording span in a punch take, and a test of the case with a backlog in the input queue; its (a) to (c) became this round's requirements (E254), its (d) moot. (Consult 42, its E217.)
- **E240** — At the person's Stop, every gap= line the seal writes for blocks left behind or dropped that no block the take applied follows counts in B by its own extent (E104); a block that carried only the silence after an input's declared stop gets no line and no count, and every block left behind or dropped after an early seal, or behind a device loss's final sweep, gets no line and no count (rulings E263, E264, E265, E274, E275 and E277). (Consult 42, its E218; its words with consult 46's E265 clause removed, as consult 48 left them.)
- **E241** — The Known limit for ring overflows after the final sweep while they were unbuilt; superseded by E255. (Consult 42, its E219.)
- **E242** — Per ring, the gap= line for the drops that no block the take applied from that ring follows starts at the end of the last block the take applied from it plus the blocks left behind on it, carries those drops' count, the drops the final sweep's own look found among them included, and has the existing ring-overflow warning; overflow-blocks gains those drops; a block that carried only the silence after an input's declared stop gets no line (rulings E275 and E277); no new sentence. (Consult 42, its E220.)
- **E243** — The wording of E45 and E105 while E211 (ii) was unbuilt; void once E254 built it. (Consult 42, its E221.)
- **E244** — "A block its input thread could not build or publish", in the Known limit and in future story 463. (Consult 42, its E222.)
- **E245** — From the take's Stop the cell reads "Last take dropouts K", never "Take dropouts K"; K never grows with losses after the Stop and never falls below what the cell showed for that take, also when the count read at the Stop throws; after a throw at the take's start the cell keeps the previous take's "Last take" figures. As built, the take's own file counts can still rise with its seal after the Stop. (Consult 42, its E223.)
- **E246** — A block an input thread could not build or publish after its own stream was closed counts nowhere. (Consult 42, its E224.)
- **E247** — Future story 481 adds no sentence: a Gap entry for paOutputUnderflow reuses "Device played silence (output buffer empty)". (Consult 42, its E225.)
