- **E248** — An interval whose tick read the driver's control panel open is a switch interval: its resyncs are counted, logged and marked but never a bad answer, so while the panel is open the monitor judges nothing. Extends E122 and E138 to a panel session. (Consult 43.)
- **E249** — After a session of the driver's control panel closes, the intervals of the first two polls that read the panel closed, find that session counted at their first read of the closed-session count, and are not stopped by their second reads are switch intervals: SWITCH_INTERVALS, which is 2, the same number as for the driver's answer to the application's own switch (E137); no new constant. A poll judges nothing, and is never one of the two, when it reads the panel open before it sends anything (E248) or after its answer and evidence, or when its second read of the count finds a session its first read did not (E260); a session that begins before the second of the two has judged gets its own two once it closes. E227's re-read stands: the first poll that sends anything once a poll has found the close counted at its first read of the count reads the source again; a reading that a poll's second reads stop is set aside and read again by the next poll that sends anything. The count is raised before the level falls, and a tick reads the level before the count. (Consult 43; corrected by consults 44 and 49; "the next poll that sends anything" is the fact check of consult 49's, for consult 49's "the next".) Extended to the stream that answers a driver's reset request by E271.
- **E250** — While the panel is open the badge keeps what it last measured, and a real loss of the clock is reported from the first bad answer to a poll the monitor sends after the close, dated at the first bad answer of its run, or at the measurement's start when no good answer of its measurement came before it (E269): a Known limit. (Consult 43; corrected by consults 44 and 49.)
- **E251** — An unlock declared before the panel opened stands across the session; after the close the first poll sets a new baseline and three good polls relock, with E110's notice, the take staying marked unlocked across the session. (Consult 43; corrected by consult 44.)
- **E252** — Each loss and each close forgets at most one serial, the one the badge last applied before it: at the first lost look of a loss, and at the first look that sees the stream closed after an open look, the serial of the status last applied, whatever status that look read: the status a look applies is read before that look's read of the loss, so the serial last applied is that of a stream open before the loss or the close began, while a status read at the first lost or closing look can be that of a stream opened since, whose own transitions are replayed when the badge first measures it (rulings E259 and E268); later looks of the same loss or close forget nothing more. (Consult 43; corrected by consults 44, 47 and 49.)
- **E253** — The line order of the clock lines in take.manifest, as the Resolution's "A take's dropouts and its clock marks" gives it. (Consult 43; corrected by consults 47 and 51.)
- **E254** — E211 (ii) is built in this round; E238 is withdrawn; the Known limit on losses before the rings is replaced (Known limits); E45 and E105 take their words above. A failed callback after its period's input reached the ring counts none on either backend; on PortAudio a failure of the wake of its drain thread is contained and is no failed callback (E272). (Consult 43; that Known limit's second sentence from consult 45; the sentence on a failed callback after the ring, and the Known limit it named, the engineer's acceptance read of this round's, that sentence as consult 50 rewrote it; the Known limit on PortAudio's wake, closed by E272.)
- **E255** — A block a callback drops into a full capture ring after the seal last read that ring's overflows is a Known limit with no future story: closing it would need the seal to wait on a callback that may never return. (Consult 43.)
- **E256** — At the person's Stop, late overflows, the drops no applied block follows and the blocks left behind (E240), except blocks that carried only the silence after an input's declared stop (E277), have a gap= line at E242's position; each counts whole only when its own extent reaches into the punch region (E86 (a), E104, E180, E182), and always when there is no region; E211 (ii)'s blocks carry no frame and count over the whole recording span. (Consult 43; its first bullet with consult 46's E265 clause removed; corrected by consult 51.)
- **E257** — A `VirtualMachineError` while an audio take starts recording owes a Known limit and no new words. (Consult 43.)
- **E258** — A start mark counts as an unlock under E221's cancel sentence: when it finds a relock held, neither line is written and the take stays marked unlocked. (Consult 44.)
- **E259** — A close consumes only the measurement of the stream that closed, the serial the badge last applied, in every interleaving of a close, a reopen and the badge's looks. (Consult 44.)
- **E260** — For a stream that stays open across a session of the driver's control panel, the intervals of the first two polls that read the panel closed, find the session counted at their first read of the closed-session count, and are not stopped by their second reads are switch intervals, whatever the phase of the close against a poll; so resyncs are judged again no earlier than the poll after them, and the exemption after a close lasts more than 1 s and up to about 2 s, longer when the monitor's driver calls or its thread are slow (rulings E249 and E260). (Consult 44; these words consult 49's, after the fix round's second reads.) Extended to the stream that answers a driver's reset request by E271, whose window is counted as this one is.
- **E261** — A block a backend lost before the take started recording never appears in the take's figures, apart from the race in the Known limits; when its report fails at the read made as the take starts, that backend counts from its first good read in the take, and what it loses before that read is not counted, a Known limit. (Consult 45, with its fact check; the race clause consult 49's.)
- **E262** — When a report fails mid-take and recovers, the first good read counts every block lost since the take's last good read, once; the health record's freeze of a failing source stays story 337's, a Known limit. (Consult 45, with its fact check; the Known limit's words the engineer's acceptance read of this round's, with two clauses as a check against the code corrected them.)
- **E263** — After a take is sealed early, its figures and take.manifest describe the take up to the early seal: from each ring's last look before the early seal on, a drop gets no gap= line, no place in overflow-blocks, no warning and no count. Its outcome as E274 gives it; no Known limit. (Consult 45; corrected by consult 46; its outcome and words consult 48's, its Resolution sentence and outcome as consult 51 replaced them.)
- **E264** — At a device-loss seal a block a ring receives or drops after the final sweep's look is part of the loss: no gap= line, no overflow-blocks, no warning and no count; drops of audio the final sweep's look finds with no applied block after them lie before the dropout, lined at the end of the last block the take applied and counted by their own extent, and a run an applied block follows is judged as any ring overflow (rulings E264 and E275). (Consult 45; corrected by consult 51.)
- **E265** — At the Stop, a block a ring is left holding or drops after the final sweep counts with its gap= line unless it carried only the silence after an input's declared stop, which gets no line and no count; an input whose silence never reached the take gets its input-stopped line at the seal. Built (rulings E276 and E277). (Consult 45; corrected by consults 46 and 51; consult 48 had given the backlog requirement and Known limit of future story 484, which this round built.)
- **E266** — During the take, a block a full ring drops that carried only the silence after an input's declared stop gets no line and no count, known from the stamp on the block after it, unless that block is a kept block with channels of the input, which only a reopened stream gives: then it is lined and counted with its run (ruling E302). Built (ruling E277). (Consult 45; corrected by consults 46 and 51; its last clause consult 54's.)
- **E267** — Never used.
- **E268** — Each loss, like each close, consumes at its first look only the serial the badge last applied, whatever status that look read, in every interleaving; so a status a look applies is read before that look's read of the loss. (Consult 47; its reason corrected by its fact check; the write-up sentence consult 49's, its sentence on the stopped stream corrected by the engineer's acceptance read of this round.)
- **E269** — An unlock that is its measurement's first decision, with no good poll answer of that measurement before its unlocking run, is dated no later than the measurement's start (the stream's start, or the first source read the monitor applies that names a newly chosen source or finds a change of whether the source can be measured); an unlock after a good answer keeps E62's date. Two Known limits: a newly chosen source's read set aside, and a first decision handed to the take more than about 4 s after its measurement began; an unlock handed over after an early seal is E229's Known limit (E280). Amends E62. (Consult 47; the parenthesis, the change of whether the source can be measured and the Known limits consult 49's, with its fact check.) Unchanged by E271: an unlock that is the first decision of the stream that answers a driver's reset request is dated at that stream's start, and consult 50 extended the Known limit on a first decision handed to the take late to that stream.
- **E270** — Several resyncs of one interval are dated each at its own arrival in the take's clock=resync lines, the monitor's unlock evidence at the first, and an xrun-log entry at its first: built in this round (ruling E279). Amends E123's words; corrects E221's and E253's. (Consult 47; built as consult 51 ruled.)
- **E271** — The stream that answers a driver's reset request (the reopen the engine makes for it; acceptably also, when no stream was open, the engine made no such reopen, that reopen failed before the backend's own open succeeded, or another open of the same backend came before it, the next stream the same backend opens) starts with its first two polls as switch intervals, counted as E260 counts them, whatever caused the request and whatever the order of the panel's close, the request and the closed stream's polls; a bad poll answer in an interval with no resync is still judged, and an unlock that is its first decision keeps E269's date at the stream's start. No other stream's start gets the window. With a panel open at the request, the first stream the backend opens after the panel has closed answers it: the waiting reopen's, or a Play's that comes before it (E278, E289). Closes the Known limit on a panel change that the driver answers with a reset request (the engineer's acceptance read). Extends E249 and E260. (Consult 50; its parenthesis as the engineer's acceptance read of the round's last change set corrected it against the code, with "when no stream was open, the engine made no such reopen, that reopen failed before the backend's own open succeeded, or another open of the same backend came before it" for that read's "when no stream was open or that reopen failed" as a further check against the code corrected it (the Resolution's E271 item says why), which the engineer's ruling on it accepted: the window goes to the first stream the backend opens and starts after the request, whichever open that is, and a later reopen gets none.)
- **E272** — On PortAudio a failure of the callback adapter's wake of its drain thread is contained in the adapter: the period's output plays as rendered, nothing is counted (the take's dropouts, the callback faults, the Xruns number) and nothing is reported (no notice, no xrun-log entry), and the period's block, already in the input ring, reaches the engine at the drain's next wake, the next period's or its own 20 ms backstop, whichever is first; a render block that later delivery starves counts as a starved block. Closes the engineer's acceptance read's Known limit on PortAudio's wake of its drain thread (E254). (Consult 50; its note beside the question judged the same outcome right on ASIO, where the round's last change set contains the wake too. A block a full input ring refused was counted as the ring's overflow before the wake, as before.)
- **E273** — Each run of blocks a capture ring drops between two blocks it delivers gets one gap= line at the end of the block the ring delivered before it, counting the run's blocks that carried audio a track records, written once the block after the run is applied; in a punch take the run counts, all its lined blocks, exactly when the frames it would have held, to the start of the next delivered block or by its own extent when none follows, reach into the region. overflow-blocks holds exactly the dropped blocks that have a line; a pass rewrites the manifest for its lines at most once and gives at most one ring-overflow warning, for its first run, whose total is the take's overflow-blocks; a dropped input-starved block counts as E281 says; a next block at or before the line is E285's; a silence-before-audio run is E287's. Closes consult 49's Known limit on a line placed at the end of a pass. (Consult 51; its last three clauses consult 52's.)
- **E274** — After an early seal a run of dropped blocks keeps its line, its place in overflow-blocks, its warning and its count exactly when a block the take kept from that ring follows it (applied in full before the decision: written, or passed over outside the punch region; not the block whose write failed, not the block the headroom floor discarded); every other drop and every block a ring discards or is left holding gets nothing; in a device-loss seal's final sweep, whichever ring the early seal comes on (E286). Gives E263's outcome; closes both of E263's Known limits. (Consult 51; its clause on the final sweep consult 52's.)
- **E275** — Drops no applied block follows: at the person's Stop those that carried audio join E242's late-drops line and count by its own extent, whichever look found them; at a device-loss seal not sealed early in its final sweep (ruling E286) those the final sweep's look finds are lined at the end of the last block applied and counted by their own extent, and what comes after that look is the loss's; after an early seal, nothing. Amends E213, E240, E242, E256 and E264. (Consult 51; its clause on the final sweep consult 52's.)
- **E276** — Once the render has passed the stream's own input's first zero-width block after its declared stop, a block that input's queue yields is discarded unrendered: not heard, metered or recorded, no gap= line, no count anywhere; blocks queued before the declaration are rendered first (E47). (Consult 51; consult 52 gave what its test must show.)
- **E277** — A block dropped, left behind or dropped at the Stop that carried only the silence after an input's declared stop gets no line, no overflow-blocks, no warning and no count, whether or not the take holds the input-stopped line; a run in which the input stopped is lined for its audio-carrying blocks; when none of an input's silence reached the take before a Stop's or a device loss's seal, the seal writes the input-stopped line the take would have written had that silence reached it (E282, E283), its warning in E284's words. Replaces both exceptions of E265 and E266; builds them. (Consult 51; its clause on the seal's line consult 52's. Amended by consult 54: by E302, since its premise that every delivered block of an input precedes its silence holds within one pump, not within one take, and by E303, whose "for the input's silence that did not reach the take after the last block kept from its ring" replaces its "when none of an input's silence reached the take".)
- **E278** — A driver's reset request whose reopen finds the driver's control panel open is answered by a reopen once the panel has closed, never by a device loss: while it waits the engine stays reconfiguring, with no stall, loss or Reconnect, the badge in its closed view and the window usable; requests during the wait join that one reopen at the latest request's format; the reopened stream answers the request (E271); a reopen that still fails declares the loss (E113); a loss declared meanwhile stands and nothing reopens; closing the application ends the wait. An Apply or a Restart before the reopen ends the wait (E288); a Play after the close is restored as it left the stream (E289); a later reset with no format reopens at the engine's format (E290). A take recording at the request goes on across the wait and the reopen as across any reset reopen (E41; future story 464). Closes the round's Known limit on a reset request while the panel is open. (Consult 51; its sentence on E288 to E290 consult 52's.)
- **E279** — E270 (a) is built as written; between two reads of the xrun feed, which reads every tick and carries them across a standstill, the backend keeps the arrivals of the first 256 requests, but one whose call is under way after a stretch of more than 256; a request not kept is dated at the last arrival kept before it; the monitor dates a bad interval at its first request's arrival, or at the interval's start when that arrival was not kept; no date is later than the driver's call that brought the request (E292). Closes E270's Known limit and future story 485. (Consult 51; its bound's words as consult 52 corrected them.)
- **E280** — From the capture thread's early seal until the coordinator stops the take for it, the take counts as no longer recording for clock marks and notices: an unlock's notice has no take sentence and nothing is marked, a relock is neither written nor held, a resync is logged and counted but not marked; a notice never claims a line the manifest lacks. Marks seen only after the seal are E229's Known limit (future story 482). Closes the Not-built item on the early seal's window. (Consult 51; the Resolution's sentence on the order of the reads consult 52's.)
- **E281** — A dropped input-starved block counts, with its gap= line, exactly when an applied starved block in its place would: its starved run ends with the input delivering again, or at the person's Stop or an early seal; when the run continues into the input's declared stop, or at a device-loss seal, it gets nothing; its run's one line waits for that; across a delivered block the ring dropped before the input's declared stop, the starved blocks the take received are the stop's (E294); a delivery the stop fence cut whole, claimed and never published, ends the starved run as a published or dropped one does: the input delivered it (consult 54). Settles E273's and E277's definitions; applies E49 and E90. The declared stop takes a dropped starved block only when it flags every physical track of the input (E301). (Consult 52; its clause on a dropped delivered block consult 53's; its clause on a delivery the stop fence cut whole consult 54's, documents only; amended by E301.)
- **E282** — The input-stopped line the seal writes when none of an input's silence reached the take is the line the take would have written had that silence reached it: judged by E77, E88, E176, E179 and E186 with the held block's header, or the last received block's region, loop window and legacy range over the dropped blocks' own extent; no line when the input delivered to punch-out or the take ended before the silence reached the region. Extended by E296 and E297 to silence that starts at or after punch-out of an input that ended at or before punch-in. (Consult 52.)
- **E283** — At the seal only the first silence block that would have flagged the track is read for the line; a later one held after dropped silence is not, and the resume is then the stop; a held block's lap counts the wraps among the blocks the take did not receive before it; the warning's lap is E172's (E293). Applied in a loop take by E297. (Consult 52; its clause on the warning's lap consult 53's.)
- **E284** — The capture thread's warnings after the person's Stop reach the Notifications history and are shown after the take's report (an error report stays); after a device loss, the history only; a seal-written stop says "the track holds no audio from there" / "the track holds no audio". Amends E14 and E172 for that case. "After a device loss" is a take the loss ended (E299). (Consult 52; read by consult 53.)
- **E285** — A run whose next delivered block starts at or before its line with no loop wrap between them counts, all its lined blocks, when its blocks' own extent forward from its line or back from that block's start reaches into the punch region. Amends E273 and E104 for that case. (Consult 52.)
- **E286** — An early seal in a device-loss seal's final sweep, on any ring, makes E274 govern every ring: drops no kept block follows get nothing and no seal-written stop line, whichever ring the sweep took first. Amends E275. (Consult 52.)
- **E287** — A run in which a silence-only block came before an audio-carrying one (an instrument insert removed and added while the ring was full) is lined at its line for its audio-carrying blocks and judged over the whole run's frames. Amends E277's own-extent rule for that order. (Consult 52.)
- **E288** — An Apply or a Restart Audio Engine that reaches the controller before the waiting reopen has run ends the wait: the person's settings stand, applied as on the stream the request stopped; while the panel is open its open is refused as any open then, and the engine rests stopped; a reopen that ran first is followed by the Apply. (Consult 52. Extended by E304: the Apply or the Restart also ends a request still in its 250 ms window, whether or not a reopen waits.)
- **E289** — A Play that opens the stream after the panel has closed and before the waiting reopen runs is restored as it left the stream, at the request's format; its stream takes E271's window. (Consult 52.)
- **E290** — A request that joins a waiting reopen replaces the one before it whole: a reset request with no format reopens at the engine's format, as two requests within 250 ms do with no panel. (Consult 52.)
- **E291** — A resync carried through a standstill of any length is marked at the newest frame sampled at or before its arrival: the frame history keeps the newest sample at or before the oldest arrival the feed holds or the journal owes the take. Applies E221. (Consult 52, which left it to the manager whether the same floor also takes the clock-lock monitor's evidence for a decision not yet made; the manager did not build that, and E269's Known limit on a first decision handed to the take late stands.)
- **E292** — No resync date is later than the driver's message call that brought the request; the first 256 between two feed reads are kept but one whose call is under way after a stretch of more than 256; an entry the feed cannot date is dated at its read before the one that counted it. (Consult 52.)
- **E293** — A seal-written stop's warning in a loop-record take names the lap holding the last delivered frame the take received, at that frame's position in that lap's own file (E172), however many delivered blocks the take did not receive across a wrap after it: those have their own gap= line and warning, and a lap none of whose blocks reached the take has no file in it; a held block's lap moves only the stop's frame (E283). Withdraws consult 52's "the warning names lap 2". (Consult 53.)
- **E294** — An input-starved stretch the take received ends only at a delivered block the take received; a dropped stretch ends at any delivery the producer's stamp shows (E281); they differ across a delivered block the ring dropped before the input's declared stop (Known limit). Amends E281's "exactly when an applied starved block in its place would" for that case. (Consult 53. Its Known limit's "reported under the stop" is that of a stop that flags every physical track of the input, as E301 bounds a stop's part.)
- **E295** — A block left behind at the person's Stop that the render passed input-starved counts with its gap= line (E265): it ends its starved run at the Stop with no declared stop after it, as E281 counts a dropped one; only the callback in flight can publish behind the final sweep, at most one block per ring, so nothing of that input follows it. (Consult 53; "at most one block per ring" consult 54's sharper words, from its check of consult 53's premise against the code.)
- **E296** — In a punch take whose input ended at or before punch-in, silence of that input none of which reached the take, whose first block starts at or after punch-out with no loop end before it, flags each of the input's tracks whose file holds frames at the region's first frame, as an applied block there does (E77, E88), in E284's words; the starved run before it is the stop's (E49). Adds the case to E282. (Consult 53, confirming the correction of the ring change set's second critic as built.)
- **E297** — In a loop take, silence none of which reached the take, of an input that ended at or before punch-in, whose first block the transport played at or after punch-out, flags, before the re-entry rule is considered, each of the input's tracks whose file in that block's lap holds frames, at the region's first frame in that lap, the resume equal (E283: the first silence block that would have flagged the track); only the other tracks are judged by the re-entry rule (E88, E179). Where the block was played and which file is asked are E300's. (Consult 53; amended by consult 54.)
- **E298** — An input none of whose blocks reached the take holds no frame on any track; a stop the seal writes for it stands at the take's start frame with E284's "delivered no audio; the track holds no audio". A seal that writes stops does not meet it: a ring drops only when full, and the take receives every block the ring published before its drops, in an earlier pass or in the final sweep, unless an early seal came first, and an early seal writes no stop (E274, E286). (Consult 53; its second sentence as consult 54 corrected it against the code, since blocks are received in earlier passes too, not only in the final sweep.)
- **E299** — E284's "after a device loss" is a take the loss ended (a rescue, or a count-in the loss cut short); a take the person had stopped, still being written when a device loss comes, gives its report and then its seal's warnings on the bar as any stopped take, the loss notice kept in the Notifications history with its Reconnect action where the loss gives one (a stall, or a reset whose reopen failed; a removal's notice has none). (Consult 53; its ending as consult 54 corrected it against the code, which found the loss notice in the same bar and history and kept there, and a removal's notice with no action.)
- **E300** — E297's test is made on the start where the transport played the silence's first block, after the wraps before it (E283), and asks for frames of the file that block's lap writes. With no loop recording that is the take's one file: silence first played at or after punch-out in a later lap flags each of the input's tracks whose file holds frames at the region's first frame in that lap, the resume equal, the starved run before it the stop's (E49). With loop recording a lap after the last block of that input the take received has no file, so that silence gives no stop and the kept starved run is written and counted as input-silence. Blocks of that lap the ring dropped change neither: they wrote no frame. Replaces E297's "before the loop end". (Consult 54.)
- **E301** — An input-starved block the ring dropped after the input's last delivery is the declared stop's only when that stop, by its own line or an earlier flag, flags every physical track of the input (E49). Applied, such a run waits from the stop's block until a later block of the input flags them all (the stop's, no count), the input delivers again, or the take is sealed. At the person's Stop's seal and at an early seal a run no stop covers is lined where its run begins and counted by its own extent, as a starved run with no declared stop after it (E281, E295), once for the input whichever of its tracks are flagged. At a device-loss seal it stays the loss's (E281, E286; Known limit). Restores, for that case, the line and count HEAD gave every drop. (Consult 54.)
- **E302** — An input that delivers again after its declared stop in the same take, which only a reopened stream gives (E276 holds within one pump), may have had that stop's silence dropped by a full ring: the block with which it delivers again (a kept block with channels after drops holding its silence) first gives the input's tracks the input-stopped line the seal gives such silence (E282), judged by the state of the block before it, warned as "… stopped delivering audio at m:ss.s into the take." or "… delivered no audio before it stopped.", and then lines every block the ring dropped since the last kept block, the silence among them, at the end of that block, counted as any run a kept block follows (E273, E285), the input's waiting runs ending counted (E281): the audio after them sits early in the file by that many blocks. Silence no kept block of that input follows stays E277's; the seal places a dropped silence before the audio its stamp shows after it. Amends E277's premise that every delivered block of an input precedes its silence. (Consult 54.)
- **E303** — Silence of an input the take kept that flagged none of its tracks does not stop the seal marking that input's later silence the ring dropped or left behind: the seal judges the silence dropped since the last kept block, and the blocks left behind, as E282 and E283 do whether or not silence was kept; a track already flagged gets nothing again. Amends E277's "when none of an input's silence reached the take" to "for the input's silence that did not reach the take after the last block kept from its ring". (Consult 54.)
- **E304** — An Apply of Audio Settings or a Restart Audio Engine also ends a driver's format-change request still in its 250 ms window, whether or not a reopen waits for a driver's control panel and whether or not a panel is open: the person's settings stand and nothing reopens for the request. With a panel open the Apply's open is refused as in E288, the engine rests stopped at the person's format, and the panel's close brings no reopen and no "Audio engine reconfigured"; with none, the Apply's own reopen re-initialises the driver. A driver's rate change so ended is not announced. A request that arrives after the Apply has taken up the requests still in their window is answered as before; an Apply the record guard declined ends nothing. Extends E288. (Consult 54; its "has taken up the requests still in their window" the engineer's acceptance read's, for its "has begun". As built, a finding of the last fix pass: every `applyConfiguration` call that `beginConfigurationChange()` lets through ends a request still in its window, also the start-up apply of the saved settings (`MainController.applyStartupAudioSettings`) and the Settings dialog's rollback after a failed apply (`SettingsDialog.restoreAudioRuntime`), so a request that arrived during a failed Settings apply and had not yet been taken up is ended by the rollback.)

**Readings accepted by the engineer.** R3's seven readings (consult 10, all accepted, no ruling): R1, ruling E142 decided from the device and rate at the open, so a ladder that lands on another device hides the badge at the open ("W/C 48k" on a 44.1 kHz stream is false, and the hidden gap lasts one read); R2, during a switch only the resyncs are exempt and an exempt interval counts as neither good nor bad (a resync is never good evidence); R3, an answer that lands while a poll is inside its driver call makes that poll the first window poll, judging nothing (it keeps E137's bound); R4, a resync belongs to the poll that judges it (the monitor sees resyncs only as a counter read once per poll); R5, a Settings or reconfigure read that fails while no stream is open hides the closed view (after an Apply nothing proves the old clock still holds); R6, the stream's own read with the device unknown may only confirm a label (it cannot prove it is about the stream that ran); R7, E139's sentence as a Java literal at the warning level through the same path as the other failed-Reconnect notice. The R1/R6 limit the engineer first asked to record ("a stream that closed before its device was read keeps the configured device's label") no longer exists, because the device of a stream is never unknown after F3 (consult 11, G1). F3's readings G1 (a stream that opened and closed before the badge looked is read about its own device, and E136 settles the closed view) and G2 (a Settings read in flight when a measured stream closes is void) were accepted, G2 with E144's exception. Consult 12 accepted, within E144, that a close of the stream within about a second of a clock-source switch, once the badge has read the new source, hides the badge because the driver's lock report trails the switch (a close before that read answers can keep the dim view of the source left, ruling E166); a Stop can close the stream, but while an instrument insert in the mixer keeps it running nothing closes, and the badge follows the running stream (ruling E163). The manager's reading of rulings E77 and E15 for the engineer's acceptance read: an input whose delivered end is at or before punch-in, declared stopped only after the take passed punch-out, is flagged from the punch-in frame when the track's file holds frames, because otherwise its silence in the region would be an unflagged, counted tail (`TakeMarksInPunchAndLoopTakesTest.anInputThatEndsBeforePunchInAndIsDeclaredAfterPunchOutIsFlaggedFromPunchIn`; E118 later confirmed it).

**Decision records.** The manager's design decisions (sections 0 to 5) and their amendments (A1 to A16), under the numbers the code comments cite ("amendment A5", "amendment A6", "amendment A10", "amendment A11", "amendment A13", "amendment A14", "amendment A15"), each as built; then the decisions the manager took as the review rounds went on.

- **0** — Story 338 lands before 339 (production already showed the controller's notices through MainController's sink); the OS device-change watcher and story 123's overload notice with its 60-second test become future stories; the limits of this section are recorded under Known limits.
- **1.1** — `AsioFormatChangeShim`'s selectors become the SDK enum, pinned by a test of the eight numbers; a live defect fix.
- **1.2** — The overload message is answered with ASE_OK and counted on the backend (an `AtomicLong` on `AsioBackend`); nothing is published.
- **1.3** — The resync request is handled in place with no downcall, lock or allocation on the driver thread: a count on the backend that the clock monitor and the xrun feed read.
- **1.4** — `AsioBufferSwitchShim` keeps single-writer `volatile long` counters on the device thread (starved periods once primed, input-ring overflows); the S1 fix pass added the refused and skipped output blocks.
- **1.5** — `BackendDropoutCounters` and the default `AudioBackend.dropoutCounters()`, monotonic for the backend's life; `inputBlocksDropped` counts only blocks not already reported as lost input blocks, and never a block a publisher dropped for one subscriber (amended by ruling E208).
- **1.6** — The native export `asioshim_getSamplePosition`, resolved by `AsioCapabilityShim` as an optional symbol; only the owner can build and run it.
- **1.7** — `AsioClockLockMonitor` in daw-sdk, owned by `AsioBackend`: a daemon thread per open, stopped by a flag and a generation bump and never interrupted, paused for a driver reset, never taking the driver lifecycle lock, unit-testable through an injected probe, clock and publisher. Its "lock state for the backend's lifetime" was amended by ruling E82 (fresh per stream), its invalidation after a selection by R3's `switchSent` and `switchAnswered`, and it now also publishes the `ClockLockStatus` level (R1).
- **1.8** — `MockAudioBackend.close()` no longer closes its clock publisher.
- **1.9** — The Javadoc of `AudioBackend.clockLockEvents()`, `ClockLockEvent` and `AsioCapabilityShim`'s export list made true (no `ASIOFuture(kAsioGetExternalClockLocked)`).
- **2.1** — `XrunDetector`: one real-time `recordTick`, an off-RT `publishPending` capped at 16 events, `reportDropped` and `reportGraphOverload` off-RT, a borrowing constructor, the Javadoc corrected.
- **2.2** — The pump ticks around `processBlock` only when it returned `true`, reading the detector once from `AudioEngine.setXrunDetector`.
- **2.3** — `theRenderDriveLoopIsGuardClean` follows into `XrunDetector` and must reach `recordTick`; two allow-list entries, fourteen invariants, both skill copies.
- **2.4** — The pump's handler logs, then calls `halt(failure)` in a catch-all; one method named `halt`.
- **2.5** — `CallbackBackendAdapter` counts starved periods once primed and its rings' overflows, and overrides `dropoutCounters()`; a block its drain loop's publisher drops is told to the subscriber it was dropped for and counted nowhere in the adapter (amended by ruling E208; it said "and its drain loop's drops").
- **2.6** — `AudioEngine.dropoutCounters()` and the detector's setter and getter, lock-free.
- **3.1** — Delivery counts at the top of both input subscribers' `onNext`, and an immutable volatile snapshot of the open stream's inputs.
- **3.2** — `AudioEngine.declareInputStalled(handle, kind)`, lock-free, idempotent, on the handle's own object.
- **3.3** — Story 337's input-halt treatment without touching a backend: one engine-owned counter source (`InputStallLedger`), its fault stored before its count, in `BACKEND_COUNTER_WRITERS`.
- **3.4** — `AudioEngine.isRecordingCaptureActive()`, a lock-free fact for the watchdog and the Reconnect copy.
- **3.5** — The take's losses before the rings: the blocks the engine's capture subscribers lost (the pump's queue drops and a publisher's drops for either subscriber), the dropped input blocks of the stream's backend and of every input opened beside it, the captured blocks the stream's own device lost in a failed device callback, and the input blocks the backends' own threads lost as the health record counts them (amended by rulings E206, E208 and E211), through one engine accessor (`capturedInputBlocksLost()`), baselined by the engine's read made as the take starts recording (`capturedInputBlocksLostAsATakeStarts()`, ruling E261); the starved blocks, which the decision also routed through that accessor, are counted from the take's own slots instead (amendment A6; `RecordingPipeline.getStarvedInputBlocks`).
- **3.6** — `take.manifest` gains new keys only; an older manifest reads as before; every reader and round-trip test swept.
- **3.7** — The per-track warnings of ruling E14, the position formatted m:ss.s; the routing-mismatch sentence stays for a real mismatch.
- **4.1** — `EngineHeartbeatWatchdog`: pure logic, the five-read sample order, the re-baselines, E1's window and E2's allowance, the latch only on acceptance, the input rules.
- **4.2** — The "daw-engine-health-monitor" scheduler: normal priority, 100 ms fixed delay, started only by `startEngineHealthMonitor()`, its tick guarded, stopped with `shutdown()` and never `shutdownNow()`.
- **4.3** — The stall entry re-checks under the controller's monitor and runs the removal's steps through one shared method with the stall's copy and Reconnect. Its clause "`pendingFormatChange` null" was unsound against the code (the field stays set after any driver format change) and was built as "no reopen scheduled and not yet done".
- **4.4** — The loss cause travels in `EngineStateChange` through the bridge, the transport and the record coordinator, with compatibility overloads.
- **4.5** — `NotificationManager.notify(message, actionLabel, action)` as a default method; MainController's sink shows the toast with its action.
- **4.6** — Reconnect on a virtual thread, sharing its reopen with the device's arrival; amended by rework R2 (`startAudioOutputOwned`, one at a time) and the fix round (`AudioEngine.reopenAndPark`).
- **4.7** — RECONFIGURING published after the permit and before the stream stops; a declined record guard publishes nothing.
- **4.8** — The controller's own clock-lock publisher re-bound with the device watch, and a stream-opened callback for the badge. The badge no longer subscribes to the publisher (R1); the callback only asks for an early look (F3).
- **4.9** — `XrunFeed` and `XrunJournal`, ticked by the monitor; one controller-owned xrun publisher that survives detector rebuilds; the count resets when the detector changes and on Clear.
- **4.10** — The dying stream's held device-side counts are dropped when the loss reaches the drain, and from then on no device-side count read on the lost drive or an older one joins a sustained run, as rulings E68 to E70 and E157 say; what the drain reads before that, or on a newer drive, it judges as any count (ruling E159).
- **5.1** — `EngineHealthStatus`, an FX model held by MainController; `SessionStatusStrip`'s third constructor, the two others delegating with a model that is never fed.
- **5.2** — The feeds: the bridge's first listener in the same FX turn, the drain's fault bound and unbound beside the drain, one pulse participant for the xruns and the take's figures.
- **5.3** — The cells, built in a helper beside the skin; `StatusStripCell`'s action and focus ring; the Engine cell never in the overflow; the Xruns cell's threshold between Workspace's and Schema's; copy through a text reader.
- **5.4** — `XrunLogDialog extends DawgDialog<Void>`: non-modal, one per window, the live list, Clear in the body, closed with `secondary(...)`.
- **5.5** — `ClockStatusIndicator` mounted once beside the transport status label, tokens instead of inline hex, the flash suppressed under Reduce Motion, accessible text, no pause handler. Its event subscription and generation-guarded refresh were replaced by R1's level read and R3's redesign.
- **5.6** — The record side: the stall lead, the take's dropout figures and their retention, the clock marks, and the transport and meters after a loss.
- **5.7** — MainController wiring no test can run goes through package-private factories that tests call, plus source pins in `FailureSpineWiringSourceTest`.
- **A1** — Deferred, each with its rulings as requirement: E41's reset during a take, E25's take-frame marks (the counts are built), E58, the OS device watcher, story 123's overload notice and its 60-second test.
- **A2** — The resync no longer publishes a format-change request and never stops or reopens the stream; it is counted on the backend.
- **A3** — Every input loss site counts: `AudioBackendSupport`'s drop handler, the ASIO input ring, and the PortAudio adapter's drain loop. Amended by rulings E206, E208 and E211: the one shared drop handler tells the subscriber a block was dropped for, and the engine's subscribers count their own; the counted sites are the ASIO and PortAudio input rings, the blocks an input thread could not build or publish, and a failed device callback that lost its period's input, of the stream's own device and of an input beside the stream.
- **A4** — `ClockLockEvent` gains `evidenceNanos` with a compatibility constructor.
- **A5** — One Shed entry per `TrackDegraded` by applied policy; `DoNothing` and `TrackRestored` write nothing. Amended by rulings E174, E181, E199 and E200: the entry, shown as "Budget", says only that the track went over its CPU budget, a track gets one per run of markings, and lost markings are counted.
- **A6** — The starved-block stamp: the pump sets an engine flag around `processBlock` when it polled no captured block, the stream's own capture callback copies it into the slot, and the flush thread derives the stop frame, the runs, the tail and the count from the slots.
- **A7** — Blocks already queued at a declared end are rendered before the zero-width view takes over (ruling E47).
- **A8** — The manifest keys `input-stopped`, `input-silence` and `clock`, in transport frames at the take's rate.
- **A9** — The warnings of rulings E14, E51 and E54, told apart by a per-track "routed a delivered block" flag on the flush thread.
- **A10** — A never-delivering input is declared through the same entry but raises no input halt; "a take" for the watchdog is the engine's recording-callback slot being occupied, count-in included.
- **A11** — Clock marks: the coordinator samples (time, transport frame) on each FX pulse into a short history while a take records, and a mark uses the newest sample at or before the event's evidence. Amended by ruling E221: a relock uses the oldest sample at or after its evidence, held for one when none exists yet.
- **A12** — After a loss: the removal and arrival copy say "stopped", the input meters are silenced, a playback-only loss stops the transport in place, and the playhead is restored after a rescue.
- **A13** — Reconnect opens only the lost device, says "it is not available" for another one, reads whether a take was rescued on the FX thread when the person clicks, and does not reset the xrun count.
- **A14** — The drain holds device-side escalation while the heartbeat has not advanced, and `streamLost(lostDriveEpoch)`, with the epoch the controller read as it declared the loss, drops the held counts, gates the readings of that epoch and older ones, and clears a fact latched for such a drive when every category latched since it last cleared is device-side; the drain stays within 900 lines by moving nested types out.
- **A15** — The xrun number: callback, render and sink faults, discarded output blocks, starved output periods, driver overloads and driver resyncs, under the feed's gates.
- **A16** — The slice order S1, S2a, S2b, S3, S4a, S4b.
- **Redesign, not another patch** — After three reviews in a row had each found a missing row in the badge's state table, each made by the fix before it, the manager traced the cause to the badge's inner design (four places chose a state, and the state on show fed the next decision) and had it redone (R3) as a model of facts with one pure decision and a table test over every row, instead of patching it a fourth time.
- **One more round after R3's confirmation** — The manager had said that another must-fix in the badge would stop the work and go to the owner. R3's confirmation found two, but judged the design right and both defects small and outside the decision, so the manager took the one fix round the process allows after a confirming review (F3), with its own confirmation.
- **F4, after F3's confirmation** — F3 put the previous stream's record back when a start failed after its open: that put-back was the manager's own decision, written into F3's brief, and it made the serial go backwards. The manager had said a must-fix in F3's confirmation would stop the work and go to the owner; the owner's instruction for the story was to resolve it fully without pausing, and the owner's rule is that a defect found in a review round is fixed in the round, so the manager corrected the one wrong decision at its root in a further round (F4) with its own confirmation, and did not stop. The clock badge's feed therefore took three rounds after the redesign (R3, F3 and F4) and a tests-only round (T1), more than the one fix round the process allows; the owner should know it.
- **T1 without a review of its own** — Round T1 changed tests and one Javadoc sentence only; it had no separate confirming review: the manager read its diff and its mutant logs, and the first of the six gates ran after it.
- **E146's two paths, a future story** — The manager filed the two paths of ruling E146 (a stream started by putting an instrument on a channel while the transport is stopped, or by pressing Record, whose start fails after its open) as future story 472, with the ruling's four Nevers as its constraints; story 338 records them as a Known limit.
- **The acceptance read and the closing round** — The acceptance read (consult 13) answered NOT ACCEPTED, with required changes to words (one tooltip, limits, the owner's checks, one future story's acceptance case) and four rulings, E147 to E150. The manager took them in one closing round: the code part (one message key, its pin and three Javadoc passages), then the gate again, then the documents, then a second fact-check that confirms each required item by quoting it. It confirmed every required item and found five Javadoc passages inexact or stale (the `EngineFault` class Javadoc, `XrunJournal`'s class Javadoc and `restartCount`, `DefaultAudioEngineController.declareDeviceLost` and `RecordCoordinator.onDeviceLost`'s `@param cause`); the manager wrote their corrections from the code and checked them afterwards, they went in as a comments-only step, and the gate then ran a third time. The engineer had written that it accepts once the items marked REQUIRED land as written; the owner's process does not take a conditional for an acceptance, so the manager asked for a second acceptance read instead of relying on it. Consult 14 answered ACCEPTED, with no condition and no new ruling.
- **After consult 14: a sweep by fact, and a fact-check before each gate** — The third fact-check found two inexact clauses and four stale passages in the code's comments, and five false or overtaken sentences of the last document touch; the second Linux run was green. Two corrections one passage at a time had left stale comments, so the manager had the second comments-only step sweep by fact, in twelve files. From then on a fact-check read every comments-only step before the next gate, not after it, so that a gate runs on comments already confirmed: the third gate had run on comments that the third fact-check then found stale. The manager set the fourth fact-check a limit of three reads. Each of the three found a new false statement, and the third reached the limit with three open. The manager passed the limit on purpose, because stopping would have left known false comments: a correction pass by enumeration, every sentence about the engine fault checked against every site that sets or clears it, and two more reads, the last of which confirmed the step.
- **The fourth gate's failed start** — Its second start failed on three daw-core tests outside the story's classes. The manager had the three classes run alone once and the whole gate run once more, and changed no test and no timeout, as the house rule for a failure outside the story's work says.
- **Behaviour questions to the engineer, and fix round L** — The fourth fact-check's fifth read listed three narrow cases near a device loss in which the Engine cell's fault does not do what a ruling says, and the fifth fact-check found that a folded input halt is more than the amended E70's "only a log record". The manager put both to the studio engineer and did not decide them, because they are about what the person at the desk sees and reads. Consult 15 accepted two cases as built and sent the third, story 337's shipped behaviour and feature-sized, to future story 473; a fact-check read the third comments-only step before the fifth gate, and another the document touch of consult 15. Consult 16 ruled three changes to the code, E155 to E157. The manager had them built in this story at once, as fix round L with a review of its own, not deferred, and stopped the Linux run planned after the fifth gate before it began, because the sources were about to change. Round L's implementer had to read E157 as covering the device-side categories only. The manager put that reading to the engineer before the gate (consult 17: yes, as built), not left for the acceptance read, because it is about what the person sees and the gate was to run on the code built on it. A fourth comments-only step corrected the two loose comment sentences the review found.
- **The engineer's own sentence, back to the engineer** — The seventh fact-check's first read found that a loss that clears a latched device-side fault restarts a processing run too, so consult 17's sentence in E157 was false there. The manager put the case back to the engineer (consult 18) and had it reworded neither by the manager nor by a correction agent, because the sentence was the engineer's and the case is about when the person sees an ERROR. Consult 18 accepted it as built (E158). The test it asked for was written at once, with mutants (test round M), and the fact-check agent read it as a reviewer. When the next read found one more window of the same kind, the manager had every ordering at a loss swept before the next consult, so that the engineer ruled the whole category once (consult 19: E159 to E161, all accepted as built, with three tests in test round N) instead of one window per read.
- **The engineer's words verified before they were written** — The third read found 17 sentences false, 16 of them consult 19's own words. Each false sentence found after it was written had cost a consult, a document touch and a read, so from consult 20 on the engineer's words were verified against the code before they went into the documents: replacement blocks, a verifier, revisions until clean. The sixth gate started as soon as that read had confirmed the code, the comments and the tests, and the third Linux run after the gate had ended (its Maven after another session's build had ended), both beside the wording work, because documents are not gated; they ran after round L so that the build of record and the Linux run are of the sources the owner commits.
- **One scope sentence** — When the fourth read found sentences false only with two losses in a row, the manager asked the engineer for one scope sentence (each per-case sentence describes one loss; E162 decides the rest) instead of an exception in every sentence, because exceptions added per ordering do not end.
- **A sweep of each category first** — When consult 20's verifier found, and the engineer agreed, that the documents treated a Stop and Play as a resume of the paused stream, and when the fifth read found two steps of the owner's checks that cannot be done, the manager had each whole category swept first (every sentence about what the transport does to the stream; every step of the owner's checks), then put each to the engineer in one consult (consults 21 and 22), for the same reason as the sweep of the orderings at a loss. Consult 23 then corrected the sentence of E166 that the sixth read found false, and the seventh read confirmed the documents.
- **Older comments, a future story** — The 13 test Javadoc passages that call a fake stream's open and close "Play" and "Stop" stay as they are: each describes its own fixture, which has no instrument insert (consult 21). The older comments in main sources that state or assume what a Stop does to the stream, in files this story changed, are not story 338's words. They are left out of this story and recorded as future story 479, not corrected here, because a comment change after the sixth gate would need a new gate and a new Linux run for words this story did not write.
- **A final acceptance read** — Rulings, Known limits and code changed after consult 14, so the studio engineer read the story and the build for acceptance once more, as they stood: consult 24 answered NOT ACCEPTED, with one required change to clock check 10's words (the check changes the interface's rate and did not set it back before the later clock checks, which name 48 kHz) and no new ruling. A verifier read that sentence against the code before it was written and found one gap, and consult 25 gave its final words. They were applied in a document touch, which a fact-check agent that wrote none of it read and confirmed. Consult 26 then read that change, its fact-check and the hashes and answered ACCEPTED, with no condition and no new ruling. After consult 26's ACCEPTED, two further reads of the last stretch were made: the Java agent's found the code and tests in order and one false clause in the "Skills used" list, corrected here. The studio engineer's, consult 27, found that the story's words would mislead on two things (what a take recorded at a rate mismatch sounds like; the Xruns check's set-back), required three replacement blocks and said that consult 26's ACCEPTED did not stand as given. A verifier read the blocks against the code four times before they were written, consult 28 gave their final words, taking word for word the one change of words that the fourth read gave for R1a, and they were applied in a document touch, which the eleventh fact-check, by an agent that wrote none of it, read and confirmed. Consult 29, the acceptance read after the skill audits, answered NOT ACCEPTED: reading the owner's checks in order, it found two more places where a check leaves behind state that a later check depends on (UI Scale after the status strip check; the interface's own rate before the clock checks), and required two replacement blocks, with no new ruling; they were applied in a document touch, which a fact-check agent that wrote none of it read and confirmed. Consult 30 then read that touch, its fact-check and the hashes and answered ACCEPTED, with no condition and no new ruling.
- **Declined in the rework** — G4: a daw-app test cannot reach the render pump's uncaught-exception handler without reflection across the module boundary, so the pump death's one notice is shown by composition (the handler's halt, and story 337's one notice per halt). G5 (a concurrency smoke test of `XrunJournal` and a pin of the meter-side catch) was optional and not built. Ruling E4's clamp of a take's frames at zero stays: a take's frames cannot be negative.

**Known limits.**
- A halt that throws inside the pump's last-resort handler leaves only the handler's SEVERE record in the log.
- The loss reaction holds the controller's monitor across the pump's bounded join and the backend's close, as the host's removal path already did, and a driver whose close never returns holds it for as long.
- After a backend's own counter report fails (story 337, ruling E78) its device periods read empty, and the watchdog falls back to the render drive's beats, which keep cycling on PortAudio and ASIO: a device stall there is not detected while that backend stays open.
- A narrow race between the verdict's re-check and a Record start that begins at that moment: the Record start can open its stream just after the re-check, and the loss then closes that stream, perhaps a running one. The person sees the stall's notice with Reconnect and "Recording aborted — no take was started: …", and no take is made; the new stream's own announcement, delivered after the loss, then ends the loss with no stream open, so the Engine cell's "Device lost" goes at once and the notice's Reconnect does nothing. The next Play or Record opens the device as usual. Read in the code only; no test drives this race.
- A paused stream is not watched: an unplug while the stream is paused, or parked by a Reconnect, is noticed about 5 s after a later resume of that stream, the start-up allowance of a new render drive. A Play can resume it, and so can a Record that records no audio input (a MIDI-only take, for instance), and until the notice the transport then rolls over silence; a Record with audio inputs does not resume it: past its route checks it closes the paused stream and then tries to open a new one, as any open does. A Pause can pause the stream, but not while an instrument insert in the mixer keeps it running: then the stream is watched as during playback, and a device that stops calling back while the transport is paused or stopped is declared lost after the window, with no rolling over silence (rulings E2, E9, E150 and E163).
- The watchdog notices an unplug only when the driver stops calling back. A driver that keeps calling back after its device has gone, delivering silence, is never declared lost, and a take can keep recording that silence with no flag, because delivered silence is audio (ruling E53); only the OS device watcher of future story 461 can notice it.
- `CallbackBackendAdapter` forwards no clock facts of its delegate (no lock status and no clock-lock events), so the lock of a clock source behind it is never measured.
- A render drive wedged in a backend's write keeps the old stream from letting go: Reconnect then says "the previous audio stream has not shut down yet" until the write returns (`ReconnectAgainstOtherOpensTest.aReconnectWhileTheStalledStreamHasNotShutDownSaysSoAndALaterOneReopens`).
- A hang inside audio processing reads as "stopped responding" on Java Sound and Mock and shows only as climbing xruns on PortAudio and ASIO (ruling E58; future story 465).
- A driver that asks to be reset during a take (for example after its buffer size or sample rate is changed in its own control panel while recording, or when it resets by itself) has its stream closed and reopened under the running take: nothing seals the take first, whatever reaches the interface from the request until the reopened stream delivers is not in the take, and nothing in take.manifest marks the reopen; when the request comes while the driver's control panel is open, the reopen waits for the panel to close (ruling E278), so that stretch lasts until the person closes it, while the take's transport stands still; the person sees 'Reconfiguring audio engine…', the Engine cell's 'Engine: reconfiguring…' throughout, and then 'Audio engine reconfigured' (ruling E41; future story 464). (Consult 51's words, in PR #994's second review.)
- An input's window is judged over the engine's buffer period, not the input's own, because a capture input's snapshot carries no period of its own; the 2-second floor holds either way, and the two differ only for an input whose period exceeds 250 ms (ruling E12).
- Losses before the rings carry no frame, so take.manifest has no line for them and nothing marks where the take lost them: on an input opened beside the stream, and on the stream's own input when the next captured block took a lost block's place, the audio recorded after the loss can sit one block early against the transport and the other tracks (one buffer period: about 5.3 ms at 256 frames and 48 kHz). They are counted into the take's dropouts over the whole time the take records: in a punch take its pre-roll and the time after punch-out included, and in a take sealed early the time from the early seal until the take stops. One block lost before the rings on the stream's own input, a device callback that lost its period's input included, can count twice, as that loss and as the starved block the render drive filled its place with (rulings E86 (b), E105, E209 and E211 (ii); future story 463). (Ruling E254's sentence, its second sentence consult 45's; it replaces the limit written before PR #994's second review.)
- In a loop take no frame-carrying line names its lap or lane file; `input-stopped` can carry a resume frame smaller than its stop frame across a wrap, and a loop-plus-punch re-entry flag does not say which lap it belongs to (rulings E75, E88 and E89; future story 466).
- A device callback pause shorter than the stall window, with no refused or skipped block, leaves no log entry and no count (future story 467).
- A driver's request to reset restarts the Xruns count, as a Settings apply does, also when its reopen fails and the device is declared lost; the entries logged before it stay in the xrun log (ruling E148).
- The fault hold counts an advance of the render beats, which keep cycling through a device stall on a callback backend, so a device-side fault run that began before the device periods froze (up to about 2 s of it) can escalate to an ERROR before "stopped responding"; at the loss that drive's latch is cleared when ruling E69's conditions hold, so the cell shows the loss with no fault sentence unless a fault escalates after the delivery, and the notice already on screen stays; a failure of the same kind within 30 seconds of that ERROR, of a reopened stream too, raises no warning toast of its own: one that escalates still raises its ERROR, since the window holds back no ERROR (rulings E68, E157, E159 and E161 say which escalate), and any other is counted and accounted for by that kind's next record or, about 30 seconds after the kind's last failure, by a history-only entry that repeats that ERROR at the error level when no other record of that kind came after it (rulings E114 and E160; future story 468). When a processing fault (of the render, a worker, an effect, MIDI or a plugin) has escalated since the fault last cleared, the loss clears nothing: the cell shows "Device lost" with the latched fault's sentence in its tooltip, and once the loss has ended "Engine fault: <words>" of the latest escalation, or of the latest render-drive halt while one is latched, with its action, until a successful Restart Audio Engine or one fault-free second on a drive started after the latch, or after the halted one while a render-drive halt is latched (story 337's E71), with no failure of the render, of the device side or of any latched kind (after an input halt, on a stream opened after it, ruling E154); a processing fault that keeps failing on the reopened stream keeps it on the cell as long. Read in the code only; `theLossClearsNoFactWhileAProcessingFaultIsLatched` drives it (rulings E69, E154 and E161).
- If a stream is opened before the audio health drain first reads the last counts of the lost stream, or of an input closed with it, or if the stream a device loss closes was itself opened after the engine controller read the render-drive epoch at the loss, the drain reads those counts as the newer stream's: it judges every count by the render drive newest when it reads it, never by the stream that produced it. An input halt among them raises an ERROR notice with Restart Audio Engine, and once the loss has ended the Engine cell shows 'Engine fault: audio input' over a working device; the loss does not clear it, and neither does a resume of that stream, which a Pause and Play can give (ruling E154). When that ERROR comes before the loss reaches the drain, a device-side fault already latched for the lost drive is not cleared at the loss either: it stays latched with the newer one, its fingerprint escalated, until the fact clears (ruling E162). Any other device-side count among them (a lost input block, or a callback, sink, pacing or device failure) that the drain reads after the loss reached it begins or joins a sustained run of its kind, so two more seconds in a row of failures of that kind, of the stream running then, can raise its ERROR, for example 'Audio device fault — 3 errors from “<name>” since <time>: <reason>. Audio may stay silent or broken until you restart the audio engine.', with Restart Audio Engine, one second sooner than that stream's own three, and 'Engine fault: <words>' on the Engine cell once the loss has ended. The halt's fault goes at a successful Restart Audio Engine or after one fault-free second on the next stream opened (a take that records an input opens one, and a Stop and Play can); the sustained one also after one fault-free second on a drive resumed after it (a Pause and Play can resume the stream), though while an input halt has latched since the fault last cleared, that second counts only on a stream opened after that halt was latched (ruling E154). A Stop and Play or a Pause and Play starts no drive while an instrument insert in the mixer keeps the stream running through them. Either fault also goes at a later loss that meets ruling E69's conditions, and gives way on the cell to a later escalation that replaces it (ruling E162). While a render-drive halt latched before either ERROR holds the fact, the Engine cell keeps that halt's fault instead, until a later render-drive halt replaces it, a successful Restart Audio Engine, or one fault-free second on any drive started after the halted one, which can be the stream running at the sustained ERROR, with the same condition for an input halt (ruling E162). Nothing on disk changes. Every display frame that first shows the loss notice has already read what the lost stream recorded, and only the mock backend announces a device's return, so on hardware this needs a stream opened after the loss's close and before the drain's next frame (an instrument insert's open can be that quick), a stream opened or resumed between the controller's epoch read and the close (by a Record start or an instrument insert's open, for instance), or a close deferred because the render drive did not stop in time (wedged in a backend's write or hung in audio processing, for instance) whose retry is followed by an open before the drain's next frame (the next open retries the close first: Reconnect's or an instrument insert's, for instance). Read in the code only; `aLostBlockOfTheLostStreamFirstReadOnANewerDriveJoinsThatDrivesRun` drives the sustained-run case as built (rulings E151 and E159; future story 473).
- The audio health drain learns of a device loss only when the loss reaches it, on the window's FX thread, after the engine controller's steps that close the stream. A count it reads on the lost render drive before then, even after the close, is judged as any count: an input halt raises its ERROR 'Audio device fault — …' with Restart Audio Engine before or beside the loss notice, and a device-side fault run can escalate (ruling E114); the loss then clears that fault when ruling E69's conditions hold, and the notice stays (rulings E69, E70 and E162). After the loss has reached it, a device-side count it reads on the lost render drive or an older one raises no ERROR, no latch and no sustained run, and an input halt, with the input's lost blocks read in its frame, no toast of its own; but any other device-side count whose kind has no latched fault and had no notice in the 30 seconds before, and, for a lost input block, no record of an input halt folded at a loss in those 30 seconds, still shows its warning with Restart Audio Engine beside the loss notice, in its ordinary words, for example 'The audio device “<name>” did not accept a block of output, so it was skipped: <reason>. Audio continues.' (rulings E8 and E160). Nothing on disk changes. An input halt is counted while the device fails, by the watchdog on a live input or by a Java Sound capture thread of an open stream, so the drain reads it before the loss reaches it whenever the close and the loss's delivery outlast one display frame; the warning needs a device-side failure that the lost stream counts in its last moments before its close, since the render pump and the Java Sound capture count nothing their own teardown releases. Read in the code only; `aDeviceSideCountReadOnTheLostDriveAfterTheLossStillShowsItsWarning` drives the warning as built (rulings E159 and E160).
- An input opened beside the stream for a take is closed together with the stream by a device loss, or, when that close is deferred because the render drive did not stop in time (wedged in a backend's write or hung in audio processing, for instance), by its retry. Its own halt that the audio health drain first reads after the loss reached it, on the lost render drive, is therefore counted and logged with the input's other failures, with no notice of its own and no latch. A fault of that input already latched on that drive is cleared at the loss when ruling E69's conditions hold, and its notice stays. A fault that persists shows again when the next take opens that input. Read in the code only (ruling E153).
- After an audio input halts, the Engine cell's fault waits for a stream opened after that halt, whatever the cell shows by then (story 337, ruling E87; ruling E154). So a later fault that replaced it on the cell, a plugin's for instance, stays on the cell after it stops until a successful Restart Audio Engine, one fault-free second on a stream opened after that halt was read, on a drive that the later fault's own rule lets clear it (ruling E154), or a later device loss that meets ruling E69's conditions, which a plugin's fault latched since the fault last cleared rules out, unless another escalation replaces it first. The next stream opened gives such a drive (a take that records an input opens one, and a Stop and Play can, but not while an instrument insert in the mixer keeps the stream running through them); a resume of a stream opened before that halt was read does not (a Pause and Play can give one). Clicking the cell runs that later fault's action. The input's own notice, with Restart Audio Engine, stays in the notification history.
- A stream opened between an input halt and the frame in which the drain first reads it does not count as opened after the halt. The Engine cell keeps the fault although that stream's input works, and so does a render halt's fault when both halts were read in that frame, until a successful Restart Audio Engine or one fault-free second on the next stream opened, or, for the input halt's fault alone, a later device loss that meets ruling E69's conditions, unless a later fault replaces it on the cell first (ruling E154's first Known limit). Read in the code only; no test drives this race (ruling E154; future story 473).
- While an instrument insert in the mixer keeps the stream running through the transport's Stop and Pause, a Stop and Play or a Pause and Play starts no render drive, so an engine fault latched on that stream stays on the Engine cell after its failures stop: until a successful Restart Audio Engine, one fault-free second on a drive that something else starts (an applied change in Audio Settings reopens the stream, and a take that records an input opens a new one, for instance), or a later device loss that meets ruling E69's conditions, unless a later fault replaces it on the cell. A device-side fault's cell runs Restart Audio Engine when clicked; a fault whose action is Open Log Folder, a processing fault's for instance, offers no Restart Audio Engine of its own until Restart Audio Engine is a menu command (future story 460), and for it the ways out include a fault-free second on a newer drive that something else starts: an applied change in Audio Settings, a take that records an input, the driver's own reset request, or removing every instrument insert from the mixer and then pressing Stop and Play, for instance. A render-drive halt's own close shuts the stream with an instrument insert too, so after it the next Play can open a new stream. Read in the code only (ruling E165).
- When the input has had no notice in the 30 seconds before an input halt folded at a loss and no input fault is latched, the halt's own log record opens the input's 30-second window as a notice would, although nothing was shown. A lost input block within those 30 seconds, of the reconnected stream too, then raises no warning toast of its own; one that escalates still raises its ERROR, since the window holds back no ERROR (rulings E68, E157 and E159 say which escalate). When no other record of the input comes between the fold and that entry, a history-only entry reports such blocks about 30 seconds after the input's last failure: 'Input from “<device>” was lost for a block: <the halt's reason>. If you are recording, the take has a gap there. It happened once more after that notice, …', where 'that notice' is the halt's log record, which was never shown. An input halt of a stream opened after the loss still escalates at once, unless the drain reads it in the moment between the first such stream's backend attaching and its render drive starting (ruling E159). Read in the code only (ruling E155).
- At most one block of unmarked silence at the end of a take a write failure sealed, on the tracks whose append of that starved block succeeded (ruling E117; future story 469).
- On the automatic device every loss surface uses the unnamed form; only the clock notices name the driver the backend resolved (ruling E131; future story 470).
- Notices that predate story 338 can still show an engine message or the placeholder device name (future story 471).
- Two actions start the audio stream without waiting for the engine controller: putting an instrument on a channel while the transport is stopped, and pressing Record. If such a start fails after the stream has opened, the clock badge can show the stream's live view (checking, or bright for an internal clock) until the failure lands, and then settles as after any failed start. Nothing in that stretch waits on the driver, so in practice this is at most a flicker beside the 'could not be started' notice. 'UNLOCKED' and an 'External clock lost' notice would need the start to run on for two seconds after the open. No hardware is known to do that, and a review probe produced it only by stretching the start and injecting the status. Even then no take has begun, so nothing is marked and nothing is written to disk. (Ruling E146, the engineer's sentence; future story 472.)
- After a failed start the dim view's tooltip says "when the audio stream last ran" about a stream that opened and never ran; after a change of device whose new stream fails to start, the end state (the new device's dim view, or hidden) depends on which read the driver answers first; no hardware path provokes a start that fails after its open, so the unit tests and the real-wiring tests are the only evidence (ruling E143).
- A look that falls between the engine publishing a stream and that stream reading as open asks the stream's read early, and the stream is read once more when it reads as open; this needs the opening thread to stall between two adjacent statements for the length of a driver read.
- A driver read that never returns is joined by one more read for each later stream or Settings refresh; ruling E141's "never two in flight" holds within one stream.
- The clock badge knows only what the audio driver reports: its sample position and its resync messages. An interface that loses its external clock and carries on on its own clock, while its driver keeps reporting a moving sample position and sends at most one resync message, keeps the badge on the external source with no warning; the loss then shows at most as one Resync entry in the xrun log and, during a take, one resync mark. Clock check 2 shows what the owner's interface does (ruling E147).
- After a clock-source switch an unlock can be declared up to one poll interval later than ruling E122's first words; the exemption lasts the driver's switch call plus 1 to 2 s, up to the control thread's 15 s budget on a driver whose switch call hangs; a refused switch keeps its allowance; and a relock after a refused switch can come up to the window later, while an unlock never comes from the window (rulings E124, E137 and E138, and R3's reading R2).
