- A clock source switched by a vendor's own application, with no reset request and no selection through the engine, is not re-read until a read of the kind ruling E164 names: a stream's open, an Apply or Restart Audio Engine, or a source chosen in Audio Settings, for instance; a Play from Stop can open a stream, but not while an instrument insert in the mixer keeps the stream running, and a take that records an input opens one in either case; meanwhile the badge names the old source, and an old internal source leaves the new external one unwatched; and a vendor panel that stops streaming without any message gets no start-up allowance (ruling E107).
- A clock source chosen in Audio Settings that is still waiting to be sent when the dialog closes, behind the dialog's other audio work or its device enumeration, is not sent, and nothing says so: the interface keeps its source, the badge keeps naming it, and the dialog's Clock source list is set again from the driver's list at its next open while a stream runs on the interface (with none running it lists no source). The Clock source list stays unavailable from the choice until the switch has been sent and answered (ruling E167). A switch that the driver refuses, or that fails, after the dialog has closed has its notice in that closed dialog, where nobody sees it, and in the log (ruling E171; future story 477).
- A rate changed in an ASIO driver's own control panel reaches the engine, if at all, as a reset request with no rate: the stream is reopened at the engine's rate and nothing reads the rate the device then runs, so the clock badge keeps naming the engine's rate, and a device that then runs at a rate other than the engine's is in the mismatch the next Known limit describes, with no notice that names the rate (ruling E168; future story 474).
- No ASIO open sets or reads the interface's sample rate, so a stream opens with the engine at its own rate, the saved one at the start of a session (96 kHz until one is saved), whatever rate the interface runs at; when the two differ, the metronome and the instruments that play the project's notes play at the wrong tempo, and each audio clip of the project plays at the wrong speed and pitch unless the audio the engine plays from the clip, after any conversion of rate, holds as many samples for each second performed as the interface clocks out in a second, as with a take recorded at this mismatch, while the mismatch lasts (below), or with a clip imported from a file whose header names the rate of its audio, and whose audio the application holds at the project's rate (96 kHz in a project the application creates), while the interface runs at that rate; a take is written at the engine's rate whatever rate the interface captured it at; and the clock badge names the engine's rate. While the mismatch lasts, a take recorded on such a stream plays back at the speed and pitch it was performed at, though its clip on the timeline is as long as the time recorded times the interface's rate over the engine's (15 s for 30 s recorded on a 48 kHz interface with the engine at 96 kHz), so, in a project with nothing else to play and the metronome off, the mismatch can go unheard; once the two rates agree, that take plays at the wrong speed and pitch. Nothing in the application shows the rate the interface runs at; its own software or control panel does (on an external clock it runs at the clock master's). So before recording, with playback running on the interface, compare that rate with the one the clock badge names. Where they differ, during playback, choose in Audio Settings the rate the session is to run at and press Apply: a rate other than the saved one is asked of the driver, and once the driver takes it the stream reopens at it and the rate is saved. Choosing the saved rate is no change to apply, so to keep the saved rate, apply another rate first and then the saved one again, as the paragraph at the head of the owner's checks does for 48 kHz. A rate the driver rejects is neither applied nor saved, and Audio Settings says so; then, on the interface's internal clock, set the interface to the saved rate in its own software or control panel instead. On an external clock the interface follows the clock master: choose the master's rate in Audio Settings and press Apply, or set the master to the saved rate, which needs no Apply. Then compare the two rates again (ruling E170; future story 476).
- On the ASIO interface the application loads the driver for the lifetime of a stream (story 310), so with no stream open on it Audio Settings lists no clock source, offers the default buffer sizes (32 to 2048 frames, in powers of two) instead of the driver's own, keeps "Open driver control panel" unavailable, refuses a change of sample rate with "Sample rate … Hz was rejected by the audio driver." although the driver was not asked, and replaces a saved buffer size that is not one of those defaults with 256, which an Apply or an OK then applies (a change of Audio backend to PortAudio or Java Sound replaces it too, because Audio Settings lists those defaults for both); a Play at a saved buffer size the driver does not accept, 256 (the size used until one is saved) on a driver that does not accept 256 among them, opens no stream on the interface (it goes to the provision ladder's fallback); on a driver that offers none of those defaults, the ASIO backend refuses 256 at every open, and Audio Settings, which lists the default sizes for the interface while no stream is open on it, offers none of the driver's sizes to set; and an Apply that reconfigures the engine with no stream open on the interface hides the clock badge's dim closed view, and the next stream's read on the interface shows the clock again (ruling E169; future story 475).
- On the owner's current `asioshim.dll`, built before the sample-position export, every external clock source's measured view is dim, with the tooltip "Clock source. Lock cannot be checked on this device, so the badge will not warn if the external clock is lost." and, for a screen reader, "Clock: <name>, <rate> kilohertz. Lock not checked on this device.", until the native build is redone (ruling E100).
- A resync that arrives before the stream's first period is neither logged nor marked: the feed counts nothing before the watched heartbeat first advances.
- The legacy beat-range punch, which the application never sets, is not used for gap counting (overflow gaps are treated as having no region, starved runs and truncation as whole blocks in or out); truncated frames are placed after the kept frames, as the existing `gap=` line places them; an open-ended gap at the seal counts when it starts before punch-out or the slot looped; lines that start before the loop start are not moved; a write failure excludes the whole failing block from the tail.
- Pinned by composition only, with no test of their own: the pump death's one notice (G4), and the stream-opened callback's wiring, caught only by `FailureSpineWiringSourceTest`. Pinned by no test: the meter-side catch in `DeviceLossReactions` (no seam makes `silenceAll` throw); `XrunJournal`'s monitors (no concurrency test); the seqlock of the captured-input loss count; the `sealed` component of its per-source value, added in PR #994's second review, is pinned by `CapturedInputLossTest` since that round's last change set. Ruling E81's "a close forgets the applied status" no production path can show, because every new stream has a new serial; since PR #994's second review `ClockStatusIndicatorTest.aCloseConsumesItsSerialsTransitions` pins it through a reopen under the same serial, which production never makes.
- Proved only by reading or only with fakes: the native export, which no agent compiled; ASIO behaviour on hardware (the sample-position answers, resyncs, the switch's pause); E139's warning level (the shared `notifyWithReconnect` path and the window's pinned warning sink); E120 and E122 through the feed and the take (the feed and the marks do not read the monitor); the badge's device and rate rules, whose real engine reader runs only on one device and rate; a change of device through the real apply with a failing start (the controller builds that backend itself; a review probe emulated it under the controller's monitor); and E146's record-start path, which no probe drove (it has the same structure as the instrument-insert path a probe drove). R3's readings R2, R3, R4 and R6 cannot be told apart on hardware; their unit tests are the evidence (consult 10).

**Accepted as built.** These are recorded so that nobody "fixes" them:
- The Engine cell follows the engine state and the fault, and the record state's "Device lost" stays on the transport label (ruling E26): after a loss, a stream that opens on another device ends the cell's DEVICE_LOST while the transport label can still read "Device lost" until the record coordinator's state moves.
- The status strip's "⋯" popover closes itself when its content changes (a resize across a band, or the Xruns accent arriving while it is open), and the focus is never left on a hidden cell; measured in headless JavaFX only, with an owner check on a real window.
- A focused Button already fires on Enter in JavaFX 27, so the overflow button needs no key handler of its own.
- The drain's heartbeat for the fault hold is "the render beats advanced, or the device periods advanced where both readings have them": a second backend read per frame would break story 337's one-report rule (`AudioHealthDrainTest.oneFrameReadsTheBackendOnce`), and the summed device periods stay present after a PortAudio or ASIO stream closes, so the literal "device periods where present" would hold a Java Sound stream's escalation for good.
- The xrun feed gates on the watched heartbeat, the render beats on a backend with no device periods, so a sink that refuses every block on Java Sound or Mock is one stall, never hundreds of xruns; on such a backend a gap in a tick interval with no beat at all, which needs a block longer than the 100 ms tick, is carried to the next interval in which the beats advance and counted there, and dropped only if the stream stalls, closes, pauses or reopens first (ruling E73).
- Two notices for a sink that throws, the WARNING and the stall, the WARNING usually first (ruling E8; ruling E160 for the other order); RECONFIGURING for the length of the start-up apply (ruling E63); a reset request with no stream open ends "Audio engine reconfigured" (ruling E134).
- Clear forgets only a finished take, a take still recording keeps its figures, and a MIDI-only take clears the last audio take's figures (ruling E24).
- A stream that ran on a fallback device leaves no badge, and an applied change in Audio Settings brings none back while no stream is open (ruling E145); a close of the stream within about a second of a clock-source switch hides the badge once it has read the new source while the driver's lock report still names the source left (consult 12), and a close before that read answers can keep the dim view of the source left until a later read (ruling E166); and on the ASIO interface, with no stream open, an Apply that reconfigures the engine hides the dim closed view, because no driver is loaded to prove the clock (ruling E169).
- While an instrument insert in the mixer keeps the stream running through the transport's Stop and Pause, the clock badge shows that stream's view and can raise its unlock notice while the transport is stopped or paused, and the watchdog watches the stream then (ruling E163); a source switched in a vendor's own application stays on the badge through Plays until a read of the kind ruling E164 names; and an engine fault latched on that stream stays on the Engine cell through Stop and Play (ruling E165).
- The CPU-budget enforcer's lock and publisher on the render thread stay future story 448's (ruling E21).
- The badge stays a `Label` subclass, and every method of its package-private seam `StreamClock` is abstract, `lastOpened()` and `read(DeviceId)` among them (round F3 replaced the redesign's `openDevice()` and `sampleRate()` with `lastOpened()`), because a default would have to invent a stream or a read that ignores the device.
- What keeps another device's answer out of the closed view is that the stream's own read is asked about the device the stream opened: the `DeviceId` argument of `StreamClock.read` is not redundant.
- Every new production file is at most 900 lines (Verification); no production file that was already over the limit and that this story grew is split; by `wc -l` against HEAD they are `MainController`, `AudioEngine`, `DefaultAudioEngineController`, `RecordCoordinator`, `CaptureFlushService`, `RecordingPipeline`, `TakeManifest`, `AsioBackend`, `AsioBufferSwitchShim`, `AudioBackend`, `TransportController`, `JavaxSoundBackend`, `CallbackBackendAdapter`, `TrackCapture` and `FailureFunnel`, and, since PR #994's second review, `PortAudioBackend` (1,189 lines at HEAD, 1,270 now).

**Closes from story 336 and story 337.** Each obligation their Notes and Known limits handed to story 338:
- **Story 336: the render pump's death, logged but never shown.** The handler records the death as the drive's halt, so the drain shows it once with the halt treatment and the log keeps the SEVERE record (ruling E33; `EngineStreamPumpTest.aLastResortThreadDeathIsLoggedThenRecordedAsTheDrivesHalt`; the one notice by composition, G4). The Note from Story 337's last bullet, "Surfacing that backstop is still this story's", is the same item.
- **The heartbeat is two counters.** The watchdog watches the device-period count where the backend reports one and the render drive's beats otherwise (`EngineHeartbeatWatchdogTest.theDevicePeriodsAreWatchedWhereTheBackendReportsThemWhileTheRenderDriveKeepsCycling`, `EngineHealthMonitorTest.frozenDevicePeriodsAreAStallWhileTheRenderDriveKeepsCycling`), samples the epoch and `isRenderDriveAlive()` around them, and falls back to the beats where the device periods read empty (an input device beside the stream never contributes them; a frozen counter report is a Known limit).
- **A halted drive is handled, not DEVICE_LOST.** A halted, paused or closed drive reads not alive and is never judged, and the verdict's re-check requires the drive alive (`EngineHeartbeatWatchdogTest.aDriveThatIsNotAliveIsNeverJudgedHoweverLongItsCountersFreeze`).
- **The engine-fault fact is this story's cell to render.** The Engine cell renders `engineFaultProperty()` with its words, counts, times and action (`EngineHealthCellsTest.aLatchedFaultIsCriticalWithItsWordsTimesAndAction`, `EngineFaultReachesTheEngineCellTest.theWindowsEngineHealthModelMirrorsTheDrainsFaultUntilTheWindowIsDisposed`), so story 337's ruling E87 clearing can be seen at the desk: its hardware check is the owner's, below.
- **Restart Audio Engine can be reused by the watchdog.** It is not: a loss offers Reconnect (ruling E6); the Engine cell runs a latched fault's own action, Restart Audio Engine included.
- **What the xrun feed can read.** It reads `totals()` once per tick, the two heartbeats and the epoch, and the new `dropoutCounters()`, never `AudioHealthRecord.read`, whose one consumer is still the window's drain; nothing copies `XrunEventRingBuffer.poll`.
- **The Java Sound capture heartbeat** (story 337's Known limits and ruling E84). Every capture input is judged by its delivery count; one that delivered and then stops raises the input-halt ERROR and latch, and during a take each of its armed tracks gets an `input-stopped` line and one warning while the take keeps recording and the other inputs' tracks are untouched (`InputStallThroughTheMonitorTest.aTakeWhoseInputStopsKeepsRecordingAndItsTrackIsFlaggedAndTheWarningReachesThePerson`, `InputStallCaptureTest.theStreamsInputDeclaredStalledMidTakeFlagsOnlyItsTrackAndTheTakeKeepsRecording`).
- **The take dropout counts** (story 337, ruling E47). They are the take's figures in the Xruns cell and the xrun log (rulings E24 and E45; `TakeDropoutsAndClockMarksTest.theTakesFiguresFollowItFromItsStartToAfterItsStopUntilTheNextTakeOrAClear`).
- **Story 337's ruling E23 reserved slugs** are in use as log slugs: `audio.watchdog` for the verdict and `audio.xrun` for the feed; `AudioFailureCategory`'s Javadoc now says so.

Skills used:
- `dawg-annotations-reflection`: "## 2. The eight reflection consumers" — row 8's invariant 10 now says the render-drive walk follows into `XrunDetector` and must reach `recordTick`, and invariant 14 lists `InputStallLedger`'s writer; both copies.
- `dawg-annotations-reflection`: "### Adding a new `@RealTimeSafe` method" — `recordTick`, `setCapturedInputStarved` and `isCapturedInputStarved` carry `@RealTimeSafe`, are not `synchronized`, and are pinned by name; the render-drive group's bullet names `XrunDetector.recordTick`; both copies.
- `dawg-annotations-reflection`: "### Changing the capture path (the recording callback)" — the walk reaches the slot's new setters, which `RealTimeSafeContractTest.CAPTURE_PATH_REQUIRED_REACH` lists (`setInputStarved`, `setOutputClockFrame`, `setTransportLap`, `setLoopStartFrame`, `setLoopEndFrame`), and the skill's walk and annotation-pin bullets name the clock-frame and loop setters and `isCapturedInputStarved`; both copies. The tracked `.github/instructions` copy and the ignored `.claude/skills` copy carry the same story 338 sentences; the copies' older differences (stories 334 and 336's text on the reflection caches, the public lookup, the preset restore loop, `createById`, the reflection outside the eight call sites and the FFM upcalls, and line locators) remain.
- `dawg-native-libs`: "## 3. The Java FFM layer — the rules" — `asioshim_getSamplePosition` is bound through the shim's direct library lookup as an optional symbol, its `int64_t` outputs read as `JAVA_LONG`, never a C `long`.
- `dawg-native-libs`: "### Upcall guards" — the new overload and resync cases sit inside `asioMessageUpcall`'s existing catch-all; a count several driver threads write is an `AtomicLong`, a count on the device thread a single-writer `volatile long`.
- `dawg-native-libs`: "## 5. House conventions & gotchas" — no new test assumes a non-Windows host; the production binding's test asserts "no answer without a loaded driver", which holds everywhere.
- `javafx-application-design`: "## 4. Use JavaFX Properties for everything observable" — the status model's one engine-state property and the badge's state property.
- `javafx-application-design`: "## 8. CSS styling and theming" — the cells and the badge use tokens (`-ssc-focus`, the clock classes in `styles.css`), never a raw colour.
- `javafx-application-design`: "## 10. Animations and transitions" — the unlocked flash is a pseudo-class, stopped on every other state and steady under Reduce Motion.
- `javafx-application-design`: "## 11. Threading" — the monitor, the reads of the clock source and Reconnect run off the FX thread; every FX write goes through `FxDispatcher`; no `Platform.runLater`.
- `javafx-application-design`: "## 14. Accessibility, internationalization, and quality" — every cell, the overflow button and every badge state have their spoken text and tooltip; the copy is in `Messages.properties`, formatted for `Locale.ROOT`.
- `studio-recording`: "## Session decisions" — "clocking, dropout detection, file finalization, and recovery requirements": what is counted, marked and sealed was ruled by the studio engineer.
- `studio-signal-flow`: "## Diagnosis and result" — "Find the first stage where the observed behavior diverges": the device's own period count tells a dead device from a living render drive, and a stalled input from a dead device.
- `research-daw`: "### Architectural Patterns (5 Key Lessons)" — real-time engine monitoring kept off the audio thread, the Technical Notes' research backing.
- In PR #994's second review: `studio-recording`, "## Session decisions" ("Account for clocking, dropout detection, file finalization, and recovery requirements") — what the take's dropouts count, what a take sealed early or at a device loss records behind its seal, and how its clock marks bracket their evidence, each ruled by the studio engineer; `studio-signal-flow`, "## Trace the path" ("Include the actual order found in the source") — the ring's claim, publish, pass and look order decides which drops a take sealed early keeps (ruling E263), and the panel's count raised before its level falls decides which tick sees a session (ruling E249), and the panel's close, the driver's reset request and the closed stream's last poll come in any order, so the reset window is keyed on the request, not on the panel's session (ruling E271); `studio-signal-flow`, "## Diagnosis and result" — a lost block is counted against the subscriber and the stage that lost it (rulings E208 and E211); `dawg-native-libs`, "### Upcall guards" — the failed-period input count is a single-writer `volatile long` on the device thread, stored after the fault it belongs to, reported through `AudioBackend.failedCallbackInputBlocks()`, its two writers listed in `RealTimeSafeContractTest.BACKEND_COUNTER_WRITERS`, and a failure of a bridge's wake of its input drain is contained around the wake alone, with a handler that counts, calls and logs nothing (ruling E272); and the ASIO resync branch is held to the stricter rule in its body, walked by `RealTimeSafeContractTest.theDriversResyncPathTakesNoLockAllocatesNothingAndLogsNothing` (ruling E279); `dawg-annotations-reflection`, "## 2. The eight reflection consumers" — row 8 gains the resync message path's invariant; `javafx-application-design`, "## 8. CSS styling and theming" — the ring and the outlined chip use role tokens only; "## 10. Animations and transitions" — the flash steps a pseudo-class with no interpolation, a deliberate departure from eased motion (ruling E232), stopped in `dispose()` and steady under Reduce Motion; "## 14. Accessibility, internationalization, and quality" — the badge's words reach 4.5:1 and its ring and outline 3:1 in all three themes, and the six new notices are in `Messages.properties`.
- In PR #994's second review, the reset while the driver's control panel is open (rulings E278 and E288 to E290): `studio-signal-flow`, "## Diagnosis and result" — the stop is the driver applying the person's own setting, not the device, so the reopen waits instead of declaring a loss; `studio-recording`, "## Session decisions" — clocking (E271's window goes to the first stream the backend opens after the request) and file finalization (no capture halt; a Stop seals sealed-by=stop); `javafx-application-design`, "## 11. Threading" — the wait holds no monitor the FX thread's reads take, and nothing calls into the driver under the monitor while its panel holds the driver's control thread; `dawg-native-libs`, "### Upcall guards" — the asioMessage upcall is unchanged.
- In PR #994's second review, the other closing change sets (rulings E273 to E277, E279 to E287 and E291 to E299): `studio-recording`, "## Session decisions" ("verify the resulting file's channels, peaks, duration"; "Account for clocking, dropout detection, file finalization, and recovery requirements") — each loss is lined and counted where the file lost it and once (rulings E49, E273 and E294), the punch take's tests assert the frames the file holds, the seal writes the stop the file shows, and each clock mark is dated at its own evidence; `studio-signal-flow`, "## Trace the path" ("Include the actual order found in the source") — the stamp travels with the block in the ring's claim, publish, apply and look order, the record tap's punch gating decides the seal's stop line, and the resync's taps record what they write and when (ruling E297's order of this lap's silence before the next lap's re-entry among them); "## Diagnosis and result" — a lost block is attributed to the stage that diverged, the ring, the input's stop or a starved input; `dawg-native-libs`, "### Upcall guards" ("Count, never surface") — the ring's drop counts have one writer, a plain load and a release store, each stored before the total the reader loads first; `javafx-application-design`, "## 11. Threading" and "## 13. Performance checklist" — the FX thread reads the early seal as volatile flags only and allocates nothing for it on a display frame, and the coordinator touches the bar and the history only on turns the capture thread's sink posts.
- In PR #994's second review, consult 54 and the two fix rounds after it (rulings E300 to E304): `studio-signal-flow`, "## Trace the path" ("Include the actual order found in the source"; "Label ... record taps") — the seal asks the file the silence's first block would be written into, in the lap the transport played it in, as the record tap's lane and punch gating do (ruling E300), and the stop before a delivery is judged as the first step of applying that block, by the state of the block before it (ruling E302); "## Diagnosis and result" — each loss is attributed to the stage that diverged, the ring's drop, the input's declared stop or a starved input, a declared stop taking a starved drop only when it covers every track of the input (ruling E301), and the engine runs the one stream the person's settings describe, never a second reopen at a driver's format after an Apply (ruling E304); `studio-recording`, "## Session decisions" ("verify the resulting file's channels, peaks, duration, and playback mapping"; "Account for clocking, dropout detection, file finalization, and recovery requirements") — the new tests assert the frames the file holds as premises, and the audio after a dropped silence that sits early in the file gets its `gap=` line and count (ruling E302), and the interface's rate and buffer set in Audio Settings are the session's (ruling E304); `javafx-application-design`, "## 11. Threading" — the take's frame history stays on the FX thread and the xrun feed on the monitor thread, and the count-in test's FX turn no longer waits on the capture thread; `dawg-native-libs`, "### Upcall guards" — none of the changes reaches a callback: they run on the capture thread, the monitor thread, the FX thread or under the controller's monitor.

- Story 123's overload notice with an opt-in bypass, and its 60-second test: [462](future/462-repeated-graph-overloads-raise-a-warning-with-an-opt-in-bypass.md).
- Input blocks lost before the capture ring marked at their take frame, counted inside the punch region only and once with the starved block that filled their place (rulings E25, E44, E46, E86 (b) and E105): [463](future/463-input-blocks-lost-before-the-capture-ring-are-marked-at-their-take-frame.md).
- A driver reset during a take seals the take before the reopen (ruling E41): [464](future/464-a-driver-reset-during-a-take-seals-the-take-before-the-reopen.md).
- A hang inside audio processing named as processing, not as the device (ruling E58): [465](future/465-a-hang-inside-audio-processing-is-named-as-processing-not-as-the-device.md).
- Every frame-carrying manifest line of a loop or punch take names its lap (rulings E75, E88 and E89): [466](future/466-every-frame-carrying-manifest-line-of-a-loop-or-punch-take-names-its-lap.md).
- A device callback pause shorter than the stall window logged as a gap: [467](future/467-a-device-callback-pause-shorter-than-the-stall-window-is-logged-as-a-gap.md).
- The fault hold during a stall reads only the current stream's device periods (ruling E114): [468](future/468-the-fault-hold-during-a-stall-reads-only-the-current-streams-device-periods.md).
- The tail at a write-failure seal clipped per track (ruling E117): [469](future/469-the-tail-at-a-write-failure-seal-is-clipped-per-track.md).
- Every device-loss surface names the device the stream opened (ruling E131): [470](future/470-every-device-loss-surface-names-the-device-the-stream-opened.md).
- No notice shows an engine message, a class name or the placeholder device: [471](future/471-no-notice-shows-an-engine-message-a-class-name-or-the-placeholder-device.md).
- A start that fails from an instrument insert or from Record is never measured by the clock badge (ruling E146): [472](future/472-a-start-that-fails-from-an-instrument-insert-or-record-is-never-measured.md).
- An input halt, and every other device-side count of a stream a device loss closed, is judged by the stream that produced it, never by the render drive current when the drain first reads it (rulings E151, E152, E154 and E159): [473](future/473-an-input-halt-is-judged-by-the-stream-that-produced-it.md).
- A sample rate set in an ASIO driver's own panel reaches the engine (ruling E168): [474](future/474-a-sample-rate-set-in-an-asio-drivers-own-panel-reaches-the-engine.md).
- An ASIO interface's own settings are offered and applied with no stream open (ruling E169): [475](future/475-an-asio-interfaces-own-settings-are-offered-and-applied-with-no-stream-open.md).
- An ASIO stream runs the interface at the engine's sample rate (ruling E170): [476](future/476-an-asio-stream-runs-the-interface-at-the-engines-sample-rate.md).
- A clock source chosen in Audio Settings reaches the driver and its outcome is shown, after the dialog has closed too (ruling E171): [477](future/477-a-clock-source-chosen-in-audio-settings-reaches-the-driver-and-its-outcome-is-shown.md).
- A stream that opens on a fallback device tells the person (found while the owner's checks were read): [478](future/478-a-stream-that-opens-on-a-fallback-device-tells-the-person.md).
- The older comments of files this story changed say what Stop does to the stream with an instrument insert in the mixer and without one (ruling E163; Decision records): [479](future/479-comments-say-what-stop-does-to-the-stream-with-an-instrument-insert.md).
- The render path applies a degraded track's CPU budget policy, and the log, the toast and the tooltip say what was applied (ruling E181): [480](future/480-the-render-path-applies-a-degraded-tracks-cpu-budget-policy.md).
- PortAudio's own status flags reach the take's dropouts and the Xruns count (rulings E212 and E247): [481](future/481-portaudio-status-flags-reach-the-takes-dropouts-and-the-xruns-count.md).
- A clock event inside a take is marked even when it is seen after the Stop (ruling E229): [482](future/482-a-clock-event-inside-a-take-is-marked-even-when-seen-after-the-stop.md).
- Every text drawn in the warning colour, and every other text that carries a state, reaches 4.5:1 against what is under it in every theme, in every phase of a flash and in every dimmed state, the Engine and Xruns cells' warning text among it (rulings E235 and E237): [483](future/483-state-carrying-text-meets-small-text-contrast-in-every-theme.md).
- An input beside the stream that is hung inside its delivery when its stall is declared still gets its input-stopped line (ruling E13; consult 45's unverified suspicion): [486](future/486-an-input-beside-the-stream-stalled-inside-a-delivery-still-gets-its-input-stopped-line.md).
- A Play that opens a stream while a device loss is being declared never leaves the engine out of DEVICE_LOST with no stream open (consult 47's fact check): [487](future/487-a-play-during-a-device-loss-declaration-never-ends-the-loss-with-no-stream-open.md).
- An Error thrown by the capture thread's warning sink cannot skip the take's seal (code older than story 338): [488](future/488-an-error-from-the-capture-threads-warning-sink-never-skips-the-takes-seal.md).
- The tests of a save checkpoint's bounded wait read what the checkpoint writer has done only once it has done it, so they hold on a loaded machine (two red gates of this round, outside it; Verification): [490](future/490-the-save-checkpoint-tests-hold-on-a-loaded-machine.md).
- Future stories 484 (the silence after an input's declared stop), 485 (each resync request dated at its own arrival) and 489 (a reset request while the driver's control panel is open), filed in this round, were built in it by the closing change sets (Resolution), so they are no longer deferred; their files are superseded and left for the owner to delete, and their numbers are not reused.

## Verification

Every Goals — Tests bullet is met by the tests the Resolution names, except the story 327 hardware proof, which is the owner's (below). Every agent's Maven run on Windows ran from the repository root, one Maven process at a time, with `-B -o -DskipNativeBuild=true` (targeted runs also with `-Dsurefire.failIfNoSpecifiedTests=false`), and each run's exit status was read directly, never through a pipe; the Linux runs ran in mirrors, with `clean` and the native build (below), and the commands under the owner's checks are the owner's to run. The numbers of record are those of the baseline gate, the build of record and the Linux runs, taken from their Maven logs and from the `TEST-*.xml` reports each run wrote; the slices' and rounds' intermediate module totals are superseded by the build of record and are not repeated here.

- **Baseline.** `mvn -B -o -DskipNativeBuild=true verify` at HEAD `d5332708` with a clean tree, 2026-11-16 08:26:58 to 08:49:47 (Maven's `Total time` 22:42 min), exit 0, BUILD SUCCESS: daw-sdk 168 classes and 1,390 tests (2 skipped), daw-acoustics 14 and 106, daw-core 797 and 9,465 (40 skipped), daw-fx 2 and 29, daw-app 710 and 5,503 (3 skipped); 1,691 classes, 16,493 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, 45 skipped.
- **The slices.** Each slice ran its touched classes and then its module set with their upstream modules, and saw each new or changed test fail under a deliberate break of the production change it guards, the file copied aside first, restored with `cp -p` and `touch` and compared with `cmp`. Among the breaks, each red on the named test:
  - S1 and its fix pass (daw-sdk): today's selector constants restored (`AsioFormatChangeShimTest.selectorConstantsMatchTheAsioSdkEnum`, "not in the same order"); the old ClockSourceChange publish on a resync (`resyncRequestIsCountedOncePerMessageAndPublishesNothing`); the starved count's primed guard removed (`AsioBufferSwitchShimTest.emptyCallbacksBeforeTheFirstRenderedBlockAreStartUpNotStarvation`); the skip count removed (`olderBlocksACallbackSkipsToPlayTheNewestAreCounted`); `detached()` dropping the discards (`AsioBackendDropoutCountersTest.discardedBlocksAreFoldedInAtCloseAndKeepGrowingAfterAReopen`); the monitor's `stop()` interrupting (`AsioBackendClockLockTest.closeNeverInterruptsAPollInsideTheControlThread`). One break survived as expected: removing only `&& streaming` from `AsioBufferSwitchShim.write`, a race guard behind an early return, proved by reading.
  - S2a (the detector, the pump, the adapter): an offer in `recordTick`, a logger in it and an atomic increment in it, each red on `RealTimeSafeContractTest.theRenderDriveLoopIsGuardClean`; the pump no longer ticking, red on the sentinel's reach guard and on `EngineStreamPumpTest.aRenderedBlockSlowerThanTwoPeriodsTicksTheEnginesDetectorAsDropped`; the handler without `halt` and the log after `halt`, red on `aLastResortThreadDeathIsLoggedThenRecordedAsTheDrivesHalt`; an `AtomicLong` in `CallbackBackendAdapter.deviceCallback`, red on both real-time bridge sentinels.
  - S2b (capture, manifest, meters): the pump never stamping a starved block, red on `InputStallCaptureTest.theStreamsInputDeclaredStalledMidTakeFlagsOnlyItsTrackAndTheTakeKeepsRecording`; a sibling reading the stamp (`aSiblingBlockDeliveredInsideAStarvedRenderIsNeverMarkedStarved`); the tail counted at a loss seal (`aStallRescuedSealMarksTheTrailingSilenceOnTheStreamInputsTracksOnlyAndCountsNone`); the ledger's count before its fault (`RealTimeSafeContractTest`'s invariant 14); ruling E47 reverted (`MultichannelInputCaptureStory326Test.terminatedPrimaryInputKeepsClockedSilenceUnavailableAndFlagged`).
  - S3a (the watchdog, the monitor, the stall, Reconnect): no 2-second floor (`EngineHeartbeatWatchdogTest.aTwentyMillisecondSchedulingGapAt32FramesAt192kIsNoVerdict`), no late-tick rule (`oneLateTickReBaselinesAndNeverFires`), no start-up allowance (`aStreamStillStartingGetsTheStartUpAllowance`), always the render beats (`theDevicePeriodsAreWatchedWhereTheBackendReportsThemWhileTheRenderDriveKeepsCycling` and `EngineHealthMonitorTest.frozenDevicePeriodsAreAStallWhileTheRenderDriveKeepsCycling`); no panel clause (`anOpenDriverPanelRefusesTheVerdictAndAStallThatOutlastsItIsDeclaredAfterwards`); no RECONFIGURING (`EngineReconfiguringStateTest`'s three apply tests); a detector owning its publisher (`theXrunPublisherOutlivesEachDetectorRebuildAndTheEngineTicksTheNewDetector`).
  - S3b (the feed, the journal, the drain): no heartbeat gate (`XrunFeedTest.whatAccruesWhileTheDevicePeriodsStandStillIsTheStallsAndNotCounted`), LATE raising the count (`aLateBlockIsALateEntryAndNeverRaisesTheCount`), no restart at a detector change (`aSettingsChangeRestartsTheCountAndKeepsTheEntries`); no heartbeat hold (`AudioHealthDrainTest.aDeviceSideFaultOnEveryBlockWithAStandingHeartbeatNeverEscalatesNorLatchesBeforeTheLoss`), no clear at the loss (`theLossClearsADeviceSideFactLatchedOnTheLostDrive`), no epoch comparison (`theLossClearsNeitherAHaltNorANewerDrivesFact`), no input-halt gate (`anInputHaltOfTheLostStreamRaisesNothingAndOneOfALaterStreamDoes`). A redundant `!halt()` clause whose removal survived was taken out of `streamLost`.
  - S4a (the record side): the carried growth dropped (`XrunFeedTest.aHiccupWhileTheDevicePeriodsStandStillIsCountedWithItsFullCountWhenTheyAdvanceAgain`); no stop in place (`DeviceLossLeavesTheTransportAndMetersTruthfulTest.aPlaybackOnlyLossStopsTheTransportWhereTheAudioStoppedWithOneStoppedAnnounced`); no playhead restore after a rescue (`aRescuedTakeKeepsItsOwnStopAndThePlayheadReturnsToWhereTheLossStoppedIt`); no `silenceAll` (`theInputMetersFallToTheFloorAtTheLossAndKeepTheirClipLatch`); the lead ignoring the cause (`StalledDeviceLossCopyTest` and `HeartbeatStallDuringATakeTest`); no take-frame sampling (`TakeDropoutsAndClockMarksTest.aClockEventIsDatedAtTheFrameSampledWhenItsEvidenceWasSeenAndOnlyWhileTheTakeRecords`). Two breaks survived as equivalent: the discard of carried growth at an invalid sample (an epoch change always discards first) and the guard before the stop in place (a rescue always leaves the transport stopped).
  - S4b-1 (the model, the cells, the dialog): no RECONFIGURING view (`EngineStateCellTest.theCellTracksRunningReconfiguringRunningThroughASettingsApply`); the loss in the normal severity (`aRemovedDeviceShowsTheWarningTreatment`); the fault winning over the loss (`EngineHealthCellsTest.aLossWinsOverAFaultWhoseSentenceJoinsTheTooltip`); a colon for an empty name and the old Discarded wording (`EngineHealthFeedTest.eachEntryIsWordedAsRuled`); never stamped "near" (`anEntryIsStampedWithTheTransportPositionOnlyWhileItRolls`); the drain-side catch narrowed (`DeviceLossReactionsTest.aReactionThatThrowsLeavesTheOtherDoneAndTheModelAfterThemTheLoss`).
  - S5 (punch and loop takes): no split at the wrap (`TakeMarksInPunchAndLoopTakesTest.aStarvedRunAcrossTheLoopWrapIsTwoLinesThatEndAtTheLoopEndInALoopRecordTake` and its two siblings); no clipping to the region (`aStarvedRunWhollyBeforePunchInIsNeitherWrittenNorCounted`, `aTailAcrossPunchOutAtAStopEndsAtPunchOut`); the stop not moved to punch-in (`anInputThatEndsBeforePunchInIsFlaggedFromPunchInAsHavingDeliveredNoAudio`); the extension branch removed (`anInputThatEndsBeforePunchInAndIsDeclaredAfterPunchOutIsFlaggedFromPunchIn`); the tail never counted (`aStopAfterAStarvedRunCountsItsTail`); a starved block noted before its write (`aWriteFailureSealsWithATailThatStopsBeforeTheBlockThatFailed`).
  - S4b-2 (the badge's first build and the wiring): its tests were superseded by the rework and the redesign below; its source pins of the window's wiring stay in `FailureSpineWiringSourceTest`.
- **The independent review and its rework.** Reviewer A's breaks that survived the module and are now killed: `WatchdogReactions.inputStalled` emptied, now red on all three `InputStallThroughTheMonitorTest` tests; the scheduled half of the stall refusal removed, now red on `DriverRequestWhileTheDeviceIsGoneTest.aReopenTakenOffTheQueueButNotDoneRefusesTheStallVerdict`; the stall lead at the MIDI-stop-failure site made the removal's, now red on `DeviceLossLeadsDuringATakeTest.aStallDuringATakeWhoseMidiStopFailsLeadsWithTheStall`; a `Thread.sleep(1)` in `stopInPlaceAfterTheDeviceLoss`, now red on `TransportControllerFxThreadBytecodeSentinelTest.theDeviceLossTurnTouchesNoStorageAndNeverWaits`; `synchronized` on `AudioEngine.setCapturedInputStarved`, now red on `RealTimeSafeContractTest` (an annotated method may not be `synchronized`, and the render walk reaches it); the xrun feed's start gate forced open, now red on `XrunFeedTest.blocksDiscardedBeforeTheDevicesFirstPeriodAreNeverCountedAndOneAfterItIs`. Adopted from the reviewers' probes: `InputStallThroughTheMonitorTest.theMonitorDeclaresAStreamInputThatStoppedDeliveringThroughTheProductionWiring`, and a probe of a format change after a stall verdict, adopted inverted in `DriverRequestWhileTheDeviceIsGoneTest` (it goes red only with both drop checks removed; each check has its own killing test). Left as recorded in Known limits: the meter-side catch, `XrunJournal`'s monitors, the seqlock, and two breaks equivalent in the tests (`AudioEngine.dropoutCounters()` without its stream check, behind the feed's alive gate; the shim's `&& streaming`).
- **The confirming review and the fix round.** The confirming review's own probes reproduced its must-fix items; the fix round's tests pin each: a Play that waited behind Reconnect's open (`ReconnectAgainstOtherOpensTest.aPlayThatWaitedBehindReconnectsOpenFindsTheStreamParkedAndPlaysIt` and three siblings); "<default>" on the device fault (`DeviceNamesInTheSentencesThePersonReadsTest.anAudioFailureNoticeNamesTheBackendForThePlaceholder`, `FailureCopyTest.theDeviceFaultCountsOneErrorAndSaysTheAudioDeviceWithNoNameAtAll`); the removal lead and the "still disconnected" sentences (`DeviceNamesInTheSentencesThePersonReadsTest.theRemovalLeadSaysWasDisconnected` and `theStillLostSentencesFollowTheCause`); the badge at a quick Stop (`ClockStatusIndicatorTest.aQuickPlayThenStopBeforeTheLabelReadLandsNeverHidesTheBadge`). Its surviving break on the take's resync mark (`markResyncs` given the pulse's time instead of the arrival) is now red on `EngineHealthFeedTest.aResyncIsMarkedAtTheFrameOfItsArrivalNotOfThePulseThatHandsItOver`. Its flaky new test (`DriverRequestWhileTheDeviceIsGoneTest.aResetWhoseReopenFailsOnTheDefaultDeviceSaysTheAudioDevice`, which read the last notice before the loss's had landed) was fixed in the round with the two tests of the same shape: each now awaits the notice itself, and a delayed-notice break turns the old forms red and leaves the new ones green.
- **R3, F3, F4 and T1.** R3's confirmation ran 25 mutants of its own over the redesign, F3's 20 with one survivor judged harmless (the null check of the Reconnect thread factory's result changes only the logged cause), F4's 11 with no survivor. T1's breaks, each restored and compared: `synchronized` taken off `DefaultAudioEngineController.clockSources`, red only on `ClockBadgeDuringATakeTest.anApplyWhoseRestartFailsAfterItsOpenShowsTheStartingViewThenTheClosedOne` (the failing stream was measured UNLOCKED); the requested device made equal by name only, red on `theControllersApplyOfAnUnchangedRequestKeepsTheDimView` and on `anApplyThatChangesNothingAfterAStreamOnTheConfiguredDeviceKeepsTheDimView`; the take-recording answer of the badge's take marks (`takeMarksOf`) reading every state but IDLE as recording, red on `TakeClockMarksOfTheTransportTest.aTakeBeingPreparedRecordsNothingAndAClockChangeThenMarksNothing`. `ClockBadgeDuringATakeTest` then ran three more times alone, 12 of 12 green each time.
- **The closing round.** The code part's break: `clockBadge.tooltip` put back to its former sentence, red on `ClockStatusIndicatorTest.eachStateSpeaksAndExplainsItselfAsRuled` and `aStreamOpenedCallbackArrivingAfterTheStreamWasMeasuredChangesNothing` (92 tests, 2 failures, each expecting E147's sentence), then green after the restore (92 of 92). Its targeted run of 35 classes (those that show the badge or the Engine cell, the two that read the whole message bundle, and the source scans whose roots hold the changed files) ran 270 tests, 0 failures. The three Javadoc passages are documentation only, and no test reads them. Four comments-only steps followed, each changing comment lines only, every changed line a comment line by `diff` against the copies taken before it: the first in four classes after the second gate (under the build of record, below); the second in twelve files after consult 14; the third in `AudioHealthDrain` after consult 15; and the fourth in `AudioHealthDrain`, `FailureFunnel` and `RealTimeReport` after round L's review. The second, third and fourth ran no Maven. A fact-check agent that wrote none of them read each before the next gate: the fourth fact-check read the second step five times, with a correction pass after each of the first four, and confirmed it at the fifth; the fifth fact-check read the third twice, with a correction between, and confirmed it at the second; and the seventh fact-check's first read confirmed the fourth.
- **Fix round L and test rounds M and N.** Round L wrote its tests first, from the engineer's acceptance cases, and ran them before the change: `AudioHealthDrainTest` ran 56 tests with 6 failures, `anInputHaltFoldedAtTheLossWritesARecordThatSaysItIsPartOfTheDeviceLoss` (E156: the record carried story 337's "folded into the notice of the same frame"), `aFoldedHaltAndALostBlockOfTheLostDriveAreSummedUpAsLostInput` and `aFoldedHaltAndALostBlockOfALaterStreamAreSummedUpAsLostInput` (E155: the summary repeated the audio-device-fault sentence), and `anInputHaltFoldedAtTheLossStartsNoRunThatALaterStreamCompletes`, `aLostBlockOfTheLostDriveStartsNoRunThatALaterStreamCompletes` and `aCallbackFaultOfTheLostDriveStartsNoRunThatALaterStreamCompletes` (E157: an ERROR within the later stream's first two seconds). The controls `aHaltFoldedInsideTheWindowOfAShownWarningIsSummedUpInThatWarningsWords` (E155) and `aProcessingFaultKeepsItsRunAcrossTheLoss` (E157) were green before and after. `FailureFunnelRealTimeReportTest.aDeviceLossFoldsRecordSaysItIsPartOfTheLossAndItsSentenceIsTheOneATailRepeats` (E156, the funnel's part) calls the new entry point, so it could not compile before the change. After the change the two classes ran 65 tests (56 and 9), 0 failures. Round L's mutants of production code, each applied alone, run on the two classes, restored with `cp -p` and `touch` and compared with `cmp`, were each red: the new fold with story 337's detail (the drain's E156 test and the funnel's), the drain calling the old fold (the drain's E156 test), the old sentence (E155's two), no run restart for the folded halt (E157's first case), no run restart for a lost drive's count (E157's second and third) and the restart for every category (the control); the first four ran on the round's version before `sustained = false` was added, at the same sites. Removing `sustained = false` survived as an equivalent mutant, because every lost-drive count already begins a new run, and the line stayed. Round L's targeted run of 64 classes (those that reference `AudioHealthDrain`, `FailureFunnel`, `FailureTrack` or `RealTimeReport`, those that read the whole message bundle or scan daw-app's main sources, and four bytecode sentinels) ran 650 tests, 0 failures, exit 0. The review ran eight mutants of its own on the two classes and a probe class of seven scenarios, each red, with no survivor; the drain from before the round, with the current funnel, failed the six new tests and four of the probes. Its regression run, round L's 64 classes with `EngineHealthCellsTest`, `EngineHealthFeedTest`, `EngineHealthMonitorTest`, `IconAuditTest` and the probe, ran 69 classes and 694 tests, 0 failures; the probe was then moved out of the tree. Test round M added `aLossThatClearsALatchedDeviceFaultRestartsAProcessingRun` (E158), green, 57 tests in the class; with the loss's clear restarting only the device-side runs it was red (1 failure), and with no clear at the loss it and two older clear tests were red (3). Test round N added `aLostBlockOfTheLostStreamFirstReadOnANewerDriveJoinsThatDrivesRun` (E159 (c)), `aDeviceSideCountReadOnTheLostDriveAfterTheLossStillShowsItsWarning` (E160), and `theLossClearsNoFactWhileAProcessingFaultIsLatched` with its second run `aProcessingFaultThatKeepsFailingKeepsTheFactTheLossDidNotClear` (E161), green, 61 tests in the class. A restart of the device-side runs at the first reading of a newer drive turned the first red, with six older tests (7 failures); skipping the warning path of a lost drive's count turned the second red, with one older test (2); and a clear at the loss without the device-side condition turned the third and its second run red, with one older test (3). Each mutant of rounds M and N was restored and compared the same way, the main sources hashed the same after each round's mutants as before them, and round N's production change is comment lines only. No test of these rounds branches on the operating system.
- **The docs.** This Resolution and Verification, the "As built in story 338" notes in the Failure Surfacing, Recording Reliability and Project Manager design books, the Notes in the other stories and future stories 461 to 479 were written with no Maven run, and no test reads them; so were the closing round's corrections to them, the touch after the first comments-only step, the document touches that wrote consults 15 to 23 into them, and this touch. Writer B's documents were written while the first gate ran, and this section first after it, against its logs; the closing round's corrections were written while the second gate ran, and its numbers after it, against its log and reports; the touch after the first comments-only step (the account of the closing round above, this section's account of the third gate and one sentence of future story 461) was written while the third gate ran, and its numbers after it, against its log and reports; the touch of consult 15 was written after the fourth gate and corrected while the fifth gate ran, the touches of consults 16 to 19 between the fifth and sixth gates, and those of consults 20 to 23 after the sixth; and this touch (the account after the closing round above, the Decision records of the last round, future story 479 and this section's numbers) was written after the sixth gate and the third Linux run, against their logs and reports. Each document touch and each comments-only step after the first acceptance read was read by a fact-check agent that wrote none of it, and this touch is read by one too.
