  - A fact-check agent wrote one stray file outside the repository by a mistaken redirect, for the owner to delete: `C:\Users\bestq\AppData\Local\Temp\null`.
- The owner's checks; none was run by an agent. Each that uses the application starts it with `mvn -pl daw-app javafx:run`. That command builds daw-app alone and takes the modules daw-app depends on as they are installed in the local Maven repository, so first install this tree there with `mvn -B -o -DskipNativeBuild=true -DskipTests install` at the repository root; and the application finds `asioshim.dll` by name through the system's DLL search, so the folder that holds it (`target/build/native` after the native build below) must be on that search, on `PATH` for instance. Audio Settings does not apply a change of Audio backend at its Apply or OK: it says "Restart required for: Audio backend. Changes apply when you relaunch.", and the application uses the new backend from its next start (and at a Restart Audio Engine, which a fault offers). So a check or a step on another backend than the one the session started on (PortAudio, Java Sound) runs in a session of its own: choose the backend and its devices in Audio Settings, press OK, quit and start the application again, and open the project again if it is not open; a new session starts the clock badge with no label. On the ASIO interface the application loads the driver for the lifetime of a stream (story 310), so with no stream open on it Audio Settings lists no clock source, offers the default buffer sizes, 32 to 2048 frames in powers of two, instead of the driver's own, keeps "Open driver control panel" unavailable, and refuses a change of sample rate with "Sample rate … Hz was rejected by the audio driver."; and a saved buffer size that is not one of those defaults is replaced by 256 when Audio Settings opens, with "Persisted Buffer Size (frames) … is unavailable for the selected device; falling back to 256.", which an Apply or an OK then applies (ruling E169). So choose the interface's clock source, sample rate and buffer size during playback, and before the checks set its buffer size, during playback, to one that the driver offers and that is one of those defaults, and keep it such a size through the checks (the Xruns check's small buffer included). Before that, press Play and Stop once and open the application's log (Open Log Folder in the Help menu opens its folder): if that Play wrote "ASIO driver rejected buffer size … frames: driver reports min=… max=… preferred=… granularity=…", it opened no stream on the interface (playback went to the provision ladder's fallback, the computer's default output, or did not start), because the saved buffer size is one the driver does not accept, 256 (the size used until one is saved) on a driver that does not accept 256 for instance. Then, with the transport stopped, choose in Audio Settings, which lists the default sizes as above, one that the driver accepts by that line's numbers (its preferred size alone when granularity is 0, a power of two from min to max when it is -1, and otherwise min, min plus whole steps of granularity up to max, or max), press OK and press Play again. If the driver accepts none of the defaults, no size it accepts can then be set from Audio Settings (ruling E169): if the driver's own software can change the sizes it offers, set one of the defaults there and start again from the Play; if not, report it with that line's numbers and leave out every step on the interface. If the log has, from that Play, a "Backend ladder hop failed" line that names ASIO with another cause, write it down and report it. If the driver offers none of the defaults and a stream does open on the interface, write down its sizes and report it, and keep the driver's size saved as follows. Audio Settings is a page of the Settings dialog, which looks up the selected backend's buffer sizes at every open, the open made to set UI Scale among them, and lists the default sizes for PortAudio and Java Sound too; so with no stream open on the interface, and at a change of Audio backend to PortAudio or Java Sound, it puts 256 in place of that driver's size, with the notice above, and an Apply or an OK made with no stream open on the interface then saves 256. The ASIO backend refuses 256 at every open on that driver, so a Play then opens no stream on the interface (it goes to the provision ladder's fallback), and the dialog, which then lists the default sizes for the interface, offers none of the driver's sizes to set back. So, on that driver, in a session started on ASIO: press Apply or OK in the Settings dialog while a stream is open on the interface, during playback for instance, and close the dialog with Cancel, then Discard, when it shows that notice; leave out clock checks 6 and 11, and story 327's proof when the interface is not chosen in Audio Settings already; and run every step on PortAudio or Java Sound, story 337's checks on Java Sound among them, after every step on the interface, and there press OK at the change of Audio backend they need, although it shows that notice too. Whatever the driver, set the interface's sample rate before the checks too, to 48 kHz, the rate the clock checks name ("INT 48k", "W/C 48k"), on the internal clock and during playback: choose 48 kHz in Audio Settings and press Apply, which asks the driver for that rate and reopens the stream at it, as in clock check 9; when 48 kHz is the saved rate already, choosing it is no change to apply, so first choose 44.1 kHz and press Apply. No ASIO open sets the interface's rate, so the engine runs at the saved rate, 96 kHz until one is saved, whatever rate the interface runs at, and when the two differ the audio plays at the wrong speed and pitch (ruling E170). If Audio Settings says "Sample rate … Hz was rejected by the audio driver.", the driver does not take a change of rate while streaming: go on when the badge names 48k and the interface's own software or control panel shows 48 kHz, and otherwise write down the driver and the rate the badge names, report it and leave out every step on the interface. A check that records audio needs a project with a folder (Record refuses one without, with "Recording needs a project folder — save the project first") and a track armed on the input the check names:
  - **The native build with the new export.** On Windows with the ASIO SDK, at the repository root, in a POSIX shell such as Git Bash: first configure and build as `daw-core/native/asio/README.md` says, `cmake -S lib -B target/build -DBUILD_ASIO_SHIM=ON -DASIO_SDK_DIR=<SDK folder> -A x64`, then `cmake --build target/build --config Release`; the SDK folder is then in the CMake cache of `target/build`, and `asioshim.dll` in `target/build/native`. With the SDK open, confirm that `asio.h`'s selector enum gives `kAsioResetRequest` 3, `kAsioBufferSizeChange` 4, `kAsioResyncRequest` 5, `kAsioLatenciesChanged` 6, `kAsioSupportsTimeInfo` 7 and `kAsioOverload` 15, as `AsioFormatChangeShimTest.selectorConstantsMatchTheAsioSdkEnum` pins; a difference is a defect to report. Then run `DAW_REQUIRE_ASIOSHIM=1 mvn -B -pl daw-core -am test -Dsurefire.failIfNoSpecifiedTests=false -Dtest=NativeLibraryDetectorTest,AsioStreamingIntegrationTest,AsioEngineStreamingIntegrationTest`, without `clean` (the root project's `clean` deletes `target/`, the cache with it) and without `-DskipNativeBuild=true`: daw-core's `cmake-configure` and `cmake-build` executions configure and build `target/build` again and pass no SDK folder of their own, so the folder comes from that cache (or, when the cache has none, from an `ASIO_SDK_DIR` environment variable, as the Windows ASIO-shim workflow sets it), and daw-core's Surefire loads the libraries from `target/build/native`. A `-DASIO_SDK_DIR=…` on the Maven line is read by no pom. Verified by reading `daw-core/pom.xml`, `lib/CMakeLists.txt`, `daw-core/native/asio/CMakeLists.txt` and the README, not run. Expected: `asioshim.dll` builds with no warning from `asioshim_getSamplePosition`; `asioshimExportsRequiredSymbolsOnWindows` passes with the new symbol; the two ASIO integration tests pass on the real driver. Until this build is redone, every external clock source's measured view is dim, with the tooltip "Clock source. Lock cannot be checked on this device, so the badge will not warn if the external clock is lost." (ruling E100). The ASIO integration tests skip themselves without `asioshim.dll`; the build of record did not force them.
  - **The story 327 hardware proof** (Goals — Tests bullet 2), on the ASIO multi-channel USB interface: open a project, arm tracks on the interface, press Record, and pull the interface's USB cable mid-take. Expected, within about 2 s (max(2 s, 8 buffer periods)): the take directory's `take.manifest` reads `sealed-by=device-loss`; the rescued take is registered; a WARNING "<name> stopped responding during recording. The take was saved up to the dropout: N clips on M tracks." with Review; the notice "<name> stopped responding — audio stopped. Check that it is connected and powered, then choose Reconnect." with Reconnect; the Engine cell "Device lost: <name>" in the warning accent; a notice of the dying interface before or beside the loss notice, such as 'Audio device fault — …' or 'Audio engine fault — …' with Restart Audio Engine, or a device-side warning, is expected and is not a defect (rulings E114, E159 and E160), and the Engine cell still shows only 'Device lost: <name>'; the transport label "Device lost"; REC off; the input meters at the floor; the playhead where the dropout happened; the clock badge hidden. If nothing is declared within about 5 s, "Reconfiguring audio engine…" did not appear and the input meters sit at the floor while the take rolls, the driver keeps calling back without its device: press Stop, write down the interface and the driver, and report it (future story 461). Plug the cable back in and wait until the interface is ready; a Reconnect before then may say '… could not be reopened: …', and a later one must succeed. If a loss was declared, choose Reconnect (on the notice, or a click, Enter or Space on the Engine cell). Expected: "<name> reconnected. Tracks are still armed; press Record to continue.", record state IDLE, the armed set kept, the transport at the take anchor. On the automatic device "<name>" reads "The audio device" and the cell "Device lost: audio device" (ruling E131). If the driver reports the unplug as a reset request first: "Reconfiguring audio engine…", then "<name> asked to be reset and could not be reopened — audio stopped. Check that it is connected and powered, then choose Reconnect." with Reconnect, "Device lost: <name>", and a WARNING led by "<name> could not be reopened after a driver reset during recording", still `sealed-by=device-loss` (rulings E113 and E134), and the Xruns count restarts (ruling E148); if the reset's reopen instead lands on another backend's default output (the provision ladder's fallback, as in clock check 7), the reset's notices are "Reconfiguring audio engine…" and then "Audio engine reconfigured", nothing is declared and no Reconnect is offered: press Stop, write down that device and what the take recorded, and report it (future story 464); before the next check, plug the cable back in and wait until the interface is ready, and, in a project with an instrument insert, where that Stop leaves the stream running on that device (ruling E163), also remove every instrument insert from the mixer and press Stop again.
  - **A loss during playback**, on the ASIO interface or PortAudio: press Play and pull the cable. Expected: the stall notice with Reconnect; the transport Stopped where the audio stopped; the Engine cell "Device lost: <name>"; a notice of the dying interface before or beside the loss notice, such as 'Audio device fault — …' or 'Audio engine fault — …' with Restart Audio Engine, or a device-side warning, is expected and is not a defect (rulings E114, E159 and E160), and the Engine cell still shows only 'Device lost: <name>'; one xrun log entry "Stall  Device stopped responding: <name>", and the Xruns count not jumping. If the driver reports the unplug as a reset request first, as in the story 327 proof's last sentence, a pull of the cable while playing gives instead "Reconfiguring audio engine…", then "<name> asked to be reset and could not be reopened — audio stopped. Check that it is connected and powered, then choose Reconnect." with Reconnect in place of the stall notice, with the transport and the Engine cell as above, no Stall entry and the Xruns count restarted (rulings E113 and E148); if that reset's reopen instead lands on another backend's default output (the provision ladder's fallback, as in clock check 7), the reset's notices are "Reconfiguring audio engine…" and then "Audio engine reconfigured", nothing is declared and no Reconnect is offered: press Stop, write down that device and report it, and the rest of this check does not apply; if nothing is declared within about 5 s and "Reconfiguring audio engine…" did not appear, the driver keeps calling back without its device: press Stop, write down the interface and the driver, and report it (future story 461), and the rest of this check does not apply. With the cable still out, choose Reconnect several times. Expected: one attempt at a time and one notice per attempt, "<name> could not be reopened: it is not available. …" or "…: the audio driver refused to open it. …", never engine words; the state stays "Device lost". Plug it in and choose Reconnect. Expected: "<name> is responding again. Press Play to continue." Then press Play, pull the cable and wait for 'Device lost'. Plug it back in, wait until the interface is ready, then choose Reconnect and press Play at once. Expected: Play plays with sound; never 'Playing' with silence. Last, with the stream parked after a successful Reconnect and no Play yet, pull the cable and press Play. Expected: up to about 5 s of Playing with no sound, then the stall notice with Reconnect (rulings E2, E9 and E150). On a driver that reports the unplug as a reset request first, the reset's reopen runs instead, before, during or after the Play, and the notices to expect are the reset's above (ruling E113): write down the notices and what the Play did, and report them.
  - **A healthy take**, 30 seconds on the ASIO interface and 30 seconds on PortAudio, each in a session of its own (the backend sentence at the head of these checks), each ended with Stop. Expected: no `input-silence` line in either `take.manifest`; any such line is a capture-path defect to report (ruling E79), never a reason to suppress the marks.
  - **The Xruns cell and the xrun log**, on ASIO and on PortAudio, each in a session of its own (the backend sentence at the head of these checks), at a small buffer under load. Expected: "Xruns N" rises with the warning accent; a click, Enter or Space opens "Xrun Log" with lines such as "hh:mm:ss.mmm  Gap  Device played silence (output buffer empty) ×n  near m:ss.s"; Clear brings back "Xruns 0" and an empty log ("Nothing logged."). On PortAudio, in a project with no instrument insert (ruling E163), press Play from Stop and open the log at once. Expected: no start-up "One rendered block was skipped and never played" entries. Run the status strip check below in one of this check's two sessions, while the warning accent shows, and end each of the two sessions by stopping the load and setting the buffer size back to the size it had before this check, the way the paragraph at the head of these checks sets it: clock check 3 expects Xruns to rise by the number of resyncs, and a small buffer under load would add its own gaps to that count.
  - **The status strip at narrow widths**: with the Xruns cell in its warning accent, set UI Scale (Settings, Appearance) to 125% and size the window between about 1280 and 1450 px wide, so that the strip's width divided by 1.25 falls between 1000 and 1160: the strip compares its width divided by the UI scale with its bands, and the main window is at least 1280 px wide, so at 100% the strip keeps its two rows. Expected: Schema, Lock, Disk and Journal go into "⋯" one band earlier, before the Xruns text is ellipsized; the Engine cell never goes. Press Tab until "⋯" has the focus (its tooltip, on hover, is "More status"), press Space, then Enter on the focused cell. Expected: the popover opens with its first cell that has an action focused, Schema while the Xruns accent holds (the Xruns cell, which opens "Xrun Log", once the accent has lapsed), and Enter fires it: Schema opens "Migration History", or shows "No project history to show" in a project with no folder. Close any window it opened; if the popover has closed, open it again (Tab to "⋯" if needed, then Space); press Escape. Expected: the popover closes and the focus is on "⋯". Afterwards set UI Scale back, the same way, to the scale it had before this check. UI Scale is saved, and each session starts with the window maximized: at 125%, with that window narrower than about 1460 px, the Xruns cell stays on the strip only while its warning accent shows and is otherwise in the overflow, so clock checks 2, 3 and 10, which open the xrun log from that cell or read its count, would have to look for it there.
  - **Story 337's ruling E87 at the Engine cell**, on the Java Sound rung, in a session started on Java Sound (the backend sentence at the head of these checks), with a USB interface as input and the computer's own output: arm a track on the interface, record, and unplug the interface mid-take. Expected: the ERROR whose reason reads `input from “<interface>” stopped delivering audio`, with "1 error" for one; each armed track of the interface warned "Track 'T': input from 'D' stopped delivering audio at m:ss.s into the take; the track records silence from there." and given an `input-stopped` line; the take keeps rolling; the Engine cell "Engine fault: audio input" in the critical treatment, its tooltip "Audio input failed 1 time between hh:mm:ss and hh:mm:ss. Click to restart audio engine." Press Stop. Expected: the cell still shows the fault. Plug the interface back in and click the Engine cell. Expected: Restart Audio Engine runs and the cell clears. That a resume of the paused stream never clears an input halt's fact is pinned by `AudioHealthDrainTest.anInputHaltsFactClearsOnlyOnAStreamOpenedAfterIt`, not by this check.
  - **The clock badge** on the ASIO interface, with an external word-clock source where the step says so. Before check 1, and again after check 7 once the interface is plugged back in, choose the internal clock and, with playback running on the interface, look at the rate its own software or control panel shows; if it is not 48 kHz, set 48 kHz and go on, or leave out the later steps on the interface, as the end of check 10 says. A pull of the interface's USB cable, or a session that used the interface through Java Sound or PortAudio, can leave it at another rate; no ASIO open sets that rate, and the badge names the engine's rate whatever rate the interface runs at (ruling E170). Checks 1, 6 to 9, 11 and 12 need a project with no instrument insert anywhere in the mixer (no Virtual Keyboard, Signal Generator or other plugin typed as an instrument on an insert of any track, return bus or the master, a bypassed one or one on a muted track included; a CLAP plugin does not count, because the host types every CLAP plugin as an effect; after removing one, press Stop once before the check): with one, the transport's own close at a Stop and its pause at a Pause are skipped, and a Play over a stream whose render drive runs opens none, so those checks' Stops close nothing (ruling E163). Check 15 is for a project with one. In every clock check, "dim" is the outlined chip with no fill (ruling E234) and "bright" the filled chip; the words are the same colour in both, and the fill and the dim chip's outline tell them apart. A clock source chosen in Audio Settings' Clock source list is sent when it is chosen, with nothing to Apply; after choosing one, wait until the list can be opened again before closing Audio Settings, because closing it while the choice is still waiting to be sent can drop the choice (ruling E167):
    1. Press Play, then Stop, several times (the Play button pauses a playing transport, so press Stop between Plays). Expected: once the badge shows a measured view, its tooltip never returns to "Clock source when the audio stream last ran. Lock is not checked yet on this stream." for that stream; between Plays the dim closed view shows with "Clock source when the audio stream last ran. Lock is not checked while the stream is closed."; it never disappears at Play or at Stop. Only an internal source shows bright; on the current `asioshim.dll` an external source's measured view is dim with "Clock source. Lock cannot be checked on this device, so the badge will not warn if the external clock is lost."
    2. With the rebuilt `asioshim.dll`, play on a locked word clock, then pull the clock cable. Expected: "⚠ W/C 48k UNLOCKED" within about 2–3 s; with motion allowed, a thin two-tone ring steps on and off around the badge's edge every half second: light outside and dark inside in Onyx Refined and Studio Slate, dark outside and white inside in Atelier. The words, the red under them and the badge's size stay steady. Under Reduce Motion there is no ring. Also expected: "External clock lost on <driver name> (<source>). Check the clock cable and the clock master." (never "<default>"); during a take that records an armed audio track it adds "Recording continues; the take is marked from this point." and the take keeps recording. If no '⚠ … UNLOCKED' appears within about 5 s: if '<name> stopped responding' appears instead, the interface stopped streaming when it lost the clock and the device-loss path ran (ruling E30); if nothing appears, open the xrun log, note whether a Resync entry was logged, write down the interface, the driver and what the interface's own panel or software shows for its lock, and report it. That is ruling E147's limit: the badge cannot warn on that interface. Reconnect the cable. Expected: "External clock locked again on <driver name> (<source>)." as an information notice, and, for a pull and a reconnect during such a take, the take's `take.manifest` holds `clock=unlocked|…` and `clock=locked|…`.
    3. While playing, choose another clock source in Audio Settings, then, during a take, switch the clock source again in Audio Settings too, not in the interface's own software: a switch made there gets no allowance and is not re-read (Known limits). Expected: the badge shows the new source, checking, then follows it; no "stopped responding" within 5 s of the switch; no "External clock lost" notice and no unlock mark from the resyncs around it; Xruns rises by the number of resyncs, and each resync has its mark in the take. Note how long the driver pauses its callbacks during the switch (ruling E107).
    4. While streaming, open the driver's control panel from Audio Settings ("Open driver control panel", available on a driver that has one), change the clock source there and close the panel. Expected: the badge follows the new source, on a driver whose panel keeps that button unavailable until the panel is closed, and no "External clock lost" notice and no unlock mark for the change made in the panel (rulings E248 and E249). A driver whose panel leaves the button available at once runs its panel on its own, and a switch made there is the vendor-switch Known limit's: if the badge does not follow, write down the driver.
    5. During playback, a switch in Audio Settings' Clock source list that the driver refuses (word clock with no cable, on a driver that refuses it). Expected: the old source stays on the badge, with no "External clock lost" notice and no relock notice.
    6. On a driver that offers none of Audio Settings' default buffer sizes, leave this check out (the paragraph at the head of these checks). With the badge showing on ASIO and playback stopped, choose PortAudio as the Audio backend in Audio Settings, with one of its output devices, and press OK. Expected: before the OK the dialog says "Restart required for: Audio backend. Changes apply when you relaunch.", and nothing reopens: the badge stays as it was, unless Audio Settings replaced the buffer size by itself ("Persisted Buffer Size (frames) … is unavailable for the selected device; falling back to 256.", the paragraph at the head of these checks), whose OK also applies that size and hides the badge (ruling E169). Start the application again (the backend sentence at the head of these checks) and press Play. Expected: no badge while PortAudio plays and none after Stop, because the engine controller reports no clock source for PortAudio. Choose ASIO and the interface again the same way, start the application again and press Play. Expected: hidden until the source is read, then the measured view. The badge's hide at a change of device or rate within one session is shown by checks 7 and 9 (ruling E142; R3's reading R1).
    7. With the interface's dim closed view showing after a Play and a Stop on it, unplug the interface and press Play. If playback falls back to another backend's default output (the provision ladder's fallback; it sounds on the computer's default output), expected: the badge hides at that stream's open, none shows while it plays and none after Stop, because the engine controller reports no clock source for the fallback devices (ruling E145); otherwise write down what the Play did, and the rest of this check does not apply. After that Stop, open Audio Settings, change Worker Pool Size and press Apply (Apply is enabled once a setting differs from its saved value). Expected: still no badge. If Audio Settings shows "Persisted … is unavailable for the selected device; falling back to …" when it opens, it has replaced a setting by itself: press Cancel, then Discard, and leave out the Apply. Plug the interface back in, wait until it is ready, and press Play. Expected: hidden until the interface's read answers, then its clock. Set Worker Pool Size back afterwards.
    8. A quick Play and Stop. Expected: the badge never disappears; if it vanishes at that Stop, report it (the driver's source list read just after a close would then be empty).
    9. On the internal clock, because an interface slaved to a 48 kHz word clock cannot run at 44.1 kHz, and during playback, because with no stream open Audio Settings refuses a change of rate on the interface (the paragraph at the head of these checks): with "… 48k" showing, set 44.1 kHz in Audio Settings and press Apply. Expected: the stream reopens; from the reopen at 44.1 kHz the badge is hidden until the new stream's read answers, then "… 44.1k"; once the stream has reopened at 44.1 kHz, never "48k" (ruling E142); a driver that asks to be reset at the change adds "Reconfiguring audio engine…" and then "Audio engine reconfigured", and the badge still ends on "… 44.1k". Press Stop. Expected: "… 44.1k", dim, with the closed tooltip. Set 48 kHz back the same way, during playback. If Audio Settings says "Sample rate 44100 Hz was rejected by the audio driver.", the driver did not take the change while streaming: write down the driver, and the rest of this check does not apply; any other result: write down the notices and what the badge showed, and report it.
    10. On the internal clock, as for check 9: during playback, change the rate in the driver's own control panel and close the panel. If the driver then asks the host to reset ("Reconfiguring audio engine…" appears), expected: "Audio engine reconfigured" after it; the badge keeps the rate the engine runs at ("INT 48k" in a 48 kHz session), dim while the stream reopens and then bright; it does not hide and does not show the rate the panel set (ruling E168); the Xruns count restarts at the reopen. If "Reconfiguring audio engine…" appears while the panel is still open, expected: playback stands still, the Engine cell reads "Engine: reconfiguring…" and the badge shows its closed view until you close the panel, with no loss notice; once you close it, "Audio engine reconfigured" and the badge as above (ruling E278). Write down the rate the panel then shows and whether playback changed speed or pitch, for future story 474. If "Reconfiguring audio engine…" does not appear, write down the driver, the rate its panel shows and whether playback changed speed or pitch, and the rest of this check does not apply. Any other result (a loss declared, or no badge after the reopen): write down the notices, whether the panel was still open and what the badge showed, and report it. Whatever the result, also where the rest of this check does not apply, set the interface back to 48 kHz before check 11, because the later clock checks name 48 kHz ("INT 48k", "W/C 48k") and, while the interface runs at another rate than the engine, every Play is at the wrong speed and pitch (rulings E168 and E170). First, unless playback runs with the badge bright, bring it back to the interface: close Audio Settings if it is open, then after a loss choose Reconnect and press Play, and otherwise press Stop and Play (in a project with no instrument insert, as check 11 needs). Then, during that playback, set 48 kHz in the driver's own control panel and close the panel, or set it in Audio Settings as the paragraph at the head of these checks says, and close Audio Settings if it is open. If playback then does not run with the badge bright, write down the notices, report it and bring playback back to the interface once more as above. Go on when the badge shows "INT 48k" bright during playback and the interface's own software or control panel shows 48 kHz; otherwise, also when playback does not come back to the interface, write down the driver, what the badge shows and the rate the interface's own software or control panel shows, report it and leave out the later steps on the interface.
    11. On a driver that offers none of Audio Settings' default buffer sizes, leave this check out (the paragraph at the head of these checks). With playback stopped and the dim closed view showing, open Audio Settings, change Worker Pool Size, press Apply and close Audio Settings. Expected: the dim view goes at the Apply, because with no stream open no driver is loaded on the interface, so the read the Apply asks finds no clock source and cannot prove the clock on show (ruling E169). Press Play. Expected: hidden until the read answers, then the measured view. Set Worker Pool Size back afterwards.
    12. Play on an external source ("W/C 48k" or S/PDIF). Choose Internal in Audio Settings' Clock source list, wait until the list can be opened again (the lead-in above), then close Audio Settings and press Stop at once, within about a second. Expected: one of the views ruling E166 names, most often nothing, because the driver's lock report trails the switch by up to a second; write down which. Repeat from the external source (press Play, choose it in the Clock source list as the lead-in says, and once the badge names it choose Internal again), and this time, after closing Audio Settings, wait until the badge names INT and then 2 s more before Stop. Expected: "INT 48k", dim, with the closed tooltip; if the badge is empty after that wait, the driver's lock report still names the source you left: write down the driver and report it; if it shows the source you left, write down the driver and report that too. Play again. Expected: "INT 48k" once read. A refused switch keeps the old source (ruling E138).
    13. Play on a backend that reports no clock information while a badge was on show: within one session, check 7's Play that falls back is such a Play, because a change of Audio backend in Audio Settings applies in a new session, which starts the badge with no label. Expected, there: the badge hides within a moment of that stream's open.
    14. A watch, not a test: do not try to provoke a failed start. If a start of the audio stream ever fails, look at the badge; the failure's notice is in the words of the gesture: "Audio output refused for playback — …" at Play ("… for pre-roll playback — …" at a pre-roll, "… for recording — …" at a Record with only MIDI tracks armed), "Recording aborted — no take was started: …" at a Record with an audio track armed, "Audio output could not be started: …" at an Apply or a Restart Audio Engine, "<name> asked to be reset and could not be reopened — …" at a driver's reset, "<name> could not be reopened: …" or "Reconnect could not start. …" at a Reconnect, and "Plugin audio output could not start: …" when an instrument is put on a channel. Expected: nothing, or the clock of the device that Audio Settings names, dim, with the closed tooltip; never the device used before an Apply; never bright and never "UNLOCKED"; no "External clock lost" notice and no take. When the failed start followed a press of Record, or putting an instrument on a channel with the transport stopped, a flicker of the live view during the start is allowed; if "UNLOCKED" or an "External clock lost" notice appears there, that is ruling E146's limit on real hardware and does not fail the story: write down the interface, the driver, the clock source and roughly how long it took from the press to the error notice, check that no take was added (no new clip and no new file in the project's audio folder), and report it.
    15. In a project with an instrument insert (a Virtual Keyboard or a Signal Generator on a track's insert; a CLAP plugin does not count, ruling E163), on the internal clock: press Play, then Stop, then Play, and Play again, which pauses. Expected: from its first measured view on, the bright internal label ("INT 48k", for instance) stays bright at the Stop and at the pause, the dim closed view's "Clock source when the audio stream last ran. Lock is not checked while the stream is closed." never shows, and no Play brings back "Lock is not checked yet on this stream." (ruling E163). With the rebuilt `asioshim.dll`, choose the word-clock source in Audio Settings with its cable in, press Stop once the badge shows its measured view, and pull the clock cable. Expected: "⚠ W/C 48k UNLOCKED" within about 2–3 s and "External clock lost on <driver name> (<source>). Check the clock cable and the clock master.", with no sentence about recording. If no "⚠ … UNLOCKED" appears within about 5 s: if "<name> stopped responding" appears instead, the interface stopped streaming when it lost the clock and the device-loss path ran, as check 2 allows (ruling E30): reconnect the cable, wait until the interface is ready, choose Reconnect, press Play and then Stop, and go on to the USB cable; if nothing appears, do as check 2 says for that case (ruling E147's limit), then reconnect the cable and go on to the USB cable. Otherwise reconnect the cable. Expected: "External clock locked again on <driver name> (<source>)." With the transport still stopped, pull the interface's USB cable. Expected: within about 2 s (max(2 s, 8 buffer periods)) "<name> stopped responding — audio stopped. Check that it is connected and powered, then choose Reconnect." with Reconnect, the Engine cell "Device lost: <name>" and the badge hidden, the transport still stopped; a notice of the dying interface before or beside the loss notice is not a defect (rulings E114, E159 and E160); if the driver reports the unplug as a reset request first, as in the story 327 proof's last sentence, the notices are "Reconfiguring audio engine…", then "<name> asked to be reset and could not be reopened — audio stopped. Check that it is connected and powered, then choose Reconnect." with Reconnect instead of the stall notice, with the same Engine cell and the badge hidden, the transport still stopped (ruling E113); if the reset's reopen instead lands on another backend's default output (the provision ladder's fallback, as in check 7), the reset's notices are "Reconfiguring audio engine…" and then "Audio engine reconfigured", nothing is declared, no Reconnect is offered and the stream then runs on that device: write down that device and report it, and the rest of this check does not apply; if nothing is declared within about 5 s and "Reconfiguring audio engine…" did not appear, the driver keeps calling back without its device: write down the interface and the driver and report it (future story 461). Plug it back in and wait until the interface is ready; if a loss was declared, choose Reconnect. Expected: "<name> is responding again. Press Play to continue." Press Play, then Stop. Last, remove the instrument from the insert and press Stop. Expected: the dim closed view of the clock on show, with "Clock source when the audio stream last ran. Lock is not checked while the stream is closed."
  - Not an owner check, because no hardware path provokes them: ruling E139 (a Reconnect whose thread cannot start), verified by the six tests of `ReconnectAgainstOtherOpensTest` named in the Resolution, and ruling E143 (a start that fails after its open), verified by `ClockBadgeDuringATakeTest.aPlayWhoseStartFailsAfterItsOpenIsReadOnceAndKeepsTheClosedView`, `anApplyWhoseRestartFailsAfterItsOpenShowsTheStartingViewThenTheClosedOne` and `AudioEngineOpenedStreamTest`. R3's readings R2, R3, R4 and R6 cannot be told apart on hardware from clock checks 3 and 5; their unit tests are the evidence.
  - CI on push (`ci.yml`, `ubuntu-latest`) and the Windows ASIO-shim workflow are the owner's.
- Not verified: the native export (no agent built it); any run against a real ASIO driver or audio device; starting the application; the strip's ellipsis and the popover on a real window (headless JavaFX only); the real-wiring proof of the badge's device and rate rules through a fallback to another device; and the items Known limits lists as proved only by reading.

The ASIO integration tests skipped themselves without `asioshim.dll`, as at the baseline, and were not forced; no test of the change set branches on the operating system, so no Linux run is owed.
- **The confirming review of the last change set**, read-only, by an agent that wrote none of it: pass. It found E271 built as ruled for every order the ruling names, at the monitor and, through every interleaving of the reset request with the opening thread's read and clear of it, at the backend: the stream that answers the request, or the next one that starts, gets the window, and no other stream gets one, a plain reopen included; E248 to E250, E260 and E269 unchanged; E272 built on both backends; and the drain loop's Javadoc and the padding and border comparison as the code is. One should-fix finding: the clause this change set added to the Javadoc of `AudioBackend.clockLockEvents()` names the reset window as the only exception to "a resync counting as one bad answer", though the intervals of the driver's control panel and of an answered switch are exceptions too, which that Javadoc had already left out before this change set; the clause was then rewritten to name, as one category, every interval the person's own change accounts for (rulings E122, E137, E138, E248, E249, E260 and E271). Two notes: nothing pins the ASIO drain's 20 ms backstop, which the ASIO bridge's contained wake relies on (pinned afterwards by `AsioBufferSwitchShimTest.aBlockThatReachesTheCaptureRingWithNoWakeIsStillPublished`, which, with the drain's timed park made unbounded, failed at its bounded wait, the only failure of its run; with the file restored with a plain `cp` and a `touch` and compared with `cmp`, its class then passed); and the skill sentence the specification gave named only the PortAudio adapter, so the skill now names both bridges (`dawg-native-libs`, "### Upcall guards"). What it did not check: no gate had run on the change set when it read; whether the engine's reopen after a reset request always reuses the same `AsioBackend` (Not verified, below); and whether an out-of-memory error while the outgoing publisher's close waits for its lock can also leave a drain's publisher open after its replacement was assigned, a case the drain loop's Javadoc does not name and the review could not make reachable.

- No test of the round branches on the operating system, so no Linux run is owed; CI runs the gate on `ubuntu-latest` when the owner pushes.
- New production files: `ClockLockAnnouncer.java` (219 lines; 218 before the closing change sets) and `InputBlockLossListener.java` (56), and, from the closing change sets, `DriverResyncArrivals.java` (53) and `ResyncArrivalRing.java` (147), each under 900; `ClockStatusIndicator.java` is 821 lines, `AsioClockLockMonitor.java` 881 (815 before the round's last change set, 840 before the closing change sets), and `EngineStreamPump.java` and `CaptureRing.java` 899 each (`wc -l`, when this was written); every other production file the closing change sets changed is under 900 lines too, apart from those already over 900 before them, which they did not split.
- **Not verified.** Any run against a real ASIO driver or audio device, and the application started in any form: the badge's ring and chip and the dialog's note were measured headless on colours resolved from the stylesheets in a real themed scene, never by eye, and glyph ink beyond its own font's ascent, a fallback "⚠" for instance, is not measured (ruling E233). The real drivers' behaviour that rulings E248 to E251, E260, E269 and E271 rest on: whether a driver keeps calling back while its control panel is open or its external clock is missing, when it sends its resyncs around the panel's return, a stream's start and the reopen its reset request asks for, and for how long, whether it sends its reset request inside its panel's call, at the panel's return or later, and how long its polls take. That the engine's reopen for a driver's reset request always opens the same `AsioBackend`, whose clock monitor carries the request to the reopened stream: read in the code only (`DefaultAudioEngineController.performFormatChangeReopen` calls `AudioEngine.startAudioOutput()`), and the backend tests drive the reset, the close and the open directly. Whether a contained wake of a drain ever leaves the render drive without its block in practice (ruling E272): reasoned from the code, not measured. PortAudio's open and close of the `InvokerInputLoss` record run only with the PortAudio library; `PortAudioBackendTest.theCallbackStubAttachesItsInvokerToTheFailedPeriodLedger` pins the stub's line from the class file. Each drain's 20 ms backstop, which its bridge's contained wake relies on, is pinned (the PortAudio drain's by `CallbackBackendAdapterTest.aDrainNoWakeReachesStillPublishesEveryBlockInOrder`, the ASIO drain's by `AsioBufferSwitchShimTest.aBlockThatReachesTheCaptureRingWithNoWakeIsStillPublished`); a failure of either bridge's wake of its drain thread is pinned from the bytecode only, since no test can make it happen (ruling E272); ruling E269's take with a count-in is read in the code only. Whether the display pulse runs while the window is minimized (ruling E224), and whether the badge's look and the take's frame sampler share one pulse in production. The memory ordering a ring's drop counts rest on when the ring's producer changes thread mid-take, an input beside the stream handed off to the output clock (consult 51; on one producer the counts' store and load orders are pinned from the bytecode). The suspicions of future stories 486, 487 and 488, read in the code only. From the closing change sets: the early seal's window has never been seen with a real disk sealing a take early in a running application (the app's tests set the read through the coordinator's seam; only daw-core's tests seal for real), and that a notice never claims a line take.manifest lacks rests on the memory-model ordering of the hand-over, the `sealed` store and the seal's drain, argued and not tested (ruling E280); how a real driver behaves when it applies a change while its control panel is open, whether its panel is modal to the window and whether it sends another reset when the panel closes (ruling E278): the wait was tested with a spy's panel only, and its resume from the branch where the watched device no longer matches has no test; real drivers' resync threads and rates, which the bound of 256 kept arrivals assumes are about one request per buffer period at most, and the request whose message call is under way as the feed reads, which no test holds (rulings E279 and E292); how many gap= lines a ring that stays full writes and what a manifest rewrite per pass costs on a slow disk, not measured (ruling E273); and, of the premises of rulings E295, E298 and E299 that consult 53 did not trace in the code, consult 54 traced two by reading, that a callback claims at most one slot per ring behind the producer gate and that the controller's loss notice reaches the same notification bar and history and stays there, and left the third untraced: whether the take's completion or the record coordinator says anything about a track that recorded no clip, a take.manifest stop line for it among it, which E301's seal test, whose file holds no frame, also leaves open. E299's loss notice kept in the history is pinned by no test (the test calls the coordinator's loss directly, so no controller notice is shown), and the order on the FX thread of that notice and the take's publication is not traced (the history holds both either way). From consult 54 and the fix rounds after it, settled by no agent: that an input opened beside the stream can deliver into the same take after a reopen replaces its plan (E302's tests drive the stream's own ring); that E302's silence start, at the block with which the input delivers again and at both seals, places input-starved drops that came after the silence as if before it, and counts a dropped block that carried a recorded instrument's audio after the silence both as silence and as audio after it, which places the silence one block earlier for each such block, never before the end of the last block kept; only the punch and loop judgement can feel either (the engineer's note, corrected at the acceptance read against `CaptureRing.claim`, read in the code only); how the application lets a person record with the loop on, loop recording off and a punch region, the configuration of E300's first case, whose code path is reachable (`RecordingPipeline` takes a loop-record take only when the transport's loop is on); whether production ever leaves a track's session unset, which `hasAnUncoveredTrack` counts as a track the stop does not cover, as `endStarvedRun` gives it an entry; that the stream's start after an Apply fully re-initialises an ASIO driver (E304, taken from the controller's comments, not checked in the native shim), whether the ordinary open tells the person when the device ends up at a rate other than the project's, which a rate-change request E304 ended no longer announces, and that a driver that answers the person's own reopen with a new reset request still gets its reopen (unchanged by E304); that a request arriving after the Apply's take-up stands, which no test drives, and that the Settings dialog's rollback after a failed apply ends a request that arrived during it, read in the code only; the E304 tests' schedule, which rests on the format-change worker blocking on the controller's monitor within the await's bound; the premise of the stale arrivals the last fix pass forgets, that an Apply can close and reopen the stream within one 100 ms engine-health tick (the test stops and starts the stream between two ticks); and the call of the feed's `startAfresh` at a change of the watched heartbeat's kind, which has no test of its own and shares its forget with the epoch's call, which the break covers. Left as notes by the review of resync dating: a tick whose take of the arrivals answers none while its counters do not (the stream reads not open between the two reads, or the backend's answer is `null` or throws) leaves that tick's entry undated, as E292 allows, and the next tick's too, though the feed then holds every arrival of the second, which the reviewer judged very unlikely in production (N1); the break of `AudioEngine.takeDriverResyncArrivals()` that drops only `!isStreamOpen()` survives (N2; Declined); and, with two driver threads and one preempted across a take, a read of the ring's slot can give the clock-lock monitor, for a request already taken up, the arrival of the writer of the request 256 after it, later than that request's own arrival but inside its unfinished message call, E292's accepted case of the operating system holding the driver's thread there (N3). Left as notes by the review of the reset wait: the second half of `ResetRequestWhileTheDriverControlPanelIsOpenTest.theWaitHoldsNoMonitor` holds for two reasons, the stall refusal on a render drive the reopen stopped and the panel's own refusal, so a break of the panel's check alone would pass it (the check is older than the round, and the test's monitor half is pinned; note 3); and when a Play after the panel's close opens on another rung before the resume, the resume asks the Play's backend about the request's device, inside a `try` and `catch`, a branch no test drives (note 5).
- **The owner's checks of this round**; none was run by an agent. Each that uses the application starts it as the head of the owner's checks above says: `mvn -B -o -DskipNativeBuild=true -DskipTests install` at the repository root, then `mvn -pl daw-app javafx:run`, with the interface's buffer size and rate set as that head paragraph says.
  - **The badge and the dialog by eye, in each theme** (Settings, Appearance, Theme: Onyx Refined, Studio Slate, Atelier). In a project with no instrument insert, after a Play and a Stop on the ASIO interface: the dim view is the clock's words in an outline with no fill; during playback on the internal clock, the bright view is the same words on a filled chip; going from one to the other does not move the transport status label beside it. With the rebuilt `asioshim.dll`, pull a locked word clock's cable during playback: the ring of clock check 2, the words readable in both phases. From a mixer channel's "CPU Budget…", the note under the policy choice is readable. Report any view whose words are hard to read, naming the theme and the UI Scale.
  - **An external-clock unlock and relock during a take.** With the rebuilt `asioshim.dll`, record on a locked word clock, pull the clock cable for about 5 s and put it back, then Stop. Expected: the take's `take.manifest` holds `clock=unlocked|…` then `clock=locked|…`, read in line order. Then, with the cable out and "⚠ W/C 48k UNLOCKED" on show during playback, press Record with an armed audio track and no count-in, put the cable back after about 10 s and Stop. Expected: the first clock line of that take is `clock=unlocked` at the take's `start-frame` (ruling E269), and the unlock notice comes again with "Recording continues; the take is marked from this point.", and, once the cable is back, "External clock locked again on …" and a `clock=locked` line after the `clock=unlocked` line. Write down the driver and what the badge showed in between.
  - **The driver's control panel during a take.** With the rebuilt `asioshim.dll` and the interface locked to a connected word clock ("W/C 48k" bright during playback), during a take on the interface, open "Open driver control panel" from Audio Settings, change the clock source there, close the panel and go on recording for 10 s, then Stop. Expected: no "External clock lost" notice and no `clock=unlocked` line from the panel's change; any resyncs the driver sent raise Xruns, each with a `clock=resync` line; no "stopped responding" within about 5 s of the close; the badge follows the new source. Then, with the take running again, change the clock source in the panel to internal and back to the word clock before closing it: expected, the badge stays or returns to "W/C 48k" bright with no "External clock lost" notice and no `clock=unlocked` line. A real loss of the clock while the panel is open shows only from the first bad poll after it closes (Known limits).
  - **A panel change that makes the driver reset.** With the rebuilt `asioshim.dll` and the interface locked to a connected word clock ("W/C 48k" bright during playback), record on an armed audio track; during the take open "Open driver control panel" from Audio Settings, change a setting that makes the driver ask to be reset (the buffer size, for instance), close the panel, record 15 s more without touching the clock cable, and Stop; then do the same during playback with no take. Expected: "Reconfiguring audio engine…", then "Audio engine reconfigured", and the take goes on across the reopen (future story 464); the badge shows its starting and checking views and then "W/C 48k" bright again, with no "External clock lost on …" notice, no "⚠ W/C 48k UNLOCKED" and no `clock=unlocked` line; any resyncs the driver sent raise Xruns, each with a `clock=resync` line (ruling E271). Write down how far the Xruns number rose and every `clock=` line the take holds after the reopen. A clock-loss notice, the UNLOCKED badge or a `clock=unlocked` line with the cable connected means the driver sends resync requests for more than about 2 s after its reopen; report it with the driver's name and version. With the panel still open when the driver applies the change, the Engine cell reads "Engine: reconfiguring…" until you close the panel, and then the stream reopens; "asked to be reset and could not be reopened" must not appear.
  - **A driver whose control-panel call returns at once.** On such a driver (clock check 4's last sentence), change the clock source in its panel during playback and close it. Write down whether the badge follows the new source and whether an "External clock lost" notice appears.
  - **An Apply in a driver request's window** (a watch, not a test: a hand meets the request's 250 ms window only by chance). With the rebuilt `asioshim.dll`, whenever you press Apply in Audio Settings close to a driver's reset request (a buffer size changed in the driver's own control panel, for instance), open the application's log (Open Log Folder in the Help menu opens its folder) and look for "Audio settings applied while a format change <device> asked for was in its window; the settings stand". When it is there, expected: the buffer size and rate you applied stand, in Audio Settings and on the stream, and no "Reconfiguring audio engine…" or "Audio engine reconfigured" follows for the driver's request; with no panel open the audio stops and starts once, for your Apply only; with the driver's panel still open at that moment, the notice "Audio output could not be started: …Retry once the panel closes", the audio stopped, nothing reopening when you close the panel, and the next Play opening at your buffer size (ruling E304). Write down the driver, the sizes, and how many times the audio stopped and started.
  - **An input that delivers again after a driver reset during a take** (a watch, not a test: it needs the engine to have declared an input stopped during the take, a driver reset whose reopen succeeds under the take, and a full capture ring, none of which a hand can arrange). If a take across a driver reset (future story 464) ever shows the warning "Track 'T': input from 'D' stopped delivering audio at m:ss.s into the take." ("… into lap N of the take." with loop recording) or "Track 'T': input from 'D' delivered no audio before it stopped.", with nothing after it about what the track records or holds, expected: the take's `take.manifest` holds that track's `input-stopped` line and a `gap=` line for the blocks the ring dropped, the take's dropouts count them (in a punch take, when they reach into the punch region), and that track's audio after the `gap=` line plays early by that many blocks against the timeline, the metronome and the tracks recorded before this take; the take's other tracks from the same input device move with it, so they stay together (ruling E302). Write down the warning, every `input-stopped` and `gap=` line the take holds, and what you hear there.
  - The ASIO integration tests, with the native build check above; the push, CI on it (`ci.yml`, `ubuntu-latest`) and the Windows ASIO-shim workflow.
